*Daily nutrition* is the period's total of a metric divided by the number of days that know it (for a single day, that day's own total), so a day with nothing known doesn't pull it toward zero. *Average per fully priced meal* divides the cost of the planned meals that have a price and nothing left out (no gap, and every item has a cost) by their count (`fullyPricedMeals`, `avgPerFullyPricedMealCents`): a meal with even one ingredient unpriced is a floor, and averaging floors read as a real price. *Price per gram of protein* divides what those same meals cost by their protein grams, over the ones that know their protein (`proteinPricedMeals`). Neither can say "so far" or "+", because nothing in their base is left out; each is not shown under three such meals (`MIN_FULLY_PRICED_MEALS`), and both name their base in the label. `mealsPriced` (meals with at least one priced item) now only decides whether the Total has a figure.

---

## State Machines & Logic

### 1. Quantity and cost

```
toBase(amount, unit) = { dimension: UNIT_DIMENSION[unit], value: amount × UNIT_TO_BASE[unit] }

bridge(q, targetDimension, ingredient):
  same dimension                → q
  MASS   → VOLUME (needs gramsPerMl)  value ÷ gramsPerMl
  VOLUME → MASS   (needs gramsPerMl)  value × gramsPerMl
  MASS   → COUNT  (needs gramsEach)   value ÷ gramsEach
  COUNT  → MASS   (needs gramsEach)   value × gramsEach
  VOLUME ↔ COUNT                      via MASS (needs both)
  otherwise                     → null   (a NOT_COMPARABLE gap)

lineCost(line, ingredient, product):
  no product                    → gap NO_PRODUCT
  q = bridge(toBase(line.amount, line.unit), productDimension, ingredient)
  q is null                     → gap NOT_COMPARABLE
  cost = q.value × (product.priceCents ÷ toBase(product.sizeAmount, product.sizeUnit).value)
```

Cents are rounded once, at the display edge, never per line, so a recipe's total doesn't drift from the sum of its rounded lines. A product's size of `EACH` ("12 count") compares directly with a recipe line in `EACH`, and with mass or volume only through `gramsEach`.

Worked example: chicken breast, Walmart, $9.48 (948¢) for 3 lb → 3 × 453.592 = 1360.776 g → 0.6967¢/g. A recipe line of 2 lb = 907.18 g → 632¢ ($6.32). A line of `1 cup` chicken breast has no `gramsPerMl` → `NOT_COMPARABLE`, left out and named.

### 2. Nutrition

Per metric, independently. Ingredient values are per 100 g, so a line's grams (bridged as above) give `grams ÷ 100 × per100g[metric]`. A recipe's per-serving value is the sum over lines ÷ `servings`, unless `nutritionOverride[metric]` is non-null. An ingredient with `null` for a metric, or a line that can't be bridged to grams, adds a `NO_NUTRITION` gap for that metric only. Nutrition is computed for all four metrics whether or not they're switched on; the switches only choose what is rendered and which gaps are surfaced (`gaps.ts` takes the `showNutrition` switches). In the recipe form `NutritionReview` shows the calculated per-serving values as `MacroStats` tiles (with a warning tip when some ingredients have no nutrition). Edit turns the four numbers into inputs: in place with Done from `sm` up, and on a phone in a `DetailSheet` drawer ("Nutrition per serving", four compact `Field`s, Done). A changed number is saved as that metric's `nutritionOverride` while a number equal to the calculated one stays derived (`null`), and "Reset to what we calculated" clears the overrides (in the drawer it refills the fields and leaves the drawer open). `MacroStats` and `src/utils/macroUtils.ts` are where calories (whole numbers) and macros (grams) are formatted.

**Typing an ingredient's nutrition on the label's basis.** `IngredientFormSheet` asks "Per [amount] [g | oz | serving]" above the four numbers (starting at 100 g), so what is typed is what the label prints, and `getPer100g` turns it into the stored per-100 g values (`toPer100g` over `getEntryGrams`, see Derived values; a blank metric stays `null`). The serving option needs the form's own serving size to convert to grams through `getServingGrams` (the ingredient's density or weight each): until it does the pill is disabled and a hint says so ("…set the serving size in grams or ounces first, with Serving size below." when the size is off, pointing at the Serving size chip, which is the one door to that field). A blank or non-positive amount, or a converted value outside 0 … `MAX_NUTRITION_VALUE`, shows one line and keeps Save disabled; any basis other than exactly 100 g shows "Saved as per 100 g: …" with the converted figures. An existing ingredient reopens in per 100 g, since that is what is stored. `NutritionEntry.isTyped` stays false until a number field is edited: while it is, picking g, oz or serving converts the numbers on screen to the new unit from the per-100 g value (`switchBasis`, per 100 g × grams ÷ 100, two decimals), so what is stored doesn't move; once a number has been typed, picking another unit only relabels the typed numbers and "Saved as per 100 g" shows what that makes. Display never changes a number elsewhere: the ingredient drawer and an ingredient row both read the Kitchen list's View options basis (`useListViewOptions(uid, 'ingredients').basis`, default per 100 g), and the drawer's thin Per serving / Per 100 g `PillGroup` writes that same setting rather than keeping its own. The pills are left out and the drawer shows per 100 g when one serving is exactly 100 g or can't be weighed, and a row falls back to per 100 g when the serving can't be weighed; `scaleNutrition` does the per-serving numbers. Recipes, meals, gaps and stats always compute from per 100 g. `npm run check:pinch-math` covers the conversions.

### 3. Copy-on-plan, edit semantics and the past

| Action | Result |
|---|---|
| Plan a saved meal on a day | one `plannedMeals` document: items copied with new row ids, `sourceMealId` set, `name` set |
| Plan a single recipe or ingredient | the same document with one item, `sourceMealId: null` |
| Edit a saved meal | the `meals` document only; planned days unchanged |
| Edit a planned meal | the planned document only (items, servings, date, type) |
| "Save to Saved meals" on a planned meal | a new `meals` document from its items; the planned meal's `sourceMealId` is left as it was |
| Edit a recipe | `recipes` document only; every non-frozen planned meal that points at it reads the new values |
| Edit a price, serving size, nutrition or conversion | first `freezePastMeals`, then the write |
| Day passes | its planned meals are frozen the next time the app loads that month (below) |
| "Price this day with today's prices" | every frozen meal of that day gets a fresh snapshot from the current prices and recipes, in one transaction (below) |

**A planned meal's name and the plan builder's picks.** `getPlannedMealTitle` names a planned meal everywhere it is titled (the day drawer's rows, the planned meal's header, the month peek, the calendar, the Shopping "for" line; a plate's cooking title keeps the plan's own name and otherwise names the session's dishes through `getPlateTitle`, see cooking mode); a plate with no name of its own is named by its items through `getPlateTitle` ("Adobo chicken & Cilantro lime rice", three in a list, past three two and "and 2 more", the first and "& N more" once a name runs past 30 characters, "N more" joined with no-break spaces so a heading never breaks inside it), and its `namedCount` tells the day drawer's and the month peek's muted line which items are still to say ("With …" lists only those). `updatePlannedMeal` writes only date, type, items and `refIds`, so a Modify that changes the items keeps `name`: a tweaked Taco night is still Taco night. "Save to Saved meals" (a row in the planned meal's ⋯ More) writes a new `meals` document and leaves the planned meal's `name` and `sourceMealId` as they were, so its row hides by comparing plates (kind, ref and servings), not by a link. The plan builder's recent picks are read, not stored: `rankRecentPicks` (`utils/planPicks.ts`) orders recipes and saved meals by the latest of their `createdAt`, the `createdAt` of a loaded planned meal that holds them (a recipe item, or `sourceMealId` for a saved meal) and a recipe's last cook (`selectCookSummaries`). Planned meals come only from the listener's window (shown month ± 2), so a recipe planned only long ago ranks by its cooks and creation. Two records that share an id are ranked once, so the list's keys (`RECIPE:<id>`) never repeat. The builder offers `getOfferablePicks` of them (a recipe already on the plate, and a saved meal whose items all are, are left out), at most 12, as `RecentPickRows`: six rows, then "Show N more" and "Show fewer" only when folding would hide more than three (`getRecentRowWindow(total, isExpanded, openingTotal)`), kept after the first pick and shown while the search is empty. `MealBuilder` owns the fold (`isRecentExpanded`, so typing and clearing the search doesn't fold it again) and the row count when the screen opened (`openingTotal`): a fold exists only if it opened with more than nine and stays until six or fewer remain, so a pick never makes hidden rows appear at once, and a list that opened unfolded never grows one. `ItemSearch` keeps the typed text itself and tells `MealBuilder` only when the field goes from empty to typed or back (`onTypingChange`), which is what hides the recents; a keystroke then renders the search and its results, not the plate, the Cost tile and the recents (Taylor at 4× CPU with four dishes on the plate: the median keystroke long task went from 149 ms to none, the one that starts typing stays about 200 ms). Each row reads the Recipes list's View options (`useListViewOptions(uid, 'recipes')`: prices and the stock line) and the same selectors the Recipes rows do (`selectRecipeCosts`, `selectStockByRecipe`, `selectStockByMealWithRecipes`, `getMealCost`). Leaving asks only when something changed: `getBuilderSnapshot` (the plate's rows by kind, ref and servings, the trimmed name and, for a planned meal being changed, its day and meal) is taken when the builder opens and compared with the live one, so a new empty plate and an edit left as it opened close at once. `MealBuilder` hands one `goBack` to its `Subview` as `onBack` and to Cancel, so the header, Cancel, Escape and the gesture share one confirm through `useActionModal` ("Keep editing" is the default, "Discard" destructive); `isAskingRef` ignores a second press while it is open, a write already in flight leaves without asking, and the close is `closeSubviewIf` for the subview it opened as. The search takes focus on open (a new plan with nothing on the plate) only when `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` matches, as the readers do, so a touch screen's keyboard never covers the recents and the footer; under `(max-height: 449px)` the footer's note is dropped and the disabled primary carries it. The builder's When row is `getWhenSummary` over a `SummaryEditor` with `isFramed`, and the footer's reason is `getBuilderNote` (what is missing for the primary: a day, a name, a plate). `getDefaultMealType` and `getNearDayOptions` in the same file give the starting meal and the day strip. Pure, checked in `check:pinch-math`.

`freezePastMeals` runs in two places: `useFreezePastDays` (from `Pinch.tsx`) once the kitchen has loaded and again for any unfrozen past meals a newly loaded month brings (each meal is submitted once per visit, so the list shrinking as writes land never starts another freeze of the same ones; a failed attempt isn't retried until the next visit or the next edit's freeze), and as the first step of every action that changes a value the math reads (`saveProduct`, `setDefaultProduct`, `removeProduct`, `saveRecipe`, `removeRecipe`, `saveIngredient`, `removeIngredient`, through `freezeLoadedPastMeals`). It never runs while a listener has failed, since the numbers it would freeze come from incomplete data. "Past" is the viewer's local day (`getLocalTodayDate`), the same clock the calendar shows as today, so an evening in a time zone behind UTC doesn't freeze the day still being planned. It finds loaded planned meals with `snapshot === null` and `date` before that, computes each snapshot from the current data, and writes `snapshot` field-scoped (no reads), in one `writeBatch` per 100 meals so each batch is one snapshot event for the listeners. A batch is all-or-nothing, so a refused one (a meal another device froze first, or deleted) falls back to one `updateDoc` per meal, settled independently: the refused meal is denied alone and doesn't stop the rest. The rules let the app's own freeze write `snapshot` only from `null` (a snapshot written over an existing one without an instant `repricedAt` is refused), so a stale device can't overwrite history; only the re-price below can. A freeze that fails never blocks the edit that triggered it (`freezeBeforeEdit` swallows it): a person's own price or recipe change isn't held up by bookkeeping, and the cost is only that those meals stay open until the next freeze.

A frozen meal still lists its items from the live recipes and ingredients (so a deleted-then-archived recipe still shows its title), but its totals come from the snapshot. A meal planned on a day that has already passed is frozen at once (`planMeal` writes the snapshot with the meal, unless a listener has failed, in which case it is left open and the load-time freeze takes it). A frozen meal can't be edited: `updatePlannedMeal` is a transaction that refuses it (a snapshot would no longer match its items), the planned meal view hides Modify, and the way to change what it contains is to remove it and add it again; the way to change only its prices is to price the day again. Frozen gaps still count in what a total says it leaves out but are not offered as nudges, since a new price doesn't change a fixed day by itself (`PeriodStats.openLeftOut`); once a price would change it, the drawer offers to price the day again instead. The copy for a frozen day says the same thing: its warning says a price added now counts from today on, its cost tile carries the line "Fixed when the day passed" (or "Priced with today's prices on Oct 14" after a re-price) and reads "Not priced" when nothing could be priced, a row with no price is a dash, and a fixed day's partial total reads "$0.15+" like its rows and never says "so far" (`formatCostTile`), since it can't be priced by itself (an open day says "No price yet" and "$18.40 so far", which invite a fix).

**Pricing a day again (`repriceDay`).** `RepriceDayRow` is the only caller, in the day drawer, and renders only when the day has passed, the kitchen has loaded without a failure (`hasCompleteCostData`) and `wouldRepriceChange` is true for at least one meal of that day, computed in a `useMemo` over that day's meals and the cost context, so opening a drawer costs one `getMealCost` per meal on that day and nothing else is walked (the 1,100-meal fixture included). The confirm shows the result before anything is written: at the tap, `getRepricePreview(meals, costContext)` (`utils/mealMath.ts`, covered by `npm run check:pinch-math`) runs `buildRepricedSnapshot` over the day's frozen meals from the loaded store and adds up the totals, and `getRepriceLines` (`utils/mealFormat.ts`) words them: "Before" in the cost tile's own words ("Not priced", otherwise the total), "After" as "about $0.66" (about, because the transaction reads the meals again), and "N ingredients still have no price" only above zero. It runs on the tap, not on render. The thunk checks the same preconditions again, takes the day's frozen meals from `selectPlannedByDay`, and runs one `runTransaction`: it re-reads every one of them with `transaction.get`, stops with one sentence and nothing written if any is gone, no longer frozen or no longer on that day, writes nothing when today's prices change none of them, and otherwise writes every meal of the day (a day is never half re-priced, so its label is the same for all its meals): `transaction.update(ref, { snapshot, updatedAt })` where `snapshot = buildRepricedSnapshot(...)`, which is `buildSnapshot` over the meal's own items and the current cost context (the same function the freeze uses, so the numbers equal what a freeze run today would write, nutrition included) with `frozenAt` kept and `repricedAt` set. The prices and recipes come from the loaded store (as for the freeze), the meals from the transaction's own reads. A day is limited to `MAX_REPRICE_MEALS` (400) meals so it stays under Firestore's 500 writes in one transaction; the limit is the action's, not the rules'. A transaction needs a connection, so a write that can't reach the server fails with "Check your connection and try again." (any other Firebase error is logged and shown as one plain sentence). Nothing else is written: not the meals' ids, dates, items, `refIds`, `name` or `sourceMealId`, not their cooks, the shopping list or any note. `useFreezePastDays` and `freezeBeforeEdit` select `snapshot === null`, so a re-priced day (non-null) is never touched by them; the person's own later price edits don't move it either, and they can ask again, which writes nothing and hides the row once today's prices change nothing. The copy never claims more than is known: a snapshot that has `repricedAt` reads "Priced with today's prices on {date of the latest}" (an instant, in local time), one that has none reads "Fixed when the day passed" (a day where only some meals were re-priced says "Some meals priced with today's prices on …"), the tile with no price reads "Not priced" (before and after; its detail line says which), a line with no price is a dash, and nothing says "as they were that day".

*Honest limit:* a meal is frozen from the data on the first load after its day, not the data on the day itself. A price changed during a gap in use, in a month the window never loaded, can leak into history for meals that were never frozen. The freeze-before-edit step keeps this to meals older than the loaded window.

### 3a. Archive on delete

```
removeRecipe(id):
  usedInMeals    = meals.some(m => m.items.some(i => i.kind === 'RECIPE' && i.refId === id))
  usedInPlanned  = (await getCountFromServer(query(plannedMeals, where('refIds', 'array-contains', id)))).data().count > 0
  usedInPlanned || usedInMeals  → updateDoc({ isArchived: true })   // hidden from lists and pickers; a recipe's updatedAt is left alone, since a share link compares it (§5k)
  otherwise                     → deleteDoc  (+ delete the cover from Storage)
```

The same shape serves custom ingredients (also checked against `recipes.ingredients[].ingredientId`, saved meals and `products`) and stores (against products). Products are deleted outright (the ingredient's `defaultProductId` is re-pointed to another product, or `null`, in the same transaction), and the MVP has no way to delete a family. The confirm names what will happen ("…is used in 3 planned meals and 1 saved meal, so it will be hidden, not erased"), from `getUsage` (the loaded collections plus one server-side count of the planned meals that list the id, so offline it says it can't check instead of undercounting); the remove action asks again itself, so the confirm text can never disagree with what is done to a record that changed in between. The read is a query, so it can't sit inside the delete's transaction: a planned meal added in the instant between the read and the delete is the one race left, and it would show the removed record as "Removed recipe" until the person re-adds it.

Archived items never appear in pickers, search or lists (`selectRecipes`, `selectIngredients`, `selectStores` are the visible lists; the `selectAll…` ones and every `…ById` map still hold them, so `Retired chili` keeps its title and cost on the day that used it). A catalog store the person archived shows as addable again in the store pickers. Selectors that resolve a reference (`resolveIngredient`, recipe titles for planned items) still find them.

### 4. Gaps and nudges

`getGaps(scope)` walks the lines in a scope (one recipe, one meal, a day, the month) and returns `{ kind, ingredientId, detail }[]`:

- `NO_PRODUCT` — a priced ingredient with no product, or a recipe/ingredient item with no price at all
- `NOT_COMPARABLE` — a product exists but the amount and the package size can't be bridged
- `NO_NUTRITION` — only for a metric in `showNutrition`

A gap is **hidden** when `dismissedNudges["{kind}:{ingredientId}"]` exists: the record is still read, so a line closed before stays quiet, but nothing writes new keys any more (`dismissNudge` has no caller; `saveProduct` still clears the keys for an ingredient it prices). "Not now" on a `GapNudge` quiets that line until reload, kept in a module-level set keyed by uid and the sorted gap keys, so a different set of gaps speaks again. The line names the gap that weighs most: `utils/gapOrder.ts` builds one index of where each ingredient is used (`getIngredientUses`: the listed recipes' lines and the unfrozen planned meals' lines through `getItemsLines`, each converted to rough grams, volume at 1 g/ml without a density and a piece at 50 g without a weight; memoized with `weakMapMemoize` on its four store inputs, so the drawer, the Calendar and the sheet share it) and `rankGaps` orders the gaps by it. A nudge renders only when the filtered gap list is non-empty, and only where the gap is owned: the recipe drawer and the calendar's summary sheet (the saved-meal drawer, the day drawer and the planned meal view keep their cost tile, the day's called Total, and its warning and render no nudge). A total that excludes lines always says so, whether or not its nudge is quieted, in the figure ("so far" on a tile, "+" on a per-serving cost) and through `LeftOutTip` (two sentences: no price yet vs can't be compared, and where to add them). `NudgeCountNote` and its "asks about fewer" line are gone.

The fill-in sheet takes the gap list and renders one row per ingredient with only the fields that gap needs: a product's store, price and size for `NO_PRODUCT`; the size for `NOT_COMPARABLE`; the metric for `NO_NUTRITION`. Saving writes through the same actions as the full forms. A row left blank stays unchanged; a row that is part-filled is not saved and says what it still needs, so Save never writes half a product. A saved row asks to become the ingredient's default product, and a `NOT_COMPARABLE` row rewrites the size of the default product it is about. The rows are ranked once when the sheet opens (`rankGaps` over the shared index, so saving a row never reorders the rest), each says `getUseText` ("Used in Adobo chicken and 1 more · 2.5 lb planned": the plan's need summed in the unit of its biggest line when the units convert, else the one recipe's amount), a row's store starts on the store last picked in this sheet, and its size unit on `getPackageUnit` (mass units as the recipe has them, any volume as fl oz unless ml or l, each for a count; the ingredient's serving unit when nothing uses it). The last row's Save or Skip closes the sheet onto the screen behind; returning to the drawer it came from would need the screen to remember its opener (not built). Kitchen's "Needs a price" filter hands what it shows to the sheet as `NO_PRODUCT` gaps through its "Add prices" button.

### 5. Recipe ingestion (AI)

```
idle → reading(source) → draft(recipe, unread[]) → (person edits) → saved
             │                                           
             └→ failed(message)    (always ends on idle for the next try; nothing was written)
```

- Entry point: a new recipe lists its ways to start through `RecipeStart`, rows only from the shared `MethodPicker` (required `onPick`, one `Method` per `RECIPE_START_METHODS` entry): **From text** (up to `MAX_PASTE_CHARS`), **From a file** (a photo, screenshot or PDF up to `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`; JPG, PNG, WebP, HEIC, PDF through `getUploadMimeType`; images through `compressIngestionImage`; what the picker and the drop accept is `isReadableFile`), **From a link**, **Write it yourself** (dashed, quieter) and, last and also dashed, `KEEP_AS_IDEA_METHOD` ("Keep it as an idea", which calls `onKeepAsIdea`; it is a row of the same `MethodPicker`, not a `RECIPE_START_METHODS` entry, because it makes no recipe). On a phone "New" in Recipes and Calendar's "Add your first recipe" raise `RecipeStartDrawer` (a `DetailSheet`), which `Pinch.tsx` renders once at its root from `isStartOpen` in `PinchOverlayContext` (`setIsStartOpen`), so any screen can raise it; picking a way closes the drawer and opens `RecipeSubview` already on that way (`{ kind: 'recipe', id: null, method, fromDrawer: true }`; `fromCalendar` marks one started from the calendar's empty state, carried through the drawer by a ref in `Pinch.tsx` and read by the form's save for its "Added … to your recipes" toast). From `sm` up they open the subview with no `method`, whose first stage is the same list. Editing an existing recipe goes straight to the form.
- `RecipeSubview` stages are `start | text | link | file | idea | form`. Picking text, link or file shows `RecipeReader` for that way (`components/recipes/RecipeReader.tsx` holds the three views), titled with the way's name; "Write it yourself" (on the list, and as the dashed `MethodPicker` row each reader ends with) goes to the form (asking first when a link or text is typed), and a finished reading goes to the form with its result. `RecipeSubview` passes `Subview` an `onBack` (`goBack`), which the header's back button, Escape and the browser's or phone's back gesture all call. On a reading screen every way out of typed work goes through `confirmDiscardTyped(action)`, which asks `confirm` "Discard this link?" / "Discard this text?" (Keep editing / Discard, destructive; the message names the action: "If you go back now…", "…start a blank recipe now…", "…paste its text instead…") while `isTypedRef` says the reader holds a pasted link or text (the reader reports it through `onTypedChange`; an address that came with an idea doesn't count), and returns whether to go on: `stepBackFromReader` (the header, Back, Escape, the gesture), `startBlank` (the dashed "Write it yourself" row) and `switchToText` ("Paste its text instead", which also clears `kept`). Typed words are never carried into the blank form or the text screen: a link has nothing to carry, a pasted recipe is far too long for a description, and "Save as an idea" already keeps it. `askToDiscard` holds an `isAskingRef`, so a second route used while a question is open does nothing and one question is ever open (the dialog takes the first Escape and the first back gesture, the subview the next). After a Keep editing focus returns to the control the question came from. Back then raises the phone's start drawer again (`closeSubview()`, then `setIsStartOpen(true)`) when the way came from it (`fromDrawer`), closes when it came from an idea, and returns to the start stage otherwise; on the start page and the form it calls `leave`. `leave` closes the subview, first asking `useActionModal().confirm` "Discard this recipe?" (Keep editing / Discard, destructive) while `isDirtyRef` is set. `RecipeForm` sets that ref when its data differs from the initial data, when a cover is chosen or removed, and when it started from a reading; `RecipeSubview` also sets it as a reading lands. Cancel goes through the same `leave` (`onLeave`); saving and deleting close with `closeSubviewIf` and an identity check: the subview showing when the person tapped must still be the one showing, so a save that lands late never closes a screen it didn't start (a slow save, then back and Discard, then Cooking mode: the form is gone and the cooking screen stays).
- Each reader opens with an `IntroCard` (`@/components/IntroCard`), on screen before anything is sent (text, link and file alike): each leads with one sentence of what the AI does ("An AI reads the page / your text / the file and fills in the form.") and adds "You look it over before anything is saved." A reading opened from an idea says "From your idea: {title}" in the card's lead (`ideaTitle`) and that the link is opened by the function, never the person's device (for a YouTube link it says an AI watches and listens to the video instead, since nothing is opened). Each fact is said once: the card says what the AI does, the field's own hint how to get the input ("Copy the address from your browser." for a link), the footer note why the primary is off; the words between the card and the primary at 390×844 (card, hint, dashed row) are 32 for link, 30 for text and 44 for file. The input is one framed card (`FormSection` caption, the field with no border of its own, a hairline, one line that is the hint, the reading state or the failure; a failure never clears what was typed, and typing clears it), then the `ActionChips` row ("Save as an idea", or "Try this file again" after a failed file, with `RefreshCw`: `RotateCcw` is Reset on the other screens), then the quiet `MethodPicker` rows, "Paste its text instead" (only on the link way, only while the failure says the link or what was read is the trouble, and never on a reading opened from an idea) above `manual`, then `ModalFooterActions` as a direct child of the reader's root (`Subview`'s `form-footer` rules pin it): Back (`onBack`, the same `goBack` as the header), and the primary, "Fill in the recipe" (disabled and noted "Paste a link first" / "Paste the recipe first" while empty) or "Choose a file" ("Choose another file" after a failed reading). The file way keeps the picked `File` in state, shows it in `FilePickCard` (`@/components/FilePickCard`: the empty dashed slot is a button for the picker and a drop target whose `title` is its action ("Tap to choose a file", one `Upload` icon) over `accepts · limit`, the picked file shows `formatFileSize` from `@/utils/fileUtils`) and retries it from `handleFile`. `useIsMounted` drops a reading that finishes after the reader left, so it can't call `onRead` and move the person to the form. The field takes focus on mount only when `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` matches. A picked and a dropped file go through the same `handleFile`: `isReadableFile` (`@/utils/fileUtils`: `application/pdf` or `image/*` by type, or one of png, jpg, jpeg, webp, heic, heif, gif, pdf by extension when the browser left the type empty), then `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`; each refusal has its own sentence ("That file isn't a photo or a PDF."), leaves the slot empty (`rejectFile` also remounts the hidden input so the same file can be picked again) and makes no request. A link without a scheme gets `https://`, and anything that isn't an http(s) address with a dotted host is refused before any call.
- A failed reading is classified by cause, not by where it was caught: `classifyReadFailure(error, navigator.onLine)` in `utils/readFailures.ts` (pure, checked in `check:pinch-ingest`) returns `unreachable` (offline whatever the error says; a callable that failed, timed out or was refused by the platform; an AI call that never got an answer), `busy` (a 429 or a 5xx from the AI, or the function out of quota), `unavailable` (the AI refused, 403 or 404), `link` (the function said the address is bad or the page isn't one, or its own "Couldn't load that link."), `unread` (anything else, such as an answer that isn't JSON) or `empty` (the reading found no ingredients). `getReadFailureLine(cause, source)` turns it into one sentence that blames only the cause, so a lost connection never says anything about the link, the text or the file, and no sentence names a door (the rows under it are the doors); `canPasteTextInstead(cause)` is true only for `link`, `unread` and `empty`, which is when the link reader draws "Paste its text instead". `functions/unavailable` is two things: the function's own message for a page it couldn't fetch (the link) and the platform's (the reader), told apart by that message; a network failure from the AI SDK is code `error` with a message ending "Failed to fetch" (only a non-OK response is `fetch-error`, with a status), so the message is read too. Every failure is still logged with `console.error` and never shown verbatim.

- `lib/extractRecipe.ts` exports `extractRecipeFromText(text)`, `extractRecipeFromFile(file)`, `extractRecipeFromPage(page)` and `extractRecipeFromVideo(videoId)`. All call the shared `generativeModel` from `src/lib/firebase/ai.ts` with a response schema: `{ title, servings, prepMinutes, cookMinutes, description, ingredients: [{ name, amount, unit, note, alsoCalled, group }], steps: [string], fridgeDays, freezerDays, reheat, tip, unread: [string] }`, where `unit` is an enum of the app's unit labels and the four storage fields are nullable (see §5f). The six ingredient keys are all required and each but `name` is nullable, so the model always answers the note and the group (left optional, it was dropping notes, "diced" included). The prompt tells the model to leave a line it can't measure with no amount and its words in the note ("to taste", "a handful", "as needed"), to split a line naming two ingredients ("salt and pepper to taste") into two with the same group, and to give each line the heading the recipe puts it under ("For the sauce:", "Marinade") as its group, null under no heading, never inventing one, as a short plain name within the 40-character cap ("Bulgogi sauce" for "Bulgogi sauce (don't skip the onion & apple!):"); the same ingredient under two headings is two lines. It also tells the model that text in parentheses after an ingredient is never the amount (a description such as "medium or short grain, if you can" goes in the note, another name such as "(ground beef)" in `alsoCalled`), that one amount written in two measures ("500g / 1 lb") is one line with the first measure, and the note and name caps (`MAX_NOTE_LENGTH`, `MAX_NAME_LENGTH`, read from `constants.ts` into the prompt, so they cannot drift). `normalizeExtractedRecipe` is the guard for a model that slips: `shortenGroupName` (`utils/ingredientGroups.ts`) takes the markdown, any parenthetical aside (closed or cut off), everything after a colon and closing punctuation off a heading (`asGroup`), cuts a still-long name at a word (`clipAtWord`, `src/utils/stringUtils.ts`; a dangling "and" or "the" goes too) and drops the recipe's own "Ingredients" title; `readAsides` moves a parenthetical left in a name into the note (a description), `alsoCalled` (two or three plain words, never a prep word or "optional"-style note word; one word stays a note, since "oil (olive)" may be a variety) or nowhere (a second measure such as "(240 ml)"); `mergeOtherMeasures` drops a line that repeats the previous one's ingredient and heading in another unit of the same kind (mass or volume, within 20% after conversion) and keeps the first-listed measure, so "500 g" then "1 lb" is one line (a cup and a gram weight of one ingredient are not compared: no density at that point); a line split from "salt and pepper" keeps its source line's group and gives neither half a second name. Notes are cut at a word too. Files go as `inlineData` base64. Extraction calls are not cached and not Query-backed (non-idempotent), like the other extraction calls in the repo.

- **From a link** fetches the page first through `fetchPageContent` (shared and app-neutral: `src/lib/pageContent/*`; Pinch's `queries/recipePageQueries.ts` asks it for `text`, `meta` and `structuredData` filtered to `schemaTypes: ['Recipe']`, kept ten minutes and never persisted, so a retry of a failed reading doesn't refetch). The callable (`functions/src/linkMetadata/fetchPageContent.ts`) is signed-in only, takes http/https links only, uses the same private-network guard as `fetchLinkMetadata` (`safeFetch.ts`), returns only the kinds asked for and stores nothing. `extractRecipeFromPage` hands the page title and description, the Recipe data (when the page has any, marked as possibly incomplete) and the page text to the model, with a line telling it to ignore ads, comments and related links; the model writes the draft, so a page with partial, missing or odd data reads where a hand-written parser would drop it. The client maps a bad link, an unreadable page and a failed fetch to one warm sentence each.
- **A YouTube link is read as a video, not a page.** `RecipeReader.handleLink` asks `getYouTubeId` (`utils/recipeLinks.ts`; `youtu.be`, `/watch?v=`, `/shorts/`, `/embed/`, `/live/`; a playlist alone is not a video and goes the page way) and, on an id, skips `fetchPageContent` and calls `extractRecipeFromVideo`, which sends Gemini a `fileData` part (`https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=<id>`, rebuilt from the id so a playlist, tracking parameters and a start time never go, `mimeType: 'video/mp4'`) ahead of the usual `PROMPT` plus `VIDEO_PROMPT`, through the same response schema and `normalizeExtractedRecipe`, so the matching, the caps and the review-before-save are unchanged. `VIDEO_PROMPT` tells the model to use the spoken audio and the on-screen frames together: an ingredient only said, or only a caption or overlay over music, still counts; a step only shown is a step; on-screen text wins a disagreement about an amount, time or temperature and the clash goes in `unread`; an unmeasured "splash" stays a note with no amount; sponsor reads and outros are ignored; the first recipe of a video that makes several is read and the rest said in `unread`. No Function, rule, index, source value or Storage path changed: the draft is saved as `AI_LINK`, its source label starts on `youtube.com` (the channel isn't known), and a failure reads "that video" (`getReadFailureLine(cause, source, isVideo)`) while the connection and reader causes keep their own sentences. The link preview is switched off for a YouTube link (`isEnabled` in `RecipeReader`), so the preview and the reading are still one fetch, which here is none. A video that is too long, or that the model times out on (the SDK aborts at 180 s with a bare `DOMException` that `classifyReadFailure` reads as `unread`), gets the same "may be private or too long" sentence, which is true of both; a 429 reads as `busy`. Nothing aborts a reading the person has left, so one that is still running finishes unseen. `getYouTubeId` refuses the two embed paths that are as long as an id (`videoseries`, `live_stream`), and `getHostSource` treats `youtu.be` as `youtube.com`, so one video shared either way starts the same source. **Unverified from the sandbox:** the Gemini Developer API's handling of a YouTube `fileUri` through the Firebase AI SDK (the `mimeType` it wants, the video length it accepts, what it answers for a private, unlisted or age-restricted video, and that the model key is allowed to read YouTube) had no network to run against; try a few real videos (spoken only, captions only, both) and a private one before relying on it. A video is billed by its length as tokens, so a very long video costs more than a page; the client has no length cap.
- The model output is **only a draft for the form.** `matchIngredient(name)` (`utils/ingredientMatch.ts`, a thin layer over the shared `createOptionMatcher` in `src/utils/optionMatchUtils.ts`, which also serves Nine Lives and Waypoint) resolves each ingredient name to an ingredient id against the person's custom ingredients first, then catalog names and aliases (compared lowercase, singular, punctuation stripped). The match only fires when sure, in four steps: the exact name or alias; the same name once the line's filler and prep words (fresh, chopped, boneless, bone-in, skin-on…) are dropped, from the line only and never from an ingredient's own name ("pork" is not "Ground pork"; ground, whole, minced and toasted are not filler), when only one ingredient is that (two are left for the person, the person's own ingredient winning a tie with the catalog's); the same words in another order, which Pinch turns off ("egg white" is not "white egg"); and a typo, which is one misspelled word of six or more letters with the same first letter and not a bare final s or d, with a whole-name similarity of at least 0.82 and one clearly best candidate (two equally close match neither). "Goat milk", "1% milk", "green tea", "grapeseed oil" and "beef stick" therefore stay unmatched. A line may give two names (`alsoCalled`, "beef mince (ground beef)"): `resolveReadName` matches each on its own by these same rules, so the second name links nothing the first could not, the person's own ingredient wins over the catalog's when they differ, and when neither matches the first name makes the new ingredient and the second is kept in the note. The catalog's ground meats answer to "X mince" as well as "minced X" (`scripts/catalog/catalogSource.ts`). Pinch supplies only its names, its filler words and `splitPrep` ("bone-in" and "skin-on") as the matcher's `names`, `ignoreTypedWords` and `prepare`; the matcher caches each ingredient's compared names in a `WeakMap`. No match → a name with no `findNearMatches` candidates is staged up front (`newName` on the row, one id shared by lines naming it, a "New" chip) and written in the same save as the recipe (one `writeBatch`); a name with candidates stays unresolved (`proposedName`, a "Choose" chip) until the person picks one or adds it as new, and only those rows block saving. Uniqueness of new names is the form's alone: a rename onto another new name merges the two (one id) when the field is left (while typing, only the row's own name changes, and emptying the field restores the name it opened with), a name equal to a saved ingredient swaps to it, and nothing in the rules stops two same-named ingredients from two devices. `getDataFromExtracted` writes a name nothing matched as the label `resolveIngredient` returns (prep words dropped, then `toDisplayCase`: first letter capital, the rest lowercase, short all-capitals words such as BBQ kept; `toIngredientName` in `utils/ingredientNames.ts` is the same casing for typed names) and appends the prep words it removed to the line's note (`addPrepToNote`, skipping a word the note already says), for matched lines too; only words that never name a different product are split off (bone-in, skin-on, boneless, skinless, trimmed, peeled, deveined, pitted, cored, seeded, rinsed, drained, softened, melted), so "diced tomatoes" and "crushed tomatoes" keep theirs. The reader's schema and prompt ask for the same shape (a singular, sentence-case name with prep in the note). The row's drawer opens on a Name field (`NewNameField`, shared with a staged ingredient's drawer: edits commit as the person types when not blank, casing is applied when the field is left, and an empty field returns to the last name) and then offers up to three close names (`findNearMatches` in `utils/catalogSearch.ts`: each word that names a thing, with filler and words like "shredded" dropped, finds the ingredients with a word of their name, then an alias, starting with it; the words take turns from the last), excluding archived ones and those already in the recipe.
- The prompt tells the model the pasted text is data, not instructions, and asks it to list anything it could not read in `unread`; the draft never writes anything itself, so injected text can at most fill form fields the person reviews.
- **What the link and file screens hold besides the box (round 8 fix).** `RecipeReader` on the link way, opened with no idea, lists the person's own saved links under the box: `selectIdeaGroups().toTry` filtered to ideas with a web address (`toWebLink`), as `IdeaRow`s in one hairline `rounded-2xl border divide-y` frame under "Links from your ideas", three at first and "Show N more" (`ShowMoreButton`, only past six, never more than ten in all) for the rest; a tap sets the link text through the same `handleTyped` a keystroke does, so the footer's primary wakes and nothing is sent until it is tapped. The block is drawn only while such an idea exists (a first run, or one with no saved link, sees no block and no line about ideas). On the file way the dashed box, and on the link way the framed address field, take the free height of a phone through `FormSection`'s `isFilling` (`max-sm:` only); the link field is a `Textarea` (`inputMode='url'`, one row at least, `field-sizing-content` so a long address wraps and grows, Enter starts the reading like the old input and never types a newline). Empty share of the screen between the last content and the footer at 390×844: link 24% to 3% with no ideas and 4% to 1% with Alex's (the page scrolls at 320×568 with his), file 36% to 3%; at 320×568 with no ideas link 8% to 3%.
- **The reading becomes the form's starting values.** Because the start list and the reading screens come before the form, `RecipeSubview` holds a finished reading (`{ recipe, source, label }`, the label being "your text", the file's name or the page's host) and hands it to `RecipeForm` as `initialRead`; `getDataFromExtracted` builds the form's initial data from it (a second line of an ingredient already a row under the same heading adds to it when the units agree, and stays its own line under another heading or in another unit; unmatched names become "Add as new ingredient" rows; the rows come out in group order). The form then says what it was filled from and lists `unread`, and nothing is saved until the person saves. `normalizeExtractedRecipe` also cuts every value to the limits the form and `firestore.rules` enforce (title, description, note, step lengths, servings, minutes, counts), so a long answer can't leave Add disabled or fail the write. It also reads an unmeasured line the way a cook means it when the model slips: "salt to taste" or "parmesan, for serving" moves the phrase to the note, "handful of fresh parsley" becomes parsley noted "a handful", and "salt and pepper" (no amount, two parts of one to four words, not the same word twice) is two lines, but only on a signal: a to-taste or for-garnish tail was found, or both halves are salt or pepper kinds (`SEASONING`), so "macaroni and cheese", "sweet and sour sauce" and "half and half" stay whole; a measured line is left as read and the split lines still stop at 60. `getDataFromExtracted` marks a line with no amount `isAmountless`, so it saves as it is.
- **The form's basics are summary rows.** `RecipeBasics.tsx` holds `RecipeTitleField`, `RecipeTimesField` and `RecipeDescriptionField`, each a `SummaryEditor` over the same form values as before (`title`, `basics.prep` / `basics.cook` as text, `description`), so dirty tracking, `onTitleChange` and the header's live name work through the editors unchanged; only Servings is still an inline `Stepper`. The title editor opens by itself once (`defaultOpen`) when `recipeId` is null and there is no `initialRead`. Validation is the same predicates as before (`title` required and at most 200 characters, `description` at most 2,000, `isValidMinutes` for prep and cook) and each shows on its row as well as in its editor; the pinned footer's note names the first thing to fix ("Give it a title to save.", "Shorten the title to save.", "Check the prep and cook time to save."). The two minutes fields are `type='number'` with a visible "min" suffix and an `aria-label` of "Prep time in minutes" / "Cook time in minutes"; no label carries "(minutes)".
- **Removals in the form are undoable.** `RecipeForm` owns one `useUndoRemoval` and passes `showUndo` to `RecipeIngredients` and `RecipeSteps` (`onRemoved`), so an ingredient and a step removed in a row can't show two bars at once; `MealBuilder` owns its own for the plate (and puts the saved meal's name back when Undo returns the last item). The `UndoSnackbar` is the form's first child, not its last, because `Subview overlay` stretches its last child to fill the screen. Dreamer UI's toast was not used: its action label is fixed when the toast is added, so it can't show the seconds left without removing and re-adding the toast every second.
- A reading that finds no ingredients at all is a failure ("We couldn't find a recipe in that"). Errors map to one warm sentence each (busy, offline, unavailable, unreadable) and the detail is logged, never shown.
- `source` is saved as `AI_TEXT` / `AI_FILE` / `AI_LINK` when the draft came from the reader, `MANUAL` otherwise (even if the person edited it).

### 5a. Recipe sources

- **Choosing one.** The recipe form's "Where it's from" row (the first row of the extras card after Nutrition; `RecipeSourceField`, a `SummaryEditor` whose editor holds the `PickOrCreate`; the row says the chosen source's name or the typed new one) is a `PickOrCreate` over the sources that at least one listed recipe uses (`selectSourceCounts.bySource`), plus the form's own starting choice, so a source left with no recipe stops being offered; a typed label still reuses any loaded source with that name, used or not (existing labels as pills, a dashed "New source" first, nothing preselected for a new recipe), with a text field in its place while there are no sources yet. The form keeps the answer as `{ choice, newText }`: a source id, `NEW_CHOICE` with the label typed, or empty. Saving resolves it in `RecipeForm`: a typed label that matches a loaded source (ignoring case and spaces) reuses its id, otherwise it becomes `draft.newSource` (a new id and the label) that `saveRecipe` writes in the same `writeBatch` as the recipe, like `newIngredients`, so a failed save leaves no orphan source and an offline save is queued whole; a picked id that no longer exists becomes `null`; the result is `draft.sourceId`. While a source is chosen the picker shows a visible "Remove source" chip (`PickOrCreate`'s optional `clearLabel`, an `ActionChips` chip with an ✕), besides tapping the chosen pill again.
- **Reading from a link.** A recipe read from a link (`source === 'AI_LINK'`) starts on its site: `getReadSourceChoice` takes the reading's label (the page's host), drops a leading `www.`, and picks the existing source whose label matches the host the same way (so "seriouseats.com" matches "www.seriouseats.com"), otherwise starts "New source" with the host as the text (cut to 80 characters). Only the form's starting value is set, so what the person picks or types afterwards is never replaced; the text field takes no focus on its own (`isFocusedOnOpen={false}`) so the keyboard doesn't open over the form. A recipe read from text or a file starts with none.

- **What a reading offers beyond the recipe (`utils/readExtras.ts`).** `RecipeReader`'s `run` takes a `read` that returns `{ recipe, extras }`, and `onRead` carries `extras` into `RecipeRead`, so `RecipeForm` gets it with the draft. `ReadExtras` is `{ photos: WebPhoto[], link: { kind: 'HOW_TO' | 'ORIGINAL', url } | null }`; a text or file reading returns `NO_EXTRAS`. **A page** (`getPageExtras`) offers `meta.image` (from the `meta` the page query already asks for, so no new request; kept only if `isCoverUrl` accepts it: https, no credentials, at most `MAX_COVER_URL_LENGTH`) with the page's host as `WebPhoto.host` (the site, not the CDN the file sits on), and the page's own address (`page.url`, else the typed one) as an `ORIGINAL` link. **A video** (`getVideoExtras`) offers `i.ytimg.com/vi/<id>/maxresdefault.jpg` then `hqdefault.jpg` (host `youtube.com`; both built from the id, so nothing is fetched to know them) and `getYouTubeWatchUrl` as the `HOW_TO` link: the usual `watch?v=` address, a start time kept only when it is digits with `h`, `m`, `s`, and a playlist, `si=` or any other parameter dropped. `getDataFromExtracted(extracted, ingredients, extras)` starts `links` from `getExtraLinks(extras)` (a fresh id, no label, so the default label shows), and `getDataFromIdea` does the same from `getIdeaExtras(idea)` (a LINK idea: its link, the picture it kept, or a video's thumbnail; a few words and a photo offer nothing). **The cover is set by the form, after a probe.** `RecipeForm` keeps the offered photos in a `useState` initializer and, in one effect, tries them in order with `canLoadImage(url, PHOTO_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS, MIN_COVER_WIDTH)` (`@/utils/imageUtils`, the check `RecipePhotoSuggestions` used to keep to itself: an `Image` with `referrerPolicy = 'no-referrer'`, so a host that refuses to be shown elsewhere is refused here, not after saving), and the first that loads becomes `sourcePhoto` and, if the person hasn't picked a cover meanwhile (`setWebCover((current) => current ?? photo)`), the cover. YouTube answers a missing large thumbnail with a 120 px stand-in, which `MIN_COVER_WIDTH` (200) refuses, so the standard one is used. It is set in an async callback, never in the effect body, and touches no dirty flag (an idea's untouched form still leaves without asking). Saving is the existing path: `coverUrl` is `webCover.imageUrl`, validated by `isCoverUrl` again in `saveRecipe`, kept as a web link, not a copy. `sourcePhoto` is also the first tile of the Photo editor's grid (`RecipePhotoSuggestions`), ahead of at most `PHOTO_RESULT_COUNT - 1` search results with the same address left out, so the grid stays six tiles and picking another never loses it; with photo suggestions switched off the editor has its upload only, as before. **Fallbacks for the words:** `extractRecipeFromPage` returns `withPageFallbacks(extracted, page)`: an empty title becomes the page's `<title>` up to the first ` | `, ` - `, ` – `, ` — ` or ` • ` (cut at a word to the title cap), an empty description the page's meta description (cut at a word to the description cap); a title or description the reader returned is never replaced. A video has no page, so it has neither. No Function, rule, index, Storage path or type changed. **What leaves the device:** reading is unchanged (the page is fetched by the Function, the video by Google's AI); what is new is that the person's browser requests the offered picture once (the probe, then the form's thumbnail), from the page's image host or `i.ytimg.com`, as it already does for a web photo the person picks. `scripts/checks/pinchReadExtras.ts` (in `check:pinch-ingest`) covers the watch address, both extras, the cover guard, the idea path, the fallbacks and the link the form starts with. **Unverified from the sandbox** (no route to `i.ytimg.com`): that `maxresdefault.jpg` answers an error, not a 200 with a small image, for a video without one (either is refused: an error by `onerror`, a small image by `MIN_COVER_WIDTH`), and what a page's social-preview image looks like across real recipe sites (some are logos or collages; the person sees it in the Photo row and the grid and can pick another).
- **Writing.** `saveRecipe` writes `sourceId` on every save (new or edit), so an edit backfills the key on a recipe that lacked it and the field-scoped `updateDoc` never touches it elsewhere. `renameSource` is a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `label` and `updatedAt` after checking the loaded list for another source with that label. `removeSource` is one `writeBatch` per 400 writes holding the source's delete (in the first batch) and `sourceId: null` on every loaded recipe that names it, archived ones included; a recipe's `updatedAt` is left alone because it versions the cover image. A recipe created on another device at the same moment, or one that wasn't loaded yet, keeps a pointer to a source that no longer exists, which reads as none (above); if a later batch fails the source is already gone and the rest read the same way, and the error is shown.
- **Failure.** The sources listener is an optional enhancement: its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded sources, never reaches the fatal load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out), and its first answer or error ends the loading gate. While it has failed the form leaves the section out and keeps a recipe's own `sourceId` as it was, so editing never drops a label it couldn't read; the Source filter question and the header's Sources icon simply don't appear.
- **Reading and counting.** `selectSources` (alphabetical), `selectSourcesById` and `selectSourceCounts` (`{ bySource: Map<id, count>, withoutSource }` over the listed, non-archived recipes, one pass) feed the Recipes screen. The Source filter is a `FilterBar` with one `FilterSection` holding a pick-one `PillGroup`: "Any", each source used by at least one listed recipe as "Label · count", and "No source · n" only when some recipe has none; a picked source or "No source" stays in the list while picked even if its count falls to zero. The Source question exists while any source exists (`sources.length > 0`); its pills appear while at least one listed recipe has a source, otherwise one line says none does, and it has no link to the Sources screen (that is the header's icon, below). The picked value falls back to "Any" when its source is gone or the pills disappear, and saved meals are never filtered by source. The way into the Sources screen is one icon button in `RecipesScreen`'s `SectionHeader` action, beside the New button in the same `flex gap-1` group the Calendar header uses for its eye and Plan: a quiet `tertiary` round `size-10` button with the lucide `BookOpen` icon, `aria-label` and `title` "Sources", drawn while `hasSourceSection` holds (the Recipes view and `sources.length > 0`, the Source question's own condition), so a first run, Saved meals and Ideas have none; it calls `openSubview({ kind: 'sources' })`. The recipe drawer has no door to the Sources screen (the owner wants the way to the sources on the Recipes screen, not inside a recipe), but it names its recipe's source: `RecipeDrawer` reads `selectSourcesById` and `getRecipeSource` and draws a `LabelChip` first in its who-it-is group, above the description and facts line, nothing for a recipe with no source or a deleted one. It is a door only while the source holds other recipes: `selectSourceCounts.bySource` (the one definition the Source filter's pills and the Sources screen count with, the open recipe counting itself) says more than one, and the chip is then a button ("From {label}" and a chevron, `aria-label` "From {label}: show its recipes"); a source that holds only this recipe draws the same soft pill as a plain label (a `span`: no chevron, no button, no hit area), since its list would be the one recipe already open. `keepEnd` cuts a long name in the middle ("From The big spiral-bound … church fair") by splitting the last word or two off into a span that doesn't shrink while the head truncates. A tap calls the required `onShowSource(sourceId)`, which `RecipesScreen` answers by clearing `openId` (the drawer unmounts, and its history entry steps back), calling `showSource` (the same four setters `useOnSourceShown` runs: Recipes pill, search and In stock cleared, source picked, so it works from the Ideas pill's "Open the recipe" too) and `scrollToTopAfterSheetCloses()`: a sheet's history step is followed by the browser restoring the scroll the page had when it opened, which would leave the chip above the viewport on a long list, so the jump to the top waits for that `popstate` (and gives up after a second). The drawer is mounted only by `RecipesScreen`, so no other screen needs a door of its own; a host that mounts it elsewhere has to say what `onShowSource` does there. `SourcesSubview` lists `selectSources` (a search from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD`) with `selectSourceCounts`. Every row is one `Button` with a chevron that sets `editingId` and opens `SourceFormSheet` (a source on no recipe too; there is no pencil), and focus returns to the row when the sheet closes (`closeSheet` focuses the remembered opener on the next frame, unless the row is gone because the source was deleted). `SourceFormSheet` takes `onShowRecipes`: for a source with listed recipes its first row is a roomy `ActionRow` ("Show the N recipes from X", a 📖 chip, a chevron) that the subview answers with `announceSourceShown(id)` and then `closeSubview()`, which unmounts the sheet with it (its history layer closes with it, so the next back press leaves the Recipes tab as usual); a source on no recipe shows the line "Not on any recipe" there instead, and the one delete is the trash in the sheet's footer, whose confirm says "It isn't on any recipe" for such a source. The subview is rendered by `Pinch.tsx` outside `PinchSessionContext` and the Recipes tab stays mounted underneath it, so it cannot write `recipes:view`, `recipes:query`, `recipes:source` and `recipes:stock` itself (its own copy of the hook would not reach the mounted screen's state): `announceSourceShown` (`hooks/useSourceShown.ts`, a module-level set of listeners like `useRecipeAdded`) tells the mounted `RecipesScreen`, whose `useOnSourceShown` callback (`showSource`) sets the Recipes pill, clears the search and the In stock filter and picks that source, all before the subview unmounts, so the tab reappears already filtered with the chip "From X". The Sources subview no longer creates sources (they are made in the recipe form's batch).
- **Searching recipes.** `buildRecipeSearchIndex(recipes, ingredientsById, sourcesById)` (`utils/catalogSearch.ts`) normalizes each recipe's title and the rest of what a person would type (ingredient names and aliases, the source's label, the description) once; `RecipesScreen` builds it only while a search is typed (memoized on recipes, ingredients and sources) and `searchRecipes(index, query)` keeps a recipe when every word is in the title or starts a word of the rest, titles first, each group A to Z. The source and In stock filters apply to that result, so an empty filtered result can say how many recipes match outside it. With no recipe match the screen counts saved meals (`searchByName`) and ideas (`getIdeaSearchText`) and offers to show them; `IdeasView` takes `initialQuery` so the search follows. The row chunks are keyed on the filters, not the typed text, so a keystroke updates rows instead of remounting all of them (Taylor at 4× CPU: about 100–145 ms per keystroke once the result narrows). Checked in `scripts/checks/pinchMath.ts` (R2 block).
- **After a new recipe is saved.** `RecipeForm` toasts "Added X to your recipes" for every new recipe (from New, an idea or the Calendar) with a Plan it action that calls `openSubviewFromToast({ kind: 'plan', … fromRecipeId })`; that overlay helper (`Pinch.tsx`) does nothing while a subview is up or a dialog is open, like `showCalendarDay`. It then calls `announceRecipeAdded(id)` (`hooks/useRecipeAdded.ts`, a module-level set of listeners); a mounted `RecipesScreen` answers in the listener callback by switching to the Recipes pill, clearing search and filters and setting `revealId`, which makes the row's chunk eager and an effect scrolls `[data-recipe-id]` into view. The old "Recipe added · moved to Made into recipes" toast is gone.
- **Deleting a recipe says what goes.** The confirm (built in `RecipeForm` at the tap, from `store.getState()`) names what a hard delete takes: "Its cooking history (4 times) and its 3 notes are deleted too" from `selectCookSummaries` and `selectNotesByRecipe` (or "Its cooking history and notes go with it." while either listener hasn't landed or has failed), and for a recipe an idea was made into, on delete and on archive alike, "“X”, the idea you made it from, goes back to your ideas." What is deleted or archived is unchanged.

### 5a.2. Recipe collections and favorite saved meals

- **Making one.** Only in the recipe form's Collections row (`RecipeCollectionsField`, a `SummaryEditor`: one line, "High-protein, Weeknight" or "Add to a collection", a drawer on a phone and a popover from `sm`, no field focused on open so no keyboard covers the pills). The editor is `PillOptions` over every collection (staged new ones first, then A to Z; a search past 12 and "Show all N" from `PillGroup`'s own rules) with a dashed "New collection" pill first that reveals a name field and an Add button (Enter adds, and never closes the editor or saves the recipe). The form holds `{ ids, added }` (`CollectionsChoice`); a typed name that matches a collection that exists or one already staged (`getCollectionKey`) picks it instead of making a second, a new one gets its id from `generateUuid` at once, and unpicking a staged one drops it. Saving runs `getCollectionsToSave` (ids that still name a collection, new ones still picked) into `draft.collectionIds` and `draft.newCollections`; `saveRecipe` writes each new collection in the same `writeBatch` as the recipe, like `newSource`, so a failed or offline save leaves no orphan and queues whole.
- **Caps.** At most 50 collections a person (`MAX_COLLECTIONS`): the form stops offering "New collection" and says so, the client only, since the rules cannot count documents (a second device can pass it; nothing breaks). At most 20 per recipe (`MAX_RECIPE_COLLECTIONS`): the form stops at 20 and says so, and the rules refuse a longer `collectionIds`. A label is 40 characters in the form and the rules. `check:pinch-math` reads `firestore.rules` and fails if the rules' 20 and 40 drift from the constants.
- **Writing and legacy.** An edit writes only the difference of `collectionIds` against the ids the form opened with (`getCollectionChange(opened, saved)` gives `{ add, remove }`; the draft carries `collectionChange`, not a list): an `arrayRemove` and an `arrayUnion` in the recipe's own batch (two updates of the one document when a save does both, removals first, since an array field takes one transform per update), and nothing at all while the row is untouched, so a collection another device added or took out after the form opened stays. A new recipe writes the key with its `add`. A recipe from before collections gains `[]` on its first save (or its `add` as an `arrayUnion` on the missing key). A form that couldn't offer the row (the collections listener had not landed, or had failed, when it opened: `ownsCollections` is read once at mount) sends `collectionChange: null`, which writes nothing (an older recipe still gains `[]`). The 20-collection cap is the client's check against the opened list plus the rule's `size() <= 20` on the result: a second device that filled the list in between makes this save fail with the generic message (an `arrayUnion` cannot respect a cap, and a transaction would not queue offline). Changing a recipe's collections is organizing, not changing the dish, so it records no tweak (`getRecipeChanges` doesn't read it). Driven with a recipe edited on one tab while another device changed its collections: the row untouched keeps `[Weeknight, Bulk]` (before: `[Weeknight]`), and remove Weeknight plus add Bulk beside another device's Party ends `[Party, Bulk]` (before: `[Bulk]`).
- **Deleting and renaming.** `renameCollection` is a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `label` and `updatedAt` after checking the loaded list for another collection with the same key. `removeCollection` takes the collection off every recipe in the loaded store that names it, archived ones included (`getRemovalChunks`, chunks of `COLLECTION_BATCH_SIZE` = 200), one `runTransaction` per chunk that re-reads each recipe and `update`s only those whose stored list still names it (`withoutCollection`, which returns null for a missing key, a non-list or a list that doesn't have it, so a recipe another device already took it off is not written and not counted), leaving `updatedAt` alone because it versions the cover; the chunks run in parallel and their counts add up to the number really changed; then it deletes the collection document last, so a chunk that fails leaves the collection in place to be deleted again. The confirm says how many listed recipes it is on ("They stay, and only come out of this collection"), the toast how many it came off. A recipe added to it on another device after the chunks were picked keeps an id nothing names, which every reader ignores. A transaction needs a connection, so an offline delete fails with "Check your connection and try again." and leaves everything as it was.
- **Reading.** `selectCollections` (A to Z), `selectCollectionsById`, `selectCollectionCounts` (`{ byCollection, withoutCollection }` over the listed, non-archived recipes in one pass; a recipe naming only a deleted collection counts in `withoutCollection`) and `selectIsCollectionsReady` (loaded without error). The listener (`startCollectionsListener`, from `usePinchSync`) is an optional extra like sources: its error is logged, clears the list, shows only in the UI it feeds (the form's row, the filter's section and the drawer's chips disappear), is not part of `selectIsPinchLoaded` and is left out of `selectPinchLoadError`.
- **The filter.** A Collections question in the Recipes `FilterBar`, drawn while any collection exists and the Recipes pill is on (`hasCollectionSection`): a pick-one `PillGroup` from `getCollectionFilterOptions` ("Any", each collection that holds a listed recipe as "Label · count", the picked one while it is empty, and "No collection · n" only while some recipe is in none), `filterByCollection` applies it between the source and stock filters, the session key is `recipes:collection`, it falls back to Any when the picked collection is gone or no recipe is in any, and the active chip reads "In High-protein" or "In no collection" (removing it or Clear clears it). One pick, not several: two picked pills could mean "either" or "both", and the pills' counts would then be wrong either way, so the intersection of two collections is not offered. The question's title line carries a 40px pencil (`Edit collections`) that closes the filter panel first (`FilterBar` hands its children a `close`) and opens `CollectionsSheet`: one `FormSheet` whose list (a tinted intro card under eight, a search from eight, a framed list capped so it scrolls on its own with a floor of about three rows at 320×568; each row is a 40px chip and `getRecipesLine`, "N recipes · " and `joinSourceNames(titles, 2)` (`A and B`, `A, B and C`, `A, B and N more`), `text-sm` clamped to two lines in a `min-h-16` row, 88px at 390×844 with two lines, the same anatomy as the drawer's rows) swaps in place to one collection's name and trash behind a `BackLink` and a destructive confirm on top. Collections are not made there. A collection's label is in the recipes search (`buildRecipeSearchIndex`'s optional fourth argument adds the labels of the collections a recipe names to the part of the haystack a word has to start a word of, after the title), so typing "weeknight" finds that collection's recipes; a collection named like an ingredient adds results after the title matches, which was judged helpful rather than noisy, and the collection filter is one tap away for the exact set.
- **The drawer.** `RecipeDrawer` draws where the recipe is from and its collections on one pill row (`flex`, no wrap; the source pill's name is cut in the middle first): the source pill, then one collection pill from `getCollectionsPill(labels)` (pure, in `utils/collections.ts`, checked in `pinchOrganize.ts`): nothing for none, the collection's name for one (a door with a chevron, `aria-label` "In X: show its recipes", only while it holds other recipes by `selectCollectionCounts`, else a plain label), and "In N collections" for several, a button that sets the drawer's `view` to `'collections'`, which swaps the body in place (`BackLink`, so the back gesture closes the layer) to `CollectionRows`, an `ActionGroup isRoomy` of `GroupRow isRoomy` rows, A to Z: a 🗂️ chip, the name and a line naming the other recipes in it ("With Chili and Soup", "With A, B and 3 more" through `joinSourceNames` with two names shown, or "Only this recipe"; the titles are grouped once per change of `selectRecipes` in a `useMemo`), a door only while `onShowCollection` is passed and `othersByCollection` has other recipes for that collection, and otherwise a chevron-less `div` (`GroupRow` without `onClick`, so no `aria-label` either): a collection holding only this recipe, and every row where it is not passed (the Pantry's drawer). It replaced `getCollectionChipWindow` and its two constants, which folded past seven pills on a second row: Adobo's ingredients start 142px under the title at 390 and 320, were 176px. A tap calls `onShowCollection`, which `RecipesScreen` answers like `onShowSource` (drawer closed, Recipes pill, search, source and stock cleared, the collection picked, scroll to the top after the sheet's history step). This puts them in the drawer's first group, who it is, with no fourth group and no fact said twice; a recipe with no source and no collection draws nothing, as before.
- **Suggestions.** `getSuggestedCollections(source, collections, memberIds)` is pure and checked. High-protein: protein per serving, rounded to the whole grams the form shows, at or above `HIGH_PROTEIN_GRAMS` (25). The protein is `getTrustedProtein`: the person's own typed number, else the added-up one only when no ingredient with an amount lacked a protein value (`getRecipeNutrition`'s `missing`) and every line is complete; a sum with an unknown ingredient is not trusted. Quick: prep plus cook at or under `QUICK_MAX_MINUTES` (30), both known and the total above zero (a missing time is not zero). Freezer-friendly: the recipe's care has `freezerDays`. A suggestion names the collection that matches by `getCollectionKey` (under the person's own spelling) or says it would make one, is left out once the recipe is in it, and is left out when it would make a 51st. They appear as `SuggestionChips` in the editor, and as "Suggested: High-protein, Quick" in place of "Add to a collection" on the closed row while the recipe is in none; a tap adds the recipe (making the collection in the same save), nothing is ever added on its own. **Vegetarian is left out on purpose:** nothing stored says it without guessing (the catalog's "Sauces & condiments" holds both fish sauce and soy sauce, "Broth & stock" both chicken and vegetable broth, and a custom ingredient has no family), and a wrong "Vegetarian" is worse than none.
- **Favorites.** `Meal.isFavorite` (a boolean; a saved meal from before has no key and reads `false` through `isStarred`). `setMealFavorite` is a field-scoped `updateDoc({ isFavorite, updatedAt })` of the value asked for, never a toggle read from a cache; `saveMeal`'s edit path writes only `name`, `categories`, `items` and `updatedAt` and never `isFavorite`, and a new meal writes `false`, so renaming a meal can't un-star it nor starring it lose a rename. The star is a `StarButton` (`src/components`, a 40px icon-only tertiary button whose icon fills when on, `aria-pressed`, a constant label "Favorite Adobo and rice") on `MealRow`, beside the chevron on the first-line band (a sibling of the row's button, positioned over its right edge, because a button can't sit inside a button), and in `MealDrawer`'s title line through `DetailSheet`'s `actions`. `useMealFavorite(uid)` is the one tap: it ignores a tap while the previous write for that meal hasn't shown yet (`shouldIgnoreStarTap`, so a double tap can't read stale state, and one deliberate second tap later goes through even while the first still waits for a connection) and says "Couldn't star X" in a toast when the write fails. `selectMeals` is `sortFavoritesFirst` of newest first (a stable partition: each group keeps its order), so every reader (the list, the plan builder's search, the container form) gets favorites first, and `rankRecentPicks` puts starred saved meals first in the plan builder's recents. The Recipes `FilterBar` gets a Favorites question (one "★ Favorites · n" pill, session key `recipes:favorites`, ignored once nothing is starred) on the Saved meals pill only while a meal is starred and there are at least eight saved meals (`LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD`, the list's own search rule: with fewer, the starred ones already lead and a filter bar would have nothing to narrow); its chip reads "Favorites". **Recipes are starred the same way:** `Recipe.isFavorite` (a recipe from before has no key and reads `false` through `isStarred`), `setRecipeFavorite` a field-scoped `updateDoc({ isFavorite })` of the value asked for, never touching `updatedAt` (it versions the cover), `saveRecipe`'s edit path (a `batch.update` of the form's fields) never writes it and a new recipe writes `false`, `useRecipeFavorite` the one tap (same guard and toast as the meals'), a `StarButton` on `RecipeRow` and in `RecipeDrawer`'s title line (beside the history door, through `DetailSheet`'s `actions`; the drawer calls the hook itself so every place that opens it gets the star); `RecipesScreen` sorts `sortFavoritesFirst` over the filtered list (the base order is alphabetical) and shows the Favorites question from the same threshold (`favoriteSource`: the shown list's own length reaches `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` and one is starred). The rules take the key (`isRecipe`: in `hasOnly`, not in `hasAll`, `data.get('isFavorite', false) is bool`). The recipe drawer has no star yet.
- **Adding many recipes to collections (`addRecipesToCollections`, `undoAddToCollections`).** Recipes' `useSelection` mode (`RecipesScreen`; the same `SelectionBar` and hook the fridge uses; `useBackStep` gives the tab one back layer, since it is not a subview) ends in `AddToCollectionSheet`, a `FormSheet` over the form's own `CollectionsEditor` (exported from `RecipeCollectionsField`, suggestions off). `addRecipesToCollections({ uid, recipeIds, collectionIds, newCollections })` runs `getChunks(recipeIds)` (200, the removal's `COLLECTION_BATCH_SIZE`) one after another, each a `runTransaction` that reads every recipe with `transaction.get`, writes the collections typed in the picker in the first chunk's transaction (so a failed act leaves none behind, and a chunk that retries sets them again, harmlessly), and updates only `collectionIds` through the pure `planAddToCollections(stored, ids, cap)`: a recipe written before collections reads as none, the ids it lacks are appended in the order picked up to `MAX_RECIPE_COLLECTIONS` (the rules' own `size() <= 20`, so `arrayUnion` could not be used: it cannot respect a cap), and the plan says `isAtCap` when some were left out. It returns `{ changed: [{ id, added }], alreadyIn, atCap, gone, createdIds, error }` (a recipe at the cap counts as at the cap, not as already in it; a later chunk's failure keeps what earlier ones wrote and says so; when nothing changed, the collections it made are deleted again). A recipe's `updatedAt` is not written: it versions the cover. `useAddToCollections` raises one toast (`getAddedToCollectionsText`: "Added 10 recipes to Bulk and Weeknight", then what it left alone, by why) with an Undo for six seconds, or resolves to the sheet's own sentence (`getNothingAddedText`) when nothing changed, and a rejection shows in the sheet. `undoAddToCollections` re-reads each recipe in chunks and removes exactly the ids that act added (`planRemoveFromCollections`: nothing is written to a recipe that no longer has them, and an id another act put there stays), then deletes a collection the act created if no recipe outside the undone ones is in it (the listener may not have caught up, so the undone recipes are not asked) and only when every chunk succeeded. Offline, a transaction fails with "Check your connection and try again." and nothing is written. **Rules:** `isRecipe` gains `isFavorite` (additive: in `hasOnly`, read with `get(…, false)`, not required), checked on the emulator as owner, another user and signed out, allowed and denied, against the previous rules, with a legacy recipe (no key): only the owner's star and a create carrying `isFavorite: false` are new allows. **Index:** none (no new query).
- **Categories (`DishCategory`, `Recipe.categories`, `Meal.categories`).** The kind of dish, from a fixed list of nine in `constants.ts` (`DISH_CATEGORIES`: value, label, emoji; the rules repeat the values, and `pinchSort.ts` fails if the two drift).
