A dish holds any number of them, so a smoothie is Drinks and Breakfast; there is no Lunch or Dinner (a dish can be either, and the plan's meal slots say when), and no "Other": none is the normal start and reads as "No category". Collections stay the person's own named sets; a category is the app's fixed vocabulary, and the two never share a field. `getCategories(item)` (`utils/categories.ts`) is the only reader: a missing key, null, an unknown value and repeats all come out as the known values in the list's own order. Every save writes the key: `saveRecipe` (the form's `categories` in `fields`, so an edit gains the key on a legacy recipe and a new recipe carries it), `saveMeal` (edit `updateDoc` and create), and the import and `savePlannedAsMeal` write `[]`. It is a plain field-scoped list, not an `arrayUnion`: only the form edits it. Rules: `isDishCategories` (a list of at most nine, `hasOnly` the nine values, `toSet().size() == size()`) in `isRecipe` and `isMeal`, in `hasOnly` and read with `get('categories', [])`, never in `hasAll`; unverified against the emulator from this branch (`List.hasOnly` and `toSet` are the two calls to confirm). A share leaves categories out on purpose (the copy names its fields; nothing in the share, its snapshot or the Functions changed), so an imported dish starts with none. The three readings do not suggest categories. **Filter:** `getCategoryCounts` counts a dish once under each category it holds (one pass over the listed recipes, or the saved meals on that pill), `getCategoryFilterOptions` lists "Any", the categories present with their counts and "No category · n"; `RecipesScreen` draws the Category question first, only while some listed dish has a category (`hasCategoryFilter`, which also brings the filter bar), keeps the pick in the session (`recipes:category`, reset on a view switch, a new recipe, and a source or collection shown from a drawer) and falls back to Any when the picked category has no dish left. **Sorting:** `utils/listSort.ts` (`sortList`, pure) orders by `createdAt`, `updatedAt` (the creation time stands in for a missing one) or the title through one module-level `Intl.Collator` (case and accents ignored, numbers read as numbers); the direction flips the comparison, never the list, so equal items keep their order either way. Default is the caller's own order (recipes are already alphabetical, saved meals newest first, ideas newest first; `RecipesScreen` still applies `sortFavoritesFirst` to recipes, and `selectMeals` already has stars first), and any other sort ignores stars. The choice is `usePinchSessionState` per view (`recipes:sort:recipes`, `…:meals`, `…:ideas`), shown through `ListSortControl` only where the search is (from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` rows, or whenever the filter bar shows, and at least two rows) and read as Default otherwise, so the control disappearing never leaves a hidden sort. **Titles:** a row title is `line-clamp-2` on its own span (never with `block`); the lazy lists estimate a row from the title's length (26 characters a line on a phone, 46 wider), a guess that only places unmounted chunks.

### 5b. Shopping list

- **Lines are pure functions** in `utils/shopping.ts`, checked by `npm run check:pinch-math`. `getRecipeLines(recipe, ingredientsById, servings)` scales each recipe line by `servings / recipe.servings`. `getItemsLines(items, recipesById, ingredientsById)` serves a saved meal, a planned meal and a whole day: a recipe item scales its recipe by the item's `servings` over the recipe's own, an ingredient item is its `servings` times the ingredient's serving size. A line whose recipe or ingredient is gone is left out. `mergeLines` adds lines with the same ingredient and unit, rounds once at the end to two decimals (thirds add up to one before rounding), caps an amount at 1,000,000 and drops a line that rounds to nothing. Units are never converted. A recipe line with no amount is `{ amount: null, unit: 'EACH' }` at every serving count; `mergeLines` keeps two of them as one name-only line, lets an amount take in a name-only line of the same item, and drops a name-only line when the batch has the ingredient with an amount in any unit (and keeps it when that amount rounds to nothing).
- **Adding** is `addToShoppingList({ uid, lines })` where each line carries its `source` (or null for a typed add): the lines are merged per item with every source behind them (`mergeSourcedLines`, which also drops a name-only line that an amount of the same ingredient absorbs and hands its sources to that amount, so a day with "Parsley" and "Parsley 30 g" reads as on the list) and checked (not empty, at most 200 distinct, an id of 80 characters or fewer, a known unit, an amount above zero or none in `EACH`), then one `runTransaction` reads every target document first and writes each: a missing item is created (`isChecked: false`), an unchecked one gains the amount (rounded to two decimals, capped), and a checked one restarts with the new amount and `isChecked: false` (a name-only line adds nothing to an open amount and restarts a checked item as just the name). Each written item's `sources` is `mergeSources(existing, added)` (a set, newest last, capped at 60), backfilling the key on an item written before it. That decision is the pure `getMergedFields(existing, line, now, isChecked)` in `utils/shopping.ts`, so `npm run check:pinch-math` covers it (a new line, an open item gaining an amount, a checked item restarting, the 1,000,000 cap, a unit move carrying its flag, an amount that rounds to zero, a recipe with no servings); the transaction code only reads and writes around it. `mergeLines` also drops a non-finite amount, so a recipe whose servings are 0 gives no lines instead of an endless one. Two devices adding at the same time retry on conflict, so neither amount is lost (checked in the browser with two pages adding one recipe at once). The manual "+ Add" uses the same action with one line at the ingredient's serving size and no source. A transaction does not queue offline, so adding needs a connection and says so by failing; ticking, changing and removing are ordinary writes and queue. `ShoppingPick` (in `components/shared/AddToShoppingSheet.tsx`, the body both the drawers' `AddToShoppingSheet` and the Shopping tab's "From your plan" render) builds its rows with `groupShopLines`: one row per ingredient and kind of unit (`getShopRowId` = ingredient + `UNIT_DIMENSION`), each row's tick writing all its lines; a line with just the name joins the amount row of the same ingredient (the first by id when several have one), so "Salt" is one row, not two. A row starts unticked when this sheet's own sources already put it on the list (below) or `isUsuallyAtHome` (section `spices`, or `USUALLY_AT_HOME_IDS`: water). A range request reads `selectPlannedByDay` through `getPlanInRange(plannedByDay, range, todayKey)` (days before the viewer's local today are left out, `isClamped` says so); the range is clamped to the loaded planned-meals window (`getMonthWindow(windowKey)`), the calendar can't pick outside it, and a sheet opened while the window doesn't hold today says the plan isn't loaded. The sheet's list and the Week summary's list keep a floor of 9rem on screens under 800px high (`[@media(max-height:800px)]:min-h-36`), so at 375×667 and 320×568 they show about three rows instead of none; on those heights the drawer body may scroll a little, the accepted fallback.
- **Defaults.** A sheet builds its lines per source (`getSourcedLines`: a planned meal's lines carry its id, unrounded, so shared amounts round once when they meet; a recipe's carry `recipe:<id>:<servings>` with the stepper's servings, a saved meal's `meal:<id>`). Sources are compared by key (`getSourceKey`: a recipe's id without the servings), so one recipe added at 4 and at 8 servings is one source everywhere it is read. An add onto an open item adds the servings up (`addSources`: 4 then 8 is `recipe:<id>:12`, an older `recipe:<id>` stays beside the new entry), and an add onto a got item, whose amount starts again, keeps only the new add's servings. `getRowOnList(lines, rowItems)` says, per row, which of the sheet's sources already put it on the list (open or got), whether every one has (`isCovered`, the row starts unticked), and what ticking adds: the lines of sources not yet there, or all of them once every source is there. An open item with no `sources` key covers its row (the guard every line had before). `getOnListHint` writes the row's line ("Already on your list for Thu dinner", "Already got it for …", "…; this adds the rest", or "On your list · 2 lb" when something else put it there), and the line under the sheet's "Adding 3 of 6" count is "Untick what you already have." while any row starts ticked, else "Tick what you need.". The day drawer's row uses `getDayOnList` (same helpers): "On your list ✓" when every planned meal with lines is a source of any item, else the count of covered rows.
- **The other writes are field-scoped.** A tick is one `writeBatch` of `update({ isChecked, updatedAt })` over every stored item behind the row (usually one; two for a row that combines units), so it still queues offline and touches only its own row (no surface-wide busy state). Changing an amount is `updateDoc({ amount, updatedAt })`. A tick and an amount edit never touch `sources`. Changing the unit moves the item in one transaction: the old document is deleted and the amount lands on the new unit's document by the same rules as an add, except that a new document takes the moved line's `isChecked`, so a ticked line stays ticked, and the row's sources (the union of the items it folds) move with it. Moving into an existing document follows the add rules: an open one gains the amount, a ticked one restarts open with only the moved amount, and the row sheet's note says which before saving. Remove is a `writeBatch` of deletes for the row's items, dispatched without waiting (so it queues offline) and followed by an Undo toast; Undo waits for the delete, then runs `restoreShoppingItems`: one `runTransaction` per chunk of 250 that recreates each item exactly as it was (its `createdAt` and `isChecked` kept, `updatedAt` now) unless it exists again, which keeps what it has. Like an add, Undo needs a connection. Saving a row that stands for two units passes `mergedIds` to `updateShoppingItem`, whose transaction deletes the row's other items and writes the typed amount to the target unit. "Clear" takes the ids of the checked items from the store when it is dispatched, then deletes them in chunks of 250, one `runTransaction` per chunk: each re-reads its documents and deletes only the ones that are still checked, so an item another device un-ticked or removed in the meantime survives (a stale cache can't delete it), and the thunk resolves to the items really deleted, as they were, so the toast can count them by row ("Cleared 15 items", with "The rest changed in the meantime" when fewer than were ticked at the tap) and its Undo can put them back with `restoreShoppingItems`. There is no confirm. Clearing or removing deletes the document, so its sources go with it, and Undo puts them back. Chunks are independent transactions, so several chunks are not atomic together: one that fails leaves the others cleared and the list shows what is left. A Firebase error is logged and replaced by one plain sentence, "Check your connection and try again." for `unavailable` or when the browser is offline; the app's own sentences ("This is already off your list.") are shown as written.
- **The caps are limits of the app, not of the database.** 200 distinct lines per add and about 300 items on the list are enforced in the action and the screens only: the rules can't count a collection, so another client could pass either. Nothing breaks if it does (the list is longer, and a section still draws 100 rows then "Show N more").
- **Reading.** `selectShoppingRows` groups once per change (`groupShoppingItems`): what is left by shop section in walking order (`SHOP_SECTIONS`; `getShopSection` takes `SHOP_SECTION_BY_INGREDIENT`, then `SHOP_SECTION_BY_FAMILY`, else `other`, so a custom family and a gone ingredient are Other), one row per ingredient and kind of unit (`ShoppingRowData.itemIds`, largest first; ids end in `/got` in the Got it half so the halves never share one), rows A to Z by name; a row's amount is `getShopAmount` (summed in the unit of the largest item, a count rounded up, null for under a tablespoon or 15 g of a spice or oil) and its text is `formatShopAmount`: cups and spoons through `formatKitchenAmount` as the cooking tray reads them (0.38 cup is "6 tbsp", 1.5 cups "1½ cups"), weights and volumes to two significant figures (2056.35 g is "2100 g", 2 lb and 4 oz "2.3 lb", 0.37 lb stays), counts and pinches as they were; it is display only, the item keeps its exact amount and its own sheet edits that. Every surface that prints a shopping amount goes through it: the list's rows, the add sheets' rows and their "on your list" text, the item sheet's per-meal shares and its notes ("On your list as 6 tbsp and 2 cups"). Not through it on purpose: the item sheet's amount field (the exact number), and the pantry step-through's "On your list" line, which already reads through `formatKitchenTotal`; what is checked off is grouped the same way into the Got it half (`groupShoppingItems` returns `{ toBuy, got }`); a row in the optional `held` map is placed by its held side instead of its stored flag (see "Holding a ticked row"). `selectShoppingOpenCount` feeds the Shopping tab's badge. `selectShoppingCost` prices the unchecked lines through `getLineCost` with each ingredient's default product, the same way a recipe's lines are priced, so it prices the amount on the list and not whole packages; what it can't price comes back as `Gap`s (`NO_PRODUCT`, `NOT_COMPARABLE`), which the help icon beside the cost line lists by ingredient; it also returns `rowCount` and `pricedRowCount` (rows as the list draws them, priced when every line is). `getShoppingCostCoverage` turns that into `none`, `tooFew` (under half), `some` or `all`; the screen draws the line ("About $3.16 for 3 of 4 things" or "for all 4 things") only for `some` and `all`, on To buy and All and under the search field (so the field doesn't move between To buy and Got it), and draws nothing for the other two, so Shopping never says "Not enough prices yet" or "No price yet". The icon (`HelpTip`, a modal on a phone or touch screen with its button saying "OK", and a tooltip with a mouse) is a plain help icon when nothing is left out and a warning when something is; beside a figure it says the amounts aren't whole packages, and it names what has no price ("Prices come from what you've told Pinch about where you buy things."); a `NOT_COMPARABLE` gap keeps its Kitchen pointer (a fill-in sheet never opens over this screen's own sheets, so the text points the way). A name-only item joins the row of the same ingredient that has an amount on its side of the list (`getItemRowIds`), so ticking, removing or editing that row covers it; the badge counts drawn rows (`countOpenShopRows`). The add sheet reads what a row stands for through `getItemsByRow(rows, items)` (every item, for a day's "On your list ✓" in `getDayOnList`) or `getOpenItemsByRow` (the open ones): a name-only row maps to the row the list draws for that ingredient, and a row that holds a name-only line also picks up that ingredient's name-only items. The "Got it" count and "N are ticked off" count rows (`countRows`), the same unit as the rows under them; the "rest changed in the meantime" check on Clear counts stored items. `selectShoppingCost` leaves a name-only line out of both the total and the row counts: it has nothing to price.
- **What a line is for.** `selectShoppingSourceLabels` (the name, kind, and a planned meal's day and meal of every source on the list that still resolves, by key) feeds `getShoppingSourcesText(sources, labels, todayKey)`, which names every source and never only counts them: a planned meal by its slot (`formatSourceSlot`: "Thu dinner", a day in the coming week by weekday and otherwise by date, "Oct 23 lunch"; two plates in one slot add their dish, "Thu dinner (Taco night)"), a recipe or a saved meal by its own name, in the list's order (`compareSourceLabels`: planned meals by day, the ones that have passed first, then meal, then recipes and saved meals by name). Three names are said in full ("Thu dinner, Fri lunch and Adobo chicken"), more as two and "and 5 more" (`joinSourceNames`, which names a single leftover rather than writing "and 1 more"), null when nothing resolves. The screen works each row's line out once per set of sources and change of names (a `Map` in a closure made per change of the labels, read by both the row and the chunk's height estimate), so a tick re-renders no names. The row's detail line adds "· for …" and wraps (two lines at most for two names and a count at 390 and 320px, three with three long names at 320; the separator is glued to the amount with non-breaking spaces so it never ends a line); "For …" alone for a line with no amount. The row sheet lists every source instead (`getRowSourceShares`): the slot or name, under it the dish for a planned meal (`selectShoppingPlannedParts`: for an unnamed plate of two or more items, the one item that wants the ingredient, else the plate's name), and on the right what that source asks of the row now (`getKeyShares`, the same share a stop takes off, in the row's units; nothing for a name-only line), rounded by largest remainder so the shares add up to the amount under them (0.13 and 0.12 cup above 0.25; whole counts that add up to the count to pick up) when every share is in the row's unit, each in its own unit otherwise, five shown and "Show N more" only when four or more would hide. A source whose planned meal is outside the loaded months reads like a deleted one until the calendar loads it.
- **What the list is for, and stopping one meal.** `selectShoppingMeals(state, todayKey)` (`getShoppingMeals`) lists every source that still resolves with the rows it put on the list, counted the way the list draws them (`getItemRowIds` per half, so a row in each half counts twice, like the All count), how many are still to buy, and whether its day has passed, in the list's order. The Shopping tab shows it as one `EntryTile`, "Meals on your list", in a `nav` under the search (or under the switch below 8 things), only while that list has something in it and nothing is typed in the search: a first run, a list of typed adds only and a list from before sources were kept have no door. Its status names the soonest meals (`getShoppingMealsText`: two by name, a slot two plates share said once, "and 74 more"). It opens `ShoppingMealsSheet`, a `DetailSheet` (a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`, which ends in a Done that takes the first focus so Enter never removes a meal): a line saying what Remove does, a `SearchInput` from 8 meals (slot, dish, "Recipe", "Saved meal"), and one scrolling list capped by its column (`max-h-[max(12rem,calc(92dvh-12rem))]`, `sm:max-h-[calc(100dvh-21rem)]`, a floor of `min-h-36` under 800px high) of 40 rows and "Show N more". A row is the slot (planned) or the name, a `FactsLine` ("Passed" for a day gone by, the dish or "Recipe" / "Saved meal", "3 of 8 to buy", "8 to buy" or "Got everything") and a secondary "Remove" (`aria-label` "Remove Thu dinner from your list", a 40px hit area from a pseudo-element, centred on the title line) only while something of it is still to buy. A removed row stays where it was, struck through, saying "Taken off your list" with the clause sentence under it, until the sheet closes, so the rows under a second tap never move. The sheet keeps each meal it is taking off or has taken off in a `held` map (`HeldMeal`, `getMealRows` in `utils/shopping.ts`) and draws that row from the snapshot taken at the tap: the shopping listener lands before the thunk resolves, so a row drawn only from the live meals dropped out for a frame and came back (the flicker). Remove has no `loading` or `disabled` state (a spinner and a faded button were the other half of the flicker): a ref set ignores a second tap, `aria-busy` says it is working, and the same button then reads "Undo" in the same place, ignoring a tap for its first 600 ms (`event.timeStamp` against the time it appeared, since `Date.now()` in a handler fails the compiler's purity rule). Remove dispatches `stopShoppingFor({ uid, source })` with the source's key: it checks the source still resolves, reads the other sources' shares from the store (`getOtherShares`), then, in chunks of 125 items run one after another, one `runTransaction` re-reads each item carrying the source and the name-only (`EACH`) item of each measured ingredient among them, and applies the pure `planStopShopping`: a got item only loses the tag; one to buy loses the source's share (`getKeyShares`: each entry of the source on an item is worked out the way its add was, a recipe at the servings that entry kept or its own for an older entry, a planned or saved meal's items as they are, and the entries add up; within an entry `getSourceShares` lands a line on the item of its ingredient and unit, else on one of the same ingredient and kind of unit, converted, for an amount the person moved to another unit) and is deleted when nothing is left (an amount within 0.02 of zero, since each add and each share round to hundredths, or a name-only line nothing else wants); what is left with no other source behind it stays, untagged, and is reported, never deleted, since it may be an amount the person typed onto the line before the add (a typed line and a meal's share are one document); when the share runs past what is left and something else wants the item, it keeps that one's share but never grows (`min`), keeps its amount when one of the others no longer resolves, or becomes just the name (in place for `EACH`, else moved into that ingredient's name-only item, created or merged into). An item the person typed on the list has no source, so it never carries the tag and is never touched. Each write is a full `set` of the item as planned (same `id`, `ingredientId`, `unit` and `createdAt`, so the update rule holds) or a delete; each entry records what happened to its line (`removed`, `kept` for one that still has another source, `reduced` for a share taken off with nothing else behind the rest, `left` for one the source asks nothing of now, `got`), and the thunk resolves to the entries and `countStopRows` over the list as drawn at the tap: rows, not stored items, so a cup and two tablespoons of soy sauce are one thing. The row says only what happened, clause by clause ("Took Thu dinner off your list", "5 things came off your list.", "…; 1 thing went down by this meal's share" ("this recipe's" for a recipe), "…; 1 thing stays, since something else on your list still needs it" only when the line still has another source, "…; 1 thing stays as it was"). There is no confirm: the row's Undo (until the sheet closes, which then raises one 6 s toast with the same Undo; one `undone` set keyed by the stop's id makes it run once, whichever is tapped) runs `undoStopShopping`, one transaction per chunk of 250 that puts each item back as it was (`updatedAt` now) only while it is still as the stop left it (`isAsStopLeft`: still gone, or the same `updatedAt`), so a line ticked or changed on another device in the meantime keeps what it has, and says "Put back 3 of 5" when it skipped some. **What can drift:** the share is worked out from the meal as it is now, so a meal or recipe edited after it was added takes off its new amounts, clamped at zero; a recipe's servings are kept, so the stepper never drifts, but a planned or saved meal added a second time on purpose (a row that was already on the list, ticked again) holds two shares under one tag and loses one; an amount the person typed on top of a meal's share, or a line the meal no longer has, stays, untagged, and the toast says it went down or stayed as it was; a planned meal deleted from the plan can't be removed here (it no longer resolves, so it isn't listed). **Offline:** a transaction needs the server, so with the browser offline Remove doesn't start and the sheet says "Removing a meal needs a connection, so Thu dinner is still on your list. Try again once you're back online."; a failure while it runs is one red line in the sheet with the action's sentence ("Check your connection and try again." for `unavailable`). No rules or index change: every write keeps the round-6 shape (`amount` null only in `EACH`, at most 60 `sources`, which the rules check only as a list, so the servings suffix needs nothing, immutable `ingredientId`, `unit` and `createdAt`), proven against the emulator as the owner (a lowered line with fewer sources, a delete, a new name-only line, a merge into a line with no `sources` key, each Undo write) and refused as another signed-in user and signed out.
- **Searching.** The list is one thin `PillGroup` of "To buy · n", "Got it · n" and "All · n" (counts of the rows drawn, which follow the search; All is the sum). `ShoppingView` is `ShoppingHalf | 'all'` and `groupShoppingItems` still returns the two halves (`Record<ShoppingHalf, …>`); All renders the to-buy half as usual and the got half as one flat run of rows (`ShoppingRows`, chunked and lazy like a group, eager only when the to-buy part is short) under a `SectionDivider` "Got it" that carries the Clear button. The shown view is derived each render by `getShownShoppingView(pick, query, matches)` (All is always kept; a half is the person's pick unless text typed since the pick matches nothing there and something in the other half), never mirrored with an effect, and with nothing left to buy the To buy half says so and offers "See what you got". From `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` (8) items the screen shows a `SearchInput` under the switch that filters both halves by ingredient name (`searchByName`, accent-insensitive, every word must match) from the typed text, not a debounced value. Nothing matching says so in one muted line ("Nothing on your list matches “milk”."). The pill counts show the matches; "Clear what you got" (a quiet line in the Got it half and on All's divider, only while something is got and never while searching) still clears every ticked item (with an Undo). If the list drops under 8 items the field disappears and the filter with it.
- **Searching the catalog.** `searchIngredients` (`utils/catalogSearch.ts`) matches every typed word against the name, aliases and the catalog family's name, and ranks: exact name, exact alias, name starts with the text, every word starts a word of the name, member of a matching family, alias starts with it, anything else; A to Z within a rank. `ItemSearch` keeps its 8-result cap and draws its results, and a search with no match (one line and the "New ingredient" row), in one framed block, and while the field is empty a host's `suggestions` take that place (decision 27); Kitchen's search uses the same matching, so a family's name finds its members there too ("pasta" lists the pastas).
- **Holding a ticked row.** A tick used to move its row into "Got it" within one frame, so the row below jumped up by a row height and a quick second tap landed on the wrong item. Now the stored flag, `selectShoppingOpenCount` (the tab badge) and `selectShoppingCost` change at once, and only where a row is drawn lags: `useHeldRows` in `ShoppingScreen` records, before the write, the side the row was drawn on (`groupShoppingItems(…, held)` places by that side while the row keeps its stored `isChecked`, so the checkbox and the strike-through are the truth) and lets go after `SHOPPING_MOVE_DELAY_MS` (1.2 s). An un-tick in "Got it" leaves it the same way, a second toggle of one row keeps its first side and restarts only its own timer, and the other rows' timers are untouched. The held map is a ref, read by the selector closure when the store changes: holding renders nothing by itself (the snapshot that follows the write does, once, as before), and only letting go renders, so a tick costs no extra render of the screen. Timers are cleared when the screen unmounts, so leaving the tab mid-hold leaves nothing stuck (the row is simply in "Got it" when you return); a hold that never sees its write land (a failed tick) just lets go. "Clear" counts what is stored, so a row still drawn in its group is cleared with the rest, and the "To buy · N" / "Got it · N" labels count what is drawn, so for a second they are one behind the badge. A tick holds every item behind its row. Checked on the 80-item list of the scale kitchen at 4× CPU: click to next frame for a tick is a median of 86 ms before and 93 ms after (5 interleaved runs each); a tick renders about 30 components and the move after the hold about 48, against about 51 in one go before.
- **Drawing.** A section's rows are chunks of 12, each a `LazyMount` with `defer-offscreen` on the chunk (a row estimated at 57 px; the first chunk of the first three sections mounts at once), so one long section (the scale kitchen's 64 custom-family rows in Other) never mounts whole and can flow across both `lg` columns; under 8 rows the list is one run with no dividers; a section draws 100 rows then "Show N more" (and nothing is hidden for 103 or fewer), and the "Got it" rows mount only while that half is open. The drawers' add sheets (`AddToShoppingSheet`, titled "Shopping list") live in the screen that owns the drawer (`RecipesScreen`, `CalendarScreen`) and open after the drawer closes, as `FillInSheet` does.
- **Failure.** The shopping listener is an optional extra: its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded items and sets `loadError`, never reaches the load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out) and is not part of the loading gate (`selectIsPinchLoaded`). The screen then says it couldn't load the list and offers no Add, and the tab's badge shows nothing. While the first snapshot hasn't landed the screen says "Loading your list…". Both add sheets wait for `state.pinch.shopping.isLoaded` (and a null `loadError`) before they let anything through: `AddToShoppingSheet` disables Add, "Tick all" and its checkboxes with "Loading your list…" or "Your list couldn't load, so adding is paused." in the pinned footer, because its defaults (what this sheet's own sources already put on the list starts unticked) come from the listener's data, and `ShoppingAddSheet` locks its results with the same lines and also checks the 300-item cap when a result is picked, not only when it opens. The gate deliberately leaves the shopping slice out of `selectIsPinchLoaded`, so a shopping failure never reaches the fatal screen.

### 5c. Recipe ideas

- **Adding.** "New idea" opens one `FormSheet` (`IdeaAddSheet`: a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`), whose first view is the `MethodPicker` of the three ways (`IDEA_START_METHODS`; *Jot it down* is last and quieter). A way's own view swaps in place behind a `BackLink` to the list, so there is never a second overlay, and Escape or the back gesture closes the one sheet. Each way is a Dreamer UI `Form` of custom fields with the footer pinned (`Cancel`, "Save idea" disabled until valid); the title of the sheet is "New idea" on the list and "Idea" on a way. The sheet is also mounted by `Pinch.tsx` (`openIdeaSheet` on the overlay context) for the New recipe list's last row, "Keep it as an idea", with `confirmsWithToast` and no `onOpenExisting` (a duplicate link then only says so).
- **Saving without waiting.** The ways run `getNewIdeaProblem(draft, ideasState)` (the checks `createIdea` makes, exported from `ideaActions.ts`) before anything else, so a problem stays in the sheet. A LINK or TEXT idea then gets its id from the caller (`createIdea({ uid, draft, id })`), the thunk is dispatched without being awaited and the sheet closes at once; `trackIdeaSave(id, promise)` (`hooks/useIdeaSaving.ts`) marks the idea as waiting if the write hasn't been acknowledged after 1.5 s, and `IdeaRow` (`useIsIdeaWaiting`) shows "Saves when you're back online" until it settles; a refusal raises a "That idea didn't save" toast. A PHOTO idea still awaits the upload and the write; its Cancel becomes Close while it runs (the save carries on; a failure after the sheet closed is a toast). Firestore's persistent cache keeps a queued write across a reload.
- **Save as an idea from a reading screen.** `RecipeReader` takes `onSaveAsIdea` (a chip on the text and link screens, disabled while empty or reading; `RecipeSubview` passes it only when not opened from an idea) and `initialText` / `initialLink`, so the reader keeps what was typed when it mounts again. `RecipeSubview` holds the kept `{ text } | { link }` and a stage `'idea'` (header "Idea"; back, Escape and the way's Cancel return to the reading stage): a link renders `IdeaLinkWay` with `initialUrl` (the pasted text run through `toWebLink`), text renders `IdeaTextWay` with `initialTitle` (the first non-blank line, `clipIdeaTitle`) and `initialNote` (the text cut to `MAX_IDEA_NOTE_LENGTH`, with a line saying so). Saving closes the subview and toasts "Saved … to your ideas" (with the offline line when `navigator.onLine` is false). The file way isn't covered: it reads a file as soon as it is picked.
- **From a link.** The field is `type='url'` (the submit button carries `formNoValidate`, since a link with no scheme is fine), `toWebLink` (`@/utils/urlUtils`) trims the ends, picks the one `http(s)://` token out of pasted text that has words around it (two such tokens give no link, and the field says "That looks like more than one link. Paste one at a time."), joins a scheme-less link that wrapped over lines, adds `https://` when no scheme is typed and refuses anything that isn't an http(s) address with a dotted host and no user name or password, and `toIdeaUrl` also caps it at 2,000 characters. A paste into the field that holds more than one word is run through it first (the browser would otherwise glue or drop the line breaks of a pasted line). After `DEBOUNCE_MS.linkDetection` the page is looked up with `fetchPageContent` through a TanStack Query factory (`queries/ideaPageQueries.ts`): `include: ['meta']` only, a callable timeout of `IDEA_LOOKUP_TIMEOUT_MS`, the shared 10-minute stale time for a success, no retry and no `persist`; a failure is a query error, never data, so it is never kept as an empty answer and a repeat of the same link asks again. `meta` carries the page's `<title>` (as `title`), `og:site_name` and `og:image` or `twitter:image` (resolved to an absolute address); the `images` kind is not asked for, because its other entries are the page's own `<img>` tags (logos, ads) and a wrong picture is worse than the tile. The line before anything is sent says what happens ("We fetch the page to find its title and picture. Nothing is saved from it except those."). While the lookup runs the sheet says so; when it returns, the preview shows the picture, a "Looks like" title field the person can change, and the site name; when it fails or times out, a title field with the host as its placeholder and "Couldn't read that page. You can type your own title." (a page that answered with no title gets the same field and "That page didn't give a title. You can type your own."). Saving waits for the lookup to settle (a result, a failure or the `IDEA_LOOKUP_TIMEOUT_MS` limit, plus the debounce): until then Save is disabled and the pinned footer says "Looking up the page. You can save in a moment.", so a link is never saved before its page's title could arrive. Once settled the title is what was typed, else the page's title for this very link, else (in `createIdea`, for a title left blank after a failed or empty lookup) the host, and `siteName` is the page's or the host. A typed title belongs to the link it was typed for (`{ link, title }`), so pasting another link starts again. A link already on the list (`findIdeaByUrl`, comparing `getComparableUrl`: no scheme, `www.`, fragment, trailing slash or tracking parameters, the other parameters in order) shows "That one's already in your ideas" with an "Open it" button, which closes the sheet and opens that idea's drawer, and disables saving; the lookup isn't made for it. The debounce, link check, page read (`ideaPageQueryOptions`, six seconds) and clipping are the shared `useLinkLookup` (`src/hooks/useLinkPreview.ts`); the sheet adds its duplicate check and storage limits.
- **From a photo.** `PhotoPicker` (the shared file picker; its new `uploadLabel` and `changeLabel` props and optional `onRemove` are general) and `useImageUpload` (images only, up to 5 MB) feed `createIdea`, which resizes the file with `resizeImageToWebp` to `IDEA_PHOTO_MAX_DIMENSION` (a result that isn't `image/webp` counts as unreadable, and the resize happens before anything is uploaded, so a refused file touches no Storage object), uploads it to `getIdeaPhotoPath(uid, id)` before the document is written, and deletes it again if the write fails. A photo idea needs a title (the field starts empty with "What is it?") and a picture; a failed upload keeps the form and says why through `getStorageErrorMessage` (a neutral "We couldn't save that picture." when the code has no message of its own). A save failure shows as one red line in the footer's `note` slot (the pinned footer, beside the buttons, never below the form where the footer would cover it) and clears when the form changes or a picture is picked.
- **Jot it down.** A title and, shown from the start, a note.
- **The note** is a visible optional second field: an "Add a note" chip (`RevealChip`) with a "Remove note" chip on the link and photo ways, open from the start on the text way and in the edit sheet.
- **The drawer.** `IdeaDrawer` (a `DetailSheet`, titled "Idea") shows the picture (only if there is one; a failed one leaves nothing), the title, the note (pre-wrapped), a made idea's tinted "Made into <recipe>" row, and for a link a quiet "Open <site name>" row (`Button href`, `target='_blank'`, `rel='noopener noreferrer'`, only for an http(s) address). Its footer is Modify (secondary) beside one primary: "Turn it into a recipe", or "Open the recipe" once made. Modify closes the drawer and opens `IdeaFormSheet`; "Open the recipe" closes it and opens the recipe's own drawer (`RecipesScreen`'s `openId`).
- **Editing.** `IdeaFormSheet` (title, link for a LINK, a photo with "Replace photo" and no remove for a PHOTO, note) saves through `updateIdea`, a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `title`, `note`, `updatedAt`, plus `url` (and `siteName`, `imageUrl: null` when the link names another page; the same page spelled differently keeps its site and picture) and `photoPath` when a replacement was picked, plus the keys an older document lacks. A replaced photo overwrites the same Storage object, and the picture's download URL is keyed on `updatedAt`, so it is fetched again. Delete is a destructive `useActionModal().confirm` ("Delete this idea?" naming it; for a made idea it says the recipe stays), then `removeIdea` (`deleteDoc`, then the photo object, best effort).
- **Turning an idea into a recipe.** The primary opens the recipe subview with `method: getIdeaRecipeMethod(idea)` (`'link'` for a LINK whose address `toWebLink` accepts, `'manual'` for a photo, a few words or an unusable address) and `fromIdeaId`: `{ kind: 'recipe', id: null, method, fromIdeaId }`. `RecipeSubview` reads the idea once, when it mounts. On the link way it hands `RecipeReader` the idea's address as `initialUrl` (the field starts filled; nothing is sent until the button is tapped, so the way's own line is the disclosure) and shows "From your idea: <title>" above it; `RecipeReader` with an `initialUrl` never draws the "Paste its text instead" row after a failed link, since the text way isn't one tap away from an idea. Back, Escape and the phone's back gesture from that way call `closeSubview` (to the Ideas list, never to the list of ways), the dashed "Write it yourself" row goes to the form below (asking first only if the address was edited), and a reading goes through `getReadingForIdea` before it reaches the form: the idea's note replaces the page's description when it has one, and the idea's title stays unless it is only the link's host (what a failed lookup saved), which gives way to the recipe's title; everything else (ingredients, steps, servings, times, the website as the source) is the reading's, and `fromIdeaId` still reaches `saveRecipe`, so the idea is marked made whichever way the form was reached. `getDataFromIdea` starts the form with the title, the note as the description and, for a LINK, `getSiteSourceChoice(getWebLinkHost(url), sources)`: the existing source named for the link's host (the `getHostSource` matching, as PR A's link reading does), else "New source" with the host, created with the recipe. It deliberately uses the host and not the page's own site name, so a person with a source called "seriouseats.com" doesn't get a second "Serious Eats". Nothing else is read from the idea: a photo is not copied and stays with the idea (the form says so, and points to "From a file", for a PHOTO idea; the file reading can't take a picture from storage). The form shows no extra line except, for a photo idea, the one saying its photo stays with the idea, and its title editor doesn't open by itself. Leaving an untouched prefilled form asks nothing, and discarding changes nothing. `saveRecipe` takes `fromIdeaId`. The recipe's batch (the recipe, its new source and its new ingredients) is committed on its own, exactly as for any recipe, so an idea that has gone from the server can never take a recipe down with it (a batch queued offline is refused whole if one of its documents is missing). Only after that commit, and only if the idea is still in the loaded store, one field-scoped `updateDoc` writes `{ …missing keys, recipeId, updatedAt }` on the idea; a failure there is caught, logged with `console.error` and not thrown, and `saveRecipe` returns `{ recipeId, linkedIdea }`. The form's toast is the same "Added X to your recipes" with Plan it as for any new recipe, whatever `linkedIdea` says. The recipe's `source` is `MANUAL`.
- **Reading.** `selectIdeas` (newest first), `selectIdeasById` and `selectIdeaGroups` (`{ toTry, made }`: a set of the listed recipes' ids, then two filters over the ideas) feed `IdeasView`, which shows "Still to try" and "Made into recipes" with `SectionDivider` once both have rows, a search (`SearchInput`) from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` ideas that looks through title, site name and note, and rows in chunks of twenty (`LazyMount` with `defer-offscreen`, the first chunk eager) that are as tall as their lines: a `IdeaThumbnail` (the link's own picture through `FallbackImage` with the emoji tile as its fallback, a photo's download URL, or the tile), a title of up to three lines, one muted line (`getIdeaDetail`: a link's site, else the first line of the note, and never a line that only repeats the title, as a link saved with its host as the title would) and a chevron pinned to the first line. Two columns from `lg`.
- **The cap.** `SOFT_MAX_IDEAS` (200) is enforced by `createIdea` against the loaded list and by the screens (the "New idea" button is replaced by "Your list holds up to 200 ideas. Delete one to add another."), not by the rules, which can't count a collection.
- **Failure.** The ideas listener is an optional extra: its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded ideas and sets `loadError`, never reaches the load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out) and is not part of the loading gate. "New idea" is disabled until the first snapshot has landed (the list says "Loading your ideas…") and stays disabled after a failure (the list says it couldn't load them and shows no search), and `createIdea` refuses in both states with a plain sentence, so the guard holds even if the button is reached some other way; Recipes, Meals and Kitchen are unaffected.
- **Not cascaded.** Deleting a recipe never touches the ideas that name it (they read as not made), deleting an idea never touches its recipe, and a recipe made from an idea keeps no pointer back.

### 5c.2. Cooking mode

- **No data of its own.** The screen reads `selectRecipesById` and `selectIngredientsById`, which the app already keeps (and, in `CookStepDock` and the ingredient view's `RecipeNotes`, the notes selectors of §5e), and listens to nothing new. Its writes are the cook Finish records (and its servings, a field-scoped `updateCook`), which belongs to the cooks collection (§5d), and the note typed there about that cook (§5e), neither of them cooking mode's own: with no cooks listener the panel isn't offered, and with no notes listener it has no note link.
- **Entry.** "Start cooking" is a row in the recipe drawer's ⋯ More ("Steps one at a time, with a timer"), only when the recipe has a step, and in the planned meal view, as its primary tonight (a row in its ⋯ More on a day ahead or while a due box is the primary), only on a day that has not passed, only when a recipe item has steps and only for a recipe not yet cooked for that meal (`getPlannedMealOffer`); the recipe drawer makes it the footer's primary instead when the recipe is on today's plan and not yet cooked there (§5d). Both close their drawer and call `openSubview({ kind: 'cook', … })` through `usePinchOverlay`. The planned meal works out its plate with `getPlateCookRecipes(items, recipesById, offer.cookRecipeIds)` (`utils/cooks.ts`, covered by `check:pinch-math`): each recipe with steps that isn't marked cooked for this meal yet, once, in plate order, its items' servings summed (capped at 1,000). One opens like a recipe (`recipeId`, `servings`, `plannedMealId`; cooking mode reads `servings` as what the plan has for tonight and starts at `getCookStartServings(recipe.servings, planned)`: the recipe's own, or the plan's when it is more); two or more pass `plate` too and open one session. There is no chooser. A recipe the day names but that has no steps, an ingredient item and an archived or deleted recipe are not offered. `Pinch.tsx` renders `CookingSubview` keyed on the recipe, the planned meal and the plate's recipe ids, and keeps the tabs mounted and visible underneath (an overlay, like the recipe form), so closing returns to the same list position.
- **State, all local to `CookingSession`.** `view: 'steps' | 'ingredients'` (shared by the session; the "Ingredients" button beside the step counter sets it, and on the ingredient list the subview's one `onBack` (header back, Escape, back gesture) sets it back before anything leaves), `currentId` (the recipe on screen) and `plate: Record<recipeId, PlateCookState>` (`getPlateStart`: each recipe's `stepIndex`, `servings` (`getCookStartServings` in `utils/cooks.ts`: the recipe's own, 1 when that isn't above zero, or the plan's count when it is more, capped at 1,000; the steppers map each tap through `stepCookServings`: whole servings, `MIN_COOK_SERVINGS` (0.5) only below one; both are checked by `check:pinch-math`) and `gotOut: Record<lineId, boolean>`) and the timers (with which one is in focus, kept by `useCookTimers`) sit side by side in the session (one recipe is the one-entry case), not in the view that shows them: the ingredients view and the steps view unmount when the person switches, so state held there would be lost (the ticks were, before this moved up). A tick re-renders the session, which is cheap because the rows are `memo` components with plain-value props and a stable `onToggle`. The index is clamped at render (`Math.min(stepIndex, lastIndex)`), so a recipe edited on another device while open can't leave it past the end; a recipe that loses its steps says "This recipe has no steps yet." and disables Next. `isFinished`, `isMarking`, a `markError` line and `cookedIds: Record<recipeId, cookId>` (the cooks Finish wrote; the panel trusts them at once, so a transaction whose snapshot lags never flickers, and only an Undo whose local delete has landed reads one as gone; per-recipe records (written, already marked, history full) are one `useMemo` over the store's cooks), `undoState` ('PENDING' | 'FAILED' | null) and a `finishedAt` ref (Done ignores a tap within `DONE_GUARD_MS`, 600 ms, of Finish) sit beside them, and `useCookCapture` keeps the one composer open for one recipe at a time (`draft`, `isSaving`, `error`) and the ids this screen has written (`captured`); nothing is kept when the screen closes.
- **A recipe that changes while the screen is open.** `CookingSubview` keeps the last recipe it saw: the store's `selectRecipesById` entry while there is one, and a snapshot that a `store.subscribe` callback updates (state is set from that callback, not during render or in an effect body, and a ref can't be read in render) for when the document goes. An edit shows at once; an archive (the selector includes archived recipes), a delete or a changed `updatedAt` adds one muted `role="status"` line, "This recipe was changed or removed elsewhere."; the session, its timers and its wake lock are never remounted. The "isn't in your kitchen anymore" line is only for a screen opened on a recipe that was never there.
- **Leaving.** While the timer view is up, the header's back, Escape and the back gesture put the timer in the background and show the step that was on screen (`backToStep`; the timer keeps running in the pill row), one layer per press. Otherwise the header's back, Escape (`Subview`'s capture-phase handler, which ignores a dialog on top) and the back gesture (`useSubviewHistory`, which restores its history entry before calling `onBack`) all call the same `leave()`, as does the final panel's Done (and "Update the recipe" on a planned change, which then opens the recipe form): it first asks "Discard this entry?" (Keep editing / Discard, destructive) while a note is typed and unsaved (`hasDraft`: it counts only while the cook is in the store and the note isn't saving, so Back during a save leaves and lets the write finish, and after an Undo the hidden box is not asked about; the note's own Cancel asks the same question), then, with no live timer, it calls `closeIfStillOpen()` (`closeSubviewIf` for this recipe's cooking screen, like every close after an await); with one or more running or paused (`liveCount`) it awaits `useActionModal().confirm({ title: 'Stop timers and leave?', confirmText: 'Leave', cancelText: 'Stay', destructive: true })` whose message is a small `LeaveMessage` reading the live count from a tiny store the session feeds (`useSyncExternalStore`, so a timer that finishes behind the open dialog stops being "still going" in its text: "Your timer has finished. Leaving closes cooking mode."), and every confirm in the screen goes through `ask`, which puts keyboard focus on the footer's primary when the dialog closes if a focus move was skipped while it was open. A finished timer and a dismissed one don't count. One Escape or one back gesture over the confirm closes only the confirm (the existing dialog-on-top rule), and Stay is the focused button. Checked from each of the four with a running timer, a paused one, only a finished one and none. The page layer is part of the same history stack as every drawer (see "The back gesture, one layer at a time").
- **Steps view.** `CookStepView` shows "Step n of m" with a thin progress bar (`flex-1`, 24 to 128px, so the row never wraps at 320px whatever the step count) and, at the row's end, the `action` the session passes (the "Ingredients" button, none from `lg`), then the step inside one polite, atomic live region that carries a visually hidden "Step n of m." before the text (the visible counter row is `aria-hidden`, so the button's label isn't read with every step; a screen reader says "Step 3 of 14" and the step after Next, an arrow key or a swipe, and focus stays on Next so Enter can press it again). The step is 28px (`text-[1.75rem]`, 30px from `lg`), 24px over 160 characters and 20px over 400, a size smaller on a screen under 640px tall (so a 568 or 560px phone fits the step and its card above the pinned stack), `font-medium` with `whitespace-pre-wrap`; `getStepType` returns that class and a ready card's digits' class from one ramp, the digits a size below the step (24/20/18px, never larger than the words); the step sits in a block with `pt-8` (`pt-5` under 640px tall) from the counter row and its ready timer cards 32px under it (20px under 640px tall), and under that block one bottom stack (`mt-auto`, `pt-8`, `empty:hidden`) holds `CookStepDock` (a 40px row of bubbles; its open panel is absolutely positioned above the row and adds no height), so the instruction has the same room above and below on every screen that has room and the stack stays against the pinned buttons. The step's line height is 1.35 (it was 1.3). The step's amounts are the scale bubble's panel in `CookStepDock`: `getAmountsSummary(rows)` (`utils/cooks.ts`, `check:pinch-math`) supplies the caption "For this step · 4 items" and the panel lists every line through `CookAmountRows` (shared with `CookStepAmounts`, which the timer view's "Show this step" still draws as a framed block), with the muted "Amounts written in this step stay as they are." at its foot on a scaled recipe; the dock is keyed by `stepNumber`, so the next step starts closed, and a step that names nothing has no scale bubble. On a short landscape phone (`max-height:500px` and `min-width:640px`) the overlay's `max-w-4xl` replaces `max-w-2xl`, the dishes and the counter row share one line (the counter row is the width of the right column, `w-88`), and the step's block and the bottom stack sit in a two-column grid (`1fr` and `22rem`) so neither pushes the other under the buttons. A plate's recipes are `CookDishStrip`, passed into `CookStepView` as `dishes` on the steps view (so it can share the counter's line) and drawn by the session itself above the ingredient list and from `lg` (a `role="group"` of `aria-pressed` `Button`s, 40px tall, `rounded-xl`; the dish on screen has no status because its counter is just below, the others read "1 of 2" after the name, `aria-label` the full title and step; a name is `truncate`; three or more are `max-w-[70%]` in a sideways scroller with snap points that scrolls the dish on screen into view, never a `Pill` or `PillGroup`). The header is `Subview`'s with `isTitleWrapped` (an opt-in `line-clamp-2` in place of `truncate`); a plate's title is the planned meal's own name when it has one, else `getPlateTitle` (`utils/mealFormat.ts`, the one helper that words a plate everywhere, `check:pinch-math`) over the session's recipes, never an ingredient item: "A & B", "A, B and C", "A, B and 2 more", and "A & 3 more" once a name runs past 30 characters, the count glued to its words with non-breaking spaces. Back and Next are 56px tall in a footer pinned by `Subview overlay`'s `.form-footer` rule (44px on a short landscape screen), with the pill row of timers running in the background above them (one row with them under 500px tall); the one scroller is the overlay layer, so a long step scrolls between the sticky header and footer, and a new step scrolls it back to the top. Nothing scrolls the step on its own: the dishes, the counter row with Ingredients and the bubble row are the screen's controls and stay where the content starts (an earlier layout effect scrolled a timer card above the footer, and on a plate at 320×568 or held sideways that took the dish switch and Ingredients under the sticky header, so it was removed); the bubble row, which sits below everything else, is reached on a short phone by scrolling the one scroller when a five-line step and its timer leave no room, and `document.elementFromPoint` at the centre of each dish, each bubble, the counter row and Ingredients returns that control at 320×568, 390×560, 375×667, 390×844 and 844×390 on a plate's timed step (with a timer in the pill row) and on a 414-character step; Start, Back and Next were checked the same way, with Start reached by scrolling to the end where it sits under the footer. Swipe is the shared `SwipeArea` (`src/components`, no app words) on the step area (touch and pen only, at least 60px sideways and 1.5 times the vertical travel, with `touch-action: pan-y pinch-zoom`, so vertical scrolling is the browser's and pinch-zoom still works on the text; only the first finger counts, and a swipe is ignored while `visualViewport.scale` is above 1.05, so zooming a step never turns the page; a touch that starts inside an element marked `data-no-swipe`, the dock's panel and its sideways card rows, is not a swipe, so scrolling cards never turns the step); ArrowLeft and ArrowRight work in the steps view unless a field has focus, a modifier is down or a dialog is open. Cooking a plate, the wide button follows `getPlateNext` (`utils/cooks.ts`, `check:pinch-math`): the next step, then "Next: {recipe}" for the next recipe still short of its last step (wrapping), and Finish only when every recipe is on its last; a recipe whose steps were all removed counts as done.
- **Amounts on the step.** `matchStepIngredients(steps, lines)` (`utils/stepIngredients.ts`, pure, checked by `check:pinch-math`) runs once per recipe in a `useMemo` keyed on the steps, the lines and `selectIngredientsById` (about 2.5 ms for 40 lines and 25 steps unthrottled). Each step lists the lines whose ingredient it is the first to name (an amount is measured once): names and catalog aliases are normalized like `normalizeName` with describing words dropped, whole phrases are matched longest first on word positions not yet claimed (so "garlic powder" never also reads as "garlic"), then the first or last word of an ingredient's own name when only one ingredient in the recipe has it and it isn't a vague word (colours, "sauce", "powder", "and", "half"…), never a word from inside an alias. Every line of a matched ingredient is listed, in mention order, except that an ingredient with lines in several groups is shared out: each step that names it takes the next group's lines (in the order the groups show) and the last step that names it takes what is left, so "marinate in the soy sauce" lists the soy sauce for the chicken and "add the rest of the soy sauce" the soy sauce for the sauce, each labelled with its group. `hasWrittenAmount` is a number followed by a measure word ("2 cups", "350g"), and only on a scaled step does it show "Amounts written in this step stay as they are." It is a `bg-muted/50` block with a hairline and a "For this step" caption, rows in 14px (the step above is 28px), so the amounts read as a reference under the instruction rather than a second subject. From `lg` the step's list is hidden and the side column tints the same lines instead (one fact, one place).
- **Ingredients view.** `CookIngredientsView` scales each recipe line with `scaleAmount(amount, servings, recipe.servings)` (`utils/quantity.ts`), the same expression `getRecipeLines` uses for the shopping list, so a line reads identically in both (checked at 6, 2.5 and 0.5 servings against the shopping sheet), and formats it with `formatKitchenAmount` (`utils/kitchenMeasure.ts`, display only, checked by `check:pinch-math`): ¼ ⅓ ½ ⅔ ¾ (⅛ for teaspoons), a cup under ¼ (or one that is not within 5 % of a kitchen fraction, up to ½ cup) in tablespoons, a tablespoon under 1 in teaspoons, a pound under ¼ in ounces, under 0.1 kg or l in g or ml, whole items rounded to a whole (from 3) or a half with "about", "a pinch" for a sliver of a teaspoon, and "about" whenever it rounded by more than 5 %. The amount itself and `scaleAmount` are unchanged, so the shopping list's numbers are not affected, and the rows are built once in the session and shared with the step lists. Units are only ever stepped down for display, and a recipe whose servings aren't above zero is left unscaled instead of dividing by zero (the shopping list leaves such a recipe out). Rows are `memo` components with plain-value props, so a tick re-renders its own row; the ticks come in as a prop from the session. A line that names an ingredient that is gone reads "Removed ingredient". A line with no amount shows its note where the amount goes, in muted text, and never scales. A recipe with groups lists its lines under the same `SectionDivider` headings as the drawer, through `groupIngredientLines`; the "N of M got out" count is the whole recipe.
- **Timers.** `useCookTimers` (`hooks/useCookTimers.ts`) holds timers as a discriminated union (`running` with `endsAt` and `startedAt`, `paused` with `remainingMs`, `done` with `endedAt`, when it rang), each with the `durationMs` it was started with (for the progress bar), in state mirrored by a ref that every action reads and writes, so a callback never works from a stale list. Only `running` and `paused` timers are live and count toward `MAX_COOK_TIMERS` (10, a dish's timers and the person's own together); a finished one waits for Dismiss, and starting a timer drops the oldest finished ones beyond ten so the list can't grow without end. A countdown runs to a wall-clock deadline and `settle` (mark each ended timer done once with its `endedAt`, ranked by `getEndedAts` from its deadline and kept past any Time's up already waiting so a stepped-back clock can't reorder them, bring the view up and raise the alerts) runs from three places: the nearest `setTimeout` (the deadline plus 20 ms), which re-arms for what is left if it wakes before the deadline (the clock stepped back, so it can't leave a timer at 0:00 unalerted); `useOnTabReturn`, so a timer that ended while the tab was hidden shows done the moment the page returns; and the 250 ms tick of the pill row and of the timer view, which catches a deadline a forward clock step jumped over and a page that was frozen without a `visibilitychange` (a real CDP freeze dispatched no event at all, and the tick found the timer 100 ms after the thaw). Each path was proved alone, with the other two removed in the test page (`setInterval` of 250 ms and the app's own `visibilitychange`, `focus` and `blur` listeners dropped), against a fake clock whose system time was stepped without running a timer, which is what a suspended page looks like. The two views also read the clock again on `useOnTabReturn` (`setNow(Date.now())`): `CookTimerView` and the pill row keep `now` in state set by their tick, and a timer that rang while the screen was off left the view showing "Rang just now" (and the pills a stale time) until the next minute's timeout or tick, because nothing was running to refresh it. **A Time's up that nobody dismisses rings again**: `ringsRef` holds `{ id, lastAt, repeats }` per waiting timer, and a timeout armed from `getNextAlertRepeatAt` (a minute after the soonest `lastAt`, `ALERT_REPEAT_MS`; re-armed after each repeat and when the set of waiting timers changes, cleared on Dismiss, `clear` and unmount) buzzes and beeps through `getAlertRepeatsDue` up to `MAX_ALERT_REPEATS` (5) more times per timer, skipping the sound (not the count) while `hasDialogOnTop()`, with no toast for a repeat; the timer view says "Rang just now" / "Rang 2 min ago" from `endedAt` (`getRangAgo`), re-rendered by a timeout aimed at the next minute. `dismiss` of a finished timer adds its slot (`getTimerSlotKey`: recipe, step, length) to `finishedSlots` (state, memory only, returned as a `Set`), cleared when a timer starts in that slot, so the step view's card reads "Done · 30 min" with a quiet "Start again" (`CookStepTimer`'s `isDone`) instead of offering the time as if it never ran. The alerts are the timer view's "Time's up", `navigator.vibrate` when it exists, three short beeps through one `AudioContext` created and resumed in the tap that started the first timer and resumed again before the beeps (a browser can suspend it in the background), each in its own `try` that logs and carries on, and a toast ("Timer done", "Simmer everything · 25 min", 10 seconds) only while `document.visibilityState` is hidden or `hasDialogOnTop()`; each timer carries `stepName` (`getStepName`, `utils/cooks.ts`: the step's first three words without a trailing little word), which names it in place of "Step 3". **Focus is one value in the hook:** `focus = { isShown, pickedId }`, and `focused` (the `CookTimer` the timer view shows, null while every timer runs in the background) is derived each render through `getFocusedTimerId(timers, pickedId)` (`utils/cooks.ts`, `check:pinch-math`): the Time's up that has waited longest first (the smallest `endedAt`, so a later finish queues behind the one in front whatever started first, and a pick that finishes later doesn't take the front), then the one picked (started or tapped), which keeps focus while paused, else the running one ending soonest. `start` picks the new timer and shows the view (and returns its id, or null at the cap), `focus(id)` picks one, `unfocus()` hides the view (the timers keep running), `settle` shows the view over whatever is on screen (`getFocusAfterFinish`: it keeps the pick while the view was up, so Dismiss returns to the timer the person was watching, and forgets one already in the background), and `dismiss(id)` (Reset when paused, Dismiss when done; `getFocusAfterDismiss`) shows the next finished timer, else the one still picked (watched, or just started while a finished one waited), else hides the view, and ending a timer from its row changes nothing; `pause` and `resume` change nothing about focus. Both transitions are pure and in `check:pinch-math`. The session hides the view while the Finish panel holds a typed note or open put-away details (a finish then waits, lit in the pill row, until the draft closes), and an effect keyed on the focused timer and whether it is done moves keyboard focus to the footer's primary (Pause, Resume or Dismiss; back to Next when the view closes), skips the move while a dialog is on top (the dialog keeps focus; `ask` restores it when it closes), scrolls the overlay to the top when the view appears and starts the 0.6 s tap guard (`DONE_GUARD_MS`, the constant the Finish panel's Done uses) that makes Pause, Resume or Dismiss, and a Time's up's Next, ignore a tap right after the view or a Time's up appears (a ref, not state: the button looks the same). The voice PR reads `focused` for "how much time is left" (`getRemainingMs(focused, Date.now())`) and calls `pause`, `resume`, `dismiss` and `start` with its id, and `focus(id)` to show one. The step on screen shows a `CookStepTimer` card only for a time it mentions that has no timer yet: `getReadyStepTimers(timers, shown)` (`utils/cooks.ts`, `check:pinch-math`) takes the shown step's `findDurations` (the first `MAX_STEP_TIMERS`, 4, as many as can run at once) and keeps those with no timer of the same recipe, step number and label (`formatDuration`); `shown` is null on the ingredient list and the Finish panel. A card is only ever ready: "⏲️ Timer", the length and a filled Start; Start hands the screen to the timer view, so a started time is never also a card (one fact, one home). Cards are keyed by step number and label, and the step view by recipe. Nothing starts a timer but a tap. With ten live timers a ready card's Start is disabled and "10 timers are going. Another can start once one finishes or is reset." shows under the cards (ten finished timers leave Start enabled), in an always-mounted `role="status"` wrapper that is `empty:hidden`. **The pill row** (`CookTimerStrip`) is what the step view, the ingredient list and the Finish panel show of the timers, in `getTimerRows(timers, null)` order (finished first, then in the order they started, so nothing moves under a finger): one 40px row of pills, each one button with only the time left (`formatClock`, tabular) and the step's first words (its length before them when two come from one step, so a cut name keeps what tells them apart), muted digits and "Paused ·" while paused (⏸ is the Pause action, never a state), a bell, "Time's up" and the accent tint once done; a tap brings that timer into focus. A pill is as wide as its words (at most 18rem, never shrunk to a fragment) and the row scrolls sideways when they don't all fit. The row's inner component mounts only while there are timers, so its `now` is read afresh on every return from the timer view (a tick-driven value seeded at first mount showed each timer's full length for a tick). The pills are plain text while the Finish panel holds a draft. There is no pill row while the timer view is up. Leaving asks first while a timer is live (see Leaving) and then ends every timer (the hook's state and its pending timeout go with the component). Each timer carries `recipeId` and, on a plate, `recipeTitle`; `getTimerCaption` names it ("Adobo chicken · Marinate the chicken", the step alone for one recipe) in aria labels and the hidden-screen toast, and `countLiveTimers` / `canStartTimer` hold the cap of four for the whole session.
- **The timer view** (`CookTimerView`, keyed by the focused timer so "Show this step" starts closed for each). While a timer is in focus it replaces the step (and the dishes, the bubble row, the "changed elsewhere" line, the ingredient list or the Finish panel) inside the same overlay: a waiting screen that shows only what helps while waiting. From the top: a plain muted line "Step 2 of 4 · Show this step" with a chevron (40px, `aria-expanded`) that opens the timer's step in place (20px text and its "For this step" block, `CookStepAmounts`, whose rows `CookAmountRows` the step view's tray shares; the amounts are listed from `lg` too, since there is no side column); the hero card (`@container`, `rounded-3xl`): on a plate the timer's recipe in muted text, the name ("Marinate the chicken · 30 min"), the countdown in `font-medium tracking-tight tabular-nums` at `clamp(4.5rem, 28cqi, 9rem)` (72px at 320, 88px at 390, 144px from `lg`; an hour or more at `clamp(4rem, 22cqi, 7rem)`, so "1:30:00" is 64px and 246px wide in a 248px card at 320; a bold weight would not fit), a progress bar (elapsed over `durationMs`), under it one reserved 24px row (so pausing never grows the card: "Paused · ↺ Reset" while paused, the Reset a 40px target by pseudo-element, "Rang 2 min ago" when done, empty while it runs) and a `role="status"` "The 25 min timer is done."; "Show this step" opens the step under the card, never above it; running is the drizzle tint with an orange edge, paused the muted tint with a hairline and muted digits, done the accent tint with an ink edge, a bell and "Time's up" at `clamp(2.5rem, 17cqi, 5rem)` and a full bar. Under it, "Other timers": the session's other timers (`getTimerRows(timers, focusedId)`) as rows in one hairline card (the name, wrapping, leading with the length when two share a step; the recipe on a plate; the time left; the row is one button that brings that timer into focus, and its own 40px Pause or Resume sits 12px to the right), or, while a Time's up is in front, plain text rows with no control (a tap on a row used to do nothing, because a finished timer outranks the pick); then, not at the cap and not over a Time's up, the timer's step's other times with no timer yet as one muted disclosure line ("2 more timers on this step · Show") that opens rows of length and Start ("Start again" for a slot in `finishedSlots`), so two ready rows never push "Up next" under the footer at 320×568. Then "Up next" (`getTimerNext(positions, timer)`, `check:pinch-math`: the step after the timer's step while the person is before it; nothing once they are on it or past it; on a recipe's last step the next recipe still short of its last; nothing on the last step of all or on the Finish panel) with the first sentence of that step (`getFirstSentence`: up to a stop, question or exclamation mark before a capital or digit, or a line break; six lines at most, three under 640px tall). On a plate, while the timer isn't finished and `getMeanwhile` finds another recipe still short of its last step that "Up next" doesn't already name, one tertiary row, "Meanwhile: {recipe} · Step 1 of 2" with a chevron, calls `jumpTo` on that recipe's own step. Last, while the timer runs, the keep-the-screen-open line (below). On a phone held upright the hero and its rows sit a third of the way down the free space and "Up next" just above its button; under 500px tall it is a two-column grid, the hero (and the line) on the left and the step line, the rows and "Up next" on the right; from `lg` it is one centered `max-w-xl` column. The footer is the timer's: a secondary chosen by `getTimerSecondary(next, status)` — "Next" (the step footer's word, so one act has one name; "Next recipe" with `aria-label` "Next recipe: Cilantro lime rice" on a plate's last step) while there is something up next, which puts the timer in the background and shows that step (`jumpTo(recipeId, stepIndex)`; from "Time's up" it also dismisses the finished timer, ignored for 0.6 s after the Time's up appears); "Back to step 3" (`aria-label` naming the recipe on a plate; "Back to ingredients" or "Back" over those screens) for a running or paused timer with nothing up next, which is a peek from a pill while the person is further on; and nothing for a Time's up over a later step, so no two buttons end at the same screen; and one wide primary: Pause while it runs (the only control a running timer has), Resume while paused, Dismiss when done (`aria-label` "Pause the 30 min timer" and so on). ArrowRight or a swipe to the left does what Next does; ArrowLeft or a swipe to the right goes back to the step, like the header's back. Checked with `document.elementFromPoint` at the centre of Pause, Resume, Reset, Dismiss, Next, "Show this step" and every row's control, running, paused, with the step shown, two on one step, an hour long, four live (at the cap), "Time's up" with three others and on the last step, at 320×568, 390×560, 375×667, 390×844, 844×390 and 1280×900, and on a plate. From `lg` the footer's content is capped to the same `max-w-xl` column as the timer (only while the view is up and taller than 500px), so Pause is never a bar wider than the column.
- **Finding the times.** `findDurations` (`src/utils/durationUtils.ts`, no recipe words) reads a text for lengths of time: a number (integer, decimal, `1/2`, `1 1/2`, `½ ¼ ¾` alone or after a whole number) then hours, minutes or seconds in their written forms (`hour`, `hr`, `min`, `sec` and plurals), "an hour" and "half an hour"; a range (`10-15`, `10–15`, `2 to 3`, `1 or 2`) takes its longer end; "1 hr 30 min", "1 hour and 15 minutes" and "2 hours, 30 minutes" join into one length when only a comma or "and" sits between them and the units step down; repeats are dropped, and zero, anything over 24 hours, anything past the first 5,000 characters and any number written with a decimal comma or thousands commas ("1,5 hours" could be 1.5 or 15, "2,500 seconds" 2,500 or 500; a wrong timer is worse than none) is left out. The parser is linear (`joinPieces` builds one array in a single pass) and `findDurations` takes an optional `maxChars` (5,000 by default) so a check can lift the cap. Words like "tablespoons", "minced" and "second batch" without a number in front are not times. It returns `{ seconds, text }`; `formatDuration` ("25 min", "1 h 30 min") labels the chip and `formatClock` ("25:00") the countdown, rounding up so it reads 0:00 only at the deadline. Its cases, including the comma rule, the 5,000-character boundary (a text just under and just over, which fails if the cap is removed) and benchmarks with the cap raised (250,000 repeats of "5 min " and other hostile bodies, each under a second), are in `scripts/checks/durations.ts`, run by `npm run check:pinch-math` with the shopping math (which also covers `scaleAmount`). English only.
- **Keeping the screen on.** `useWakeLock(isActive)` (`src/hooks/useWakeLock.ts`, general) returns `{ isSupported, isHeld, hasFailed }`: it requests `navigator.wakeLock.request('screen')` while active, asks again each time `useOnTabReturn` fires (a browser lets go of the lock when the page is hidden, which also clears `isHeld` through the sentinel's `release` event), releases on unmount, and logs a refusal or a failed release instead of throwing. It mirrors the sentinel carefully: a sentinel whose `released` is true counts as absent, so the next return asks again even if its `release` event hasn't arrived; a `release` event changes `isHeld` only when it comes from the sentinel the hook currently holds, so a late one from an old sentinel can't clear a newer lock; and a request that resolves after the person left is released with its own `catch`, so a refused release is logged and never sets `hasFailed`. Cooking mode shows "Keep this screen open: timers stop if you leave it." at the foot of the timer view while its timer runs or is paused (never over a Time's up, so pausing never moves the digits), from the first timer until every timer is gone; the step view, the pill row and the footer never carry it. The old no-wake-lock sentence ("This screen may turn off…") is gone: the cooking screen holds the lock with a bare `useWakeLock(true)` and says nothing about it, and what a browser that cannot hold the screen on or push can do is said in the timer-buzz modal below. Wake Lock needs a secure context, so a plain-HTTP LAN page shows the line. It was checked with a stub (one request on open, none before, released when hidden, requested again on return, released on close, a refusal shows the line, a late `release` event from an old sentinel, a released sentinel without its event, and a refused release after leaving, each of which fails on the earlier hook); headless Chromium refuses the real call, which showed the line.
- **Timers when the screen is off, asked at the moment a timer starts (`CookTimerBuzz`).** While the focused timer runs, `CookTimerView` takes a `buzz` slot under the card; `CookingSubview` fills it with `CookTimerBuzz` only for a running timer. The component reads the device with `devicePushQueryOptions(uid)` (the browser's state, never Firestore) and the person's `reminders.timers`, and `getTimerBuzzOffer(device, isTimersOn)` (pure, `utils/cooks.ts`, checked in `cookSession.ts`) says `none` (state unread, or on with timer pushes on: nothing is drawn), `ask` (off, or on with the switch off), `blocked` or `unsupported`. The row is `PromptRow isCompact` (a 42px capsule: bell, "Buzz me if the screen is off", a Turn on button with a 40px target from a pseudo-element); it opens a `Modal` (hairline border in dark, `useSheetHistory` so back and Escape close only it) titled with the Settings row's own title, "Timers when the screen is off". Turn on calls `turnOnDevicePush(uid)` on that tap (skipped when the device is already on) and then `save({ reminders: { timers: true } })` through `usePinchSettings`, so both switches Settings shows change together and `useTimerPush` schedules the already-running timer's push as soon as `isOn` flips; blocked and unsupported say why in the pinned footer and leave Turn on disabled, a closed browser question says "Nothing changed", a failure reuses `getTurnOnFailureText`, and success shows one confirming line with Done. The modal lives in the component that stays mounted after the offer disappears, so the confirmation is not unmounted by its own success. No row appears in the put-away form or Finish panel, and Settings stays the one place to turn it off.
- **Limits.** Timers end when the screen closes and don't ring in the background or on a locked phone. Vibration is Android only and needs a recent tap. Sound is prepared in the tap that starts a timer, because a browser won't start audio on its own later.
