A step's text is read only to list the ingredients it names, never to rewrite it, so amounts written in it don't scale (a scaled step that has one says so).
- **Picking a cook up again** (`utils/cookSession.ts`, pure and checked by `scripts/checks/cookSession.ts` in `check:pinch-math`; `lib/cookingSessionStorage.ts`, `lib/cookingSessionPresence.ts`; `CookingResumeRow`). A reload or a browser that discards a hidden tab used to end a cook, so `CookingSession` saves a versioned snapshot in `localStorage` under `pinch:{uid}:cooking-session`, a per-viewer convenience: every read and write is in `try`/`catch`, a browser without storage reads as no session and the cook is unchanged. It holds `version`, `uid`, a per-session `sessionId`, `savedAt`, `open` (what opened the screen: `recipeId`, `servings`, `plannedMealId`, the meal's day `plannedMealDate` (null when it wasn't loaded; a copy saved before it existed reads as null), `plate`, so a restore opens it the same way) and `resume` (`currentId`, each recipe's `stepIndex` and `servings`, and each timer as `recipeId`, `stepNumber`, `label`, `durationMs` and by status a wall-clock `endsAt` with `startedAt` and the `reminderId` of its push, `remainingMs` when paused, or `endedAt` once it rang), copied field by field (`toResumeTimer`, `buildCookingSnapshot`): no recipe text, notes, ticks, amounts or names. It is written by an effect keyed on the session's state (the step, servings, dish, the timers array and `isFinished`), never by a tick: the timers array changes only on an action or a settle, so a running countdown writes nothing. It is cleared by Finish (the effect, while `isFinished`; "Back to the last step" writes it again), by `leave()` after the "Stop timers and leave?" answer (which covers the header's back, Escape, the back gesture and Done), by "Update the recipe" (leaving to the recipe form), by "Not now", and on sign-out or a change of person (`clearViewerSessions(uid)` in `AuthContext`, general: it removes every `<app>:<uid>:*-session` key of that uid, so a second app's unfinished state follows the same name). `parseCookingSnapshot` reads it only when the version and uid match, `savedAt` is within 12 hours (and not more than five minutes ahead of a clock set back), and every field has the shape that was written, with caps on timers and recipes; anything else is removed from storage when found. `resolveCookingResume(snapshot, recipesById, loadedMeals)` drops recipes that are gone, archived or have no steps, holds every step and timer to what the recipes have now, lets go of a planned meal only when it is provably gone (`isPlannedMealGone`: its saved day is inside the days the calendar's listener has loaded, `getMonthWindow(windowKey)`, and the meal is not among them, so Finish doesn't mark it), keeps one outside those days or of an unknown day (the planned-meals slice holds only the shown month and two either side, so a meal beyond them is not in it even though it exists; it used to be let go, and Finish then wrote a plain cook that left the meal unmarked), and returns null (and the row clears the key) when nothing is left. `CookingResumeRow` is the first child of every tab's content in `Pinch.tsx` (not drawn while cooking is open, so it re-reads storage when cooking closes): a tinted block, not an overlay, "Pick up step {n}?" with "{timer line} · {recipe}" under it (a plate names the dish whose step it is), each of the two lines one line and the row 48px at every width: the detail line is one fixed 16px line (`flex-wrap`, overflow hidden) with the timer first, so the name is the part that gives way: it is cut with an ellipsis down to 56px and drops out of sight (wraps onto a clipped second line) when the timer line leaves it less, while the timer line is never cut; under 360px the row's padding and gap tighten so the longest timer line fits; the timer line is `getResumeTimerLine` ("Timer 12:30 left", "Timer paused with 12:30 left", "A timer rang 8 min ago": a timer that rang is told before a running one, running before paused; the countdown is `useNow(1000)` inside `ResumeCard`, so the clock ticks only while a row is drawn and every tab is otherwise quiet), the row itself is the "Keep cooking" button (with a chevron) and an icon-only ✕ "Not now". "Keep cooking" is `openSubview({ kind: 'cook', ...open, resume })`, the same subview the drawers open; `CookingSubview` rebuilds the entries from the recipes, `CookingSession` seeds `currentId` and `plate` through `applyResumePositions` and hands `restoreTimers` to `useCookTimers(initialTimers)`, so a running timer whose deadline passed settles on mount through the hook's own timeout (it comes to the front as "Rang 8 min ago", with the alert's sound skipped by the browser until a tap). Two tabs: a session answers over a `BroadcastChannel` (`announceCookingSession(sessionId)`), and the row asks for 400 ms (`askIfCookingSessionLive`) and stays hidden when the snapshot's own session answers, so a second tab doesn't offer a cook that is still running; a `storage` event re-reads the key, so "Not now" in one tab clears the row in the other. Not saved: ticks, the "Got it" on a note, the view (steps or ingredients), a dismissed Time's up slot. Two sessions that cook at the same time overwrite each other's copy (the last write wins).
- **Wide screens.** From `lg` (`useMediaQuery().isAtLeast('lg')`) the overlay widens to `max-w-5xl` and draws the step (with its bubble row, minus the last-time bubble, and the ready timer cards) and `CookIngredientsView` with the recipe's `RecipeNotes` under it side by side, no Ingredients button (the list tints the step's lines), with the dishes across the top; the footer is unchanged, the Finish panel stays one centered `max-w-md` column and the timer view one centered `max-w-xl` column, with no ingredient list.

- **Kitchen timers and the Timers screen.** A timer the person sets for something no step mentions is a *kitchen timer*: `CookTimer.recipeId` is `null`, `stepNumber` 0 and `stepName` holds its name (`toKitchenTimerName`: spaces tidied, the first letter up, at most `MAX_KITCHEN_TIMER_NAME` (40), "Timer" when nameless), so every consumer of a dish's timer (`getTimerNext`, `getMeanwhile`, `getTimerSlotKey`, the pill row's shared-step test) reads a kitchen timer as one with nothing after it and nothing beside it, and its timer view has no "Show this step", no "Up next" and no "Meanwhile". `ResumeTimer` gained `recipeId: string | null` and `name: string | null`; `parseTimer` accepts a kitchen timer only with a name (at most 40 characters) and step 0, reads a saved timer from before them (no `name` key) as a dish's timer with none, and ignores a name on a dish's timer; `resolveCookingResume` keeps a kitchen timer when a recipe is deleted (it belongs to none). `MAX_SAVED_TIMERS` follows the cap (twice it). `start(base, seconds, label, shouldFocus = true)` takes a fourth argument so a kitchen timer runs in the background and never takes the screen from what the person is doing (a dish's timer still does, since starting a wait hands the screen to it); `extend(id, ms)` adds time to a running timer (`endsAt` and `durationMs` together, so its push is rescheduled by the same rule that handles any changed deadline) or a paused one (`remainingMs`), and starts a Time's up again for just `ms` (it needs a free slot, drops its ring and its finished slot, and comes into focus); it refuses what would pass a day (`MAX_KITCHEN_TIMER_SECONDS`). **The Timers screen** is a third `CookView` (`'timers'`, swapped in place like the ingredient list, so Back, Escape and the back gesture step out of it, and a timer opened from it returns to it: its footer's secondary reads "Back to timers"): a framed "New timer" card (an optional Name, `PillGroup` lengths from `KITCHEN_TIMER_MINUTES`, and "Other" revealing whole minutes up to 1,440 only once chosen) over "Your timers", one row per timer (the row opens the timer view; chips for Pause or Resume, `+1 min` and `+5 min` (`TIMER_EXTEND_MINUTES`), and an icon Reset or Dismiss), in the pill row's order (finished first, then as they started) so nothing moves under a finger. The footer's primary is **Start timer** (the draft lives in the session as `KitchenTimerDraft`, so typing survives a trip to a timer; `getDraftSeconds` says when it is valid, and the footer's note says what is missing or that the session is at ten); the form clears only after the timer started. It is reached from a 40px timer icon beside "Ingredients" in the step counter's row (every size) and from the timer view's "N more · All M timers" row, which stands in for the rows past `OTHER_TIMER_ROWS` (3) only once more than three would hide (`getVisibleOtherTimers`); the pill row is not drawn on the Timers screen, since the screen lists every timer (one fact, one home). The timer view's `+1 min` / `+5 min` share the row that held "Paused" and "Rang 2 min ago" (a blank row while running), so the card is no taller than before; "Paused" moved to the title line, and a Time's up at the cap says "10 timers are going." in place of the chips instead of showing chips that do nothing. Measured against the version before it at 320×568, 375×667, 390×560, 844×390 and 390×844 with a running timer and two others: the same overflow (38px at 320×568 and 390×560 from the buzz capsule, 0 elsewhere) and the footer's primary the element at its own centre. Scale: with ten live timers and one finished the Timers screen is one scroller of about 1,700px at 390×844 with the form and footer pinned at the ends, the pill row scrolls sideways, and the timer view lists three others and folds the rest.
- **Timers by voice: a name, a length, "all", more time, and a question when it can't tell.** `CookVoiceContext` carries the session's `timers` (id, every name it goes by, its length) and `choice` (the question waiting, while the mic is on). A timer command names its target by `CookTimerTarget { seconds, name, isAll }`: "the ten minute timer", "the pasta timer", "all timers"; none of the three means "the timer". `START_TIMER` takes `{ seconds, name }` ("set a pasta timer for ten minutes", "set a timer for ten minutes for the rice"), `EXTEND_TIMER` takes the amount and a target ("add two minutes", "five more minutes on the rice", "give the rice timer three more minutes"), and "how much time is left on the pasta" and "what timers are running" are `TIME_LEFT` with a target. **What counts as a timer command stays narrow:** the verb needs the word "timer" (or "alarm", "countdown"), or a bare "pause", "resume", "stop", "dismiss" or "silence" with nothing after it (or "all"), or a name that one of this session's timers has (`isKnownTimerName`, so "pause the pasta" acts only while a pasta timer exists and "stop the oven" never does), so "stop it", "set the table", "add salt", "give me a minute" and "bake for ten minutes" are still not commands. `resolveVoiceTimers(timers, { statuses, target, focusedId })` finds the timer: by what was said; with none said, the one in front on screen, a Time's up (the one that rang first), or the only one that fits; **with several that fit and nothing to tell them apart it asks** (the one change in behaviour: "pause" with two running timers and none in front used to take the one ending soonest). The screen then sets a pending question in `CookingSession` (`PendingQuestion`: which of these timers for this verb, or how long a new one runs, for `COOK_CHOICE_MS` (20 s), and only while the mic is on), speaks it whatever Read steps aloud is set to ("Which timer should I pause? Pasta water or 5 min timer?", up to four names and "N more", `describeTimerQuestion`; two timers with one name are told apart by their length) and keeps the window open by the same `COMMAND_HEARD` that every command sends, so the answer needs no wake phrase. While a question waits the grammar also hears an answer (`TIMER_ANSWER`): a name, "the second one" or "3" or "last" (the order it was asked in), a length ("the fifteen minute one"), "both" or "all of them", or "never mind"; a length two timers share asks again between them, and for "how long?" ("set a timer", "set a timer for the rice") a length, or a bare number of minutes, starts it. A real command said instead still runs, and anything else is not an answer. "Stop all" does what "stop" does one timer at a time (dismiss what rang; else pause what runs and say "Say “stop all” again to reset them"; else reset what is paused). "Start the ten minute timer" still starts the step's timer of that length when the step has one and starts a kitchen timer of that length when it doesn't (it used to refuse), and "start the timer" with no length starts the step's, or asks how long when the step has none. A kitchen timer started by voice never takes the screen. **Forgiving and spoken:** `PHRASES.SHOW_TIMERS` lists the singular and the run-together forms a recognizer writes; a named target with no exact match goes through `createOptionMatcher({ typos: 'loose' })` and returns `GUESS` (asked as "Did you mean …?", `isConfirm`, answered yes or no) or, with none close, `ASK` with the name it missed (a lone timer is a `GUESS`); every timer action returns a `speak` as well as a `show`; `RENAME_TIMER` ("rename the pasta timer to noodles", "call this timer soup", "rename it to soup", not for something that is not a timer) renames through `useCookTimers().rename` (a kitchen timer's name is saved with the session; a step timer's is not, since it is derived from its step). The Timers screen opens with the shared `BackLink`, and a timer view reached from it or from a pill has one first under its title (the footer's secondary then shows only Next). Extending keeps a timer's `label` (its slot identity), so "the fifteen minute timer" still means the length it started with; extending a Time's up takes it into focus like any finished timer. Bare "add a minute" is a command only while the session has a timer. A saved session with a kitchen timer is not read by an older build (`recipeId: null` is refused and the copy dropped); `COOKING_SESSION_VERSION` stays 1. "Time left" tells at most four timers by name and the rest as a count, and tells a timer the person named by its name. `check:pinch-voice` covers each phrase above, the narrowness list, the resolver (focus, ringing, named, ambiguous, all) and the answers; real devices and speech services are unverified, so the flows were driven in a browser with a fake recognizer instead.
- **Voice commands.** Three layers, so the lifecycle and the grammar can serve another app. (1) `useSpeechCommands({ lang, speechLang, onFinalTranscript, idleStopMs, onHalt })` (`src/hooks`, no app words) returns `{ isSupported, status: 'off' | 'starting' | 'listening' | 'speaking', isOn, error, start, stop, say, quiet }`. `isSupported` is `window.isSecureContext && (SpeechRecognition ?? webkitSpeechRecognition)`; where it is false the caller draws nothing. The machine is `createSpeechListener(setStatus, setError)`, a plain object made once in a `useState` initializer (no React refs, so the compiler's render checks have nothing to flag) and driven without React by `scripts/checks/speechListener.ts` (part of `npm run check:pinch-voice`) against a fake `window`, recognizer and voice. Its one recognition object is made on the first `start` (a tap), never in an effect, so Strict Mode's double mount creates none; unmount (and Strict Mode's fake one) detaches the four handlers and calls `abort()`. `start` also speaks one silent, empty utterance, since some browsers (Safari) only speak later once a tap has. It runs `continuous`, `interimResults: true` and three alternatives, passes the words of a phrase still being said to `onInterimTranscript` (shown by cooking mode's dictation card while it waits for a tip) and hands each final result's alternatives, best first, to `onFinalTranscript` once (a per-session set of handled result indexes); the callback returns whether it used the phrase, and `idleStopMs` after the start or the last used phrase it stops with `error: 'idle'` (Pinch passes none: its time limits belong to the listening states below). `onHalt(error)` runs each time it stops by itself, for any of the errors below or idle, and never for a `stop` the caller asked for or a teardown, so the caller's own state follows the listener's instead of mirroring it in an effect. On `end` it restarts while the person has it on: a session that heard something, or lasted 1.5 s, restarts after 250 ms and resets the failure count; a quicker end with nothing heard (or after a `network` or other error) counts as a failure and waits 500 ms, 1, 2, then 4 s; five in a row stop it with `error: 'unavailable'`. `no-speech` and `aborted` are normal. `not-allowed` stops it at once with `'denied'`, `service-not-allowed` (Safari with Siri and Dictation off) with `'service'`, `audio-capture` with `'no-microphone'`, `language-not-supported` with `'language'`; none of them retries, so a refused microphone is never asked for in a loop. `start()` after an `abort()` whose `end` hasn't arrived (Chrome throws `InvalidStateError`) waits for that `end`. `say(text)` aborts the session, reads through `speakText` and starts listening again only when the reading's own `end` arrives (a token drops a cancelled reading's `end`), so the app never hears itself; `quiet()` cuts a reading short; a reading whose `start` hasn't come within 1.5 s is given up at once and nothing more is read for the session, so a browser with no working voice never leaves the mic deaf for the length guard. A hidden page (`visibilitychange`) aborts and cancels the reading; `useOnTabReturn` starts it again only if the person still had it on, so a microphone the browser stopped is never re-granted silently. State is set only in recognition, speech, timer and visibility callbacks and in the tap handlers, never in an effect body. (2) `speakText(text, { lang, onStart, onEnd, startTimeoutMs, onNoStart })`, `pickVoice` and `primeSpeech` (`src/utils/speechUtils.ts`): cancels what was being read, picks a voice for the language (exact, then the same language; within that the most natural-sounding by name through `getVoiceQuality`: Edge's "Natural" voices, Apple's Premium, Enhanced and Siri voices and Google's rank above a plain system voice, eSpeak and Apple "compact" voices below it, then one on the device, then the browser's default; a network voice sends what it reads to its vendor, which the explainer says, and is now chosen over a local plain one because it sounds better), keeps the utterance referenced until it ends (Chrome otherwise drops its `end`), and calls `onEnd` once on end, error, cancel, no start in time or a guard of 4 s plus 90 ms a character (60 s at most), since some browsers never send `end`. (3) The grammar is `readCookCommand(alternatives, context)` (and `parseCookCommand`, its command alone; `utils/cookVoice.ts`, pure, `npm run check:pinch-voice`): each guess is lower-cased, accents and punctuation removed, a leading "okay", "hey", "pinch", "please", "can you" and a trailing "please", "thanks" or "now" dropped, and only a guess that is a whole command counts (more than 80 characters never is). The recognizer's top guess is tried first; a lower-ranked one counts only as a phrase of two or more words that isn't a move (an amount, a dish, a timer with "timer" in it, "stop listening"), so "black" heard as "back" or "text" as "next" never acts, and the line names the guess that did. Plain commands are exact phrase lists holding only what "What to say" shows and plain synonyms of it (next, "go to the next step", back, previous, repeat, "read the step", ingredients, steps, how much time is left, "timer check", stop listening, next or previous recipe, and finish, done, "I'm done", "mark as cooked", which are refused); everyday words said to people too ("go on", "keep going", "continue", "again", "what was that", "how long", "the other dish") are not commands. Timers are one pattern (start, set, pause, hold, resume, unpause, continue, stop, dismiss, silence, cancel, reset, clear, turn off… then an optional "the" and length and "timer" or "alarm"): "start" needs "timer", a bare "pause", "resume", "unpause", "stop", "dismiss" or "silence" is allowed, a bare stop or "the alarm" only silences a ringing timer (`scope: 'RINGING'`), and a length is read from words or digits ("twenty five", "an hour and a half", "half an hour") through `findDurations` and must be nothing but a length, so "start the oven" and "pause for effect" are not commands. "How much X" (or "how many") matches X against the lines of the recipe in front, every name and alias, by whole words after a rough singular ("tomatoes", "bay leaves"), looking past a leading quantity and "of" when the whole query finds nothing ("cups of rice", "tablespoons of vinegar", "cloves of garlic"; "cream of tartar" stays whole): an exact name beats a longer one ("garlic" is not "garlic powder"), the step's own lines win when the step names one (otherwise every line of that name), at most three come back, and a query of more than four ingredient words or with words like "do", "you", "time" or "cost" is not a command; X with no match is still the question, answered with the words searched ("I can't find “cups of cilantro” in this recipe's ingredients."). "Switch to / go to / show me X" (nothing else: "cook the chicken" and "open rice" are not commands) matches the plate's titles by words: an exact title wins, otherwise every title holding the words is a candidate, one other than the dish in front is chosen, and several ask which; no match is not a command. (4) `useCookVoice({ uid, step, onTurnOn })` (`hooks/useCookVoice.ts`) wires them: the explainer's Turn on (`pinch:{uid}:voice:agreed`) and Read steps aloud (`pinch:{uid}:voice:read-aloud`) through `useLocalStoragePreference` (try/catch); `onTurnOn` runs inside the tap that starts listening (the screen passes the timers' `prepareSound`, so a Time's up started by voice can still beep in Safari); the message line (2.5 s for what it heard, 8 s for an answer, 5 s for a refusal; a phrase that is no command clears it back to the hint and is never echoed, except in an open command window, which quotes it for 3.5 s as "Not a command: “can you pass the salt”" and does nothing); the stop line (8 s for each stop, then nothing, since the unlit mic carries the state; a tap on the mic after any stop but a quiet-time or lock one reopens the explainer with "Last time:" and the whole cause); the listening states, in the next bullet, which replace the old fifteen-minute idle stop; the 1.2 s guard against the same command twice; ignoring everything but "stop listening" while `hasDialogOnTop()`; and one effect keyed on the shown step that reads it aloud while listening with Read steps aloud on, once per showing (listening coming back after a hidden tab or a reading doesn't read it again), and otherwise calls `quiet()`, so a step change cancels any reading. Recognition listens in `navigator.language` when it is English and in `en-US` otherwise (the grammar is English); readings use the page's language. `CookingSession` hands it one function, an effect event (`useEffectEvent`) that builds the actions and runs the command (`runCookCommand`), through `connect` in an effect, so no action closure leaves the screen; with the actions connected the compiler's purity check flags every `Date.now()` read in the screen, none of which runs during render (the Finish path's `handleNext`, `writeCooks` and `handleDone` run from a tap, and the timer view's tap guards are set in an effect), so those four lines carry a `react-hooks/purity` disable with that reason (tried: building the actions in render as a plain object, handing them to a child component or connecting them from an effect flags the same lines; it takes the start-timer action together with any action that changes the view). The voice path reads its own clock in utils (`withTimeLeft`, `isBeforeDeadline`). The actions do what a tap on the screen in view does: `next` (`goBy(1)`, or the next dish with "Showing Cilantro lime rice"; it never calls `handleNext`, so it can't open the Finish panel that records the cook; on the ingredient list, whose only button is "Back to the steps", it refuses with "Say “steps” to go back to the steps."; in the timer view it follows the footer's secondary (`getTimerSecondary`): `NEXT` is `goNextFromTimer`, the footer's Next or Next recipe, with the timer left running, `BACK` is `backToStep()` and shows "Back to step 3", and with none (a Time's up over a later step, where the footer has only Dismiss) it refuses with "Time's up: say “dismiss” first."), `back` (`goBy(-1)`; on the ingredient list it is "Back to the steps", `showView('steps')`, to the step the cook left; `backToStep()` from the timer view), `repeat` (reads "Adobo chicken. Step 2 of 3. …", in the timer view the step being waited on), `showIngredients`/`showSteps` (`showView`, after `backToStep()` from the timer view), and `finish`, which refuses: "Last step: tap Finish yourself." on the plate's last step ("That's the last step. Tap Finish when you're done." aloud) and "Finish is on the last step." before it. Voice calls the session's functions, never the footer's guarded handlers, and has its own guard sized to the recognizer's delay instead: for 2 s after a Time's up takes the front (`VOICE_DONE_GUARD_MS`, set with the 0.6 s tap guard in the focus effect), "next" and "stop"/"dismiss" on it are held back ("Just rang: say that again to go on."). Timers follow the timer view's API: "start the timer" takes the step in front's first time with no timer yet (`getReadyStepTimers`), or the time of the length said, and calls the session's `startTimer(recipe, stepNumber, seconds, label)`, which focuses the new timer, so the timer view takes the screen ("Started the 30 min timer"; aloud, the first start of a cook adds "Say next step to keep going", or "Say back to see the step" when `getTimerNext` finds nothing ahead of it); a running one of that length is brought to the front with `timers.focus`, a paused one resumed, a finished one dismissed and started again, and at the cap (`timers.isFull`, or a null id) it shows "4 timers are going already." and says "4 timers are going. Another can start once one finishes or is reset.". Pause, resume and stop pick with the pure `pickVoiceTimer` over the timers with their time left: the one in `focused` (the session's `timers.focused`, null while a typed note or a put-away is open on the Finish panel) when it fits, else the one that ends soonest (among Time's ups, the earliest `endedAt`), of the length said; while a Time's up shows, pause and resume refuse ("That timer is ringing. Say “dismiss”."), so voice never pauses a timer nobody can see. "Stop the timer" follows the footer's controls, since a running timer's only control is Pause: the Time's up in front is dismissed (`endTimer`; the focused one, else the earliest `endedAt`, or one of the length said), one per command like the footer's Dismiss, so a queued Time's up is never dismissed unseen, a running one is paused ("Paused. Say “stop” again to reset it."), a paused one is reset (`endTimer`); a bare "stop", "silence" or "dismiss" only dismisses a ringing one. "How much time is left" tells `pickTimersToTell` (pure): the focused timer (else the one with the earliest `endedAt`, else the soonest running, else a paused one) and then the soonest other running timer, through `describeTimersLeft` (two running timers show as "24:59 and 29:55 left", the pills naming them, and are named aloud). Amounts come from the scaled rows of the recipe in front (`getRows`; in the timer view, the timer's dish, and the grammar's context is that dish and step too), through `describeAmounts` (said through `toSpokenAmount`: "1 and a half tablespoons"; an ingredient in two groups gives each amount with its group), and a dish switch is `showRecipe`, or `jumpTo(recipeId, its step)` from the timer view. Each result is `{ ok: true, show?, speak? } | { ok: false, reason, spoken?, isSpoken? }`: an answer is always read aloud, and a refusal is read aloud with Read steps aloud on or when `isSpoken` (it answers a question or a ringing timer: not found, no timer, which dish, a Time's up, Finish); a refusal's shown text fits one line at 320px and its `spoken` form is the full sentence. Tapping Finish calls `voice.stop()`, and the mic isn't drawn on the final panel. The step that read-aloud follows is null while the timer view shows, so nothing is read over a timer, and the step is read again on the way back. The mic is the shared `Subview`'s `headerAction` (one screen-wide switch at the end of the title row, never a call to action; tinted with an edge and a pulse while it listens, and its icon filled while on), so it is on the steps, the ingredient list and the timer view; the line is the first child of the footer's stack, one line tall above the pill row and the buttons on a phone (above Pause and Next in the timer view), which adds 32px, and the first item of the footer's one row on a short landscape screen (`max-w-44`, sized to its text, two lines at most, so the footer doesn't grow there; while listening with nothing to say it is only the chip, so one timer pill fits beside it). It starts with the state chip, a 24px thin pill (a 40px target from a pseudo-element, `aria-label` "Voice options") that names what the mic is doing and opens the voice sheet: "Listening" with a pulsing dot and a bar that empties over the window's ten seconds (a CSS animation keyed on `windowKey`, so each command refills it; static under reduced motion), "Waiting" with a steady dot, the lock icon and the time left (`LockCountdown`, a leaf that redraws each second; the digits are `aria-hidden`), "Starting…" or "Reading"; the text after it is the hint, what it heard, an answer, a refusal, "Reading aloud… tap Next to skip" or, while the browser may be asking, "Allow the microphone if asked."; after a stop the line is only why, for 8 s. Every state is that one line, and the message truncates first. The two modals lead with what matters and fold the rest (`CookVoice.tsx`): the explainer shows two sentences (what to say; the browser asks for the microphone and its speech service hears it; Pinch keeps nothing), then two rows that ask how it should listen (Tap to talk, Say “Hey Pepper”), above a "How it works" line; and the voice sheet (a `Modal` with `useSheetHistory`, mounted by `CookVoiceLine` outside the line's own conditions so it survives the mic turning off under it, its open state in the hook) has two panes chosen with a thin `PillGroup`: "How it listens" (the three ways as rows, `aria-pressed`, one chosen, the lock row counting down; "Last time: …" after a stop that needs explaining; the Read steps aloud switch) and "What to say" (the five commands of a step, led by "hey pepper" in wake mode, above "More commands"). The sheet is 58% of a 390×844 screen. Each fold is a tertiary button with `aria-expanded` and a turning chevron, opens in place with one fact or phrase a line, adds no history layer, and is folded again whenever its modal opens (the explainer folds on every close path, "What to say" on its open); opened, the modal's overlay is the one scroller and the buttons stay pinned (`sticky`, with a hairline only while folded content is open); focus starts on Not now and on Done, after the modal's own focus lands on the fold. With no reveal scroll, what a short screen already leaves under the footer (a plate's step-2 Start behind the pill row, the timer view's Meanwhile line, at 320×568 and 390×560) needs 32px more scroll, and nothing visible at scroll 0 with voice off is covered by it. Nothing here touches Firestore, Storage, indexes or rules.
- **Voice: where it works.** Chrome and Edge on desktop and Android expose `webkitSpeechRecognition` and send audio to their vendor's service (Google for Chrome); Safari 14.1+ on macOS and iOS exposes it and uses Apple's (Siri or Dictation must be on, or it stops with `service-not-allowed` and the "speech service is off" line; continuous listening there ends sooner); Firefox has no recognition constructor, so no mic shows; Brave exposes the constructor but its service fails with `network`, which reads as five failed starts and "Voice control stopped: no answer."; any plain-HTTP page (the LAN address, share links) isn't a secure context, so no mic shows. `speechSynthesis` is in all of them; where it is missing, a reading ends at once and listening carries on. None of this was checked on a real device here (Open questions, item 24).
- **Voice: how the mic listens (`utils/cookVoiceMode.ts` over the shared `@/lib/speech/listeningMode`, pure, `npm run check:pinch-voice`).** The person's choice is a **mode** (`TAP` or `WAKE`, saved with the account as `PinchSettings.voice.isWaiting`; `voice.hasAgreed` and `voice.isReadingAloud` are the other two, each `boolean | null` where null means not chosen on any device yet and the device's own earlier choice under `pinch:{uid}:voice:wake`, `…:agreed` and `…:read-aloud` still stands; every change writes both the device copy, so it shows at once and offline, and the account's dotted path, and the account's value wins when it has one), the mic's condition a **phase**: `VoiceState = { mode, phase: 'OFF' | 'WAITING' | 'WINDOW' | 'LOCKED', epoch, lockEndsAt, stopped }`, moved only by `reduceVoice(state, event)` (a step that changes nothing returns the same object) and carried out by `getVoiceEffect(previous, next)` (`START` when the mic opens, `STOP` when it closes). `useCookVoice` keeps one machine in a ref mirrored by state and a `send(event)` that every tap, recognizer callback and timer goes through, so a start or stop never depends on an effect. OFF has no recognition. WAITING exists only in wake mode: recognition runs and each final phrase is looked at only for the wake phrase. WINDOW takes commands for `COOK_WINDOW_MS` (10 s) counted from the mic really being open, restarted by each command or by the phrase, paused while a reading is spoken (the effect is keyed on `[phase, epoch, isListening]`), and ends in WAITING in wake mode, in OFF in tap mode. LOCKED takes commands with no phrase until `lockEndsAt`, `COOK_LOCK_MS` (15 min) after the tap, never extended; its expiry is a self-re-arming timeout aimed at the wall-clock deadline (so a timer that wakes early re-arms for what is left), settled again by `useOnTabReturn`, and its countdown is drawn by a leaf component and a CSS animation started from the time already elapsed, so the cooking screen does not re-render each second. Waiting turns itself off after `COOK_WAKE_IDLE_MS` (10 min) of quiet since it last entered WAITING, so each window that ends restarts the ten minutes, and the clock stands still while a timer runs and for `COOK_WAKE_TIMER_GRACE_MS` (2 min) after one rings, never past `COOK_WAKE_MAX_MS` (60 min) from entering: `useCookVoice` takes `runningTimers` and `lastRingAt` from the cooking screen, `recordWakeBusy(spans, seen)` keeps the stretches of the current wait (a stretch opens when the first timer runs, closes when none does, and each ring adds its grace once) in a ref, and the timeout is aimed at `getWakeWaitEnd(enteredAt, spans)` and re-aimed whenever those inputs change (both pure, in `cookVoiceMode.ts`, checked in `pinchWake.ts` against ten cases and driven in the browser with Playwright's clock: no timer off at 10, a ten-minute timer started at minute 1 still waiting at 21 and off by 23); 15 minutes was the old limit and the owner asked for less continuous listening, and a simmer step rarely waits longer than ten minutes unattended. A tap steps down one level (a window to waiting, or off in tap mode; waiting and the lock to off); a tap on the mic from off opens a first window in either mode.

  | Event | OFF | WAITING | WINDOW | LOCKED |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `TAP_MIC` | WINDOW (start) | OFF (stop) | WAITING (wake) or OFF (tap, stop) | OFF (stop) |
  | `WAKE_HEARD` (the phrase, alone or with a command) | ignored | WINDOW | WINDOW, restarted | ignored |
  | `COMMAND_HEARD` | ignored | ignored | WINDOW, restarted | ignored |
  | `WINDOW_ENDED` (10 s) | ignored | ignored | WAITING (wake) or OFF (tap, stop) | ignored |
  | `WAIT_ENDED` (10 quiet minutes, paused by a running timer) | ignored | OFF (stop), "wake-idle" | ignored | ignored |
  | `LOCK_ENDED` (15 min) | ignored | ignored | ignored | OFF (stop), "lock-ended" |
  | `STOP` ("stop listening", Finish, leaving) | ignored | OFF (stop) | OFF (stop) | OFF (stop) |
  | `ENGINE_STOPPED` (`onHalt`) | ignored | OFF (stop), the reason | OFF (stop), the reason | OFF (stop), the reason |
  | `CHOOSE TAP` (sheet row) | remembers tap mode | OFF (stop) | stays, ends in OFF | OFF (stop) |
  | `CHOOSE WAKE` | WAITING (start) | stays | stays, ends in WAITING | WAITING |
  | `CHOOSE LOCK` | LOCKED (start) | LOCKED | LOCKED | stays (the deadline is kept) |
  | `MODE` (first-use rows) | remembers the mode, starts nothing | as `CHOOSE` without a start | as `CHOOSE` | as `CHOOSE` |

  `check:pinch-voice` runs that whole table (every event from every state a person can be in, 91 cells, plus named scenarios with the effect each one asks for) and 200 random walks of 60 events that hold its invariants (waiting only in wake mode; `lockEndsAt` set exactly while LOCKED; a stop reason only while OFF; a start asked exactly when the mic opens and a stop exactly when it closes). The **wake phrase** is `readWakeWord(alternatives)`: each guess is put through the grammar's plain-text step, up to two leading fillers ("um", "okay", "well") are dropped, and it counts only when what is left starts with one of `COOK_WAKE_SPELLINGS` (`constants.ts`, the one place: "hey pepper", "hay pepper", "heypepper", "hey peper", and the ways a recognizer mishears it: "hey paper", "hay paper", "heypaper", "hey papper", "hey peppa", "hey pepa") followed by a space or nothing; the matching is the shared `readWakePhrase(alternatives, spellings)` (`@/lib/speech/speechText`). So it is anchored at the start of the phrase: "pepper", "the pepper", "a pepper mill", "hey peppers", "hey papers", "yes pepper", "say hey paper" and "I said hey pepper" never wake it (the checks read the hand-written steps, each of their sentences and every string of the seed data aloud, and mutations that unanchor the phrase or stop a window from restarting each fail the check). The phrase is the owner's choice: two easy syllables, a word every recognizer knows, and not the app's name, so "pinch" stays an ingredient word. "Pepper" is also an ingredient word and a common name, so the match needs both words at the start of what was heard: a step, an answer or "how much pepper" never wakes it, and the one cost is that a person who says "hey pepper" to someone (or something) else in the room opens a ten-second window, which does nothing without a command. Any ranked guess may carry the phrase (waking costs ten seconds of listening and nothing else), but what follows it keeps its rank, so a one-word command in a lower guess ("back" heard as "black") still never runs. What follows goes through the ordinary grammar in the same breath ("hey pepper next step" does the command and opens the window); the phrase alone opens it with nothing run. `planHeard(phase, alternatives, context)` is the one pure place that says what a finished phrase means in each phase (`IGNORE`; `CLEAR`, a non-command while kept open; `COMMAND`; `WAKE` with the command after it or none; `MISSED`, talk in an open window to quote back), checked for 26 phase and phrase cases, and `useCookVoice` only acts on its answer. Waiting ignores the phrase while a dialog is on top, and "stop listening" says the whole thing off from any phase and with a dialog open, "hey pepper, stop listening" included.
- **Voice: the listening sound, the natural voice and what is shared.** Everything that is not about cooking lives in `@/lib/speech` and `useSpeechCommands`: `listeningMode.ts` (the OFF / WAITING / WINDOW / LOCKED machine, `getWaitEnd`, `recordBusy`, `getListeningCue`; `cookVoiceMode.ts` binds Pinch's constants to it and keeps `planHeard`), `speechText.ts` (`toPlainText`, `readWakePhrase`), `listeningSound.ts` and `audioContext.ts` (the chime and the one shared `AudioContext`, resumed in the tap that turns voice on and, for a start with no tap, on the next tap anywhere), `readAloud.ts` (one reading function: the natural voice when it is ready, the browser's voice otherwise and on any failure; it first runs the text through `spokenText.ts`'s `toSpokenText`, so both voices get "half a teaspoon of salt" for "1/2 tsp salt", "350 degrees Fahrenheit" for "350°F", "2 tablespoons of butter" for "2 tbsp. butter", "10 to 12 minutes" for "10-12 min" and "9 by 13" for "9x13"; short units are written out only after a number, a measure gets its "of", a full stop that belonged to an abbreviation no longer ends a sentence for the sentence splitter, and text already in words passes through unchanged; the table of cases is in `speechShared.ts`) and `naturalVoice/`. **The chime** is a two-note sine tone from WebAudio, no asset: rising (E5 to A5) when listening starts; nothing sounds when a window closes, the wait runs out or the mic is turned off (the owner asked for a sound on starting only). `useCookVoice.send` asks `getVoiceCue(previous, next)` on every change of phase (none for a window a command extended, none when a failure stopped the mic, none when the phrase arrived with its command), and `useSpeechCommands.cue` plays it and makes the listener drop what the recognizer returns until the chime and 200 ms are over (`mute`, checked in `speechListener.ts`). It does not wait for the chime before starting the recognizer, because some browsers refuse a start that is not inside the tap. It stays silent while a cooking timer waits to be dismissed (`isAlarmRinging`), on a hidden page and until a tap has resumed the context. The sound itself is WebAudio and no check plays it; the pure parts (which change of phase gets which cue, the mute window) are checked. **The natural voice** (`useNaturalVoice('pinch:{uid}:voice:natural')`, off to start, remembered per person on the device, a row in the voice sheet: `NaturalVoiceOption`) runs a Piper VITS voice (`en_US-ljspeech-medium`, English only) in a dedicated Web Worker with ONNX Runtime Web (WebAssembly SIMD, one thread) and the espeak-based phonemizer of `@diffusionstudio/piper-wasm`. Turning it on downloads the voice (about 63 MB, from `huggingface.co`, or from `VITE_NATURAL_VOICE_BASE_URL` when a copy sits on our own host) into Cache Storage; the runtime files (about 29 MB: the two wasm files and the phonemizer data) are this site's own assets, fetched when the voice first starts. Nothing is requested before the tap and nothing the person says or reads is sent; "Remove the 90 MB download" deletes it, and turning the switch off frees the memory and keeps the download. A reading is cut into sentences (`splitSentences`) and each is synthesized in the worker while the one before it plays, so the first is heard as soon as it is made; a step change or a stop cancels the queued sentences. Any failure (no WebAssembly SIMD, under 4 GB of memory, a plain-HTTP page, a failed download, a voice that gives no sound within 7 seconds, which after two in a row sets the voice aside for the session) leaves the reading to `speechSynthesis`, so the existing voice never waits on it. The worker chunk (about 230 kB) is lazy and kept out of the service worker's precache (`globIgnores` in `vite.config.ts`). Not verified without a device: the audio, the download from Hugging Face, inference in the worker and its latency on a phone, the licence text of the voice's dataset, and the microphone permission check on Safari. `npm run check:pinch-voice` includes `speechShared.ts`, which also fails if a file under `src/lib/speech` or the shared hooks names a recipe, a cook, Pinch or a step.
- **Voice: what the wake phrase cannot do.** (a) Waiting is continuous recognition: the browser's mic indicator is on and, in Chrome, audio streams to Google for as long as it waits, which the first-use rows and How it works say in plain words; nothing is detected on the device. (b) A recognizer session ends by itself after silence or about a minute (Chrome) and sooner in Safari: `useSpeechCommands` restarts it after 250 ms with no visible change, so a phrase spoken in that gap is missed. (c) A phrase is final only after a pause (about a second), so "Hey Pepper" shows its window about a second after it is said. (d) It listens in the page's English; a mishearing that is not one of the spellings ("hey jeff") does nothing and the person says it again or taps. (i) Waiting starts by itself as cooking opens only when the wake phrase is the remembered mode, the explainer has been agreed to, the page can listen and `navigator.permissions` already reports the microphone as `granted` (`readMicrophonePermission`; where the Permissions API cannot say, or the answer is `prompt`, nothing starts and the first tap on the mic does, as before). It starts silently, since no tap says "now", and a browser that refuses a start without a tap stops it with the usual "microphone is blocked" line. Before this, waiting existed only after a tap on the mic or a row in the sheet, which is why saying the phrase to a closed mic did nothing. A web page cannot listen with the screen off, the tab hidden or the browser closed: the phrase works only while the cooking screen is open and awake. (e) Screens that turn off, backgrounded tabs and locked phones end recognition: the page's wake lock (`useWakeLock`) keeps a cooking screen on, a hidden page aborts and `useOnTabReturn` restarts it only if the mic was on, and a phone that locks anyway loses it until the page is back (the deadline of a lock keeps running). (f) A ringing timer can drown the person out; the Time's up is never delayed or quieted by voice (nothing in this code plays a sound; it only reads text aloud when asked), and "dismiss" within 2 s of it ringing is held back as before. (g) A noisy kitchen misrecognizes: the window quotes what it heard as a non-command so the person sees it, and every command is one tap on the screen. (h) Nothing here is sent anywhere by Pinch's own code; no data model, rule, index or Function changed.

### 5d. Marking a recipe cooked

- **Where it happens.** (1) The recipe drawer's "Mark as cooked" row in its ⋯ More ("Keeps a record you can change later"; disabled until its write settles, so a double tap or a tap during the acknowledgement lag can't make two cooks and two Undo toasts, which offline means it waits for the write to be acknowledged) adds a cook at the recipe's own servings (`getCookServings`: kept inside 0 to 1,000) with `cookedAt` now, and a toast says "Marked Adobo chicken as cooked" with an Undo that deletes exactly that cook's id. (2) Cooking mode's Finish (last step) records the cook as it opens the final panel: `markRecipeCooked(recipe, servings, { isToasted: false })` from a recipe, `markPlannedPlateCooked({ plannedMealId, entries, cookedAt })` (no toast; one entry per recipe of the session, one for a single recipe) from a planned meal, guarded by `isMarking` so it runs once; the panel (`CookRecord`) says "Marking as cooked…", then "Marked as cooked" with an Undo (`removeCooks` over the ids this screen wrote, once, all or none: "Undoing…", then "Not marked as cooked" with "Mark as cooked"; hidden once a box was put away or a note saved from the panel, `hasFollowUps`: each has its own Undo, and removing the cook under them would orphan them) and "Servings made" for each recipe, whose taps write the cook with `updateCook` (field-scoped, reading `cookedAt` from the store at the tap). Timers are not stopped by Finish; Done goes through `leave()` and its "Stop timers and leave?" question. A plan's cook already in the store before this session wrote one makes the card "Already in your cooking history" (a recipe whose `markPlannedPlateCooked` result says `isNew: false` reads the same): Finish writes the session's servings onto it (`updateCook`), there is no Undo for it (Undo never deletes a cook this screen didn't write), and put-away and the note attach to that cook; a plate whose other recipes were marked elsewhere lists a recipe left unmarked as "Not marked", with "Mark as cooked" in the card's title row. A repeat Finish (after "Back to the last step") writes the servings again instead of a second cook. A failed write is a red line in the card with "Mark as cooked" to retry; a full history (500) records nothing and says why. Leaving before Finish writes nothing. Finish only records when `canMark` holds; otherwise it leaves as before. Started from a planned meal (`DayDrawer`'s `onStartCooking` puts `plannedMealId` on the subview state; a recipe's own drawer leaves it out) it is the same transaction and id as the row's "Mark as cooked", so one dinner can never be two cooks, and a meal can be a day ahead (the cook is written at once and the row shows it from that day). Every close that follows an awaited step (Done, a saved note, `leave()`) is `closeSubviewIf(...)`, which closes only if the screen showing is still this recipe's (and this plan's) cooking screen (a functional `setSubview` update in `Pinch.tsx`): the write can sit queued offline and resolve long after the person has left, and a bare `closeSubview()` used to close whatever was open by then (a half-typed recipe form: reproduced with a delayed write, then fixed). The header's back is not disabled while the write is in flight, since offline that would trap the person on the screen: it leaves, the write carries on and a failed one shows its sentence in the panel while it is open. (2b) The recipe drawer's footer primary "Start cooking", shown when the recipe is on today's plan and that planned meal has no cook yet: it opens cooking mode with that item's servings and `plannedMealId`, so Finish writes `{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}` like the planned meal's own Start cooking. The today key is read once when the drawer opens. (3) A planned meal on today or a day that has passed (`meal.date <= getLocalTodayDate()`) has one "Mark as cooked" chip per recipe in it, on that recipe's row of the plate, turning into "(✓) Cooked · Not cooked" once its cook exists (the toast's Undo covers the seconds after a mark, but this standing one can come weeks later and takes a cook out of the history, so it asks first, "Not cooked after all?", naming the recipe and when it was cooked). A recipe listed twice in one meal is one row with the servings added up (`getMarkableRecipes`), because the deterministic id can only hold one cook; an ingredient item has no row. (4) The drawer's clock icon on the title line (`DoorButton`, `aria-label='Cooking history'`, drawn only once `cookSummary` exists) opens the history sheet; the old "Cooked 4 times, last Oct 12" row and the view it sat in are gone, and the Recipes list and the drawer's front show no cook count.
- **Writes.** `addCook({ uid, recipeId, servings, cookedAt })` is a plain `setDoc` of a new document with a fresh id and returns the id (the drawer toast's Undo, the Finish panel's Undo and the history's "+ Add an earlier cook" use it); `markPlannedCooked(...)` is one `runTransaction` that reads the deterministic document and writes nothing when it exists, resolving `{ id, isNew }` (the decision is the pure `mergePlannedCook`, which `npm run check:pinch-math` covers with the other cook helpers), and a `false` from the planned meal's own row shows an "Already marked" toast; `markPlannedPlateCooked({ plannedMealId, entries, cookedAt })` is the same decision for a plate's recipes in one `runTransaction` (every document read first, then each missing one set), resolving `[{ recipeId, id, isNew }]`: cooking mode's Finish uses it for any planned session, one recipe or several, and treats an `isNew: false` as already marked (the panel says so; no toast); it never reads the planned meal, so a meal that is gone or outside the loaded days still gets its cooks (a cook whose `plannedMealId` points at a meal that no longer exists is already an ordinary state: deleting a meal leaves its cooks); `removeCooks({ ids })` deletes several in one `writeBatch`, so the panel's Undo takes back all of a plate's cooks or none; `updateCook` is a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `servings`, `cookedAt`, `updatedAt` (plus a missing `plannedMealId` as `null`) and never writes a cached cook back; `removeCook` is `deleteDoc` of one id (Undo, and the history's Delete after a destructive confirm). The two adds refuse, with a sentence the screen shows, when the cooks listener hasn't landed or has failed, when the recipe isn't in the loaded recipes, when the servings aren't above zero and up to 1,000, when `cookedAt` is more than a minute ahead, and when the recipe already has `SOFT_MAX_COOKS_PER_RECIPE` (500) cooks. The screens gate the same predicate: the rows are disabled while the history loads, say "This recipe's history is full" at 500, and are left out when the listener failed. A transaction doesn't queue offline, so marking from a planned meal needs a connection: every cook write logs a Firebase error and shows "Check your connection and try again." for `unavailable` or an offline browser (the shopping list's rule), else one plain sentence; the recipe drawer's mark is an ordinary write whose toast arrives when the write is acknowledged, while the history sheet already shows it from the local cache.
- **The history sheet.** "Cooking history" (opened by the clock icon on the recipe drawer's title line, drawn only when the recipe has a cook, so for a recipe never cooked the way in is to mark it and change the date in its history) closes the drawer and opens `CookingHistorySheet`, a `FormSheet` titled "Cooking history" (a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`), from `RecipesScreen`. It lists the recipe's cooks newest first as "Oct 12 · 6 servings" (with "From your plan for Sun, Oct 11" when `plannedMealId` is set), the first `COOK_HISTORY_FIRST_PAGE` (8) and then "Show N more" in pages of 50, but only while more than three would be hidden (so 11 cooks show all of them, 12 show 8 and "Show 4 more"), with "Show fewer" once everything is open. A fixed header (the recipe's name and "+ Add an earlier cook") sits over the one scrolling list, capped with `max-h-[max(12rem,calc(92dvh-14rem))]`. A row, or the add link, swaps the sheet's content in place for `CookEditView` behind a `BackLink` (never a second overlay; each opening carries a token, and the close after a save or delete is `closeView(token)`, a functional update that does nothing once another view is showing, so a write that resolves late can't close the wrong one): a day and a time (`Input type='date'` with `max` today, stacked below a narrow container), a servings `Stepper`, Cancel and Save, and for an existing cook an icon-only `DeleteIconButton` on the left of the footer whose destructive confirm ("Delete this cook?") names the day and time. Save is disabled until something changed and says "Pick a time that has already passed." for a moment in the future (compared against `useNow()`, the 15-second clock); a save that leaves the day and time untouched keeps the stored `cookedAt` to the millisecond instead of the minute the inputs show. The edited cook is read from the live store by id, so one deleted on another device returns to the list. When the listener failed the sheet says so and offers no add; a full history says so and hides "+ Add an earlier cook". Under each row that has no note a quiet "Add a note" (and an "Add a note" row under Day, Time and Servings in such a cook's own view, disabled with "Save your changes first" while that view is dirty) swaps the same content for `NoteForm` (§5e), carrying a token too, so its close is the same functional update; both rows are left out when the notes listener hasn't landed or failed. **How it turned out.** `setCookReview({ uid, id, rating?, photoFile? })` is a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `rating` and `photoPath` (plus any missing `plannedMealId`), so it never overwrites a concurrent edit of the day or servings; it rejects a rating that is not a half step from 0.5 to 5. A photo is resized to `COOK_PHOTO_MAX_DIMENSION` (1,280) and stored as WebP at `getCookPhotoPath(uid, id, now)`; every upload has a new name, so there is no cache version to bump and the Storage rule (`storage.rules`, `cooks/{cookId}/photo-<ms>.webp`, owner only, under 5 MB, `image/*`, no `list`) allows create and delete but never an overwrite. The new file is deleted if the write fails, the replaced one after the write lands, and removing or deleting a cook deletes its photo too (best effort: a cook is gone either way, and nothing reads an orphaned file). `CookResultFields` (stars and photo; the Finish panel's `CookResultCard` and the cook's own view) writes each change as it is made, with no Save; in the Finish card the photo and the stars sit side by side and, below 360px, stack **centred** (`max-[359px]:flex-col` with the stars' column `max-[359px]:items-center`, the photo's box centred by the column); the cook's own page (`isLarge`) keeps a full-width photo with the stars left-aligned under it; `CookPhotoThumb` and `useCookPhotoUrl` resolve the download URL through `useStorageImageUrl`, and every picture is a `FallbackImage`. The history's average is `getRatingAverage` over rated cooks only and says how many ("4.3 average from 4 ratings"); the Photos grid is the latest 30 photos (`PHOTOS_SHOWN` in the sheet) and every one is also a row of All cooks. Not checked by the rules: that the photo object exists, or that its folder matches a cook that exists (the path match only ties it to this kitchen and this cook id). Ratings and photos are not in `buildRecipeShare`'s allowlist, so no link carries them. **What came out of a cook (round 12c).** Each row gains one quiet line from `getWrittenLine` ("A note and a change", "A change", "2 notes") built from `writtenByCook`, a `Map` of the notes and changes (and tweaks) that carry that cook's id (a tip never does); `notedCookIds` counts NOTE kind only, so "Add a note" still shows under a cook that has only a change. The cook's own page (`CookEditView`, the `edit` view) takes a `journal` prop, drawn first in its form as a `custom` field only when the cook has entries: `CookJournal` (`components/recipes`), "From this cook", `NoteEntry` rows (with `isInCook`, so the cook's own day and the "written before" line are left out, since they would repeat the page) oldest first over `selectNotesByRecipe` with that `cookId`, with Edit (swaps the sheet to `NoteForm` with the note's id: the `note` view kind carries `noteId`) and, on a planned change, "Update the recipe", which closes the sheet and opens the recipe form with `fromChangeIds`. A tip is the recipe's, so it is never in a cook's page.
- **Reading.** `selectCooksByRecipe` (a `Map<recipeId, Cook[]>`, newest first by `cookedAt`, then `createdAt`, then id), `selectCookSummaries` (a `Map<recipeId, { count, lastCookedAt }>` built in one pass over every cook), `selectCooksById` and `selectCooksLoadError`, all `createSelector`s over the slice's items. The recipe drawer, the delete confirm, the plan builder's recent picks and cooking mode read the summaries (the count and the last date); a planned meal's row reads its own `Cook` object and whether the recipe's history is full, so every visible row of a meal re-renders when the cooks change, which costs nothing next to the few rows of one meal; a Recipes row reads no cook count. Cooks whose `recipeId` names no recipe are never read by anything, so they are neither shown nor counted.
- **Listener and failure.** `startCooksListener` (a plain collection `onSnapshot`) is started from `usePinchSync` with the other optional collections. Its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded cooks and sets `loadError`; it never reaches the load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out) and is not part of the loading gate (`selectIsPinchLoaded`). Without cooks the app is unchanged except that the drawer shows no Cooked row or "Mark as cooked" row, and a planned meal shows no cook rows.
- **Archive and delete.** Archiving a recipe (it is on a planned day or in a saved meal) leaves its cooks alone, so the history is still there; a hard delete takes the recipe's cooks with it in `deleteCooks`, which reads them from Firestore (`getDocs` with `where('recipeId', '==', id)`, one equality, so no index) instead of the store, because the store is empty while the cooks load or after the listener fails, and deletes them in `writeBatch`es of `COOK_BATCH_SIZE` (400), after the recipe's own delete has succeeded and as best effort (a failure is logged and the delete still finishes). Anything that survives (a cook written after the read, a failed batch) names a recipe that no longer exists and is ignored by every reader. Cooks made from a planned meal are not removed when the planned meal is: the person did make it.
- **The cap.** `SOFT_MAX_COOKS_PER_RECIPE` (500) is checked by the add actions against the loaded cooks and by the screens, not by the rules, which can't count a collection. It is per recipe, so the whole collection is not bounded.
- **A toast over a drawer.** The Undo toast is raised while the recipe drawer is open, so the toast stack sits above the drawers' overlay (`z-index: 110` on the notifications region in `index.css`; drawers and modals are `z-[100]`): below it the toast showed dimmed behind the overlay and its button couldn't be tapped. It is a central rule and reaches every mini-app's toasts.


### 5e. Recipe notes and tweaks

- **Where a person writes one.** (1) The foot of the recipe drawer's front, `RecipeEntryRows`: with no tip and no note it is one slim dashed row, "Add a tip or note", under a "Tips and notes" header; otherwise each kind that has entries is its own row (below) ending in an add card of that kind, and a kind with none while the other has some is a slim dashed "Add a tip" or "Add a note" row. The "Note" sheet (`NoteForm`, through `NoteSheet`'s `initialKind`, which `onOpenNote(recipeId, noteId, kind)` carries from `RecipeDrawer` through `RecipesScreen` and `PantryRecipeDrawer`) opens on `NoteKindPills` (Tip, Change, Note), the words, a change's why (`NoteReasonField`) and `NoteStepPicker`. (2) "Add a note" under each time in the cooking history that has no NOTE yet, and in such a cook's page (a `custom` field of its form). (3) Cooking mode: the dashed + bubble at the step's foot (`CookStepDock`'s `onAdd`; `CookingSubview` passes `openStepCapture` as `onAddEntry` through `CookStepView`, and null where `capture.isAvailable` is false or the recipe is removed; the old notebook-and-pen button in the counter row is gone) opens the composer (`CookCaptureView`) for the step on screen, and the last screen's review card (`CookReview`; see "Cooking mode's review" below) adds one for the whole recipe. (4) By voice: "add a tip" and its kin dictate one (below). An entry written on a cook carries that cook's id as `cookId` (a NOTE or a CHANGE; a TIP writes `null`, which the rules enforce). An entry written during cooking, before the cook is recorded at Finish, has none yet: see "A cook's entries" below.
- **The recipe front's snap rows.** `RecipeEntryRows` (`components/recipes`, rendered last by `RecipeFront`, which takes `onOpenEntry(noteId | null, kind | null)` from `RecipeDrawer`; it replaces `RecipeJournalRow`, which mixed four kinds) draws the person's TIPs and their NOTEs as two separate `FormSection`s, "Tips" and "Notes", each only when it has entries and each a horizontal scroll-snap row (`snap-x snap-mandatory`, cards `snap-start`, the width on the `li`: `w-[80%]` on a phone and `sm:w-72`, so the next card peeks as the cue to swipe; a percentage width on the card's own button collapsed the add card to 46px). Tips run by step (the whole recipe's first), then oldest first; notes newest first. A card is a button (emoji, "Step N" on a tip or a step's note, the date on a note, the words at `line-clamp-4` on a span that is not `block`; its `aria-label` carries the kind, step, date and words) that calls `onOpenEntry(note.id, null)`, the drawer's `onOpenNote` (the same `NoteSheet` the Edit chip opens). A dashed add card ends each row (`onOpenEntry(null, kind)`). A planned CHANGE and a TWEAK are never cards: a change is an action (the recipe's ⋯ More has "Update the recipe", drawn only while a change is planned, "With the change you planned" or "With the N changes you planned", first in its group; it closes the drawer and opens the recipe form with `fromChangeIds`), and both kinds belong to the cook they came from (below). The ones with no cook to sit under (written from the recipe, or whose cook was deleted) are listed in the same ⋯ More view by `RecipeChanges` (`components/recipes`), "Changes", after the stock rows: the CHANGEs and TWEAKs with no `cookId` or a `cookId` that is not in `selectCooksById`, planned ones first then newest, as `NoteEntry` rows (Edit and "Update the recipe" on a CHANGE, a tweak read-only), three at a time with `ShowMoreButton`; it draws nothing when there are none, and a change that came from a cook is on that cook's page only (one home each). A tip card with no step draws no header row. The rows render nothing while the notes listener is loading or has failed. The drawer's clock door (`DoorButton`, `History` icon, `aria-label='Cooking history'`) is drawn only when the recipe has cooks (`cookSummary !== undefined`) and calls `onOpenHistory`, which closes the drawer and opens `CookingHistorySheet` (as Modify closes it before the form); the drawer has no `history` view and no `RecipeNotes` any more.
- **A cook's entries (round 12c).** A CHANGE carries the cook it was written for, like a NOTE: `addNote` writes `cookId` for every kind but TIP (`getAddProblem` checks the cook exists for both) and `useCookCapture` passes the cook recorded for the recipe, which is null until Finish records it. So entries written on a step during cooking are tied afterwards: `attachNotesToCook({ uid, recipeId, cookId, noteIds })` (`noteActions.ts`) takes this session's captured entry ids, keeps those still in the store with the same recipe, a kind other than TIP and no cook, and sets only `cookId` on them in one `writeBatch` (a field-scoped `update`, so `updatedAt`, and with it a share's change stamp, are untouched); a failure is logged and returns 0, never shown. `CookingSubview` calls it from an effect once a record's `recordedCookId` exists (its deps are the notes and cooks stores: it works out which captured entries still need a cook, skips ids whose write is in flight (`tyingToCook`), and clears them when the write settles, so a failure or notes that load late are retried on the next change instead of being marked done early). The thunk treats an entry as unattached when it has no cook or when its cook is no longer in the store (a cook deleted, or undone on the Finish panel and recorded again). `firestore.rules` (notes update) allows `cookId` to be set once while the document has none (`resource.data.get('cookId', null) == null`), or again when the old cook no longer exists (`!exists(…/cooks/$(resource.data.cookId))`), and otherwise never moved or removed; a TIP can't have one (`isRecipeNote`). Checked against the emulator as owner, another user and signed out (12 checks: a cookless NOTE and CHANGE can be tied; a note whose cook was deleted can be tied to another; a TIP, a tied note moving to another live cook or losing it, another user and a signed-out write are denied; a words edit still works) and in the browser (a change written on step 1 of Cilantro lime rice carried the recorded cook's id afterwards, its `updatedAt` unchanged). The preview can't set it until the rule deploys on merge; before then the tie is refused and logged and the entry stays cookless, as every entry from before this was. Applying a planned change keeps its `cookId` on the TWEAK it becomes (`getTweakPlan`), checked in the browser from a cook's page: the seeded "Double the garlic" change tied to a cook became the tweak with the same cook id and the save went through.
- **The full journal list.** `RecipeNotes` reads `selectNotesByRecipe` and `selectNoteSummaries` for its recipe and `selectCooksById`, and draws `NoteEntry` rows: the date (`formatDateShort`, with a year when it isn't this one), written "Changed on Oct 6" with no tag for a TWEAK (the word "tweak" is never on screen) so it reads as a point in time (a TIP and a CHANGE carry their own tag, "Tip" and "To change", and "Step 2" when `resolveNoteStep` finds their step),  then "After cooking on Oct 12" when `cookId` names a cook that still exists, a TWEAK's "You changed:" list, the text (`whitespace-pre-wrap`, four lines and a "Show more" row (`ShowMoreButton`, "Show less" once open) only when it overflows, measured by a `ResizeObserver` callback, so the state is set from a subscription and not an effect body) and, for a NOTE written before the recipe's latest tweak, "Written before your Oct 20 tweak"; and one `ActionChips` row of 40px hairline pills ends the entry: "Update the recipe" on a CHANGE only (`onApplyChange`, below), then Edit (`onEdit`; named "Edit the note from Oct 6", so its accessible name holds its visible word, and a TWEAK shows Edit alone). The first 5 entries show and "Show N more" (pages of 50) only when more than three would be hidden, with "Show fewer" once everything is open. The drawer no longer draws it (its front has `RecipeEntryRows`, above, and the history behind the clock shows each cook's entries with `NoteEntry`, below); it stays in cooking mode's ingredient list and its wide side column. The latest NOTE is the dock's "Last time" view (below), where next time starts. `RecipeNotes` takes `onAdd`, `onEdit` and `onApplyChange` as `null` to read the journal without its add row and Edit chips, which is how cooking mode uses it (a sheet never opens over an overlay subview). A failed notes listener turns the section into "Your notes couldn't load just now." with no add row.
- **In cooking mode.** The note from last time is the first bubble (🕘) of `CookStepDock`'s row, not a card of its own (`CookNoteCard` is gone). On a recipe's first step `CookingSubview` passes `lastTime = { recipeId, isDismissed, onGotIt }` to `CookStepView` (null on later steps, in the timer view, the ingredient list and the final panel, and from `lg`, where the ingredient column shows `RecipeNotes`, so the same note is never on screen twice); `isDismissed` is `!isCookNoteShown(stepIndex, gotItNotes, recipeId)` (pure, `check:pinch-math`: step 0 of that recipe and not in the session's `gotItNotes`, a list of recipe ids kept in `CookingSession` beside the ticks so the ingredient list and back keep it away; nothing is stored). The dock reads the same selectors the card did (`selectNotesByRecipe`, `selectNoteSummaries`, plus `notes.isLoaded` and `loadError`) in its own component, so the session doesn't re-render for a note, and has a last-time bubble only once the notes have loaded without error and the recipe has a NOTE about the whole recipe (`getLastTimeNote`: tweaks, tips, changes and a note about one step are skipped; the recipe already reflects tweaks and a step's own entries show with that step). Its panel is titled "Last time · Oct 12" over the note's words from `getNoteSummary` (white space squeezed) through `leadWithAdvice` (`utils/notes.ts`, `check:pinch-math`: from the "Next time" sentence on when something comes before it, with a leading "…"), in full inside the panel's scrolling body, then "You've changed the recipe since." when `lastTweakAt` is later; the "Got it" button is in the panel's header beside the ✕, adds the recipe to `gotItNotes` and closes the panel. While the note is not dismissed its bubble is tinted (`bg-accent!` with a hairline); once dismissed the 💬 bubble stays, untinted, so the note can be read again, without the button. No Apply: a note that wants the recipe changed is written as a change, whose "Update the recipe" is on the step's entries and in the question Done asks (see "Updating from a planned change" and "Editing the recipe from cooking mode"). The older whole-recipe notes are the 🗒️ bubble's entries on the first step. The ingredient list (and the side column from `lg`) draws `RecipeNotes` with `onAdd` and `onEdit` null, the one stable door to the whole history; it renders nothing for a recipe with no notes, and a failed listener shows its one "couldn't load" line there.
- **Cooking step 1 on a short screen (round 8 fix, reworked in rounds 12c and 12e).** Closed, the foot is one 40px row of bubbles at every screen height, so there is nothing to fold; what a short screen changes is the open panel, whose body is `max-h-44` (176px) and, under 700px tall, `max-h-32` (128px) and scrolls inside itself (a media query in the class; the dock no longer calls `useIsShortScreen`). The panel is absolutely positioned above the row (`bottom-full`, `z-20`; below it on a short landscape phone), so it overlays the lower part of the step instead of pushing it, and on the shortest screens it can cover part of the instruction (ISSUES.md). `CookStepView` takes the gap between the timer and what follows from 32px to 12px and between a plate's dish strip and the counter row from 16px to 8px under 640px tall (the air above and below the instruction is untouched), and the shared `Subview` clamps a wrapped title to one line under 700px tall. The check at 320×568, 375×667, 390×560 and 844×390 (single recipe and plate) is that the footer's buttons, the timers row and every bubble are the element at their own centre, closed and with the heaviest panel open.

- **The note sheet.** `NoteSheet` is a `FormSheet` ("Note": a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`) mounted by `RecipesScreen` after the drawer closes, like the cooking history; `NoteForm` is its body and is also what the history sheet swaps in place (behind a `BackLink`, never a second overlay). One textarea (`NoteTextField`, focused once the sheet has settled, with a counter from 1,800 characters) and, for a new note on a recipe that has cooks and no `initialCookId` (a note started from a cook doesn't ask), "Is this about a time you cooked it?" as a `PillGroup`, starting on a cook from today (local day, labelled "Today") when there is one: "No, just a note" first, then the latest four cooks (`getNoteCookChoices`, plus an older cook that was preselected), each labelled with its day, and with its time when two share a day (`getCookChoiceLabels`). Save is disabled until the text has a character (a blank paste stays disabled) and the footer says why. Editing offers only the words (the date and the cook show as a muted line, a TWEAK's "You changed:" list is read-only), an icon-only `DeleteIconButton` asks "Delete this note?" first. Saving a new note toasts "Added a note to Adobo chicken". A sheet whose notes haven't loaded says so and offers nothing.
- **Writes.** `addNote({ uid, recipeId, kind, text, reason, cookId, stepIndex })` (`kind` TIP, CHANGE or NOTE) is a plain `setDoc` with a fresh `generateUuid()` id and every key, the step's words worked out from the recipe in the store (`getStepAnchor`), `cookId` kept for a NOTE only and `reason` for a CHANGE only; it refuses, with a sentence the sheet shows, until the notes listener has landed, when it failed, for a recipe that isn't in the store, for text that is blank or over 2,000 characters, for a `stepIndex` the recipe has no step for, for a `cookId` that isn't one of that recipe's loaded cooks (a NOTE) and at 300 entries for the recipe. `updateNote({ uid, id, text, reason?, stepIndex? })` is a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `text` (blank is refused for a TIP, CHANGE and NOTE and becomes `null` for a TWEAK), a CHANGE's `reason`, the step (`stepIndex` and its words together, never for a TWEAK) and `updatedAt`, plus any key an older document lacks; it never writes a cached note back. `removeNote` is `deleteDoc` after a destructive confirm. The 300 is the app's cap, not the rules'.
- **A tweak is part of the recipe's save.** `saveRecipe` builds the TWEAK itself (`getTweakPlan`: none, a new entry, or the rewrite of a planned change into the tweak, see "Updating from a planned change") and puts it in the recipe's own `writeBatch`, beside the recipe update, any new source and any new ingredients, so the note exists exactly when the recipe write did (a failed save leaves no orphan note, and an offline save is queued whole). It does so only for an edit (never a new recipe), only when `selectTweakTracking` is `'on'` (the recipe has a cook, the cooks and notes listeners have landed without error and the journal has room), and only when `getRecipeChanges(existing, draft, …)` returns a line. A cover-only edit has none, neither has one that changes only the recipe's care (§5f), and neither has one that changes nothing the person could see (stray spaces, an empty step or description). The note's `createdAt` is the recipe's own `updatedAt` for that save, its `text` is the reason trimmed (or `null`) and its `cookId` is `null`. The reason is asked by the form (below), not by `saveRecipe`, which only writes `draft.tweakReason`. The lines are compared with the recipe as this device last had it, so an edit made on another device in between can show in them.
- **Undoing the latest change.** A TWEAK also stores `previous`, a `RecipeSnapshot` (title, description, servings, prep and cook minutes, ingredients, steps, nutrition override, source id: exactly what `getRecipeChanges` compares) of the recipe as this device last had it, taken by `getRecipeSnapshot` in `getTweakPlan` and written in the same `writeBatch` as the tweak; every other kind and every tweak written before undo existed has `previous: null` (a missing key reads as null; `getMissingNoteFields` backfills it when such a note is edited). `getUndoableTweakIds` offers Undo only on the newest tweak (or all tweaks sharing its `createdAt`) while each has a `previous` and the recipe's `updatedAt` equals that `createdAt`: a save always sets both to the same `now`, so a later save of any kind (even one that wrote no tweak, such as a cover-only edit) moves `updatedAt` and ends the offer, and a transaction cannot run the "is there a newer tweak" query, so this is also what the transaction checks. `undoRecipeChange` runs `freezeBeforeEdit` (the recipe's content moves past days' costs), then one `runTransaction` that reads the recipe and each tweak, hands them to the pure `planTweakUndo` (refuses with a sentence when the recipe is gone, a tweak is gone or has no `previous`, or `updatedAt` differs from a tweak's date) and otherwise updates only the nine compared fields and `updatedAt: now` (a field-scoped update; the cover, care, links, collections, favorite and archive state are untouched) and deletes the tweaks. It needs a connection.
