Accepted limits: the snapshot is the recipe as this device last saw it, so an edit made on another device just before is also put back; a custom ingredient or source made with the edit stays (nothing references it, and Kitchen lists it); a change applied from a planned one is not planned again after an undo; once the latest is undone the next-older one is not offered, since the recipe's `updatedAt` has moved past its date (going back in time would make a shared link, which compares it, look unchanged). `firestore.rules`: `previous` is in `hasOnly` but not `hasAll`, may be set only on a TWEAK, is `null` or a map with exactly the nine keys, the title, description, servings, minutes and nutrition checked as the recipe's own rules do and the ingredient and step lists capped at 60 (never walked: the client shapes each line and step), is immutable on an edit and free only on the CHANGE-to-TWEAK step, and a full 60-line, 60-step recipe passes. The history's Changes list and a cook's page show the chip; the share never carries it (`buildSharedRecipe` copies tip and note words only; `check:pinch-share` marks it). `scripts/checks/pinchTweakUndo.ts`, run by `check:pinch-math`, covers the snapshot, which tweaks are undoable, every refusal and a restore round trip.
- **`getRecipeChanges(before, after, ingredientsById, sourcesById?)`** (`utils/recipeChanges.ts`, pure, covered by `npm run check:pinch-math`) compares the two recipes after trimming and ignoring blank steps, a blank description and a blank line note, and returns plain-word lines in the form's order: "Renamed to “…”", "Serves 4 → 6", "Prep time 10 → 15 min" (or "Cook time 35 min → 1 h 10 min" past an hour, "set to", "removed"), a source change, then ingredients by line id ("Garlic 4 → 6 each", "Garlic 4 each → 0.5 cup", "Added Lime juice, 2 tbsp", "Removed Salt", "Salt 1 tsp → no amount", "Salt no amount → 2 tsp", "Added Parsley" for a line with no amount (an amountless line's unit is not compared), "Swapped Butter for Olive oil", a line note added, changed or removed, and "Ingredients reordered" when the lines both lists share changed their order), steps ("Step 2 now reads: “…”" quoted to 80 characters, "Added step 4: …", "Removed step 2: …", or "Steps reordered" when the same steps changed order; a shared start and end are skipped, so a step put in the middle is one line, not a rewrite of what follows) and nutrition per typed metric ("Calories 520 → 600", "Fat set to 20 g", "Protein back to the calculated number"). There is no line when nothing changed, and a safety line ("The recipe changed") covers any difference the rest missed, so "no lines" always means "no change". At most 8 lines, the rules' cap: past eight the first seven stay and the eighth says "and N more", and a line over 200 characters is cut at a whole character and ends in "…", counting 16-bit units the way the rules' `size()` does (tried in the emulator: 100 emoji pass, 101 are refused; counting code points would let a line of emoji through the check and fail the save). Amounts are compared as they print (rounded to two places), so 4 → 4.001 is not a line of its own and a save whose only change is one of those reads "The recipe changed". A check runs 600 random pairs of edits (lines exactly when the recipe changed, never more than eight).
- **Updating from a planned change.** "Update the recipe" on a CHANGE entry (`NoteEntry`'s `onApplyChange`; also the recipe's ⋯ More row and its "Changes" list, a cook's page in the history sheet (`CookJournal`), cooking mode's dock and the question Done asks) opens the recipe form: from the drawer's ⋯ More or a cook's page (`{ kind: 'recipe', id, fromChangeIds }`) it closes the drawer or sheet first; from cooking mode it opens the same form inside the cooking screen (below) and the session stays. The form shows each change as a read-only quote at the top ("Applying the change you planned", its words and "Why: …") and starts the reason box with `getJoinedChangeReason` (each different reason once, in order). A NOTE has no such door. Saving is `getTweakPlan` in `saveRecipe`: the planned changes are read from the store, the edit's lines come from the same `getDraftChanges`, and when there are lines the **oldest planned change is rewritten into the tweak** (`batch.set` of the same id: `kind` TWEAK, `changes` the lines, `text` the reason from the box, `reason` null, `createdAt` and `updatedAt` now, its step and its cook kept: the rule refuses a changed `cookId`, so a change tied to a cook would not otherwise apply) while the others the edit applied are deleted in the same batch; with no lines every change stays planned, and with no planned change left in the store (deleted elsewhere) the plan falls back to the ordinary new tweak. Applying does not need `selectTweakTracking` to be `'on'`: the person asked for the record. `firestore.rules` allows exactly that one `kind` change (CHANGE to TWEAK, with `changes` and `createdAt` free for it) and no other; every other update keeps `kind`, `changes` and `createdAt`, and the cook once it has one (it may be set once while there is none: below). A CHANGE applied on another device in the meantime is already a TWEAK in the store, so it is never planned twice. Edge cases, as built: applying a change to a recipe that was never cooked still writes the tweak (the plan rewrite doesn't read cooks); an edit that only reorganizes (groups, order) records nothing and leaves the change planned; when more than one change is planned the form lists each with a checkbox (`appliesChangeIds`), the oldest becomes the tweak and the other ticked ones are deleted in the same batch, and unticked ones stay planned; and if the rules refuse the plan's rewrite (the app is ahead of a rules deploy) the save says so in one sentence and nothing is written, so retrying after the deploy works.
- **The form asks why when saving, in place (replaces the old "What changed and why?" row).** On a submit, `RecipeForm.handleSubmit` checks only while tracking is `'on'` and the form wasn't opened with planned changes that already carry a reason: it builds the draft (`getDraft`, the one `save` writes) and calls `getDraftChanges(recipe, draft, ingredientsById, sourcesById)` (`utils/recipeChanges.ts`, the same call `saveRecipe`'s `getTweak` makes, so the lines shown are the lines recorded). When there are lines and `isWhyAsked(topics)` (`getChangeTopics`: some ingredient line was added, removed, swapped, re-measured or re-noted, or some step was added, removed or reworded; a pure reorder of either list, a rename, servings, times, description, source, nutrition or care are not topics), it sets `ask` instead of saving and renders `RecipeWhyStep` after the form. The form stays mounted underneath with `hidden!` (a `Form` reads `initialData` once, so unmounting would lose every edit, the upload and the undo), and the step is the last child so the Subview's `*:last:` rules give its footer the same pinned place. The step has no back control of its own (`ask` is cleared by the Subview's header back, Escape and the back gesture through `backStepRef`, so there is one way back), the heading "Why did you change it?" with one sentence, the lines in a framed card (`ChangeList`), `SuggestionChips` "Start with one" (`getWhyChoices`: "Tasted better / Ran out of it / Too much / Not enough" for ingredients, "Easier this way / Faster / Tasted better / Fixing a mistake" for steps, a mix for both) whose tap runs `applyWhyChoice` (fills an empty box or one holding only a chip, otherwise appends after a full stop, never twice and never past `MAX_RECIPE_NOTE_LENGTH`), a three-row `NoteTextField` (not focused on open, so a phone's keyboard doesn't cover the chips) and one primary, "Save without a reason" until the box has words and "Save" after. The step's Save calls `save(ask.data)` with the box as `tweakReason`; a failed save shows its sentence in the step's footer note and stays. Asking never blocks: no answer is required, and a form with only a rename or a time change saves in one tap. The reason lives in the form's `why` state, so going back and saving again keeps what was written. At `'full'` a muted line at the end of the form says this change won't be noted; otherwise nothing shows, so a recipe never cooked, a failed listener and a new recipe say nothing false. There is still no way to save an edit without its tweak (open question 19), and the only way to add a reason to a change that isn't to ingredients or steps is the tweak's own Edit in the cook's page in the history sheet. `check:pinch-math` (`scripts/checks/cookSession.ts`) pins the topics, the choices and the chip text.
- **Editing the recipe from cooking mode.** The form is not opened as a second subview over cooking (that would break the single `subview` value and give two Escape listeners): `CookingSession` holds `edit` (`{ recipeId, changeIds, token, isFromFinish }`) and, while it is set, hides its own content with `hidden!` (so the ticks in `plate`, the step, servings, timers, the open bubble panel (`dockOpen`) and every child's local state stay mounted) and draws `CookRecipeEdit` after it, which wraps `RecipeForm` with `onSaved` (so it never calls `closeSubviewIf` and offers no delete). The header shows the recipe's name as it is typed and no mic, `voice.stop()` runs on entry and the voice step is null; `focused` is null (a finished timer still beeps, and comes forward on return) and the arrow-key handler is off. The Subview's back, Escape and the back gesture all call `leaveEdit`: first `backStepRef` to leave the why step, then Keep editing / Discard when `isDirtyRef` is set, then `setEdit(null)`. The overlay's scroll is saved on entry and restored by a layout effect when `edit` clears. Starting an edit asks `confirmDiscard` (an open note or put-away draft) and never `confirmLeave`: nothing is stopped or cleared, and the saved copy in `localStorage` keeps being written. `onSaved` runs `finishEdit(saved)`: it adds the recipe to `editedIds` (the quiet line "Cooking now follows your changes." replaces "changed elsewhere" for a recipe saved here; `entries` already read the live recipe, so the steps and amounts show the saved text, and a step beyond the new last is held to it by the existing `Math.min`), records `followUps[recipeId] = 'CHANGED'` only when the edit started from the final panel, and closes the edit if it is still the same one (`token`). Tick state is keyed by ingredient line id, which an edit keeps, so a removed line's tick simply goes.
- **Cooking mode's review.** `CookReview` replaces the old follow-up card. For each recipe with a recorded cook, while no composer or put-away draft is open, a centered framed card "Anything to remember or change?" lists what this screen wrote for the recipe (`getReviewEntries`: the ids in `useCookCapture().captured`, resolved through `selectNotesById`, so an entry deleted elsewhere, or one already turned into a tweak, drops out; each row is a 48px `Button` with the kind's emoji, the words clamped to two lines, "Step 2" or "Whole recipe" and a change's reason, opening the composer on it), then `ActionChips`: "Add a tip, note or change" (`capture.open({ stepIndex: null, kind: 'NOTE' })`; a note here follows the recorded cook), "Change the recipe" with none planned (`startEdit(recipeId, [])`) and a quiet "Nothing to add" while nothing has been written (`followUps[recipeId] = 'NOTHING'`). There is no "Update the recipe" chip and no footer button for a planned change (they said the same act as Done's question; `CookReview` lost `isUpdateInFooter` and `onApplyChanges`). After an edit started from the panel the card also says "Recipe changed." (`followUps[recipeId] = 'CHANGED'`). A plate gets one card per recipe with its name above. **Done asks.** `changeTargets` lists the recorded recipes that have a written change still planned (`ids`, `texts`, `title`). `handleDone` ignores a tap inside `DONE_GUARD_MS` of the panel opening, runs the same discard and "Stop timers and leave?" questions, then `askToUpdate(changeTargets)`: for the first target a `confirm` titled "Update the recipe?" ("Update {title}?" on a plate) saying "You planned a change: “…”. Update the recipe with it now? Not now keeps it planned." (several: "You planned N changes. … with them now? …"), buttons "Not now" and "Update now"; a no (or Escape) asks about the next recipe, a yes returns that target. `leaveNow(target)` then clears the session and swaps the cook subview for the recipe form with `replaceSubviewIf(isStillOpen, { kind: 'recipe', id, fromChangeIds })` (a new member of the overlay context, `Pinch.tsx`: a functional `setSubview` that replaces only when the cook subview is still the open one, so an answer that lands late never replaces another subview holding typed work), or closes the cook with `closeIfStillOpen` when nothing was said yes to; unlike the dock's "Update the recipe" it does not return to the panel. Writing an entry hides the record card's Undo like a put-away does (`hasFollowUps`); Undo clears `followUps` but never deletes an entry. `isFinishShown` stays on when the recipe has no steps left but its cook is recorded.
- **Writing while cooking.** `useCookCapture` (`hooks`) replaces `useCookNote`. It holds the one open draft (`{ recipeId, noteId, kind, text, reason, stepIndex, initial }`; `open`, `edit`, `setKind`, `setText`, `setReason`, `setStepIndex`, `save`, `remove`, `cancel`), the ids it wrote (`captured`) and two writes without a draft (`quickAdd`, `removeById`, used by voice). `save` dispatches `addNote` or `updateNote` and closes only after the write resolved; an entry is written as it is saved, so leaving the screen never loses one, and `captured` rides in the saved session (`resume.captured`, `parseCaptured`: a session saved before them, a list that is not ids and a list over 60 read as none). `CookCaptureView` draws the composer in place of the step (a `BackLink` first, whose Back, Escape and the back gesture all call `cancelCapture`, which asks Keep editing / Discard when `hasDraft`) and, on the last panel, in place of the cards; the footer's Cancel and Save are one branch of the pinned footer's chain. While it is open `isDraftOpen` is true, so a timer that rings waits (`focused` is null) and the voice's step is null, and the arrow keys are off. Deleting is a destructive confirm (`deleteCaptured`) on a red `ActionRow` in the composer.
- **A step's entries on screen.** `getEntriesAt(recipe, at, withLastTime)` (in `CookingSession`) splits the recipe's notes with `groupEntriesByStep` (`utils/notes.ts`: tips, then changes, then notes, each newest first; a TWEAK is never shown; an entry whose step was rewritten lands in the whole recipe's group) and, on the first step, adds the whole recipe's entries, leaving out the note the dock's last-time view leads with (`getLastTimeNote`: the latest NOTE with no resolved step). `CookStepView` draws them through `CookStepDock` (`components/cooking`) in its bottom stack, no sheet: a `role='group'` row (`aria-label='Written and used in this step'`, `min-h-10`, right-aligned, `gap-2`) of bubbles (`Bubble`: a tertiary `Button`, `size-9` and fully round with a 40px hit area from a pseudo-element, its count a small badge on the corner, `aria-pressed` and, where it opens the panel, `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls='cook-step-panel'`; the open or not-yet-dismissed one is `bg-accent!` with a hairline): 💬 for the note from last time (first step only; a small dot until "Got it"), ⚖️ for what the step uses (only when it names lines), one per kind that exists, its emoji with a count badge (`aria-label='2 tips'`, "1 change to make" for a change), and the dashed `+` (`aria-label` "Add a tip, note or change for this step", `onAdd`). With nothing to show and no `onAdd` the dock renders nothing. The open view is a `section` (`id='cook-step-panel'`, `data-no-swipe`, popover colours, `absolute inset-x-0 bottom-full`, or `top-full` with `mt-2` on a short landscape phone, `max-height:500px` and `min-width:640px`, where there is no room above) with a header row (the title, then at its right "Got it" on the last-time note, the speaker on a tip, note or change view ("Read the tips / notes / changes aloud", `onRead(kind)`: `readEntries` in `CookingSubview`, `getSpokenEntries` over the step's entries of that kind, TIPS for a tip, NOTES for a note, CHANGES for a change; null when the browser cannot speak) and a ✕) over a body capped at `max-h-44` (`max-h-32` under 700px tall) that scrolls on the y axis: the last-time words, the amounts rows, or a kind's entries as a scroll-snap row (`data-no-swipe`, `snap-x snap-mandatory`, `overflow-x-auto`, the scrollbar hidden) of cards: each `li` is `w-[88%]` so the next peeks (`w-full` for a lone entry), and the card is one `Button` with the emoji and words in a `line-clamp-3` span and, for a CHANGE, "Why:" in a one-line span, whose tap calls `onEdit(id)` (the composer opens on the entry to read and change it in full); a CHANGE's card also has one `PenLine` bubble at its right, `aria-label` "Update the recipe with this change" (`onApplyChange`). **Which view is open is the screen's, not the dock's:** `CookingSubview` holds `dockOpen = { key: `${recipe.id}:${index}`, view }` and hands `shownView` and `onShowView` down through `CookStepView` as the dock's controlled `shown` and `onShow`; `shownView` is null unless the key matches the step on screen, so moving to another step or recipe closes the panel, and a `shown` view whose bubble no longer exists (its entries went away) reads as closed. The dock registers `useBackStep(open !== null, close)`, so the back gesture and Escape close the panel before anything else, and a tap on the open bubble toggles it; the voice actions (`showEntries`, `closeEntries`, and `readTips`/`readNotes`, which open the matching panel while they read) call the same `showDockView`, and only while no timer is in focus. Writing is the + bubble (`openStepCapture`). The counter row (`CookStepView`'s `action`) is Timers and Ingredients only; the whole ingredient list and the timers list are the row's two buttons (a different job from the scale bubble, which is this step's lines). The panel is part of the step's own layer, not a sheet or popover, so nothing stacks over the subview. Closed, the row is 40px; the old container was 174px at 390×844 and 142px at 320×568, and only an open panel overlaps the step.
- **Steps are found again by their words.** Steps are strings without ids, so an entry about a step stores `stepIndex` and `stepText` (`getStepAnchor`: the step squashed and cut at a word to `NOTE_STEP_ANCHOR_LENGTH`, 80). `resolveNoteStep(note, steps)` returns the stored number while that step still reads as it did, else the first step with those words, else `null` (the whole recipe; `isStepLost` tells that apart from an entry that never had a step so the drawer can say "Written for a step that has changed since"). A key an older document lacks reads as `null`; `getMissingNoteFields` backfills the three on any edit. Two identical steps are one step to this lookup (the first wins); the client caps `stepIndex` below 60 as the rules do.
- **Tips and notes by voice.** `parseCookCommand` gains `READ_TIPS`, `READ_NOTES`, `READ_CHANGES`, `ADD_ENTRY` (a tip, a note or a change), `DICTATION` and `UNDO_ENTRY`. The read commands run `getSpokenEntries(entries, 'TIPS' | 'NOTES' | 'CHANGES', formatDay)` (`utils/notes.ts`, pure, `check:pinch-math`; it reads exactly one kind per call, via `SPOKEN_KINDS`) over the shown step's entries (the lead note included, for notes) and speak the result, or say "No tips for this step.", "No notes for this step." or "No planned changes for this step."; `READ_TIPS`, `READ_NOTES` and the new `READ_CHANGES` ("read/repeat the changes", "repeat the planned changes", "any changes", "what are the changes") are three separate commands, so "read the notes" no longer reads planned changes. The plain repeat (`REPEAT`, the step's own words) also takes "repeat the instructions", "read the instructions", "repeat instructions", "say the instructions again", "repeat the directions" and "read the directions" (`PHRASES`). each entry is cut at a word to `MAX_SPOKEN_NOTE_CHARS` (400) so one long note never holds the kitchen up. "Add a tip" sets `pending = { kind: 'DICTATE', entryKind, recipeId, stepIndex }`, which `getVoiceChoice` hands the grammar as `{ kind: 'DICTATE' }`: while it is set, `parseOne` takes everything said as the words (`parseDictation`: up to `MAX_COOK_DICTATION_CHARS`, 400, cut at a word; "never mind" and "stop listening" are the ways out, and a command word is only words) before any other grammar runs, for `COOK_CHOICE_MS` (20 s) like a timer question. `dictation` writes through `capture.quickAdd`, remembers the id in `lastDictatedId` and answers aloud; "undo that" calls `removeById` on it. A failed write speaks "Sorry, I couldn't save that tip." The commands are listed in the voice sheet's "Tips and notes" group ("show me the tips", "show my notes", "close the note" and "read the tips" among its first rows); `check:pinch-voice` covers the phrases, the dictation choice and the cap. **Showing and closing.** `parseCookCommand` also gains `SHOW_ENTRIES` (`{ entryKind }`) and `CLOSE_ENTRIES`: `SHOW_PHRASES` is built from the verbs show, show me, open and see, the determiners none, "the" and "my", and the nouns tip(s), note(s) and change(s) (also "planned change(s)" and "changes to make"), looked up with `Object.hasOwn`; `CLOSE_ENTRIES` is "close that / it / this / the panel", "hide that / it / this" and close or hide with each noun. `showEntries` refuses off the steps view, while a timer is in focus and on the final panel ("Go to a step to see that."), answers "No tips for this step." when the step has none of that kind and otherwise opens the panel and says "Showing 2 tips."; `closeEntries` answers "Nothing is open." when none is. `readTips`, `readNotes` and `readChanges` also open the matching panel (💡, 🗒️, ✏️) while they read, and leave it open (only when the step has entries of that kind). **Auto-read:** `PinchVoiceSettings.isReadingNotes` (saved with the account, `firestore.rules` read it with `get(key, null)`, a localStorage fallback like the others, off to start; labelled "Read tips, notes and changes after each step" and disabled while Read steps aloud is off) is a second reading in `useCookVoice`, never appended to the step's: the step's own reading is `step.spoken` alone. It is a queue of segments (`step.notesSegments`, from `getSpokenSegments` in `CookingSession`: `{ view, text }` for the lead note (`LAST`, first step only), then the tips (`TIP`), the notes (`NOTE`) and the planned changes (`CHANGE`) written for that very step, a kind with nothing to say left out; each segment reads the entries its panel lists, so the first step's whole-recipe notes are read with its notes, and a `LAST` segment exists only where the dock draws that bubble). The queue is the pure `stepCookReading` (`utils/cookVoice.ts`, `check:pinch-voice`): it stops when the step changed, the setting went off, or the person opened, changed or closed a view of their own (`openView`), and its end takes down only the view it opened; a spoken answer or a manual read clears it. The effect that reads the step arms `notesQueue = { key, next: 0 }` (switch on, at least one segment); two more carry it: one records `stepSpokenKey` when the speech status turns to `speaking` for the step that was just read (`readStepKey`), the other, each time the status is back to `listening` for that same step key, waits `NOTES_PAUSE_MS` (1 second) and then takes the segment at `next`: it calls `onShowView(segment.view)` (the session's `showDockView`, so the matching 💬/💡/🗒️/✏️ panel is on screen while it is read) and says it as its own utterance, the first one prefixed with `NOTES_LEAD` ("Your tips and notes for this step."); when `next` runs past the end the same timeout calls `onShowView(null)`, taking the panel down about a second after the last segment ended, and clears the queue. It runs once per step showing (`begin` and `voice.speak` clear the queue; entries written after the step was read, or the switch turned on after it, find no queue and read nothing until the next showing), moving to another step changes `stepKey` and the effect's cleanup cancels a pending timeout; it depends on the browser reporting the step reading as started and ended (ISSUES.md); `voice.speak` is `useSpeechCommands().say`, which the 🔊 buttons call so a tap reads with the mic off.
- **Listener and failure.** `startNotesListener` (a plain collection `onSnapshot`) is started from `usePinchSync` with the other optional collections. Its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded notes and sets `loadError`; it never reaches the load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out) and is not part of the loading gate (`selectIsPinchLoaded`). Without notes the app is unchanged except that the drawer says "Your notes couldn't load just now.", no "Add a note" row or "changed" date shows, the history and cooking mode offer no note, and a recipe edit saves without a tweak.
- **Archive and delete.** Archiving a recipe leaves its notes alone. A hard delete reads the recipe's notes from Firestore (`getDocs` with `where('recipeId', '==', id)`, one equality, so no index) instead of the store, which is empty while the notes load or after they fail to and would leave them orphaned, and removes them with `deleteNotes`, `writeBatch`es of 400, after the recipe's own delete and as best effort (a failure is logged and the delete still finishes), like cooks; anything that survives names a recipe that no longer exists and is ignored by every reader. Deleting a cook leaves its notes, which then read as plain notes while the dangling `cookId` stays in the document.
- **Reading and performance.** The grouping and the summary are `createSelector`s over the slice's items (the summary in one pass). `RecipeRow` is a `memo` component, so opening a drawer or a sheet, or adding a note, re-renders no row of the list (it re-rendered every mounted row before); a recipe save still re-renders them, as every recipe snapshot does, because each snapshot gives every recipe a new object.


### 5f. Recipe care

- **Where a person writes it.** The recipe form's "Keeping it" row (`RecipeCareField`, a `SummaryEditor`) is the third row of the extras card that ends the form (after Nutrition, see "The form's extras are one card"): a caption, the summary (`getCareSummary`, or "Add how to keep it" in muted text while empty, two lines at most) and a chevron. Its editor is a drawer on a phone or a short screen and a popover from `sm`, ending in Done like the others, and holds two compact number fields, "Fridge" and "Freezer", each with a visible "days" suffix (`type='number'`, 1 to 365, labelled "Fridge in days" and "Freezer in days"), the line "Whole days. A month is 30, so 90 shows as 3 months.", a "Reheat" and a "Tip" textarea (two lines from `sm`, three on a phone, since each can hold 300 characters). Captions are small and inputs 40px (`Field isCompact`, `COMPACT_INPUT_CLASS`); Enter in a days field closes the editor, and in Reheat or Tip it adds a line break (the drawer keeps it) and never saves the recipe. Days are typed as days and stored as one number, not chosen as weeks or months: a unit picker would double the editor's height for the one case that wants it (a freezer measured in months), so the summary does the converting on the way out ("90" reads back as "3 months", as 90 minutes reads back as "1 h 30 min" in Prep and cook). A bad value (days that aren't whole numbers from 1 to 365, a reheating line or tip over 300) shows in red under the row and in the editor, and the pinned footer says "Check the fridge and freezer days to save."
- **Form data.** `RecipeFormData.care` is a `CareText` (four strings), so typing never loses a half-typed number. `getInitialData` builds it from `getRecipeCare(recipe)`, `getDataFromExtracted` from the reading, and the save turns it into `RecipeCare | null` with `parseCareText` (blank parts null, all blank null). The form's dirty check includes it like any field, so Keep editing and Discard behave as before.
- **Writing.** `saveRecipe` puts `care` in the same field-scoped `updateDoc` (or the new recipe's `set`) as every other field the form owns, on every save: a recipe written before care gains the key the first time it is saved from the form, as `null` unless the person filled the row in, and a save that never opens the row can't lose what was there. A change to care never records a tweak (§5e): `getRecipeChanges` leaves it out of what it compares and of the check that guards its "no lines means nothing changed", so a save that touches only care writes no note and leaves the drawer's "changed" date and every older note's "Written before your … tweak" label as they were, and one that also changes something the tweak does cover records only that line (open question 20).
- **The drawer.** `RecipeCareSection` is a `FormSection` titled "Keeping it" between Steps and Notes, drawn only when `getRecipeCare` isn't null. Each part is a line with a fixed 20px emoji column: 🧊 "Keeps 3 days in the fridge", ❄️ "Keeps 3 months in the freezer", ♨️ "Reheat: …" (not 🔥, which means calories everywhere in Pinch) and 💡 the tip in the person's words (pre-wrapped, long words break); a part that is null is left out.
- **Cooking mode.** The Finish panel's put-away pills carry the keeping times ("Fridge · keeps 3 days", "Freezer · keeps 3 months", from `care` through `formatCareDays`); there is no separate leftovers line.
- **The readers.** The text, photo and link readings share one schema and one prompt, extended with `fridgeDays`, `freezerDays`, `reheat` and `tip` (all nullable) and the rule "fill in … only when the recipe itself says how long leftovers keep, how to reheat them or how to store them. Never estimate how long food keeps, and use null when the recipe is silent." The schema tells the model to answer in whole days (a week is 7, a month is 30) and, for a range, with its shorter end: a storage time is a safety figure, so the cautious end is read, where cooking mode's timers take the longer end of a cooking range. `normalizeExtractedRecipe` turns the answer into `ExtractedRecipe.care` with `toCare`: days are rounded down (a storage time is a safety figure, so 36 hours is 1 day and half a day is dropped) and must then be 1 to 365 (anything else is null, so "2 years" is dropped and never cut to a year), text is trimmed and cut to 300, a wrong type is null, and a record with nothing in it is null. A reading that finds no storage lines leaves `care` null, so the form's row stays quiet and nothing is listed under "We couldn't read"; the disclosure lines ("An AI reads it and fills in the form. It isn't saved.") are unchanged and still true. The form is filled for review only, as before, and the storage text a person typed is never sent to the AI.
- **Copy.** Pinch has no copy-to-clipboard of recipes (the Markdown copy was removed in the redesign). The one way care leaves the app is a share (§5k): the allowlist keeps its four parts (days 1 to 365, `reheat` and `tip` trimmed to 300 characters, all four null makes it `null`), and the public page draws them as text under "Keeping leftovers", with nothing for a recipe without care. If a copy returns it should add the lines under the steps with the person's text escaped for the format, and add nothing for a recipe without care.
- **Legacy and rules.** `isRecipe` reads `care` with `data.get('care', null)`, so a document without the key is valid on create and on update, and `isRecipeCare` allows null or a map with exactly `fridgeDays`, `freezerDays`, `reheat` and `tip` (days null or an int from 1 to 365, text null or 1 to 300 characters). It doesn't insist that the map holds something (the client writes null for that) and it changes nobody's access: the owner writes recipes as before, another signed-in user and a signed-out visitor are refused as before.
- **No new listener, function, secret or index.** `care` rides on the recipes the app already listens to; `firestore.indexes.json` and `functions/` are unchanged.

### 5f.2. Recipe links

- **Model.** `Recipe.links` is a required key typed `RecipeLink[]`; a recipe written before it has no key and reads as `[]` through `getRecipeLinks` (`utils/recipeLinks.ts`), which runs every stored list through `cleanLinks`: entries without a usable http(s) address are dropped, the first `HOW_TO` wins and a later one reads as `OTHER`, an unknown `kind` reads as `OTHER`, a label is trimmed and cut at 40 characters (blank is null), a missing id reads as `link-<index>` (stable across renders) and no more than `MAX_RECIPE_LINKS` (8) are kept. Default labels and emoji are constants (`RECIPE_LINK_LABELS`, `RECIPE_LINK_EMOJIS`), and a custom label replaces the default (`label: null` means the default). Every save of the form writes `links` (field-scoped `updateDoc`, or the new recipe's `set`), so a recipe saved after links existed gains the key even when it has none (`[]`). Links are not in `getRecipeChanges`: editing them never records a tweak (nothing about the dish changed), and a save that changes only them is not a "recipe changed" for notes.
- **Addresses.** `normalizeLinkUrl` is `toWebLink` (`@/utils/urlUtils`: trim, the one `http(s)://` token among words, a wrapped link with no scheme joined, several links refused, credentials and any other scheme refused) plus a length cap of `MAX_LINK_URL_LENGTH` (2,000). `getYouTubeId` reads `youtu.be/<id>`, `/watch?v=<id>`, `/shorts/`, `/embed/`, `/live/` and `/v/` on `youtube.com`, `m.`, `music.` and `youtube-nocookie.com` (`www.` stripped, exact host match so `evilyoutube.com` and `youtube.com.evil.example` have none), id exactly 11 URL-safe characters; a playlist alone or a channel has none. `scripts/checks/pinchLinks.ts` (chained onto `npm run check:pinch-ingest`) holds the parser, normalizer, cap and one-how-to cases and was mutation-tested (19 mutants of the helpers, all caught).
- **Form.** `RecipeFormData.links` is a `LinksText` (`{ howTo: { id, label, url }, others: LinkDraft[] }`: addresses as typed, so a half-typed one is never lost); `getLinksText` builds it from the recipe and `toRecipeLinks` turns it into `RecipeLink[]` on save (the how-to first, blank slots and rows dropped, a custom label kept only while `isCustom`). A how-to label written by something else is carried through the slot, which has only an address. `RecipeLinksField` is a `SummaryEditor` ("Links") whose editor (`LinksEditor`) keeps the open row in its own state (one at a time, in place: the drawer holds one level) and takes its removals through a local `useUndoRemoval`; its `UndoSnackbar` has `isOverSheet`, which draws the bar through a portal at the page's root at `z-[105]` (drawers are `z-100`, toasts `z-110`) and positions it over the drawer's own footer (`SummaryEditor`'s drawer footer now carries the `form-footer` class `UndoSnackbar` looks for). `SummaryEditor` also gained `leading` (a picture before the text) and `shouldFocus` (off where the first field is already filled in or the editor has none), and its popover sizes to the editor with no scroller of its own (it hangs from the page, which scrolls, so a second scroller beside the page's doubled the scroll; Dreamer UI's `Popover` flips above the row only when the whole popover fits there). A bad address (`getLinkUrlProblem`) shows in red on the row and in the editor and blocks saving; more links than the cap (the how-to slot counts once it holds an address; a blank row never counts, and a row still waiting for its address holds back "Add a link") shows "Take a link out to save".
- **The form's extras are one card (round 8 fix).** After Steps and Nutrition, `RecipeForm` builds one list of rows (Where it's from, Collections when it owns them, Keeping it, Links) and gives each a `position` (`first`, `middle`, `last`, `only`); `SUMMARY_GROUP_FRAME[position]` (`src/components/summaryGroup.ts`) goes on the Form's grid cell for that field as its `className` (the cells pull up over the grid's `gap-4` with `-mt-4`, and carry the hairline, so five cells read as one `rounded-2xl` card with `divide-y` lines, and no `overflow-hidden` is needed, which would clip the desktop popovers), and the same `position` goes to `SummaryEditor`'s `groupPosition`, which makes the row roomy, shows its icon chip (📚, 🗂️, 🧊, 🔗, 📝) and gives it the card's corners. The title, servings, prep and cook, description and photo stay flat above, the way they were. Nutrition sits above the card. A link is removed from its own open row in the Links editor (a red `Remove` chip; the trash that sat beside its chevron is gone), with the same Undo.
- **Drawer.** `RecipeLinksSection` is drawn last in `RecipeDrawer`'s recipe view (after the Cost tile, its note and the macros), nothing for `[]`. The how-to video plays in place when it is a YouTube or Vimeo link (`getInlineVideo`: a YouTube address becomes `youtube-nocookie.com/embed/<id>`, keeping a valid `t=` start; a Vimeo address `vimeo.com/<id>`, with a private link's hash): `HowToVideo` is react-player's `light` mode, lazy-loaded (`createReactPlayer` from `react-player/ReactPlayer` over only the YouTube and Vimeo element packages, so the HLS, DASH, Mux and other players of its default list never reach the build or the service worker's precache; react-player's `light={true}` would also ask noembed.com for a picture, which no one has agreed to, so `light` is always an element). It draws the video's picture (YouTube's `i.ytimg.com/vi/<id>/hqdefault.jpg` through `FallbackImage`, `referrerPolicy='no-referrer'` by default, passed as `light` in a memoized element because react-player shows the preview again whenever `light` changes identity; Vimeo has no address we can name without asking, so a tinted tile) with a play button, and nothing else loads until the person taps it: that tap mounts the host's player, whose scripts (`youtube.com/iframe_api`, the iframe, or Vimeo's player) set the host's own cookies and see the IP address and user agent. Opening a recipe with a YouTube video still asks Google's image host for one picture without a tap (an IP address and a user agent reach it; no cookie, no script). The video has no heading, label or "Watch" button of its own (the picture and the play button say it); a label the person wrote shows once, as a muted line under it, and the section for the other links is titled "Links". Any other address (an article, a video host react-player could play but we don't load), and the how-to video itself on a page someone shared (`isPlayerHidden`: no player, no picture), is a `Button href target='_blank' rel='noopener noreferrer'` row per link, so the other site opens in a new tab from the tap itself. No lookup of a page title is made: labels are the defaults or the person's own words. `react-player` and the two element packages are in `package.json` (`light` mode, the `playing` prop set by `onClickPreview`, and iOS needing a second tap before sound are the library's; unverified here).
- **Rules.** `isRecipe` allows the `links` key (`hasOnly` only, never `hasAll`) and requires `data.get('links', []) is list && size() <= 8`. The per-link shape (kind, label, url, at most one how-to) is the client's, like ingredient lines and `group`: checking even one field of eight entries by index would add to a recipe rule that already runs near the evaluator's 1,000-expression budget. A read of a hostile list is safe because every reader goes through `cleanLinks` and an address only ever reaches an `href` after `normalizeLinkUrl` has checked its scheme. No index, Storage rule or Function changed. Proven on the emulator as the owner, another signed-in user and a signed-out visitor, base rules against the branch's (a recipe with links, 8 links, an empty list and a backfill onto a legacy recipe are the only outcomes that differ, denied then allowed; 9 links, a string, null, an unknown key, a changed `createdAt`, and every other user's and signed-out request are denied on both; a legacy recipe still reads, edits and archives on both).
- **Seed.** Alex's Adobo chicken has a how-to video and an article link; every other seeded recipe keeps the older shape (no key) so the legacy path stays exercised.

### 5g. Fridge & freezer boxes

- **Where one is made.** (1) Cooking mode's Finish panel, once the cook is recorded (below); (2) a recipe's or saved meal's drawer, the "Put some away" row in ⋯ More (a `ContainerFormSheet` mounted by `RecipesScreen` after the drawer closes, opened on that recipe or meal with nothing to pick); (3) the Fridge & freezer subview, "Add" (the same sheet, which first asks what it is: a search over the listed recipes and saved meals capped at four rows, ending in "Call it “text”" for a typed name, with a "Putting away" summary row once picked, and its search box focused once the drawer has settled). The sheet's title is "Fridge & freezer" and, once picked, the dish is named above the fields in a `SummaryRow` captioned "Putting away" (its chevron swaps the sheet back to the search, a back step that keeps `DetailsForm` mounted but hidden so its draft survives, led by a `BackLink`; plain text when the sheet opened on one dish; no "Recipe / Saved meal / Typed name" line under it); its fields are how many servings (a whole-number stepper, 1 to 100), where (Fridge or Freezer, a `PillGroup`), **how many boxes** (a stepper from 1 to `MAX_CONTAINERS_PER_PUT_AWAY` (20), never more than the servings, with a line that says how the servings split: "2 servings in each", or "3 servings in the first, 2 in each of the others", the extra servings going one each to the first) and eat by (a date input with the hint "Your call. …"), inside the repo's `Form` as one `custom` field. It writes through `useContainerActions().putAway(inputs)`, which checks the cap (the soft cap counts containers: what is in stock plus what is added), builds the containers (`buildContainer`, a fresh `generateUuid()` each, one shared `createdAt`) and dispatches `writeContainers`: **one `writeBatch`**, so N containers land whole or not at all. Each document is an ordinary create, so the rules did not change; a batch of 20 was written against the emulator with the real rules. The batch is not awaited: the write is accepted locally at once, the sheet and the Finish panel answer from the store, and only a refusal that arrives later raises a toast ("That didn't save"), so a slow or offline network never holds the screen (offline, the toast adds that it was saved on this phone and will sync). The saved toast ("Saved 4 boxes (4 servings) in the freezer, eat by Jan 12, 2027", or "Saved 2 servings in the freezer, …" for one) carries an Undo (`undoContainerBatch`): one transaction that re-reads each document and deletes the batch only while none of them has changed since (`updatedAt` still equals `createdAt`), else it leaves them and says so. It reads server state, so an Undo tapped within one round trip of a save that is still in flight can find nothing to delete, and the put-away then lands anyway (narrow, accepted).
- **Eat-by defaults (`getEatByDefault`, `getEatByHint`, `getMoveNotes`).** Counted from the viewer's local today. Fridge: the recipe's `care.fridgeDays`, else the person's usual days (`CONTAINER_FRIDGE_DAYS` until they choose; §5h). Freezer: `care.freezerDays`, else their usual (`CONTAINER_FREEZER_DAYS`). Thawing out of the freezer: the shorter of `CONTAINER_THAWED_DAYS`, `care.fridgeDays` and their usual fridge days. **A move starts from what the container already has** (`currentEatBy`): a container already past its date keeps that date through any move (the hint reads "Your call. The date stays as it was." and the move view says "It was past its date before it was moved."); a thaw starts at the earlier of its current eat-by and today plus the thaw days ("Your call. It already had an earlier date, so that stays." when the current date wins); moving an in-date fridge container into the freezer starts fresh from today. A container that has been frozen before says so in the freeze view ("It's been frozen once before.", "twice", "N times"), with "Your call." kept and no claim about safety. The hint otherwise starts "Your call." and says where the number came from ("Taken from the freezer time in your recipe.", "Once thawed, this starts at 2 days.", "A starting point of 3 days; change it to what you trust.", and for a number the person set in Settings "Starts at your usual 5 days for the fridge; change it to what you trust."; none says how long food keeps or that it is safe, and pinchMath guards that). The sheet's move rows read "Sets a new eat-by date" in every state. Until the person types a date, the date follows the default for the place; a typed date is kept when the place changes and never replaced by a default. A saved meal and a typed name have no care, so they use the named defaults.
- **The list.** `ContainersSubview` (`{ kind: 'containers'; recipeId?; mealId?; place? }`, a `Subview`, so its back button and the back gesture close it, and a sheet over it takes the gesture first) shows one place at a time through a thin `PillGroup` of Fridge and Freezer counted in servings ("Fridge · 10 servings"; the sum of what matches the scope chip and the search; the shown place is derived each render, never mirrored with an effect: the person's pick (a drawer's stock row starts the screen with its place picked: the subview's `place`), or the other place while a search matches only there, and before any pick the place holding more of what wants a look (`getLandingPlace`: the in-scope in-stock boxes past their date or due by tomorrow counted per place, then those past their date, then the fridge, so the oldest date in a freezer no longer decides, as `dueFirstPlace` did), falling to the other place while that one is empty; both places keep their option when empty, so the switch never reshuffles), soonest eat-by first (`selectContainers` sorts once), drawing `CONTAINER_LIST_PAGE_SIZE` rows per place and "Show N more" only when more than three would hide, and a search from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` rows in the place the switch would show before any search (`basePlace`, so typing never makes the field vanish, and one with text stays so it can be cleared). Rows are batches: `groupBatches` (one pass into a `Map` keyed by `getBatchKey`: `createdAt`, place, eat-by, recipe, meal and title, so boxes one put-away wrote stay together and a box moved or re-dated alone leaves) draws one row per batch in the order of its first box, each batch's boxes in take order (fewest servings first); the data is still one document per box, and pagination counts rows. A row says its servings (`getBatchServingsText`: "2 servings", "3 boxes of 2 servings", "3 boxes · 7 servings") and one eat-by item (`getEatByLabel`): plain words ("Eat by Oct 19", read in UTC) or, due today or tomorrow (`CONTAINER_EAT_SOON_DAYS`) or past, the same words in a soft-tinted `EatByBadge` ("Eat today", "Eat by tomorrow", "Past its date"), never a solid fill. A freezer row leads with `getFrozenAt` (the last FREEZE, else `createdAt`: "Frozen Oct 5") and names its eat-by only when `isEatByShown` (within `CONTAINER_FREEZER_DATE_DAYS` or past). Two fridge rows with the same title are told apart by a "Made Oct 14" tag, and a thawed one always says "Thawed". Opened from a drawer it shows the containers of one recipe, or of one saved meal and of the recipes in it (the same roll-up as the Meals row), with an "Only Adobo chicken ×" chip that clears the filter. With nothing in stock the subview shows no switch and says "Nothing in the fridge or freezer right now"; the "What you finished" door (a 🕘 `HeaderIconButton` in the header cluster, with a same-size invisible placeholder while its place is held, so Add never moves) shows only once something is finished, so the unscoped list reads the first page of finished boxes as soon as it is ready (the same `finishedContainersQueryOptions(uid, 25)` the step uses, so opening it is instant, 25 reads once per 30 s), `getFinishedDoor` takes the read's state (`getFinishedRead(isError, hasData)`: LOADING, LOADED or FAILED, so a failure is never folded into "nothing") and whether a door is expected (`hasSeenFinished(uid)`, a `pinch:{uid}:has-finished` flag in localStorage that the finished read rewrites each time it lands, read once on mount): it shows the tile once something is finished or the read FAILED, holds its place while LOADING only when expected (a wrong guess moves the rows once), and draws nothing for a LOADED empty answer; a scoped list, whose read can be 200 containers, reads only while the scope has nothing in stock and shows the door while it has stock. "Clear out the fridge" is a red hairline `ActionChips` chip (`isDestructive`) on its own line above the rows. The header's "Add" is disabled until the listener has landed.
- **Finished.** The "What you finished" door (a step of the subview: `Subview`'s `onBack` returns to the list, so the back gesture does too, and the header has no Add) reads `finishedContainersQueryOptions(uid, count)`: a TanStack Query over `where('status', '==', 'DONE')`, `orderBy('updatedAt', 'desc')`, `limit(count)`, which needs the composite index `containers (status, updatedAt desc)` in `firestore.indexes.json` (the emulator never enforces indexes, so it is unverified until deployed; a Finished view that can't load says so in one line and nothing else is affected). "Show 25 more" raises the limit and keeps the page on screen while the new one loads. The queries are invalidated by every write that finishes, reopens or deletes a container (`refreshFinished`), and persisted nowhere (`meta.persist` is not set: they are Firestore reads). A finished row says how it ended against how much went in: "Ate 4 servings · Oct 12", "Tossed 1 of 4 · Oct 14", "Tossed 4 servings" or "Cleared out 3 of 6" (`getFinishedText`: the end event, plus the EAT events summed, so a corrected count reads true; a history at the cap, which may have lost an EAT, says "Ate it all" or names only what ended it). When the list is scoped ("Only Adobo chicken"), Finished reads that recipe's or meal's containers instead (`scopedContainersQueryOptions`: one equality, no index, at most 200 per recipe or meal, filtered to DONE, merged by id and sorted newest first), so an older finished container of the scope is never hidden behind the newest 25 of everything and "Nothing from … is finished yet" is true. Both reads go through `readContainers` (`queries/containerQueries.ts`), which throws `OfflineEmptyReadError` when the answer is empty and came only from the local cache (`metadata.fromCache`), so offline the step says "Couldn't load what you finished while you're offline." instead of a false "Nothing is finished yet"; a cached non-empty answer still shows. A failed read (that, or `FAILED_PRECONDITION` while the `containers (status, updatedAt desc)` index is still building) keeps the door drawn; `ContainersSubview` refetches the errored active reads of `containerKeys.all(uid)` through `refetchQueries` when `useNetworkStatus` reports a verified reconnect and on `useOnTabReturn` (the shared client sets `refetchOnReconnect: false`, and `retry` stays off), and `readContainers` logs any failure that is not `unavailable` with `console.error`. The cooking history reads `.data` only and treats the error as no data, as before. A meal reads its own containers plus one query per distinct recipe, so only the first 25 are drawn, with "Show 25 more" only when more than three would stay hidden (as the in-stock groups do). Ordering the reads by `updatedAt` would need a composite index (an equality plus an `orderBy` on another field), so they stay unordered and a recipe or meal past 200 containers shows an arbitrary 200, the same limit as the cooks' history below.
- **The sheet (`ContainerSheet`, a `FormSheet`: a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`).** Titled with the dish. Details first: where it is and how many are left (a freezer box adds "Frozen Sep 14"), the eat-by once as a plain line (the tinted pill when due, no pencil: "Change the eat-by date" is a row of the actions group that swaps in the date view), "Thawed in the fridge on Oct 14 · Frozen twice" when that is so; then one big filled button that follows the box's state through `getSelectionPrimary` (the helper the Select footer uses; the boxes of a batch, or the one box picked from it): "Ate one" (it closes the sheet; a batch's is "Ate a box", or "Ate one" when each box is one serving, and eats the first box in take order whole) until every box is past its date, then "Toss it" or "Toss them" with a `Ban` glyph (`toss`, or `endBatch` for a batch, each with its Undo toast), when "Ate one" is the block's first row; then one `bg-muted/50` `ActionGroup` of three actions and a door: "It's gone" ("Keeps it in What you finished": swaps to Ate it / Tossed, **no confirm**, the Undo toast is the net), "Put in the freezer" or "Thaw in the fridge" ("Sets a new eat-by date"; one box of a batch; it closes the sheet with a toast) and "How many are left?" (no subtitle; `getCountChange`: fewer is `eatContainer` with the difference, 0 is `ALL`, more is `correctContainerCount`, so the history says "Ate 2 servings" or "Count 1 → 3"; a batch counts boxes down with `getBoxCountCopy`); and a last `GroupRow`, "History · 3 entries" (a batch: "Each box"), swaps in the rest (a batch's "Each box" is an `ActionGroup isRoomy` of `GroupRow isRoomy` rows, the place's emoji as the chip, "Box 1" over `getBoxLine` (`utils/containers.ts`: "2 servings", or "1 of 2 servings left" once the history's first place event started it higher), each opening that box; checked in `pinchFridge.ts`); the pinned footer holds the trash (a destructive confirm that says the history is erased and, for an in-stock one, points at "It's gone") and Done as a secondary button. "It's gone", the move, the date, the count, the history and a batch's boxes swap the sheet's content in place behind a `BackLink`, never a second overlay; each owns its state, so it resets on every open. The record is the live store's, read by id, with a finished one's snapshot only for the Finished view; a container that has left the store shows "This isn't in your fridge or freezer anymore" and Done. `ActionRow`'s subtitle is optional (a row with no consequence to state has none). A write that fails says one plain sentence inside the sheet. **Taps are guarded in one place:** every container action goes through `useContainerActions`, which ignores a second tap on the same container while its first change is in flight (`once`), so two taps 80 ms apart eat one serving in the sheet and in the planned meal's list alike. Each toast's Undo is guarded the same way: Dreamer UI's toast action only calls `onClick` and never dismisses, so the toast gets a known id (`addUndoToast`), is removed on the first tap, and its id is remembered, so a second tap, even one that lands while the first undo is being written or the toast is still fading, does nothing; a later Undo of a different event is a different toast and still works. Done carries `data-modal-action`, which Dreamer UI's modal focuses first, so the sheet never opens with focus on an action and Enter, Enter cannot eat a serving.
- **Changes are transactions over pure plans.** `eatContainer({ id, count: number | 'ALL' })` (`ALL` means whatever is left when the transaction reads it), `tossContainer`, `moveContainer({ id, to, eatBy })`, `changeContainerEatBy`, `correctContainerCount({ id, count })`, `undoContainerEnd({ id, at })` each run `applyPlan`: `runTransaction`, `transaction.get`, the plan for that action over the fresh document, then `transaction.update` of only the fields the plan owns (`servings` and/or `status`, `place`, `eatBy`, always `history` and `updatedAt`). A batch's "It's gone", date and delete are one write over every box: `endContainers` and `changeContainersEatBy` run `applyPlanToAll` (one `runTransaction` re-reading each box and applying `planEat(ALL)`/`planToss`/`planEatBy`; a box that changed or is gone aborts the whole batch with one sentence), `removeContainers` one `writeBatch` of deletes; a batch is at most `MAX_CONTAINERS_PER_PUT_AWAY` (20) boxes. The end's Undo is `undoClearOut` over the ended ids (it reverses any end event while it is still the last one). A plan refuses with one sentence when the container is already finished ("This one is already finished."), when a count is more than is left ("Only 1 serving is left, so nothing was changed."), when it is already in that place, or when an eat-by isn't a whole UTC day; the thunks never let `servings` leave 1 to 100. A transaction needs a connection, so a failed write says "Check your connection and try again." The eat and toss toasts ("Ate 1 · 4 left", from the transaction's own result, "Finished Adobo chicken", "Tossed Adobo chicken", "Ate a box · 2 left", "Ate all 3 boxes") carry an Undo (`planUndoEnd`) that works only while the undone event is still the last one, and gives the portions back (a finished container comes back in stock with the portions it ended with).
- **Clear out a place (`changeContainersInPlace` with `{ kind: 'CLEAR' }`, `undoClearOut`).** After a power cut, a clean-out or a holiday the person would otherwise finish every container one by one, so the Fridge & freezer list has one bulk end per place, an eraser button in the header, shown only for the whole place (not while a recipe or meal scope chip or a search narrows the list). It is `runInChunks`: `CONTAINER_CLEAR_CHUNK_SIZE` (200, the action's limit and not the rules', under Firestore's 500) container ids at a time, each chunk its own `runTransaction` that re-reads every container with `transaction.get`, plans it with `planBulk` (`planClearOut` is its `CLEAR` case; a container that is gone, already finished or in the other place since the tap is skipped) and writes only the fields `planClear` owns (`status` DONE, `history` + one `CLEAR` event carrying what was left, `updatedAt`; `servings` and `place` stay, so Finished still says what was left and where). It resolves to the ids and instants it really cleared, so the toast's number is the number written; a chunk that already committed stays committed when a later one fails (the sentence says how many were cleared before it stopped); nothing cleared is a plain sentence and no toast. `CLEAR` is an end event like `EAT` and `TOSS` (`END_KINDS`): it is the last event, so `appendEvent` never drops it, `planUndoEnd` reverses it only while it is still the last event at that instant, `getFinishedText` reads "Cleared out 3 of 6" and `getEventText` "Cleared out · 3 servings". The Undo toast (once per toast, the in-flight guard keyed on the place so a double confirm cannot clear twice) is `undoClearOut`, chunked the same way, putting back only what is still as the clear-out left it and saying how many came back ("Put back 7 of 9; the rest changed since"). Any finished box's sheet offers "Put it back in the freezer" (`canPutBack`: DONE and its last event an EAT, TOSS or CLEAR with servings) through `putBack`, the same `planUndoEnd`, which respects `SOFT_MAX_CONTAINERS`. The clear-out's message says "out of the fridge" and "What you finished" (`getClearOutMessage(count, servings, place)`). A container another device put away after the tap is not cleared; deleting a container (which erases its history) stays a separate, per-container action. The rules' `kind` allowlist gained `CLEAR`; documents written before it are untouched.
- **Bulk acts: the past-its-date row and Select mode (`changeContainersInPlace`, `planBulk`, `planUndoMove`).** One thunk, `changeContainersInPlace({ ids, action })`, serves the clear-out, a toss, "Ate it all" and a move; `action` is a `BulkAction` (`kind` CLEAR, TOSS, EAT or MOVE; `from`, the place the screen showed; `isPastOnly`; `isEitherPlace`, which a toss or an eat from the screen always sets so a tick in the other place is acted on, never a move; for a move `cares`, each recipe's care by id). It is the clear-out's `runInChunks` (200 per `runTransaction`, each chunk re-reading every box with `transaction.get`) over the pure `planBulk`, which leaves a box alone when it is gone, finished, in the other place by now or (a toss of what the screen called past its date, `isPastOnly`) no longer past its date, counts what it left by reason (`SkipCounts`: GONE, FINISHED, MOVED, REDATED; the status check comes first, so a finished box in the other place reads as finished) and writes only the fields each plan owns. TOSS, EAT (`planEat(…, 'ALL')`, each box's own servings) and CLEAR end a box exactly as the single-box acts do, so a bulk toss leaves the same history as three single tosses; MOVE is `planMove` with the date a single move would suggest for that box (`getEatByDefault` with its recipe's care and its own current date, so a past date stays past and a thaw is never later than the box had) and one FREEZE or THAW event per box, so the eat-by logic and the history stay right. It resolves to `{ changed, skipped, error }`: `changed` holds each box's id and instant (and, for a move, the place and date before and the date after), so the toast's number is the number written ("Tossed 3 of 4 boxes · One was already finished."), a chunk that committed stays committed when a later one fails, and a total failure rejects with the storage message. `useContainerActions().changeInPlace` adds the in-flight guard (keyed `bulk:<place>`, so a double tap or a double confirm acts once) and one Undo toast per act (`getBulkToast`). `undoClearOut` (chunked the same way) puts back each changed box that is still as the act left it: `planUndoEnd` for an end (its last event is that EAT, TOSS or CLEAR at that instant) and `planUndoMove` for a move (the last event is that FRIDGE, FREEZE or THAW at that instant and the box is still in stock and still in the new place; it restores the place and date and drops the event), and says how many came back ("Put back 2 of 3; the rest changed since"). The history cap can have dropped an older event when the act was written; the Undo never brings that one back, as for any other end. **Rules, indexes, data:** unchanged. The owner's update of `status`, `place`, `eatBy`, `history` and `updatedAt` was already allowed, there is no new field (a box written before this reads and acts as it always did), no new query and no index. **The screen:** the boxes after the search, both places; `getPastDueBoxes` over all of them (in stock and `getEatByState` PAST, so due today or tomorrow is not past due and a frozen box past its date counts, as its row says) sits behind a `PromptRow` ("3 are past their date", "Toss them") only while the whole list is shown (no scope chip, no search) and Select mode is off, so it says what Kitchen's tile says whichever place is shown; its confirm names three rows with how many boxes and the day each was due (`getBulkNames`; `getPlacedTitle` adds "· freezer" to a row's name when `getSharedPlace` finds the boxes in both places) and counts the rest in boxes. Select mode is `useSelection` (`src/hooks`, no Pinch words: a set of ticked ids, `toggle(ids)` ticks a row's boxes or unticks them when all are ticked, Escape ends the mode unless a dialog or an open menu is above it, and a back gesture that ends it hands the page back the scroll it had) with `SelectionBar` (`src/components`, the pinned footer, fixed to the bottom of the screen). The screen reads the ticks against the boxes it still shows, so a box finished on another device drops out of "3 selected" at once, and the place switch keeps its ticks: "All past their date" spans both places, so the ticks may hold both and Toss and "Ate it all" act on every tick, `getSelectionNote` says "2 in the fridge, 1 in the freezer. Moving needs one place." in the footer, and a move is built only when `getSharedPlace` finds one place for every tick (from that place, not the one shown). Select's pills sit in the header row (the `SectionHeader`'s `lead`) and Cancel steps aside under 700px tall; `SelectionBar` tightens its padding and buttons under 700px tall and sets `data-selection-bar` on `<html>` while it is mounted, which `pinch.css` uses to hide the phone's tab bar. The subview's header, back button and back gesture end the mode through its `onBack`, one layer per press. On the scale kitchen at 4× CPU (134 boxes in the fridge, 20 rows drawn) entering the mode takes about 450 ms, a tick 130 ms and "All past their date" (31 boxes) 150 ms, against 70, 20 and 50 ms unthrottled; `ContainerRow` is `memo` over plain props, so a tick renders only its own row (two renders of the 20 on screen) and the pills' select-all renders the rows whose tick changed. **Which act is filled** in the Select footer is `getSelectionPrimary(selected, now)`: Toss when every ticked box is past its date, else Ate it all (the other act is quiet); a pure helper checked in `pinchFridge.ts`, read by the footer.
- **History and the cap.** `appendEvent` keeps at most `MAX_CONTAINER_HISTORY` (10) events: the first is never dropped, the oldest EAT goes next, then the oldest EDIT, then the oldest move after the first place event that is not a FREEZE, and only then the event right after the first place event (out of reach while moves alternate between freezing and thawing); the event just added is never the one dropped. The first place event and the FREEZEs go last because they are what `getStartServings` (what went in), `getFreezeLine` ("Frozen twice", "Frozen 3 times", nothing for one) and the sheet's "Thawed in the fridge on …" line (`getLastPlaceEvent`, the last event that says where it went, so the line survives an eat or an edit) read: a cook's container (MADE, FREEZE) with many eat-by edits keeps its FREEZE, and a freeze-heavy history (a thaw and a refreeze past the cap) still counts every freeze. A long run of moves can still drop the oldest thaws after the first place event (`pinchMath` puts away 8, then freezes and thaws it five times: the 8 that went in and all five freezes stay, the oldest thaw goes). A cook's container starts with MADE, which records what the cook made (a whole number from 1 to 100, whatever the cook's half servings), and then where it went with its own servings; a container added by hand starts with where it went alone (no MADE: one event, so it spends no slot and doesn't read twice). What went in is the first place event (`getStartServings`). The cap of 10 is set by the rules evaluator's budget of 1,000 expressions a request, not only by the length checks (criterion 13).
- **Cooking mode (the Finish panel).** `usePutAway` (`hooks/usePutAway.ts`) lives in `CookingSession` beside the capture hook, takes one entry per recipe cooked (recipe, servings made, eaten tonight, cook id) with one set of details open at a time (`getEntry(recipeId)` gives a recipe's card), and `CookPutAway` draws one recipe's card on the Finish panel once a cook is recorded (marked now, or already in the history from the plan) and the containers listener has landed without error. **What a bulk meal is** (`isBulkMeal`, no new field on `Recipe`): the session's servings are `BULK_MEAL_SERVINGS` (5) or more, or the recipe says it freezes (`care.freezerDays` set; fridge days alone are not a signal), or something of that recipe is already in stock. **The question is asked only when something is left**: the servings cooked, minus what is eaten tonight (the servings planned when it was started from a planned meal, else `CONTAINER_SITTING_SERVINGS`; started from a plan, `eatenTonight` is the plan's servings and `servings` the recipe's own, so a planned batch has something left and the question appears), minus what this cook already put away (the first place event of its saved containers), is above zero; otherwise only the quiet link shows, so a recipe that says it freezes, cooked at exactly the planned servings, no longer asks about a serving that doesn't exist. A bulk meal with something left asks "Putting any away?" with three answer `Pill`s ("Fridge · keeps 3 days", "Freezer · keeps 3 months", "Not this time"); any other meal, and the panel after "Not this time" or Skip, shows the same card headed "Put some away" with the two place pills; after a save it reads "Put another away" and shows only while something is left (`usePutAway().canOffer`: nothing saved yet, or `left > 0`). A pill opens the details with the place fixed. An answer opens the details in place on the panel (no overlay over the overlay): servings (default `getPutAwayServings(left)`: what is left, whole, from 1 to 100, so "Put another away" never re-offers the whole batch), a line that works the default out (`getPutAwayBasis`: "6 left after the 2 you're eating tonight", "6 left after about 2 for tonight", "Nothing left after … and the 6 already put away"), one quiet line when the total put away from this cook would pass what it made ("That's more than the 8 you made.", never blocking), the place when the link asked for it, how many containers (above) and the eat-by from the recipe's care; the pinned footer swaps to Skip and Save. Save writes the batch described under "Where one is made" with `cookId` set to the cook just made (or, on the "Already marked" panel, that planned meal's own cook id) and history MADE (the cook's servings) then FRIDGE or FREEZE, and the panel says "Saved 3 boxes (6 servings) in the fridge" with "Eat by Oct 17" under it for each put-away still in stock from this cook (`groupSavedBatches` and `getSavedHeadline`, read from the store by `cookId`). **The panel is the confirmation, so this save raises no toast** (`putAway(inputs, { withToast: false, onWriteFailed })`; the drawers and Kitchen still toast): a toast here would stack over "Marked as cooked … Undo". Each line this screen wrote carries its own quiet "Undo" (`undoSaved`, the same guarded `undoContainerBatch` the toast used, run once: the button gives way to "Undoing…", a second tap finds nothing, and it deletes exactly the ids that save wrote, or none if one has changed since); a refused Undo or a rejected write is one plain red line inside the card, and a line from an earlier visit has no Undo. **Layout:** the panel is one centered column (`max-w-md`, never stretched at 1280): the centered title lines, then each stage in one hairline card (`bg-muted/50`, `border-border`, `rounded-2xl`, no card inside it): the question (centered heading, the fridge and freezer pills, then "Not this time"), the details as settings rows (`ContainerFields layout='rows'`: caption left and control right, the helper line under both, "Where" as a full-width `PillGroup` row, the over-note and any error at the card's foot; the drawer form keeps `stack`), the saved lines, and the note box; the "Anything you'd change?" card (`CookFollowUp`) sits under the cards. **Nothing here blocks leaving:** Save disables only its own button while the write is accepted (it isn't awaited from the server), and the header's back, Escape and the back gesture all use `leave()`, which asks "Keep editing / Discard" only when something was changed (`hasDraft`). A held write channel was used to check it: the panel showed the save at once and leaving worked. **An entry never ends the panel:** saving one returns to the panel with the new row in its list (the question still there while it is unanswered) and Done; the entry box and the details are never open together. Nothing here blocks Done, and a failed containers listener leaves the panel exactly as it was. Cooking a plate, a card shows only for a bulk recipe, with the recipe's name above its heading.
- **The cooks' history.** `CookingHistorySheet` shows "Put away 4 servings" under a cook when containers came from it, from the in-stock store merged by id with `scopedContainersQueryOptions(uid, 'recipeId', recipeId)` (one equality, no index, at most `CONTAINER_QUERY_LIMIT` (200) containers) so a container that has since been finished still counts. Finished containers are never purged, so a recipe with more than 200 would sum an arbitrary 200 of them; a purge or a rollup is a follow-up.
- **Lists.** `selectStockByRecipe` and `selectStockByMeal` come from one pass (`getStockMaps`) into `Map<id, { fridge, freezer, containers, nearestEatBy, fridgeEatBy, freezerEatBy }>` servings per place and the nearest eat-by overall and in each place. `selectStockByMealWithRecipes` rolls a saved meal's stock up into whole plates per place (`getMealStockMap`, a `PlaceStock`: `{ fridge, freezer, fridgeEatBy, freezerEatBy }`): the servings put away as that meal plus the fewest servings of any one of its dishes in that place, so 5 and 3 servings of its two recipes and 2 boxed plates are 5 plates, never 10 (a recipe listed twice is one dish, an ingredient is no dish and no stock, and a dish with nothing in a place makes no plate from the dishes there), and each place's eat-by is the nearest of what makes its plates (a dish that makes none there sets no date); a meal with no plate in either place has no entry. The roll-up feeds only displays, never a decision or a write: the Saved meals row and its `kind='meal'` `StockLine`, its In stock filter and counts (a saved meal is "in the fridge" with a whole plate there) and the meal drawer's stock rows (`formatStockAmount`: "5 plates in the fridge"); a planned meal's "Eat from fridge" and its primary come from `getPlannedMealOffer` over the matching boxes (`useMealContainers`) and count boxes, so one dish in the fridge still offers that dish to a plate while the saved meal shows no plate. A row reads its own entry as plain numbers with `shallowEqual`, and both row types are `memo` components (`MealRow` works out its own cost from `selectCostContext` instead of taking it as a prop), so a change re-renders only the rows whose numbers moved: counting renders on Alex's Meals list, a new nachos container re-rendered "Big week" and no other row, where all three rows re-rendered before. `StockLine` draws one group per place, only above zero, in `RecipeRow` and `MealRow`: "🧊 5 in the fridge" (servings for screen readers) with an `EatByBadge` ("Eat today", "Eat by tomorrow" in the soft tint, or "Past its date") beside the place whose nearest eat-by is close, so the date is tied to the place it belongs to; on a phone each place is its own line, from `sm` they sit in a row with a "·" between them that is hidden where they stack, and the words "in the fridge" are read aloud (the drawer says them in plain text). `RecipesScreen`'s height estimate counts a line per place on a phone (20px with a badge, 16 without) and came within a pixel of the real chunk on Alex's list. The Recipes and Meals filters gain an "In stock" question (`MultiPillGroup`: Fridge · n, Freezer · n, only the places that have something plus any already chosen; both chosen is "either"; counts are recipes or meals, not servings; each chosen pill is an active chip "In the freezer"), and the filter button now appears on Meals when anything is in stock. The recipe and meal drawers show `StockRows` in their ⋯ More view, after the actions (so the first two actions are on screen on a short phone), under the caption "In your fridge and freezer" (the row in the list already says it before the drawer opens, so the drawer's front doesn't): a roomy row per place with the place's emoji in its chip and the amount in words ("5 servings in the fridge" for a recipe, "5 plates in the fridge" for a saved meal, `formatStockAmount`), an `EatByBadge` under the words of the place that is due; a row closes the drawer and opens the Fridge & freezer subview scoped to that recipe, or to that meal and its recipes, on the row's place. The drawers' row is "Put some away" ("In the fridge or freezer, with an eat-by date"), the words Finish's card uses.
- **Eating from stock in a plan.** In `PlannedMealView`, on today and yesterday only (a day ahead can't be eaten yet, and an older day is history), one row per place in its ⋯ More that holds a container of one of the meal's recipes or of the saved meal it came from ("Eat from fridge", "2 boxes match this meal"), minus the boxes the meal can't mean: a box whose `cookId` is one of this meal's own cooks (`{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}`), a box of a recipe already cooked for this meal, and a saved meal's box once anything on the plate is cooked. The rules are the pure `getPlannedMealOffer` (`components/calendar/plannedMealOffer.ts`, covered by `check:pinch-math`), which also picks the one primary (today only: "Eat from fridge" when an in-date box is due today or tomorrow and nothing is cooked, else "Start cooking" for an uncooked recipe with steps), the recipes to cook (`cookRecipeIds`, the plate's session), whether "Save to Saved meals" shows (2+ items and no saved meal with the same kind, ref and servings multiset) and `more`, the ordered list ⋯ More draws (`COOK` unless cooking is the primary, an `EAT` per place with its box count unless that place is the primary, `SAVE`), checked per phase; the view adds "Remove from this day" after it, always, red and on its own (`ActionRow` `isDestructive`). The view passes the narrowed list to `EatFromStockView`, which swaps the view in place for a list, soonest eat-by first, each with a button "Ate 2": the servings planned of that recipe (whole, at least one, never more than is left), or one plate of a saved meal's container. With one box the moment has one action, so its "Ate 2" is the one filled button, portaled into the drawer's pinned footer (`footerEl`) and the row keeps none; with several the rows keep their quiet secondary buttons, since the choice is which box. It records an EAT with the same toast and Undo, returns to the meal at once (`onDone`, a functional update that acts only while the eat view still shows, so no second "Ate 2" button is left to double-tap, and a second tap while the write is in flight is ignored; its Back returns to where it was opened from, More or the meal), and writes nothing to the planned meal, its prices or its cooks. Decided over "just open the container sheet": a sheet over the day drawer would be an overlay on an overlay. Nothing is eaten without a tap.
- **Shopping and the plate search.** `AddToShoppingSheet` says "You already have 3 servings in the freezer." under its heading when the recipe, the saved meal (`ShoppingRequest.mealId`, set by the meal drawer and by a planned meal that came from one) or the recipes in the items have stock; the list and its totals never change (the app does not subtract stock; the pantry only decides which rows start ticked, §5i). `ItemSearch` shows a recipe's `StockLine` under its title in the plate builder's results; saved meals are results there too (no stock line).
- **Failure.** `startContainersListener` is an optional extra like cooks: its error is logged, clears the loaded containers and sets `loadError`, and never reaches the load-error screen. Kitchen's Fridge & freezer tile says it in its accessible name ("couldn't load just now"), the subview says so, Add is disabled, and the list lines, the filter, the drawer rows and the Finish panel's question all disappear while the rest of the app runs as before; `putAway` refuses with a sentence. Against rules that predate this PR (the preview builds against production, and CI deploys rules only on merge) the listener is denied, which is exactly this path: deploy the rules first, and nothing else in the app is affected in the meantime (checked by loading the previous rules into the emulator).
