- **Not in this PR.** No push or reminder for an eat-by date (a scheduled sender would need the containers' index and a quiet-hours rule of its own), no automatic eating, no subtracting from the shopping list, no per-portion tracking, no moving some portions of a container, and nothing for ingredient stock, which is the pantry's packages (§5i).

### 5h. Settings, reminders and the morning note

- **The tab and the screen.** Settings is the fifth tab (`'settings'` in `PINCH_TABS`, a gear at the right end of the nav; there is no `{ kind: 'settings' }` subview any more, and Kitchen's header lost its gear): `SettingsScreen` draws it under a `SectionHeader`, and every View options sheet that depends on it points there with `showSettingsTab` (below). Framed groups (`SettingsGroup`, `SettingsRow` in `src/components`, entity-free), switches are `AppToggle`s and every change saves as it is made, with no line above the first group to say so (a refused save is a toast). `RemindersSection` maps `REMINDER_KINDS`; `KeepingSection` is two `SummaryEditor groupPosition` rows, Fridge and Freezer (a chip, the name over `formatKeepDays`: `formatCareDays`, with 365 days read "1 year"), each `li` `-mx-3` so the chip lines up with the other groups' rows; the pills and the Other stepper live in `DaysEditor` inside the editor (a drawer on a phone, a popover from `sm`, `shouldFocus={false}` so no keyboard opens, its Other state resetting on close), so Settings' two rows are editors over a tab, one level deep. When the settings document can't be read (`pinchSettings.loadError`, `selectPinchSettingsLoadError`, set by the listener's error), `SettingsScreen` draws one tinted card, "Your settings couldn't load just now.", in place of the groups: the defaults stand in for the person's choices everywhere else, and a row of switches drawn from them would read as choices they never made. Driven with the settings read denied on the emulator: six switches before (the defaults), none after.
- **Numbers, first on the screen.** `NutritionSection` is the one place the four nutrition switches live (`saveSettings({ uid, showNutrition: { [metric]: isOn } })`: once the kitchen document is in the store (`selectHasKitchenSettings`) a change is a dotted-path `updateDoc`, so it shows at once from the local write, works offline and never overwrites a concurrent change to another metric; the first change is the transaction that reads the server and, offline, says so instead of queueing, like `savePinchSettings`) ; the Calendar's stat-tile switch is not here: it is only about the Calendar, so it is the first switch in the Calendar's own View options (`CalendarViewOptions`, saved through `usePinchSettings(uid).save({ calendarStats })`), the old device option `showStats` being ignored; the old "Nutrition, everywhere" section and its copy of the "About the numbers" licence line left every View options sheet, and the line now closes `SettingsScreen` (it also names the store logos, so it isn't the Numbers group's footer). `SettingsScreen` is two columns from `md` (`grid-cols-2`, a pill is still the nav there, so each column is about the width of a phone's card; DOM order unchanged so a phone reads the same), takes focus on mount (`tabIndex={-1}`, `aria-label`) and gates only the groups that read the settings document on `selectPinchSettingsLoadError`. The first number switched on while none was (`!hasAnyMetricOn(show)`, read before the write) sets `showMacros` on the Recipes and Kitchen list options once the save has succeeded (read at the tap from `store.getState()`, and written through `useListViewOptions`'s functional `update`, which merges into the value stored at that moment), so the list shows something; a refused save shows the same "That didn't save" toast as the other groups and changes neither, later switches touch no list, and a list the person turned the line off for stays off until the next time every number is off and one comes back on. The kitchen document has its own load error (`selectNutritionLoadError`): the group then shows "Your numbers couldn't load just now." in place of the four switches, the list sheets draw neither the door nor the macros switch, and the other groups, gated on `selectPinchSettingsLoadError` (the settings document), are unaffected either way. `ListViewControls` draws "Show calories and macros" as a switch only while a number is on and always ends with an `ActionRow` door ("Calories and macros · Switch them on in Settings" in place of the switch, "Your numbers · Choose which in Settings" once one is on, none when the numbers couldn't load), and `CalendarViewOptions` (the old `NutritionViewOptions`) holds "Show stats" (`PinchSettings.calendarStats`, drawn only once the settings document has loaded), "Show prices" (Week view only) and the same numbers door; the door calls `showSettingsTab`, which `Pinch.tsx` builds on the same `showTab` helper as `showShoppingTab` but without the open-dialog guard (it runs from an open sheet, so `afterBackLayersClose` steps back over the sheet's history entry first, closing it as a back gesture would, and only then changes tab; a navigation made with the sheet's entry current replaced that entry and left the shell's Calendar-index arithmetic one entry off). The Calendar's customized count and its Reset cover `showPrices` only: `calendarStats` is saved with the account, so a Reset pressed on one device never changes another's.
- **This device.** `src/lib/notifications/devicePush.ts` (app-neutral): `readDevicePushState()` is `unsupported` (no service worker, `Notification` or Firebase Messaging support), `blocked` (permission denied), `on` (granted) or `off` (not asked yet); it is read through `devicePushQueryOptions` (TanStack Query, never persisted, refetched when the window regains focus, so a permission changed in the browser's settings shows when the person comes back). `turnOnDevicePush` asks the browser only when permission is still undecided, and only from the tap on the switch, then registers the FCM token with `registerDeviceToken`; it answers `on`, `blocked`, `unsupported`, `dismissed` (the browser's question was closed without an answer: nothing changed, and the row says "Nothing changed. Allow notifications when your browser asks, then switch this on again.") or `failed` (allowed, but the browser's push service or the network did not answer; the browser's own question is never timed out, so a slow Allow still counts, and the setup after it (the service worker, the token, the token's write) is given ten seconds, since a dev server never registers the service worker it waits on; a `denied` answer is `blocked` even while `Notification.permission` still reads `default`, which is how Chrome refuses a site that was dismissed too often). There is no off switch for the device: a site-level control (`DeviceNotifications` in the Profile modal, with an off flag in `localStorage`) was built and then taken out at the owner's word, since nothing needs it for now, so the browser's own site settings stand in for it and no app's screen draws a control for the whole device. The words for a blocked or unsupported browser are in `src/lib/notifications/deviceText.ts`. **Signing out hands the device on:** `registerDeviceToken` remembers the token it registers in `localStorage` (`push-token:{uid}`, written before the Firestore write so one that lands later is still undoable, cleared once `unregisterDeviceToken` has removed it), so `logOut` (`AuthContext`) awaits `releaseDevicePush(uid)`, which does the one step that matters first: `arrayRemove` of that token from the person's `users` document, with no round trip to the browser's push service. Only then does it start `deleteToken` and not wait for it (it can hang where the service worker never becomes ready, which a dev server never registers); sign-out itself waits at most 3 seconds for the removal. A device registered before the token was remembered falls back to asking the browser for it, only when it already allows notifications. If the removal can't finish (offline), the token stays on the account until the sender drops it, which it does only for a token FCM reports as gone or expired: so on a device that is offline at sign-out, pushes naming their things can still reach the browser's next user until then, and that is the accepted limit. Without the removal the token stayed under the person's `users` document and a push naming their dishes reached whoever used the browser next. Rehearsed (a fake token, permission granted and no service worker, sign-out's 3 second race): before, the wait ran out at 3,001 ms and the token was still on the account; after, 11 ms and it was gone, with the remembered copy cleared. Signing in again registers a fresh token. The sender drops tokens FCM reports as gone (`functions/README.md`, Sending). The per-kind switches are stored on the account and are always drawn: `RemindersSection` switches a kind on with `turnOnDevicePush(uid)` first whenever `device !== 'on'` (the tap is the permission ask, with the row's own line as its reason), saves only when that answers `on`, and otherwise leaves the switch off with the failure under that row (`failed`, or a closed question's "Nothing changed…"); a blocked or unsupported browser is said once under the group instead, and its switches that are off are disabled (one that is on can still be turned off). A reminder that is on while this device is off (the browser hasn't been asked yet, or the person set the reminder on another device) says so under the group, with an "Allow on this device" button that asks on its tap. Switching a kind off never touches the device. The group's last row is "What counts as due soon" (`LookAheadEditor` in a `SummaryEditor`, a drawer on a phone and a popover from `sm`): days ahead (`reminders.leadDays`, which `selectLeadDays` hands to the pantry's own screens too, so it is drawn whether or not a note is on) and, only while eat-soon or expiring is on, the hour and zone of the morning note; the first change that sets the hour writes the device's zone with it, and `createSettings` takes the device's zone as an argument so a first change that creates the document never leaves the note at UTC. `useTimerPush` treats a settings load error as timers off, so a default that is now on never sends a push for a person whose stored choice couldn't be read.
- **The site still asks on load.** `useReminderSync` (App.tsx, all apps) calls `enablePushNotifications(uid)` for every signed-in person, which asks for permission without a tap. That predates Settings and the site's profile control. The reason it was left, that Waypoint, Nine Lives and A-List had no screen of their own to turn push on, no longer holds (the profile has one), so asking only when a person taps is the owner's call; the only change here is that it returns early for a device the person turned off, so the choice survives a reload.
- **Eat-by defaults.** `getEatByDefault(move, care, now, currentEatBy, usual)` takes `usual = getUsualDays(settings)` (`selectUsualDays`) from the three places a default is made: `useContainerDraft` (the put-away form), `usePutAway` (cooking mode's Finish panel) and `ContainerSheet`'s move view. Fridge: the recipe's days, else `usual.fridge`; freezer: the recipe's, else `usual.freezer`; thawing: the shorter of `CONTAINER_THAWED_DAYS` (2), the recipe's fridge days and `usual.fridge` (so a person with a one-day fridge gets one, anyone else the same two as before). `isFromSettings` is true only for a fridge or freezer start taken from a number that differs from the built-in one, and then the hint reads "Your call. Starts at your usual 5 days for the fridge; change it to what you trust."; otherwise the older hints are unchanged, and none claims food is safe (`check:pinch-math` guards the words).
- **The morning note: `sendEatSoonDigest`** (`functions/src/apps/pinch/`, scheduled every 30 minutes, listed in `functions/README.md`). Each run lists `apps/pinch/settings` where `reminders.eatSoon == true` or `reminders.expiring == true` (a lone equality on a map field each, no index, merged by person), and for each person works out, with `getDigestRun`, whether this run is theirs: the local hour in their `timeZone` (UTC when null or unknown) equals `hour`, it is within the hour (`SEND_WINDOW_MS` is 60 minutes: two of the half-hourly runs fall in it, so one failed or late invocation still leaves the other, and the `lastDigestDay` stamp makes it one note; a run that wakes after the hour drops the note rather than sending it late, so it never flushes a backlog; every zone offset, 15-minute ones included, gets exactly one note a day, and still one when the first run is missing, checked in `pinchDigest.ts`), and `lastDigestDay` isn't today's local day. It then reads that person's boxes (`kitchens/{uid}/containers` where `status == 'IN_STOCK'` and `eatBy` between yesterday and `leadDays` ahead, ordered by `eatBy`, at most 100: the composite index `containers (status, eatBy)` ships in `firestore.indexes.json`), groups them by dish (the stored `title`, case and spacing ignored, at the nearest date), and says: "Eat soon" with "Adobo chicken is due tomorrow" / "Chili and Soup are due today", or "Check your fridge and freezer" with "Chicken stock and Chili are past their date" / "Chicken stock is past its date, and 2 more are due soon" (two names at most, then "and N more"; no figures, no store, price or note). **What counts**: past its date counts only for one day (a box a day late is told once; older ones are the list's job, so a person with a long past-date backlog isn't nagged daily); due means within `leadDays` (1 by default, the window the app tints). Nothing due sends nothing and stamps nothing. **Once a day**: `lastDigestDay` is written in a transaction before the push is sent, so two overlapping runs send one and a failed send is a skipped day, never a doubled one. The push goes through `sendPushToUsers` in `functions/src/notifications/sendPush.ts`, the one FCM sender `sendScheduledReminders` also uses (users' `fcmTokens`, `webpush.fcmOptions.link`); the link is `/pinch?tab=kitchen&open=fridge`, which `Pinch.tsx` reads on load to open Fridge & freezer (a window that was already open is only focused by the browser, so it doesn't navigate). The day arithmetic duplicates `getDaysLeft` / `getEatByState` (this package can't import from `src`), and `scripts/checks/pinchDigest.ts` keeps the two equal across four zones and local midnight. A second source of things to act on (ingredients about to expire) is one more entry in `DIGEST_SOURCES` with its own `reminders.<flag>`; the person still gets one push. The sources are read with `Promise.allSettled`: a source that fails (the `packages (status, expiresAt)` index still building) is logged per person and the note goes out with what the others found, and a person whose every source failed counts as failed and is sent nothing. Rehearsed against the emulator with the packages query made to fail: before, both people failed and nobody was sent a note; after, the person with a box due was sent "Adobo chicken is due today" and the packages-only person was counted failed.
- **Timer pushes.** `useTimerPush(timers)` (one call in `useCookTimers`) watches the timers: while `reminders.timers` is on and this device is on (`isDevicePushOn`, a synchronous check), a running timer without a push gets one through `scheduleReminder` (`appId: 'pinch'`, `targetUids: [uid]`, `link: '/pinch'`, `scheduledFor` the timer's `endsAt` plus `TIMER_PUSH_GRACE_MS` (3 s), title "Timer done", body the step's caption and length). Pausing, resetting, dismissing, turning the switch off or leaving cooking mode cancels it with `cancelReminder` (a resumed timer gets a new reminder, since the old one is cancelled); a timer that ends while the page is open cancels it too, because the page rings by itself and the grace gives that cancel time to land first; one that ends while the page is hidden leaves it to go out (the page's own toast is only shown then too). Neither call is awaited by the timer, and a failure is logged, never shown. **One path to the person:** every timer push is marked `inAppToast: false` (a general key of any reminder, set through `scheduleReminder`'s `inAppToast`, for a page that rings by itself), and `useReminderToasts` pops no toast for a reminder carrying it, so a timer that ends while the page is hidden shows the page's own "Timer done" toast once and the push going out adds no second (the rules' create check lists no keys, so the new key needs no rule). A timer gets a push only while its deadline is ahead (`needsTimerPush`), so one picked up past its end schedules nothing (it used to write a reminder already in the past and cancel it a tick later), and what a scheduled push needs is one pure answer, `getTimerPushAction` (keep, leave, cancel; both in `check:pinch-math`). **The push survives a reload:** a running timer's push id is saved with it in the cook on this device (`reminderId` on a running `ResumeTimer`, read back only as a plain id, null for a copy saved before it existed), and `useTimerPush(timers, restoredIds)` takes the ids a picked-up session hands back, so "Keep cooking" keeps the push while the timer still runs to the same deadline and cancels it otherwise, never schedules a second, and the old one can no longer go out over a page that is ringing. "Not now", and a saved session that can no longer be opened, cancel the pushes the copy names. `cancelTimerPush` (`lib/timerPush.ts`) leaves a push the `reminders` slice shows as already sent, cancelled or expired alone, so a delivered push is not rewritten as cancelled. The saved copy is written again when a push is written (`pushRevision`), since a switch turned on mid-cook schedules after the copy was last saved. In development React's StrictMode mounts the session twice, which cancels an adopted push and schedules a fresh one; a production build keeps the adopted one. `sendScheduledReminders` now polls every minute (it was five) so a timer of a minute or more is picked up before it ends and goes out on time, and re-reads a reminder after waiting for its time so one cancelled in the meantime is not sent. Every timer start or resume writes a reminder and the rules let no client delete one, so the same function deletes finished ones (sent, cancelled, expired) more than 14 days old, 200 a run (`functions/README.md`, Pruning); the client's listener is unchanged.
- **Rules and checks.** Criterion 16. `check:pinch-math` covers the settings helpers and the settings-aware eat-by default (mutation-tested); `scripts/checks/pinchDigest.ts` (run by the same script) covers the digest's pure parts; the handler was run against Taylor's containers and six more people on the emulator with a stub sender.

### 5i. Pantry packages

- **Where one is made.** (1) The Pantry subview's Add and an ingredient sheet's "Add another" (`PackageFormSheet`, a `FormSheet`: a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`; with the ingredient unknown it first asks "What did you bring home?" over `ItemSearch`, which lists ingredients only here and, before anything is typed, offers `getPantryPickIds` under "Bought lately", decision 27); (2) an ingredient drawer's "Add a package" chip (the same sheet with the ingredient fixed). A new package starts from the newest one of that ingredient in stock (`getPackageSeed`: what it was bought as, which is its first amount unless it was edited since, then its unit, place and pack; the date stays a question), else the default product's size, else the unit its recipes use (`getPackageUnit` from `gapOrder.ts`); picking the ingredient in the first step seeds the same way, and only the add form does (the shopping step-through starts empty on purpose); (3) the Shopping tab's "Add to pantry" (`PantryFromShoppingSheet`, below). Each writes through `usePackageActions().add`, which is synchronous and throws a sentence for what the client can know (`getNewPackagesProblem`: nothing picked, a bad amount or date, the soft cap of `SOFT_MAX_PACKAGES`, the listener not landed, or failed), builds the documents with `buildPackage` (`generateUuid`, the first history event ADDED with amount and place, `amount` rounded to two places) and dispatches `writePackages`, one `writeBatch`, whose rejection goes to the sheet (an inline sentence, the sheet already closed or reset) or a toast. The batch queues offline like a box's put-away.
- **Use by is asked, never defaulted.** `DateAnswer` is `{ choice: DAYS_3 | WEEK_1 | MONTH_1 | PICK | NONE | null, picked }`; `null` is "not answered", which keeps the submit off with the footer note "Choose when it expires, or pick No date." (`getAnswerDate` is the one place an answer becomes a date or `null`). A quick answer is that many days from the viewer's local today (`getDateInDays`, so it lands on the viewer's calendar day as UTC midnight); a picked day goes through `fromDateInputValue` and must pass `isValidEatBy`. "Add another" keeps the amount, place and ingredient, clears only the answer, and holds the written package's id for one Undo (`undoPackageAdd`, a transaction that deletes it only while `updatedAt` still equals `createdAt`).
- **Changes are transactions over pure plans.** `consumePackage({ id, amount: number | 'ALL' })`, `tossPackage`, `movePackage({ id, to, expiresAt? })`, `editPackage({ id, edit })`, `undoPackageEnd({ id, at })` each run `applyPlan`: `runTransaction`, `transaction.get`, the plan for that act over the fresh document (`planUse`, `planPackageToss`, `planPackageMove`, `planPackageEdit`, `planUndoEnd`), then `transaction.update` of only the fields the plan owns (`amount`/`status`/`place`/`expiresAt`/`productId`/`ingredientName`/`unit`, `openedAt` when it changes, always `history` and `updatedAt`). A plan answers `{ fields }` or `{ error }` ("changed since"), so a package another device finished, moved or deleted is never written over, and the screen says so instead of retrying. `planPackageEdit` writes `unit` only when it changed and records one EDITED event naming what changed. `removePackage` is a `deleteDoc` after a destructive confirm that says the history goes too. `usePackageActions` wraps them with the in-flight guard (`once`, per package id: a second tap during the write is ignored), the Undo toast (6 s, one tap, guarded against a double tap) and a failure message (`getWriteMessage`, the same shape the boxes use: "Check your connection and try again." only when the SDK says `unavailable` or the browser is offline, else the act's own sentence, never a retry hint for a refusal).
- **Undo reverses only what is still as the act left it.** `planUndoEnd(pkg, at)` needs the package's last event to be the use or toss at `at` and puts the amount back (a finished one returns with the amount it ended with, an unfinished one gets what was used added back, and `openedAt` returns to `null` when that use was the one that set it); `planUndoPackageMove(pkg, at, before)` puts back the place and date a move changed, only while that move is still the last event. Anything else answers "changed since", and a bulk Undo reports how many it restored.
- **Bulk acts: the past-its-date row and Select mode (`changePackagesInPlace`, `planPackageBulk`, `undoPackageChanges`).** One thunk serves a toss, "Used it up" and a move: `runInChunks` (shared with the containers, 200 ids per `runTransaction`, each chunk re-reading every package with `transaction.get`) over the pure `planPackageBulk`, which leaves a package alone when it is gone, no longer in stock, already in the place a move names, or (a toss of what the screen called past its date, `isPastOnly`) has been given a new date that is no longer past, counts what it left by reason (`PackageSkipCounts`: GONE, FINISHED, SAME_PLACE, REDATED, status first) and writes only the fields each plan owns. The result is `{ changed: { id, at, before }[], skipped, error }`; the toast's Undo is `undoPackageChanges(changed)`, chunked the same way and restoring each package only while the event it wrote is still its last (`planUndoEnd` or `planUndoPackageMove`). `getPackageBulkToast` titles it with the real number ("Tossed 3 packages", "Moved 2 of 3 packages to the freezer") and puts what was skipped, or the names, under it. "Select" is `useSelection` + `SelectionBar` + `PromptRow` as in Fridge & freezer; an ingredient's tick ticks its shown packages, "All past their date" ticks the past-due ones in the shown scope, and the row above the list acts on the past-due packages of the whole list only while no search or place narrows it. **Which act is filled** in the Select footer is `getPackageSelectionPrimary(selected, now)`: Toss when every ticked package is past its date (a package with no date is not), else Used it up; checked in `pinchPantry.ts`.
- **The list and the numbers.** `selectPackagesByIngredient` (`Map<ingredientId, Package[]>`, soonest first through `byUseBy`: date, no date last, then the older package, then id, so two reads never reshuffle) feeds `getStockView` (one pass per ingredient: `packages`, `totals` by dimension, `pastCount`, `soonCount`, `nearest`); `PantrySubview` builds its rows once per snapshot and scope in a `useMemo`, orders them needs-a-look first then by nearest date then name, counts the switch by ingredient rows (so a place's count and All don't have to add up), derives the shown place each render (a pick that matches nothing falls to All, never mirrored by an effect), and draws 20 rows then "Show N more" unless the tail is three or fewer. The place switch exists only while two places hold something, the search from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` (8) ingredients, the divider split from `PANTRY_SPLIT_MIN_ROWS`. An ingredient's row has no emoji (a shop section's emoji, `PANTRY_SECTION_EMOJIS`, was removed because it said "steak" for chicken thighs); only a package inside a sheet leads with its place's.
- **Use it up (a door, not a write).** `getUseItUpRecipes(recipes, packagesByIngredient, ingredientId, getName, now, soonDays)` lists the person's unarchived recipes that use the ingredient, ordered by the earliest date among the due-soon ingredients each would also use, then by how many, then title, at most 20, with `dueNames` for the line under each ("Also uses Cilantro"). `PantryRecipeDrawer` renders it in place in the package sheet; picking one opens the recipe through `usePinchOverlay`. It plans, shops and writes nothing.
- **Recipe lines.** `getLineStock(line, packages, now, conversions)` answers `HAVE` (enough, in the line's own kind or bridged), `SOME` (some, with the total in the line's kind), `OTHER` (usable packages exist but none measures like the line and none can be bridged), `PAST` (every package of it is past its date) or `NONE`; `getLineMarkers` turns a recipe's answers into words with the exception rule (PAST always; otherwise the minority of present against absent, ties flagging absent) plus the one summary ("1 of 9 in your pantry", null when nothing is in the pantry). Nothing in the pantry of a staple (`isUsuallyAtHome`: the Spices & oils section and water, what the add sheet leaves unticked) is `STAPLE`, not `NONE`: `getLineStock`'s optional fifth argument is the ingredient, so the family is read where the call is made, and a `STAPLE` line draws no mark and is not counted in the share (a staple that is in the pantry counts like anything else). `RecipeDrawer` reads the pantry only through `selectPackagesByIngredient` and the ingredient's conversions, `LineStockMarker` draws a mark, and a recipe with no package of anything shows nothing at all. `AddToShoppingSheet` uses the same `getLineStock` per row (`getPantry`, `hasEnough`, `startsOn`): a row with enough starts unticked and says why; a row with some, none or only another kind starts as before.
- **Shopping, "Add to pantry".** The chip draws under "Got it" while the list holds ticked lines that are not yet put away: a line is put away while its `pantryAddedAt` equals its `updatedAt` (`isPutAway`), so ticking, unticking or editing it again moves `updatedAt` on and offers it once more. `add` takes `putAway` (the sheet's lines with their `updatedAt`) and `writePackages` stamps each line with a field-scoped `updateDoc` of its own, issued before the packages' batch commits (so the mark is local-first and another device stops offering the line as soon as it syncs), never inside that batch (a line another device already cleared would refuse the whole batch and lose the packages; its failure is only logged), and un-stamps with `null` when the batch is refused (the write's "That didn't save" toast, above the sheet, is the one place a refusal is told, since the sheet has moved on by then). The old device-local `localStorage` ledger is gone. `PantryFromShoppingSheet` walks the lines one at a time: the amount from the ingredient's default product size when it has one and otherwise empty ("How much did you buy?", `PackageFields`' `amountLabel`; Save's note says "Say how much there is."), the unit from the product or the list line, place from `getDefaultPlace(getShopSection(...))`, date unanswered; "Skip" moves on, "Save and next" writes through `add` (a failure is shown in the step and the step stays), the last step closes with one toast that opens Pantry through `openSubviewFromToast` (which only dismisses while a subview holds typed work or a dialog is on top). The list line's amount is only the hint ("On your list: ⅔ cup", `formatKitchenTotal`): what the plan wanted is not what was bought, and a prefilled number saved untouched recorded a purchase nobody made (round 8 review, Cilantro 0.63 cup). The list's own ticking is unchanged and nothing is required.
- **Cooking takes out what it used.** `usePantryTake` lives in `CookingSession` and `CookPantryTake` draws a card on the Finish panel under the cook's record once the packages have loaded and at least one recipe line can be compared. `planTakeOut(lines, packages, now, getConversions)` adds a recipe's lines per ingredient in a common measure (`toCommon`, bridging through the ingredient's own numbers), draws each ingredient from its packages soonest date first, leaves past-date packages alone, takes what a short package has and reports the shortfall, skips a line with no amount and an ingredient it cannot compare, and returns `{ takes, shortfalls }` (`getTakeGroups` groups the rows for the card). Nothing is taken until "Take out" is tapped, with a tick per ingredient (all ticked to start); `takeFromPackages` is one local-first `writeBatch` built from the loaded packages (`selectPackagesById`), through `planUse`: a partial take writes `increment(-amount)` with `openedAt`, `history` and `updatedAt`, one that empties a package writes `status: 'DONE'` with the history, and the history is this device's copy plus one event, capped at ten like every append. The thunk resolves once the batch is queued, not when the server answers, so the card reads "Took 2 things out of your pantry" at once with the same rows and one Undo, offline too (99 ms with the network cut; the server shows it after reconnect). Undo (`undoPackageChanges`) is still a transaction that re-reads each package, so it needs a connection and says so. The two-device race, stated: an `increment` keeps another device's use of the same package, so two devices taking out the same cook take twice (accepted: each took what the recipe used); the capped history can lose an event the other device appended in the same moment (it is a record, never a number anything reads); and a take that would drive an amount to zero or below is refused by the rule (`isPositiveAmount` runs on every write), which rejects the whole batch, so nothing reads below zero and no clamp is needed: the card goes back to its proposal and a toast says "They were used or finished somewhere else, so nothing was taken." (offline that arrives when the write syncs). The cook's own record is untouched: Mark as cooked from a drawer or the plan does not take anything out (a follow-up).
- **The morning note.** `ReminderKind` gains `'expiring'` (a row in `REMINDER_KINDS`, a key on `PinchReminderSettings`, a source in `DIGEST_SOURCES`). `sendEatSoonDigest` lists people by `reminders.eatSoon == true` or `reminders.expiring == true` and, for each source whose flag is on, runs its collector: the boxes' (`collectContainers`, unchanged) and `collectPackages`, which reads `kitchens/{uid}/packages` where `status == 'IN_STOCK'` and `expiresAt` is between yesterday and `leadDays` ahead, ordered by `expiresAt`, at most 100 (the composite `packages (status asc, expiresAt asc)`, shipped in `firestore.indexes.json`). `toPackageDigestItems` makes one item per ingredient at its nearest date; `mergeDigestItems` unions the sources by name (a dish and an ingredient with the same name are one thing at the nearer date, and the dish keeps it); `getDigestMessage` words it: dish-only items keep the old copy ("Eat soon" / "Adobo chicken is due tomorrow"), anything with an ingredient says "Check your kitchen" and "Cilantro and Whole milk need a look" ("X, Y and N more need a look"), and `getDigestLink` opens the pantry for ingredient-only notes, the fridge when any dish is in it. A person with only the pantry switch on gets a note only from packages; the stamp, the hour window, the 30-minute drop and the one-a-day rule are unchanged. A new index builds for minutes after deploy, and until it is READY the run logs the failed query and sends the boxes' part only.
- **Failure.** `startPackagesListener` is an optional extra like cooks and containers: its error is logged, clears the loaded packages and sets `loadError`, and never reaches the load-error screen. The Kitchen tile says so in its accessible name ("couldn't load just now"), Add is disabled with a note, and the markers, the drawer block, the add-sheet hints and the Finish card disappear while the rest of the app runs as before. Against rules that predate this change the listener is denied, which is exactly this path (deploy the rules and the index before the client).
- **Rules and checks.** Criterion 17. `check:pinch-math` runs `scripts/checks/pinchPantry.ts` (date states, soonest-first with no date last, sums per dimension and the bridge, grouping, the plans and their Undo including the one that skips a changed package, bulk skips and toasts, the take-out planner with shortfalls and bridged conversions, line stock and the marker rule, the history cap's order) and the extended `pinchDigest.ts`; both were mutation-tested (eleven edits, each caught).
- **Not in this change.** No finished view, no purge of finished packages, no mark-as-cooked take-out, no per-package notes, no barcode or receipt entry, no price from a package, no automatic expiry state, no sharing of packages (a recipe or saved meal share, §5k, carries no stock), and no stock count for an ingredient the person never added a package for (which reads as "not in your pantry" only on a recipe, and only as the minority mark).

### 5j. Photo suggestions for a new recipe

- **The flow.** In a new recipe (never an edit) the Photo row's editor shows "Photos from the web" under the upload buttons once the title has three letters. `RecipePhotoSuggestions` waits for the title to stop changing (`useDebouncedCallback` with `DEBOUNCE_MS.autocomplete`, so the line saying what is sent is on screen before the request goes), asks `webPhotosQueryOptions(getPhotoQuery(title))` (TanStack Query, key the lower-cased single-spaced query, half an hour, no retry, never persisted because the answers are third-party), shows "Searching…" over six empty tiles, then a 3×2 grid (`ImageChoiceGrid`, entity-free, in `src/components`, whose `tileClassName` makes the tiles 56px tall at 500px of height or less so both rows clear a landscape phone's footer) or one calm line for nothing found or a failure. The answer is checked again in the client (`toWebPhotos`: https only, six at most, copied by name). The text and the grid are decided from the typed title, not the debounced one, so a title changed in flight shows "Searching…" and the old answer is never drawn.
- **Picking.** A tap loads the full picture once (`probeImage`, up to 8 s) before it is chosen. The grid loads only thumbnails, and a hotlinked picture is often gone or refuses to be shown elsewhere, so the person hears it at the tap ("That photo can’t be reached right now. Try another one.", the tile dims) and a cover is never saved that failed to load for them. A chosen photo shows in the preview with "From <site>. Kept as a web link, not a copy, so it can disappear.", and as the Photo row's thumbnail. An upload and a web photo are exclusive, the one chosen last wins; Remove returns to no photo; a tap on the chosen tile does nothing (nothing is undone by a repeated tap). The selected tile has a 3px inner border and a check, drawn by an overlay because `index.css` removes the focus ring from a control the pointer just tapped.
- **Turning it off.** Settings' "Suggest photos for new recipes" (`PinchSettings.suggestPhotos`, default true) and a "Turn off" chip in the block: the recipe form is a subview holding typed work and cannot open Settings, so the one in-context off is here, and its toast's Undo turns it back on. The toast is raised at once, from the local write (it used to wait for the server, so offline there was none), and is taken back if the save is refused. Off, nothing is drawn and nothing is sent.
- **The function.** `searchRecipeImages({ query })` (signed in only; spaces collapsed, at most 80 characters) answers `{ results }`: at most six `{ thumbUrl, imageUrl, title, host }` copied by name. Both addresses must be https and a result with a non-https thumbnail is dropped, never replaced by the full picture (a thumbnail loads before the person picks). The generic layer, `functions/src/imageSearch/`, says nothing about recipes: `searchImages` (cache, order, quotas, deadline), `ddg.ts`, `google.ts`, `providers.ts`, `optionalSecret.ts`, `usage.ts`, `results.ts`, `fetchCapped.ts`.
- **One switch.** `IMAGE_SEARCH_PROVIDERS` (an env var, default `ddg,google`) lists providers in the order they are tried, and the first with pictures wins: `google` alone makes Google the only one and nothing else changes. A provider that is skipped (not configured, paused, out of budget) costs nothing, and a failure falls through to the next. Serper or Unsplash would be one file with a factory like `createGoogleProvider` and one line in `PROVIDER_FACTORIES`; none is built.
- **Cache, budget, limit.** Answers are cached in `apps/pinch/imageSearchCache` by query and provider list (a week for pictures, ten minutes for "none found", a failure never). Google's day is counted in `apps/pinch/imageSearchUsage` by Pacific day (its quota resets at Pacific midnight) and stopped at 90 of 100, DuckDuckGo's at 1,500; each person gets 10 uncached searches a minute and 100 a day. Only a search that goes out is counted (`IMAGE_SEARCH_GOOGLE_DAILY_CAP` and `IMAGE_SEARCH_DDG_DAILY_CAP` override the two caps). The whole search gives up after 10 s, a request after 4 s and 1 MB (the token page 600 KB), and no body is scanned with a pattern over the whole text.
- **DuckDuckGo (option 3).** No key, no setup, no dependency: one request for the results page, a `vqd` token from it, then the JSON its picture grid loads, safe search on. The pictures request is tried once more when it fails or is empty, and after three failures in a row the provider steps aside for five minutes so a blocked address costs a search no time.
- **Google (option 1).** The Custom Search JSON API with `searchType=image`, `num=6` and `safe=active`. `GOOGLE_CSE_KEY` and `GOOGLE_CSE_CX` are read at run time (`optionalSecret.ts`: the environment first, then Secret Manager with the function's own credentials), not bound with `defineSecret`, so an absent secret means the provider is skipped and the deploy never needs them. To turn it on: create both secrets and let the runtime account read them (`functions/README.md`).
- **Checks.** `scripts/checks/imageSearch.ts` runs both providers, the secret reader and the search against fakes (token missing, empty, malformed or deeply nested JSON, a timeout, a non-https address dropped, the fallback order, a cache hit, the budget stopping Google, hostile bodies) and `scripts/checks/pinchPhotos.ts` the client helpers; both run in `check:pinch-ingest`, with every mutant killed.

### 5k. Sharing a recipe or a saved meal

- **The model: a copy taken once, not a view.** Making a link copies a recipe, or a saved meal with each recipe it uses, into `shareSnapshots/{id}` at that moment (decision 27, Data Schema 10 and 11). Editing the original changes nothing a visitor sees until its owner takes the copy again, and the page says so ("A snapshot from Oct 16, so it won't change if the original does."). Planned meals are not shared ("Save as meal" first). There is no PIN and no expiry: anyone holding the link can read the copy until it is stopped or replaced, and the link is the only secret.
- **Three callables** (`functions/src/apps/pinch/`; each is its own Cloud Run service, listed in `functions/README.md`). `getPinchShare({ shareId })` needs no sign-in (a signed-in caller only learns whether the share is theirs) and returns `{ status: 'ok', kind, sharedAt, isOwner, ownerSourceId, recipe, meal }` or `{ status: 'not_found' }`. `changePinchShare({ action, … })` needs sign-in and takes `create`, `update`, `renew` or `delete`, returning `{ shareId }` (null after a delete). `copyPinchShareCover({ shareId, index, recipeId })` needs sign-in and returns `{ coverPath }` or `{ coverPath: null }`. `isOwner` is the server comparing the caller's uid with `ownerUid`; `ownerSourceId` (the owner's own recipe or meal id) goes to the owner alone; the uid goes to no one.
- **The allowlist runs twice, and returns nothing rather than part.** The client builds the copy by named fields (`utils/sharing.ts`: `buildSharedRecipe`, `buildSharedMeal`, never a spread; `pinchShareBuild.ts` fails when one spreads). `changePinchShare` rebuilds it from the input field by field (`toSharedRecipe`, `toSharedMeal` in `pinchShareView.ts`) before it writes, and `getPinchShare` runs the stored copy through the same functions before it answers (`readStoredCopy`), so a copy written by an older version or edited by hand in the console can't carry more than the shape allows. Strings are trimmed and capped at the app's own caps; numbers must be finite and in range; a unit must be in the list (an unknown one keeps the line's name and drops its amount, never inventing one); links are https only with no sign-in in the address, and one over 2,000 characters is dropped, never cut; a catalog id must have the `cat-` shape; care days are 1 to 365; only the first how-to link stays one; `hasCover` is set only from covers the server copied. A recipe with no title, no usable servings or neither an ingredient nor a step is refused whole, and a meal is all or nothing (an item pointing at a missing recipe, an unreadable recipe or item, or more than 300 ingredient lines in all refuses it, since cutting would hand a visitor half a meal); a list past its cap (items, recipes, steps, lines, links) is cut at the cap. A refused copy is `null`, and the callable answers invalid-argument on a write and not found on a read.
- **The id and the one not-found.** `mintShareId` draws 26 characters from the 32-character alphabet `23456789abcdefghjklmnpqrstuvwxyz` with `crypto.randomInt`, so 130 bits (the pattern `^[2-9a-hj-np-z]{26}$` is what the client, `getPinchShare` and the Storage rule all check, and `pinchShare.ts` proves the alphabet equals it and is drawn evenly). A client never supplies an id to create one. `getPinchShare` answers `{ status: 'not_found' }`, the same body, for a malformed id (without reading anything), an unknown one, a share whose snapshot is missing and a copy that no longer passes the allowlist. `update` and `renew` answer `not-found` for a missing, someone else's and a malformed share alike (an update whose kind differs from the share's is invalid-argument), and `delete` answers `{ shareId: null }` for those three alike and deletes nothing, so no call tells which ids exist.
- **Covers.** For each `{ index, recipeId }` a request names, the server copies the owner's uploaded cover from `kitchens/{uid}/recipes/{recipeId}/cover.webp` (a path it builds from the caller's uid; a client never names one) to `shares/{id}/cover-{index}.webp`, and gives every copy its own download token (`copyCoverFile`), so a public file never carries the token of a private original and `getDownloadURL` can open it (a plain copy would carry the original's token, and a file with no token can't be opened). A web-search cover (`coverUrl`) is never shared, because a visitor's browser would load a third-party site; the Share view says so when it applies. A copy that fails is logged as a short code and the first four characters of the id, never a path (a Storage error's message holds the path, which holds the id), and leaves that recipe without a cover. **Which bucket:** the Functions use the project's default bucket (`getStorage().bucket()`), which in production is the one the app uploads to (Waypoint's `deleteTrip` relies on the same); an Emulator Suite started with no network or no `firebase login` can't read the project's Admin SDK config, so its Functions default to `moondreams-dev-apps.appspot.com` while the app and the seed use `moondreams-dev-apps.firebasestorage.app`, and a cover uploaded by the app is then "not found" (`hasCover: false`) and a seeded cover can't be copied. Start such a suite with `STORAGE_BUCKET_URL=moondreams-dev-apps.firebasestorage.app` (`SEEDING.md`); with the files in the Functions' bucket every copy below ran and served. The covers are copied before the documents are written, so `hasCover` is written once and never lags; if the batch then fails the new folder is removed. The public page reads a cover with `getDownloadURL(ref(storage, path))` through a query that is never persisted, and a cover that can't load leaves the tile.
- **The owner's verbs.** *Create:* an existing share for the same owner, kind and source is retaken and its id returned (a double tap or a second device never makes a second link, and a person at the cap can still retake one), else the cap, then the covers, then one `batch.create` of both documents. *Update:* the owner is checked, covers are copied, one `runTransaction` re-reads the share (gone: its files are removed and the answer is not-found) and updates `title`, `sourceUpdatedAt`, `updatedAt` and the snapshot's copy, and covers the copy no longer names are removed. *Renew (Get a new link):* a new id, with the copy and covers written first and the old covers and documents ended after, so there is no moment without a valid link, and a failure to end the old one takes the new one back; the copy, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `sourceUpdatedAt` carry over. *Delete (Stop sharing):* the cover files first, then both documents in one batch, and a file that can't be deleted throws, so a link is never reported ended while a picture is still public and the call can be repeated; idempotent. `changePinchShare` has a 30 s timeout (the others 15 s) because a renew copies up to ten covers.
- **The cap and its race.** `MAX_PINCH_SHARES` (30) links per person, counted by `changePinchShare` (`shares where ownerUid == uid`, a `count()` aggregation) before it writes; past it the answer is `resource-exhausted`. The cap lives only in the callable, not in the rules (no client writes a share), and two creates at the same moment can both read 29 and both write, so one link past 30 is possible. The lookup for an existing share and the write are not one transaction either, so two devices creating the same source at the same instant can make two links for it. The Share view reads the loaded list to disable "Create link" at 30, which is only a convenience, and at 30 it adds a "Your shared links" chip to the reason (see the owner's side).
- **Deleting an original.** `removeRecipe` (the hard delete) and `removeMeal` call `stopSharingDeleted` after their own delete has committed: not awaited, failure logged, so the link stays listed under Shared links as "Original deleted" and can be stopped there; a share the store has not loaded is not stopped. Archiving a recipe leaves its link up (the confirm says so and where to stop it). The Delete confirms say "It's shared, so its link stops working too." only when a link exists.
- **The owner's side.** `startSharesListener(uid)` (`kitchenListeners.ts`, started from `usePinchSync`) reads `apps/pinch/shares where ownerUid == uid`, one equality (no index; `check:indexes` is unchanged), into `sharesSlice` (`items`, `isLoaded`, `loadError`; reset by `resetAllState`). It is optional like cooks and notes: a failure is logged, clears the items and sets `loadError`, and never reaches `selectPinchLoadError`; the Share view then says it couldn't load. `ShareView` is one component in two homes (a recipe's or saved meal's drawer, More then Share, and a row's sheet under Settings, Shared links). **The primary action is in the sheet's pinned footer**: each home draws a footer node and hands it back as `footerNode`, and `ShareView` portals the primary into it (Create link when not shared; Copy link and Send when shared; Update the shared copy once the original has changed, with Copy link and Send then in the body), with the error and the reason a button is off above it, so the primary is on screen when the view opens at every size (it was below the fold at 844×390 and 320×568 when it sat in the body). **The link is a selectable text block** (`select-all`, wraps, never wider than its box), not an input: Dreamer UI's `Input` replaces a passed `readOnly` with its own display-only mode, so the field took typing. Copy link is the shared `CopyTextButton` (`@/components`: the `copyToClipboard` fallback for plain-HTTP pages, "Copied" on the button for two seconds, and, when the browser refuses, the toast "Select the link above and copy it from there."), never Dreamer UI's `CopyButton`, which hands its `className` to a Fragment. **On a landscape phone** (`isLandscape`: a screen 599px tall or less, at least 520px wide by a media query of its own and below `lg`, which is the range where `DetailSheet` is a drawer on a short screen, narrowed from below so a 568×320 or 639×390 phone gets two columns too) a shared view's body is a two-column grid, the link and its cards on the left and the action group on the right so the first row starts at the body's top, and a changed link's Copy link and Send move into the footer beside Update the shared copy (Update the widest); on any screen 599px tall or less the dated line under the link and the drawers' "Share this recipe" subtitle are left out and the blocks sit 12px apart. Taller screens keep one column. At 844×390 a shared link then shows 3 full action rows above the footer (it showed 1) and a changed one 2 (it showed 0). **A cap that points elsewhere has a door:** `ShareView` takes an optional `onClose` (the drawers pass theirs; the Shared links sheet doesn't, since it never reaches the cap state); at 30 links the view leaves out its "In the copy" and "Left out" list (nothing there can be acted on, and with the taller footer it was clipped under it) and the footer's reason carries an `ActionChips` chip "Your shared links" that calls `onClose()` and then `openSubview({ kind: 'shares' })` in the same tick, so the drawer closes first and nothing stacks, and `SharedLinksSubview` closes to the screen underneath, whether the Settings tab or a Share view opened it (one back press). `saveShare`, `renewShareLink` and `stopSharing` (`shareActions.ts`) call `changePinchShare` through `httpsCallable`; `saveShare` builds the payload from `getState()` at the tap and sends `update` when a record for the source is loaded, else `create` (the server dedupes too). Each waits up to 4 s (`waitForListener`, 40 polls of 100 ms) for the listener to deliver the new record, so the screen never shows the old link between the callable's answer and the listener's, and gives up quietly since the change is already made. **Errors** are mapped, not shown: offline or `unavailable` is "Sharing needs a connection."; `resource-exhausted` is the cap line; `not-found` is "That link is already gone." (and a stop that finds the link gone counts as done); anything else is the action's try-again line, with the detail in `console.error`. Outcomes show inside the view (no toast over the drawer) and a failure stays in it. **Stale** is `getShareSourceStamp(source, …, getShareIncludes(share)) !== share.sourceUpdatedAt`, or a changed tips or notes choice in the view; `setMealFavorite` no longer writes `updatedAt`, so starring a meal doesn't flag it, and an archive writes only `isArchived` (`removeRecipe`), so archiving a recipe doesn't flag its link (it did: the archive wrote `updatedAt`); a star and a bulk add to a collection never wrote it. A recipe form save that only changes collections, source or nutrition still flags falsely, and a renamed ingredient or a recipe deleted from inside a shared meal is not detected. Measured against the emulator: create 166 to 249 ms from the tap to a settled screen (the callable alone 85 to 134), update 151 to 286, new link 280 to 393, stop 166 to 275; `changePinchShare` bumps `updatedAt` on an update, so the 4 s wait resolved at once every time.
- **The public page.** The route `pinch/shared/:shareId` (`AppRoutes.tsx`, lazy, outside the Pinch shell: no tab bar, no listeners, no kitchen reads) renders `SharedPinch.tsx`. `sharedPinchQueryOptions(shareId, uid)` (`queries/shareQueries.ts`) has the key `['pinch', 'shared', shareId, uid]`, calls `getPinchShare`, and sets `staleTime` and `gcTime` to 5 minutes, no retry, no refetch on window focus and no `meta: { persist: true }` (the result is someone's recipe and the key holds the secret). The query waits for auth (`enabled: !loading`), so an owner is never shown the visitor's page first, and it keeps a previous answer as placeholder only when its link id equals the current one, so signing in flips the slot in place while the slot shows a skeleton of its own size (`isPlaceholderData`) until the answer for the new uid arrives; a sign-in clears the query cache, so the cover's own query keeps its previous address (`keepPreviousData`) and its picture stays through the flip. **A 401 is renewed once:** the callable protocol checks the token before our code runs, so a token the server won't take is answered with `401 UNAUTHENTICATED` even by the public `getPinchShare`; `readShare` then renews the person's token (`getIdToken(true)`) and asks once more, so one bad answer is invisible, and two in a row show the failed state with Try again. A malformed id answers not-found without calling; an `ok` answer whose kind lacks its recipe or meal, or a meal item pointing at a missing recipe, is turned into not-found in the client too (`getShareTarget`). **States:** a skeleton shaped like the page; failed ("We couldn't open this link", never the server's text); not found (one screen for a stopped, mistyped and never-existing link). Both terminal screens are `Message` in `SharedStates.tsx`: `SharedBrand` (the Pinch mark and name `SharedPinch` also opens with, without its eyebrow, so they say nothing about the link) over the page's own `SlotCard` (emoji chip, the title as the `h1`, one sentence) with the one way on: "Open Pinch" (`/pinch`) as the filled button on not found, and "Try again" with "Open Pinch" beside it on failed. A malformed, an unknown and a stopped id all reach the one `SharedNotFound`, which takes no input, so the three screens are the same document (compared by DOM and by pixels at two sizes in both themes); the failed card is 358×154 and the not-found card 358×174 (its sentence is three lines at 390px wide). **Slot:** one card (`ShareSlot`), the same markup, place and size in every state: a 154px box at every viewport in both themes, because its text block reserves a lead and two hint lines (`min-h-17`), the owner leads are short enough never to wrap ("Your shared recipe", "Your shared saved meal"), and the buttons are 44px. The owner's card has Open in Pinch, which goes to `/pinch?tab=recipes`, not to the recipe, since the drawer's open state is local to `RecipesScreen`; a signed-in visitor's has "Want this in your recipes?" and "Add to my recipes" or "Add to my saved meals", and after the add the card is its own confirmation with Undo, See it in Pinch and a line saying where the copy is ("Find it under Recipes in Pinch.", "Find it under Saved meals, in Recipes."); a signed-out visitor's has "Keep this recipe" and Sign in with Google; while checking it is a skeleton of the same size. What an account gives (`Benefits` in `ShareSlot.tsx`: two lines, a third, "Keep it with your own recipes", repeated the card and was dropped) is drawn for a signed-out visitor only, directly under the card, in a framed list with a caption that says what it is about (`SHARE_BENEFITS_TITLE`, "With an account") and the same anatomy as the page's other sections (`FormSection`, a framed list), so it reads as the reason for the card above it and not as stray copy; the card itself never differs in size (a visitor's "Taken back." after Undo, and an add error, replace the card's hint instead of adding a row, so it stays 154px through add and undo). The cost is on a phone: a signed-out recipe starts lower than a signed-in one (the first ingredient row at 80% of 390×844 against 63%; 76% when the lines had no caption or frame), which the owner accepted for the lines sitting beside the sign-in. The button that is busy keeps its ink fill (Dreamer UI fades a disabled primary to pale peach with grey text, which made "Adding it to your recipes…" unreadable). **Layout:** an 80px cover tile beside the title on a phone and on a short screen (599px high or less); a wide band, at most `min(14rem, 26dvh)`, from `sm` on a tall one; from `lg`, or from `sm` on a short screen, two columns with the slot sticky on the right (`isSplit`, `useIsShortScreen`), so the action is on screen at 844×390. A recipe's description is drawn in full (at most 2,000 characters): a clamp's "Show more" is a frameless button. A saved meal lists "On the plate" rows; a dish opens in place with the shared `BackLink` chip, and the chip, Escape and the back gesture each close it, one per press; the scroll position is restored a frame after the close, because the browser restores its own after a `popstate`, and the slot stays and still means the whole saved meal.
- **No third-party loads, no secret leaks, headers and meta.** The page loads the callable, our own Storage and the same-origin Pinch logo, and nothing else until the visitor taps Sign in (a popup, so no redirect carries the address). The how-to video is a plain link row there, with no player and no YouTube thumbnail (`isPlayerHidden`), links are https only with `rel='noopener noreferrer nofollow ugc'`, a cover has `referrerPolicy='no-referrer'`, and every string is drawn as text. `useNoIndex` adds `<meta name='robots' content='noindex, nofollow'>` and `<meta name='referrer' content='no-referrer'>` while the page is mounted and removes both on leaving; `cloudflare-worker.js` has a `pages` entry for `/pinch/shared/` that sends `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` on every response under it and gives the link preview a generic title and description ("A recipe shared on Pinch", never the title); `Layout` shortens the presence path to `pinch/shared` before it reaches the world-readable presence tree and titles the tab "Shared on Pinch". The audit for the address found no analytics or error reporter in `src`, `savePostLoginRedirect` is not called on this route, and `ErrorBoundary` names only the app (the error text goes to admins and the dev build). Watched in a browser (signed out, then signed in): the page's own requests are the callable (token in the body), the cover (a metadata read and the image, token in the Storage path) and the same-origin logo, plus the shell's own Auth, Firestore and App Check traffic (App Check's exchange is the one third-party host, and it sends the origin only as `Referer`); document requests carry no referrer; the id appears in exactly the document URL, the callable body and the Storage path, and in no `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, cookie, IndexedDB store, `history.state`, console message or presence node (the presence tree holds `pinch/shared`).
- **Tips and notes in a share, each by choice.** `PinchShare` carries `includesTips` and `includesNotes` (booleans written only by `changePinchShare`; a share made before the choice has neither key and `getShareIncludes` reads it as false, so a legacy link never gains entries). `ShareView` derives its "In the copy" and "Left out" lines from the ticks (a ticked kind is added to "In the copy"; each kind that exists and is unticked is listed under "Left out" as "unless you include them"), says "The first 30 are shared." under a row whose count is over its cap, and stages two `Checkbox` rows, "Your tips · 2" and "Your notes · 3" (`IncludeChoices`; a row only while that kind exists or is already included; both off to start, since a share spends the person's words; local `tipsChoice`/`notesChoice` fall back to the live share's flags and reset after a save), and `saveShare({ kind, sourceId, includes })` builds the payload with them: `buildSharedRecipe(recipe, ingredientsById, notes, includes)` keeps only the TIPs, and, separately, only the NOTEs, with words (`toSharedEntries`: `{ text, step }`, `step` from `resolveNoteStep` against the recipe's steps right now, whole recipe first then by step, oldest first, cut to the kind's cap and length), and `buildSharePayload` sends `includesTips` and `includesNotes` beside them. The Function reads only an explicit `true` (`toShareIncludes`), runs the lists through `toEntry` and then empties the one it was not told to include (`toIncludedCopy`, on every recipe of a meal), so a client that sends notes with `includesNotes: false` shares none. **Changed since:** `getShareSourceStamp(source, recipesById, notesByRecipe, includes)` is the latest `updatedAt` among the source, its recipes and the included entries, plus the number of included entries, so deleting one moves it (a timestamp alone never would); the entries are picked with the same order and cap as the copy (`pickSharedEntries`, shared with `toSharedEntries`), so a 35th tip that is not in the copy never marks the link changed; `isShareStale` is `stamp !== sourceUpdatedAt` (it was `>`), and `getShareStatus` uses the share's own flags, so a note edit never marks a tips-only link, and takes `isNotesReady` (`selectShareStatuses` passes `notes.isLoaded` without a load error): while the notes have not landed, a share that includes tips or notes reads as not changed, so a cold open never shows a false "You've changed this recipe" and a failed listener never leaves one. A link made before the choice (neither flag, no entries) keeps the stamp it had. Changing a box on a live link shows "You changed what to include." and the Update button (`isIncludesChanged`), since the stored copy differs from the choice. A copy with tips or notes is only taken once the notes listener has landed without error (`selectShareNotesBlock`, applied only while a kind is included); **known limit:** the listener's first snapshot can come from the device's cache, and the block checks only `isLoaded`, so an update made in the first moments after opening could leave out an entry that has not yet arrived. The shared page draws "Tips" and then "Notes" near the bottom (`EntryList` in `SharedRecipeBody`: after the steps and how to keep it, before the links), each row with its step number when it has one, the notes under the caption "Notes stay on this page when you add the recipe." (the add brings tips only, and the owner's "Your notes" hint says visitors can read them but don't get a copy); the Function refuses a copy over `MAX_COPY_BYTES` (900,000, under Firestore's 1 MiB document: 10 recipes of 30 tips and 30 notes of 2,000 characters would pass it) with `invalid-argument` "This is too much to share in one link. Share fewer tips and notes, or fewer recipes at a time."; the allowlist, caps, the include logic and the hostile-input cases are in the three files `check:pinch-share` runs.
- **The import (`utils/importShare.ts`, `store/actions/importShareActions.ts`).** `planRecipeImport` and `planMealImport` map each shared line to the person's own ingredient: a `cat-` id the catalog has is kept; else `matchIngredient` over the catalog and the person's own non-archived ingredients by name; else one new custom `IngredientRecord` per distinct name (`withoutFiller` is the key) across every line, recipe and ingredient item of the share. Group, note, amount and unit are kept (a line with no amount keeps `amount: null` and `EACH`). Every document is rebuilt from an allowlist with the form's own caps (`cleanLinks`, `toCare`, `cleanGroupName`, the `MAX_*` lengths), with every key present, `source: 'MANUAL'`, and `sourceId`, `collectionIds`, `categories`, `isFavorite`, `nutritionOverride`, `coverPath` and `coverUrl` empty: nothing of the sharer's (ids, prices, notes, a cover address) is carried, except the tips (a share's `notes` list is never imported, it stays on the page): each `{ text, step }` becomes a TIP document of the new recipe (`toTips`: a fresh id, `cookId` and `reason` null, the step anchored with `getStepAnchor` against the imported steps, a step the recipe lacks read as the whole recipe), written in the same batch, at most `MAX_IMPORTED_TIPS` (150) across the whole add with the first recipes' tips kept whole so a meal of ten recipes stays under 500 writes (`MAX_IMPORT_WRITES` is 461 and the too-big refusal still counts only recipes, ingredients and the meal). Undo deletes exactly the tips it wrote (`written.tips`) with their recipes; a note the person adds to the new recipe in the seconds before an Undo is not removed with it (nothing reads a note whose recipe is gone). A meal writes each recipe once however many items name it, and each item points at what was written. The person's own ingredients are read once from the server (`getDocsFromServer`; a cache that is behind would make an ingredient they own look new). The write is one `writeBatch` of at most `MAX_IMPORT_WRITES` (311: ten recipes, 300 lines that each made an ingredient, one meal); a plan over that is refused with "This is too big to add in one go." and nothing is written. After the commit, for each recipe whose share has a cover, `copyPinchShareCover` is called, its answer accepted only when the path is exactly the visitor's own `getRecipeCoverPath`, and a field-scoped `updateDoc` writes `coverPath` and `updatedAt`; a failure removes any file and never fails the add ("The photo didn't come along."). A check (`pinchShareImport.ts`) compares the planned documents' keys with the `hasOnly` and `hasAll` lists parsed from `firestore.rules`, since the rules would otherwise be the first to say a key is missing.
- **Undo is all or none.** `undoSharedImport` reads from the server the person's recipes, meals and ingredients and, in chunks of 30, the planned meals, products and containers that point at what was written, then `planUndo` refuses everything ("You've changed or used it since, so Undo left it alone.") when a written recipe or the meal was edited since (its `updatedAt` differs from what the add left, the cover copy's own stamp included), an imported tip was edited (`WrittenTip.updatedAt` against the server's `UndoSurvey.notes`), any other note now exists on a written recipe (deleting the recipe would orphan it), or a written recipe is on a planned day, in a box or in another saved meal. Otherwise one batch deletes exactly the recipes and the meal that were written, plus the ingredients the add made that are still unchanged and unused (the others stay, counted in the line "Taken back. One ingredient you've used since stayed."), and then the copied cover files.
- **Races it does not close.** An ingredient with the same name made on another device between the add's read and its commit is duplicated. A recipe edited between the Undo's read and its batch is deleted anyway (the read spans several collections and chunked queries, so it isn't one transaction). Coming back to a link after adding offers Add again, which is a second copy: no record of the add is kept, because the id stays out of storage. The page keeps its answer for five minutes and doesn't refetch on focus, so a stopped link keeps showing until reload (it says it is a snapshot). Two creates for one new source at the same instant made two links in a run (the duplicate-source window above is real); a sequential double create makes one.
- **Checks.** `npm run check:pinch-share` runs `scripts/checks/pinchShare.ts` (the id alphabet and its draw, the allowlist at its edges, the caps against the function's copy, the owner flag, both Firestore blocks read as text and the id pattern read from `storage.rules`; 30 cases), `pinchShareBuild.ts` (the client's builders, the stamp and the status; 37) and `pinchShareImport.ts` (the mapping, the caps, the planned keys against the rules' lists, the Undo plans; 134), in CI beside `check:pinch-voice`. Eight edits to the allowlist (no list cap, no text cap, no line cap, `undefined === undefined` as the owner, the uid in the answer, `http` links, a client-claimed cover, a 31-character alphabet) each fail a named case. The rules are Security criteria 20 and 21.
- **Verified on the emulators and in a browser (phase 2).** *The server, 305 requests and assertions against the Emulator Suite as Alex (the owner), Jamie, a third person, the admin account and signed out, none failing:* the owner reads their own share and queries it by `ownerUid` (200); another user, a signed-out client and the admin account get 403 on it, and a query for someone else's or unfiltered gets 403; nobody writes a share or a snapshot (create, update, delete, each as owner, another user and signed out: 403) and nobody reads a snapshot (403, owner and admin included); a signed-out `get` of a cover answers 200 (metadata, media and with the download token) while a name outside `cover-N.webp`, a three-digit index, a short or an upper-case id answer 403, a `list` of `apps/pinch/shares/`, of one folder, of `apps/pinch/` and of the root answers 403 as signed out, Alex and Jamie, and an upload, an overwrite and a delete answer 403; the kitchen rules answer as before (owner 200, another user and signed out 403). *Against the base rules:* the branch's `firestore.rules` and the base file (`cbefb3da`) were each run through 34 probes (kitchen document, recipe, meal, a list, a settings path, an A-List share, denied writes, every share and snapshot read, query and write), then the branch's rules again: **three outcomes differ, all the intended one (the owner reads their own share: 403 on the base, 200 on the branch)** and every write is the same (the Storage half was not run against the base rules). *The callables:* a response of `getPinchShare` as the owner, another user and signed out holds none of the forbidden strings (the owner's uid, the source id outside the owner's answer, prices, notes) and exactly the allowlisted keys; **20 bodies that find no share (malformed, wrong case, a path, a missing or numeric id, unknown, deleted, no snapshot, a snapshot of the other kind, a snapshot that fails the allowlist, each signed out and as the owner) are byte-identical** (`200 {"result":{"status":"not_found"}}`; malformed 4 ms median, the others 12 to 15 ms); `changePinchShare` refusals (21 cases), create with a cover (the copy has its own token, the owner's original keeps its own), a second create returning the same id, the allowlist on a hostile input, update, ownership (another user gets `not-found` for update and renew and `{ shareId: null }` for delete, the same bodies as for an unknown id, and nothing of the owner's changes), renew (the old link, documents, folder and cover address dead, the new one live under a new token), delete (idempotent), a saved meal with two covers (update with one deletes the other, renew carries it), the cap (30 created, the 31st refused with the cap message, an existing source retaken at 30, one stopped then one allowed and the next refused); `copyPinchShareCover` (the file lands under the visitor's own path with its own token, is private to them, writes no recipe document, answers `{ coverPath: null }` for a recipe that has a cover or a web cover, one that doesn't exist, someone else's recipe id, a share without a cover at that index, an unknown or malformed share, and refuses a bad index or recipe id). The functions log holds no full id, title, uid or source id. *In a browser, as the owner:* create, copy (the clipboard equals the address), edit then update (the changed-since card and the filled Update appear, and clear), a new link (the old address answers not found), stop (record, snapshot and files gone), a saved meal (each recipe once, the star leaves `updatedAt` alone, a recipe changed inside it shows the card), a legacy-shaped recipe, the error mapping with the server's answers forced (429, 404, 503, 500: the server's own words never show), offline, a double click making one record, the cap in the drawer and in the callable, the list at 7, 8, 13, 16 and 30 rows (the search from 8, "Show N more" only from four hidden), delete and archive confirms, an original gone, one layer per press over More, the Share view, a sheet and a subview, and the plain-HTTP copy fallback (with `isSecureContext` forced false). The Share view's body never scrolls at 390×844 (621 of 621 px); at 375×667 it scrolls a little for a changed link, and on the short screens by no more than the drawer's own bottom padding, the primary pinned throughout. *After the design pass's share fixes* (same emulators, browser, both themes): a malformed, an unknown and a really stopped link give one screenshot and one DOM at 390×844 and 320×568, "Open Pinch" lands on `/pinch` from each terminal screen, and "Try again" with the callable aborted once recovered the page in a second call; the page's action card stayed 358×154 for the owner, a signed-in visitor and a signed-out one; the Share view at 844×390, 390×560, 320×568 and 390×844 shows 3 full action rows above the footer when shared and 2 when changed since (it showed 1 and 0 at 844×390); with Jamie at exactly 30 links the cap chip (163×40, hit-tested) closed the drawer, opened Shared links, and the header Back and the browser back each returned to the Recipes list in one press, while Settings' own door still returns to Settings. *After round 7's share fixes:* a changed link at 568×320 and 639×390 shows 2 full action rows above the footer (0 before), the first row's top 328 to 144px, with the floor at 520px (two columns at 520, one at 519); the cap view's body fits at every size (301 of 301 px at 320×568, where 394 of 313 clipped the "Left out" line; 313 of 313 at 390×560; 191 of 191 at 844×390; 525 of 525 at 390×844). *In a browser, on the page,* six viewports, both themes, signed out, as Jamie and as Alex (72 loads): the card is the same 154px box in every state (its top edge at 39%, 50%, 58%, 59% and 62% of a 390×844, 375×667, 320×568, 390×560 and 844×390 screen, and 27% at 1280×900), no horizontal scroll, nothing clipped or under 40px in the page, nothing underlined, one left edge per layout; sign-in flips the slot in place (same document, same scroll, a skeleton of the card for about 130 ms); a recipe add as Jamie (one recipe on a double tap, all 20 keys, the cover copied to his own path and accepted by the rules), an Undo that removes the recipe and its cover, an Undo refused after an edit, a saved meal and its two recipes added and taken back, offline refused inside the card, a brand-new account with no kitchen documents, a custom ingredient made on one add and matched on the next, a share of an added recipe (its cover served to a signed-out visitor, stopping deleted the folder), and a stopped, a mistyped and a malformed id showing the same screen to the pixel; a 110-character title, an 810-character description and a long ingredient note at 320×568 without clipping. The 401 path was run by serving real 401 bodies from the browser (one 401 is invisible, two show the failed state, Try again succeeds).
- **Not verified.** A real `navigator.share` sheet (stubbed, so only that Send appears beside Copy link); a real plain-HTTP origin (the fallback ran with `isSecureContext` forced false); the "couldn't load your shared links" message when the listener fails (never provoked); a real Storage failure inside a cover copy (so the log line's format is untested); Undo refusing for a recipe that was planned or boxed (the check proves the plan; the browser proof was an edited recipe); two creates racing the 30 cap (the cap itself held exactly; two simultaneous creates for one new source did give two ids, so that window is real); a real 401 from production (the emulator accepts any bearer, so the answer was served by the browser); the Storage rules under the base rules; a real Google sign-in popup (the page was signed in through the app's own Auth module); the Cloudflare worker's `X-Robots-Tag`, `Referrer-Policy` and link preview (the document's meta tags were checked, the worker was not run); production's invoker grant for the new `onCall` functions (`functions/README.md`); a photograph as a cover (the seeded cover is a flat colour); and, in an Emulator Suite started without `STORAGE_BUCKET_URL`, covers, which the Functions can't reach there (see Covers).

### 6. Stock tracking (what stays open)

The fridge and freezer list exists (§5g, decision 23) and so do pantry packages (§5i, decision 25), which hold what the person says they have. What stays open is "not tracked" against "out" for an ingredient nobody added a package for (today both read as not in the pantry, and only as a recipe's minority mark), and `plannedMeals.madeAt` for mark-as-made, which would let a cook from the plan take ingredients out as cooking mode's Finish panel now does. Neither is written. Both arrive as new optional-by-default fields (`null`) with the legacy-document defaults the repo already requires.

---

## Security Rules Design Criteria

1. **Everything in a kitchen is owner-only.** Every path under `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}` (the kitchen document and all fourteen subcollections) allows read and write only when `request.auth.uid == uid`. There is no member-of, admin or role branch. The two share collections (criterion 20) are the only other documents under `apps/pinch` that a person's account touches, and they hold a copy the Functions made, never the kitchen itself; no rule makes any kitchen path readable by a link.
2. **`isAdmin()` and `isDevUser()` get no access to kitchen data.** They gate only the app *catalog* document `apps/pinch`, as for every other app. A dev fixture signs in as its own `uid` like anyone else.
3. **The app document** `apps/pinch` follows the existing four-read-predicate shape (admin/dev, unrestricted-public, uid-allowed, email-allowed) with admin-only writes, in alphabetical position among the app blocks.
4. **Shape validation covers every field on every type** (a recipe's lines are the exception, criterion 14), with the `T | null` convention (the key must exist; `null` is legal only where the type says so). Enums use `in [...]` against the same strings as `constants.ts`.
