Bounds: `priceCents` is an int `0 … 100_000_000`; `sizeAmount`, `amount` and `servings` are numbers `> 0` and `≤ 1_000_000`; nutrition values are null or numbers `0 … 100_000`; `gramsPerMl` and `gramsEach` are null or `> 0`; `date` is an int and a multiple of `86_400_000`; strings have length caps (names 120, titles 200, steps 2,000, notes 200, a source's label and a recipe's `sourceId` 80); arrays are capped at `MAX_RECIPE_INGREDIENTS`, `MAX_RECIPE_STEPS`, `MAX_MEAL_ITEMS` and `refIds` (rules can't loop, so a recipe's ingredient lines and steps are the client's to enforce, while a meal's at most 10 items are each checked by index: keys, kind, string ids and `servings > 0`; adding length caps on those ids or checking the elements of `refIds` exceeds the evaluator's budget, so those stay the client's); `url` is null or an `https://` string; `dismissedNudges` has at most 500 keys. A shopping item (`isShoppingItem`) has its seven required keys and one optional one (`sources`, a list of at most 60 entries read with `get('sources', [])`: what put the line on the list), `ingredientId` of 1 to 80 characters, an `amount` that is null only when the `unit` is `EACH` (a line with only a name is always counted in EACH) or a number above zero and at most 1,000,000, a known `unit`, a boolean `isChecked`, integer timestamps, and its document id equal to both `data.id` and `ingredientId + '__' + unit`. An idea (`isIdea`) has exactly the eleven keys of the type, every one required (there is no older shape), `kind` one of `LINK`, `PHOTO`, `TEXT`, a `title` of 1 to 120 characters, a `note` of 1 to 1,000 or null, a `url` of at most 2,000 characters that starts `http://` or `https://` (lower case, so `JavaScript:` and `//x` fail) and has no user name or password before the host, or is null, a `siteName` of 1 to 120 or null, an `imageUrl` of at most 2,000 characters that starts `https://` with no user name or password, or is null, a `photoPath` that is null or exactly `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/ideas/{id}/photo.webp`, a `recipeId` of 1 to 80 characters or null, integer timestamps, and its document id equal to `data.id`; that `kind` fits the other fields (a link has a `url`) is the client's. A cook's `servings` is a number `> 0` and `≤ 1,000`, its `cookedAt` an int at most a day (`86_400_000` ms) after `request.time` (clock skew, so a moment that has not happened can't be recorded), `recipeId` and a set `plannedMealId` are 1 to 80 characters, and a set `plannedMealId` means the id is `plannedMealId + '__' + recipeId`. A recipe's `care` (`isRecipeCare`) is null or a map with exactly its four keys, `fridgeDays` and `freezerDays` null or ints from 1 to 365 and `reheat` and `tip` null or 1 to 300 characters. A note (`isRecipeNote`) has exactly its eight keys, every one required (there is no older shape), `kind` `NOTE` or `TWEAK` (a NOTE's `text` must be a string), `text` null or 1 to 2,000 characters, `recipeId` 1 to 80, `cookId` null or 1 to 80, integer timestamps, its document id equal to `data.id`, and `changes` a list of at most 8 strings of 1 to 200 characters, empty for a NOTE; rules can't loop, so each of the eight is checked by index (`isNoteChangeAt`), like a meal's items.
5. **Immutable fields only:** `id`, `uid`, `createdAt` (a source's and an idea's too), an idea's `kind`, an ingredient record's `catalogId`, a store's `catalogStoreId`, a product's `ingredientId`, a shopping item's `ingredientId` and `unit` (its id is built from them), a note's `recipeId`, `kind`, `cookId`, `changes` and `createdAt` (a tweak's record of what changed is history while its `text` stays editable; the stored `cookId` and `changes` are read with `.get(key, null)` and `.get(key, [])`, so a document made without them can still be edited with the backfill), a cook's `recipeId` and `plannedMealId` (a planned meal's cook has its id built from them; `plannedMealId` is compared with `resource.data.get('plannedMealId', null)`, so an older document lacking the key can still be edited), a container's `recipeId`, `mealId` and `cookId` (what it was made from never changes; the collection is new, so they are compared directly). Everything the UI edits stays editable, including a planned meal's `date`, `mealType` and `items`.

6. **A snapshot is written once and replaced only by a marked re-price.** A planned meal's `snapshot` may be written while the stored value is `null`; after that an update must leave it equal, or be a re-price (`isRepricedFrom`): the new snapshot carries an integer `repricedAt`, keeps the stored `frozenAt`, and the rest of the meal (`date`, `mealType`, `name`, `sourceMealId`, `items`, `refIds`) is unchanged, so its id and anything that points at it stay (a re-price that also changes any of those, or that writes the snapshot back without an instant `repricedAt`, as the freeze does, is refused). Only the owner can update, as for everything under the kitchen, so access for another signed-in user and a signed-out visitor is unchanged. `frozenAt` and `repricedAt` must each be `≤ request.time.toMillis()` plus five minutes of clock skew, so a phone whose clock runs a little fast can still freeze. The shape is checked (the five original keys required, `repricedAt` allowed and, being new, optional on a stored snapshot: `value.get('repricedAt', null)` is null or an int; bounded totals, at most 10 lines); the elements of `leftOut` and `lines` are the client's, since rules can't loop. (Rule check: `resource.data.get('snapshot', null) == null || request.resource.data.snapshot == resource.data.snapshot || isRepricedFrom(resource.data, request.resource.data)`.) The client writes `repricedAt: null` in every snapshot it freezes, so a rules deploy has to reach the backend before a client that freezes is exercised against it.
7. **Cross-field integrity the client could get wrong:** a planned meal's `refIds` equal the distinct `refId`s of its `items` is **not** enforced in rules (it's a derived index the client writes; a wrong value only affects the "in use" check, and the owner is the only writer), and the rules say so. A recipe has at least one ingredient; a meal and a planned meal have at least one item; `defaultProductId` shape is a string or null.
8. **Deletes:** the owner may delete any document in their kitchen, including the kitchen document. The client archives anything in use (Logic §3a), and the rules deliberately don't, since the data is the owner's own and a stale client must not be locked out of cleaning up.
9. **No cross-document rules.** No `get()`/`exists()`: a product doesn't have to point at an existing ingredient or store at the rules level, and a recipe's `sourceId` doesn't have to name an existing source and an idea's `recipeId` doesn't have to name an existing recipe and neither does a cook's (nor its `plannedMealId` an existing planned meal) or a note's `recipeId` (nor its `cookId` an existing cook); the client resolves a dangling reference to "Removed …" (a source to none, an idea's recipe to "not made yet").
10. **Rules tolerate older documents.** A field added later is validated on the incoming document with `request.resource.data.get('field', default)` and compared against the stored value with `resource.data.get('field', default)`; every edit action backfills the new key with its empty value in the same write. A recipe's `sourceId` is the example in use: `isRecipe` lists it in `hasOnly` but not `hasAll`, reads it with `data.get('sourceId', null)` and accepts it missing, `null` or a string of 1 to 80 characters. `care` is the second: listed in `hasOnly`, not in `hasAll`, read with `data.get('care', null)`, and written by every save of the recipe, so an older recipe gains the key. An idea has no older shape: the collection is new, `isIdea` requires all eleven keys, and `createIdea`, `updateIdea` and the recipe link still write back any key a document lacks (so a document made outside the app can be edited through the client). (Reserved, not yet written: `plannedMeals.madeAt`, the `stock` collection.)
11. **Four server-written collections (criteria 19 and 20); three indexes.** (The two photo-search collections allow no client read or write; `shares` is read by its owner only and `shareSnapshots` by nobody, and the share callables run equality-only queries, so the shares add no index.) (Containers, criterion 13, add the Finished view's `status` + `updatedAt` index below; the morning note, criterion 16 and §5h, adds `status` + `eatBy` on `containers`, which a per-person query with an equality and a range needs; and the pantry's expiring note, criterion 17, adds `status` + `expiresAt` on `packages`, the same kind of query.) The only queries are a single-field range on `plannedMeals.date` and an `array-contains` on `plannedMeals.refIds`, neither of which needs a composite index. The sources, the shopping items, the ideas, the cooks and the notes are listened to as plain collections (the lists are grouped and sorted in the client), so they add no query and no index (a cook is read with one plain collection listener, and a recipe's cooks and notes are found in the loaded store, not by a `where`); `firestore.indexes.json` is unchanged by everything before containers. A source and a shopping item have no file, so only an idea adds to the Storage rules (criterion 12).

12. **Storage rules.** `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/recipes/{recipeId}/{file}` and `…/stores/{storeId}/{file}` are readable and writable only by `request.auth.uid == uid`, only `image/*` content types, at most 5 MB, and the file name is `cover.webp` or `logo.webp`. Storage paths mirror the Firestore document paths, except that a share's folder is public by its path (criterion 21). A recipe idea's photo is `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/ideas/{ideaId}/photo.webp`, the cover's rule over again: owner only, `image/*`, under 5 MB, and the file name must be `photo.webp`; a PHOTO idea's `photoPath` is checked against that path in `firestore.rules`.

13. **Containers (`isContainer`).** Exactly its twelve keys (`data.size() == 12` and `hasOnly`, so a missing or extra key is refused), `id` equal to the path, `title` 1 to 120 characters, `recipeId`, `mealId` and `cookId` null or 1 to 80 characters with `recipeId` and `mealId` never both set, `servings` an int 1 to 100, `status` in `['IN_STOCK', 'DONE']` (a create must be `IN_STOCK`), `place` in `['FRIDGE', 'FREEZER']`, `eatBy` an int that is a multiple of 86,400,000 (UTC midnight) from 946,684,800,000 to 4,102,444,800,000 (2000-01-01 to 2100-01-01), integer timestamps, and `history` a list of 1 to 10 events. **The history is the part that is cheap on purpose.** Rules can't loop and the evaluator stops at 1,000 expressions per request (measured on the emulator, which enforces it: a first draft that shaped each event with `hasOnly`/`hasAll`/`in [...]` failed at 5 events), so each event is checked by index (`n <= i || isContainerEvent(history[i])`, with `let n = history.size()`) with the leanest shape that still holds: `size() == 5`, `kind.matches('MADE|FRIDGE|FREEZE|THAW|EAT|TOSS|EDIT')` (a whole-string match), `at is int`, `servings` null or an int 1 to 100, `place` null or `matches('FRIDGE|FREEZER')`, `note` null or a string of at most 80. Ten events with servings and place on every one (the busiest history the app writes) pass, on create and on update, in the rules matrix, so the cap of 10 is set by this budget and not only by the length checks; an event with servings, place and a note all set at once, which no action writes, can fail at ten (it is denied, not allowed). An update keeps `id` in the path and compares `recipeId`, `mealId`, `cookId` and `createdAt` with the stored ones; everything else the actions change (`servings`, `status`, `place`, `eatBy`, `history`, `updatedAt`, and `title`, which no screen edits yet) stays editable, a reopen (`DONE` back to `IN_STOCK`) is allowed, and nothing locks a history to appends only (the owner is the only writer and the client's plans are the only code that writes it). A key added later raises that count in the same PR and is read with `get(key, default)`, so a document written without it still passes. A put-away of several containers is one `writeBatch` of ordinary creates, each checked by this same rule (a batch of 20, each with a two-event history, was accepted by the emulator with these rules), so batches needed no rule change; a cook's MADE event carries a whole number from 1 to 100 (the client rounds and clamps it), and a container added by hand has a one-event history. The one index is `containers (status ASC, updatedAt DESC)`, for the Finished view; the stock listener is a lone equality and `where('recipeId', '==', id)` is one equality, so neither needs one.

14. **Recipe lines are client-shaped, the group included.** `isRecipe` caps the list at 60 lines and checks the fields around it, never a line's own fields: measured on the emulator, even the cheapest check of one field per line by index (`lines.size() <= i || lines[i].get('group', '') is string`) runs past the 1,000-expression budget at 60 lines and refuses the recipe ("Unable to evaluate the expression as the maximum of 1000 expressions to evaluate has been reached"; a `matches` check of null or 1 to 40 characters failed at 40 lines, a helper call per line at 30), with or without a create or update precondition. So a line's `group` (like its `amount`, `unit` and `note`) is shaped by the client, the 40-character cap is the client's, and the rules matrix proves only that a recipe with groups, one with `group: null` and a legacy recipe with no key are all read, created and edited by the owner and refused to another signed-in user and a signed-out client, exactly as with the rules before groups.

15. **Collections and favorites (`isCollection`, a recipe's `collectionIds`, a meal's `isFavorite`).** A collection has exactly its four keys (`hasOnly` and `hasAll`: a brand-new collection has no legacy shape, so a client that omits a key is refused), `id` equal to the path, `label` 1 to 40 characters, `createdAt` and `updatedAt` ints, `createdAt` unchanged on update; the 50-per-person cap is the client's (a rule can't count documents) and says so in §5a.2. A recipe lists `collectionIds` in `isRecipe`'s `hasOnly` but not its `hasAll`, and reads it with `data.get('collectionIds', [])` (`isCollectionIds`: a list of at most 20; the client shapes the ids, since a rule can't walk a list), so a recipe written before collections still validates and updates, one that sets the key to `null` or a string is refused, and 21 ids are refused. A saved meal lists `isFavorite` in `isMeal`'s `hasOnly` but not its `hasAll` and checks `data.get('isFavorite', false) is bool`, so older meals validate, rename and star; a recipe does the same in `isRecipe` (round 8), so a recipe with no key validates, edits and stars, a non-boolean is refused, and only the owner's star and a create carrying `isFavorite: false` are new allows (other users and a signed-out visitor are denied either way). No rule says a `collectionIds` entry names an existing collection (criterion 9: no `get()`); readers ignore an id nothing names. Verified on the emulator as the owner, another user and a signed-out visitor, with the previous rules and these (45 cases, same outcomes everywhere except the intended new allows), including a legacy-shaped recipe and meal.

The shape of the block (helpers declared in the `kitchens/{uid}` match so the nested matches reuse them):

```
// --- App: pinch ---
match /apps/pinch {
  allow read: if isAdmin() || isDevUser();
  allow read: if isUnrestrictedPublicApp(resource.data);
  allow read: if isUidAllowedApp(resource.data);
  allow read: if isEmailAllowedApp(resource.data);
  allow create, update, delete: if isAdmin();
}

match /apps/pinch/shares/{shareId}         { allow read: if request.auth != null && resource.data.ownerUid == request.auth.uid;
                                             allow write: if false; }
match /apps/pinch/shareSnapshots/{shareId} { allow read, write: if false; }

match /apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid} {
  function isOwner() { return request.auth != null && request.auth.uid == uid; }
  function isCents(v) { return v is int && v >= 0 && v <= 100000000; }
  function isPositive(v) { return v is number && v > 0 && v <= 1000000; }
  function isNullOrNutrition(v) { return v == null || (v is number && v >= 0 && v <= 100000); }
  function isUnit(v) { return v in ['G','KG','OZ','LB','ML','L','TSP','TBSP','CUP','FL_OZ','PINCH','EACH']; }
  function isMealType(v) { return v in ['BREAKFAST','LUNCH','DINNER','SNACK','DESSERT','DRINK']; }

  allow read: if isOwner();
  allow create, update: if isOwner() && validSettings(request.resource.data);
  allow delete: if isOwner();

  match /ingredients/{id}  { allow read: if isOwner(); allow create, update: if isOwner() && validIngredient(request.resource.data, id); allow delete: if isOwner(); }
  match /families/{id}     { /* same shape */ }
  match /stores/{id}       { /* same shape */ }
  match /products/{id}     { /* same shape */ }
  match /recipes/{id}      { /* same shape */ }
  match /sources/{id}      { /* same shape; id, label 1…80, createdAt, updatedAt */ }
  match /collections/{id}  { /* same shape; id, label 1…40, createdAt, updatedAt; every key required */ }
  match /shoppingItems/{id} { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isShoppingItem(request.resource.data, id);
                              allow update: if isOwner() && isShoppingItem(request.resource.data, id) && ingredientId, unit and createdAt unchanged; }
  match /ideas/{id}        { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isIdea(request.resource.data, id);
                             allow update: if isOwner() && isIdea(request.resource.data, id) && createdAt unchanged; }
  match /cooks/{id}        { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isCook(request.resource.data, id);
                             allow update: if isOwner() && isCook(request.resource.data, id) && recipeId, plannedMealId and createdAt unchanged; }
  match /notes/{id}        { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isRecipeNote(request.resource.data, id);
                             allow update: if isOwner() && isRecipeNote(request.resource.data, id) && recipeId, kind, cookId, changes and createdAt unchanged; }
  match /containers/{id}   { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isContainer(request.resource.data, id) && status == 'IN_STOCK';
                             allow update: if isOwner() && isContainer(request.resource.data, id) && recipeId, mealId, cookId and createdAt unchanged; }
  match /packages/{id}     { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isPackage(request.resource.data, id) && status == 'IN_STOCK';
                             allow update: if isOwner() && isPackage(request.resource.data, id) && ingredientId and createdAt unchanged; }
  match /meals/{id}        { /* same shape */ }
  match /plannedMeals/{id} { /* same shape, plus the snapshot rule */ }
}
```

---

16. **Settings (`isPinchSettings`).** `apps/pinch/settings/{uid}` allows read only when `request.auth.uid == uid` (a client never deletes it: a delete and re-create would reset `lastDigestDay`); create needs all five keys (`fridgeDays`, `freezerDays`, `reminders`, `updatedAt`, `lastDigestDay`) with `lastDigestDay == null`; `hasOnly` the five. `fridgeDays` is null or an integer 1 to 14, `freezerDays` null or an integer 7 to 365, `reminders` a map of exactly `eatSoon` and `timers` (bools), `leadDays` (integer 1 to 3), `hour` (integer 0 to 23) and `timeZone` (null or a string of 1 to 64 characters), `updatedAt` an integer. An update validates the whole resulting document with the same function, reads `lastDigestDay` with `get(…, null)` (a document written without it can still be updated) and refuses any change to it: only the scheduled function, which bypasses the rules, stamps it. A kind added later extends `hasOnly` on `reminders` and is read with `get(key, default)` until every document has it. A new collection under `apps/pinch` matches nothing in the older rules, so the base rules deny it; the client's first write against them is refused, so deploy the rules before the client.

17. **Packages (`isPackage`).** Exactly its thirteen keys (`data.size() == 13` and `hasOnly`), `id` equal to the path, `ingredientId` 1 to 80 characters and `ingredientName` 1 to 120, `productId` null or 1 to 80, `amount` a number above 0 and at most 1,000,000, `unit` one of the twelve, `place` and `status` in their lists, `expiresAt` null or an int that is a whole multiple of 86,400,000 between 2000-01-01 and 2100-01-01 (a date-only value that is not at UTC midnight is refused), `openedAt` null or an int, `history` a list of 1 to 10 events each with exactly five keys (`kind` in the five kinds, `at` an int, `amount` null or a positive amount, `place` null or a place, `note` null or at most 80 characters), `createdAt` and `updatedAt` ints. Create requires `status == 'IN_STOCK'`; update keeps `ingredientId` and `createdAt`. **The history cap is the evaluator's budget, not taste:** like a box's (criterion 13), each event is checked by index, and measured on the emulator ten events with an amount, a place and an 80-character note on every one are refused as too expensive, while ten as the client writes them (an ADDED with amount and place, then nine of the heaviest event the client writes, a MOVED with a place and an 80-character note, or nine USED or EDITED) are allowed, and ten with amount and place (or amount and note), or nine with all three, are refused; the note beside the cap in `firestore.rules` says so, and `check:pinch-math` (`pinchPantry.ts`) fails if a planner writes an event with all three, a second one with amount and place, or a note past 80 characters, so a heavier event or a higher cap means re-measuring on the emulator. Kept deliberately cheap: no `get()`, no cross-document rule (an ingredient that is gone doesn't strand its packages), and the soft cap of 500 packages is the client's, since rules can't count documents. The reminder settings gain one key: `isReminders` allows `expiring` (`hasOnly` lists it, `hasAll` doesn't require it, and `value.get('expiring', false) is bool`), so a settings document written before it still validates and a dotted-path `updateDoc` of `reminders.expiring` is allowed. Verified against the emulator as the owner, another signed-in user and a signed-out visitor, with the base rules beside the branch's: 72 cases each, 52 identical and 20 that differ, all of them the new collection and the new key (base denies, the branch allows the owner), and every foreign and signed-out case denied in both.

18. **Recipe covers (`isRecipe`).** `coverUrl` joins `hasOnly` but not `hasAll`, is read with `get('coverUrl', null)` and must be null or a string of at most 2,000 characters that matches `https://` and a host with no `@`, `/`, `?`, `#` or space, then an optional path, query or fragment with no space. It can't be set while `coverPath` is: both set is denied, and an edit that swaps one for the other writes both in one update (a stale client that uploads a photo over a web cover is denied until it reloads). A recipe written before the key exists is still readable, editable and savable. Verified on the emulator as the owner, another user and signed out against the base rules (`scratchpad` proof, 74 cases): only the intended writes change from denied to allowed (create, update, remove and swap of a web cover; the null key); http, `javascript:`, `data:`, credentials, whitespace, a non-string, an over-length address and both covers stay denied, and nothing narrowed.

19. **Photo search collections and the settings key.** `apps/pinch/imageSearchCache/{key}` and `apps/pinch/imageSearchUsage/{key}` allow no client read or write (`allow read, write: if false`): the function uses the Admin SDK, so no client reads another person's search or resets its own counter. `isPinchSettings` gains `suggestPhotos` in `hasOnly` (not `hasAll`) and requires `get('suggestPhotos', true) is bool`; a settings document written before it still reads and updates.

20. **Shares (`shares` and `shareSnapshots`).** Both are flat collections beside `kitchens`, addressed by an unguessable id, so the owner check that criterion 1 makes with the path is made on the body: `shares/{shareId}` allows `read` only when `request.auth != null && resource.data.ownerUid == request.auth.uid` (the owner's listener is `where('ownerUid', '==', uid)`, one equality, so the rule is list-safe and needs no index) and `write: if false`; `shareSnapshots/{shareId}` allows nothing (`read, write: if false`). No client creates, updates or deletes either: `changePinchShare` writes both with the Admin SDK, which bypasses the rules, so there is no shape check, immutable-field list or `hasAll` in the rules for them. The copy's shape is enforced by the allowlist in the Functions (criterion 4's counterpart for a document no client writes), and `scripts/checks/pinchShare.ts` reads both blocks as text so a loosened rule fails a check. A visitor reads neither: they get the copy only through `getPinchShare`, which answers one not-found for a malformed, unknown or deleted id. No wildcard rule exists under `apps/pinch`, so no older rule changes the outcome for these paths; against rules that predate them the owner's listener is denied, which is the optional-listener path (§5k), so deploy the rules before the client. The 30-link cap and one-share-per-source are the callable's, not the rules' (a rule can't count documents), and say so in §5k.

21. **Storage: a shared cover.** `apps/pinch/shares/{shareId}/{fileName}` allows `get` to anyone, signed in or not, when `shareId` matches `^[2-9a-hj-np-z]{26}$` and `fileName` matches `^cover-[0-9]{1,2}\.webp$`, and `list` and `write` to no one: the address, with the file's own download token, is the capability; nobody can list `apps/pinch/shares/` or one share's folder, and no client can upload, replace or delete a cover, so only the Functions write it. The rule does not check that the share exists (no `firestore.get`): the Function deletes the folder with the share, so a stale path finds no file. This is the one Storage path under `apps/pinch` that is readable without sign-in and the only one that isn't owner-only; the kitchen paths in criterion 12 are unchanged and a share's path does not overlap them.

## Client State Management (Redux Toolkit)

Per-app store under `src/apps/pinch/store/`, exposing `PinchState` and `pinchReducer`, mounted in the central `src/store/index.ts` as `pinch` (next to `aList`, `nineLives` and `waypoint`).

```typescript
interface SettingsState       { settings: KitchenSettings | null; isLoaded: boolean }
interface IngredientsState    { records: IngredientRecord[];      isLoaded: boolean }
interface FamiliesState       { items: Family[];                  isLoaded: boolean }
interface StoresState         { items: Store[];                   isLoaded: boolean }
interface ProductsState       { items: Product[];                 isLoaded: boolean }
interface RecipesState        { items: Recipe[];                  isLoaded: boolean }
interface SourcesState        { items: Source[];                  isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface ShoppingState       { items: ShoppingItem[];            isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface IdeasState          { items: Idea[];                    isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface CooksState          { items: Cook[];                    isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface NotesState          { items: RecipeNote[];              isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface ContainersState     { items: Container[];               isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }   // only what is in stock
interface CollectionsState    { items: Collection[];              isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }   // an optional extra, like sources
interface PackagesState       { items: Package[];                 isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }   // only what is in stock
interface SharesState         { items: PinchShare[];              isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }   // the person's own links; an optional extra, like cooks
interface MealsState          { items: Meal[];                    isLoaded: boolean }
interface PlannedMealsState   { items: PlannedMeal[]; windowKey: string | null; isLoaded: boolean }
export interface PinchState { settings: SettingsState; ingredients: IngredientsState; families: FamiliesState;
  stores: StoresState; products: ProductsState; recipes: RecipesState; sources: SourcesState; collections: CollectionsState; shopping: ShoppingState; ideas: IdeasState; cooks: CooksState; notes: NotesState; containers: ContainersState; packages: PackagesState; shares: SharesState; meals: MealsState; plannedMeals: PlannedMealsState }
```

Every slice resets on the shared `resetAllState`, so a user switch never shows the previous person's data.

**Two listener tiers**, both under `store/listeners/` and started from `usePinchSync(uid, shownMonth)` called once from `Pinch.tsx` (the only orchestrator); no tab, panel or drawer has an `onSnapshot` of its own:

1. **Signed-in user:** `startSettingsListener`, `startIngredientsListener`, `startFamiliesListener`, `startStoresListener`, `startProductsListener`, `startRecipesListener`, `startSourcesListener`, `startCollectionsListener`, `startShoppingListener`, `startIdeasListener`, `startCooksListener`, `startNotesListener`, `startContainersListener` (`where('status', '==', 'IN_STOCK')`), `startPackagesListener` (`where('status', '==', 'IN_STOCK')`, optional like containers: a failure is logged, clears the packages and sets `loadError`), `startMealsListener`, `startPinchSettingsListener` (the one document at `apps/pinch/settings/{uid}`, optional like containers: a failure is logged and the defaults apply), `startSharesListener` (`where('ownerUid', '==', uid)` on `apps/pinch/shares`, optional like cooks: a failure is logged, clears the links and sets `loadError`) — `onSnapshot` of the kitchen document and each collection under `kitchens/{uid}` (the last two are not under it). One effect keyed on `uid`.
2. **Visible window:** `startPlannedMealsListener(uid, windowKey, onChange)` — `onSnapshot` of `plannedMeals` where `date >= windowStart` and `date < windowEnd`, a single-field range covering the window's centre month (`shownMonth`) plus `MONTH_WINDOW_PADDING` (2) months on each side. The effect is keyed on that month (`"2026-10"`). `CalendarScreen` moves the centre only when the view being shown reaches past the window (`isRangeInWindow`), so month paging restarts the listener about every second page and week or day paging seldom does. The slice keeps the previous window's documents until the new snapshot lands, so the grid never blanks while paging.

**Loading gate.** `Pinch.tsx` shows a skeleton until every user-tier slice `isLoaded` and the first window has landed. An empty kitchen (no settings document, no records) is a valid state that renders the empty Calendar; there is no first-run blocking modal. The settings listener is waited for but its failure never shows the error screen (the nudges simply stay open), since it only carries an enhancement, and the sources listener is the same: its first snapshot or error ends the gate (so the filter doesn't pop in after the list), and its error is left out of `selectPinchLoadError`. The shopping listener is left out of the gate as well as of the error (a screen that needs it shows nothing until it lands), and its error is logged and clears its items. The ideas listener is the same: it is left out of the gate and of the error, the Ideas list shows nothing until it lands, and its error is logged and clears its items. So is the shares listener: a Share view says it is loading or couldn't load, and Settings draws no Shared links row until a link is loaded.

**Actions (thunks)** in `store/actions/`, writing only to the person's own paths:

| Action | Write |
|---|---|
| `saveSettings(fields)` | a field-scoped dotted-path write (`showNutrition.protein`, built with `FieldPath`) in one `runTransaction` that creates the document on first write; a closed nudge is no longer written ("Not now" lasts until the page reloads), and `dismissedNudges` keys already stored are only read and cleared when a price is added |
| `saveIngredient(draft)` | `setDoc` of the record (custom: new id; catalog: id = catalog id) with the full shape; edits are field-scoped `updateDoc` |
| `removeIngredient(id)` | the archive-or-delete check (Logic §3a) |
| `saveFamily` | `setDoc` (no delete in the MVP, so no family is ever archived) |
| `saveStore(draft)` | `setDoc` to create, field-scoped `updateDoc` to edit; a catalog store is created lazily by `ensureCatalogStore(catalogStoreId)` (create-if-absent inside a `runTransaction`, so a double tap makes one); an uploaded image goes to Storage first, then `imagePath` is written |
| `removeStore(id)` | archive when any product points at it, else delete + delete the image |
| `saveProduct(draft)` | one `writeBatch`: the product document and, when it's the ingredient's first live product or "Make this the default" was chosen, the record's `defaultProductId` (an upsert for a catalog ingredient with no record); also clears the matching `dismissedNudges` keys; preceded by `freezePastMeals` |
| `removeProduct(id)` | delete; re-points `defaultProductId` to another live product or `null` in the same batch |
| `setDefaultProduct(ingredientId, productId)` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of the record's `defaultProductId` (a transaction confirms the product still exists, so a stale device can't leave it dangling) |
| `saveRecipe(draft)` | `setDoc`/field-scoped `updateDoc` of what the form owns (never the whole cached object; `care` is one of those fields, written on every save and `null` when empty, so an older recipe gains the key); new custom ingredients from an AI draft are created in the same `writeBatch`; a cover goes to Storage first; preceded by `freezePastMeals`; a Firebase error is logged and shown as one plain sentence ("Check your connection and try again." when offline or `unavailable`, a Storage code's own plain sentence, else "Unable to save this recipe."), never its raw text, while the thunk's own sentences reach the form verbatim |
| `removeRecipe(id)` | the archive-or-delete check, preceded by `freezePastMeals` |
| `renameSource({ id, label })` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of `label` and `updatedAt`; refuses a label another loaded source has ("You already have a source called …") |
| `removeSource(id)` | `writeBatch` (400 writes each) of the source's delete and `sourceId: null` on every loaded recipe that names it |
| `addToShoppingList({ lines })` | one `runTransaction` that reads every target item first, then creates, adds to or restarts each (§5b); at most 200 distinct lines; returns how many it wrote |
| `setShoppingItemChecked({ ids })` / `updateShoppingItem({ mergedIds? })` / `removeShoppingItem({ ids })` | a field-scoped `writeBatch` of `isChecked` over a row's items, `updateDoc` of `amount` or a transaction that moves the item (and folds a row's other items into it), and a `writeBatch` of deletes |
| `clearCheckedShoppingItems()` | takes the checked ids from the store, then one `runTransaction` per chunk of 250 that re-reads each item and deletes only those still checked; returns the items really deleted, for the Undo (chunks are independent, so not atomic together) |
| `restoreShoppingItems({ items })` | one `runTransaction` per chunk of 250 that recreates each item as it was unless it exists again; returns how many it put back |
| `createIdea({ draft })` | refuses while the ideas listener hasn't landed or has failed, then checks the title, note, link (http(s), at most 2,000 characters, not already saved), the 200-idea cap and that a photo idea has a picture, then uploads a photo first (resized to webp; a file the browser couldn't decode is refused with "That picture couldn't be read. Try a JPG, PNG or WebP photo.") and `setDoc`s the idea with every key (a photo is deleted again if the write fails); an empty title on a link becomes the host; `siteName` is the page's or the host; `imageUrl` is kept only if it is https; takes an optional caller-chosen `id`, and its checks are `getNewIdeaProblem`, which the sheets run first; returns the id |
| `updateIdea({ id, title, note, url, photoFile })` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of `title`, `note`, `updatedAt`, a changed link's `url`, `siteName` and `imageUrl: null`, a replaced photo's `photoPath`, and the keys an older document lacks |
| `removeIdea(id)` | `deleteDoc`, then the photo object (best effort); the recipe an idea became is not touched |
| `saveRecipe(draft)` with `fromIdeaId` | the recipe's own batch commits first and unchanged; then, if the idea is still in the loaded store, a separate field-scoped `updateDoc` of `{ …missing keys, recipeId, updatedAt }` on the idea, whose failure is logged and never thrown; returns `{ recipeId, linkedIdea }` |
| `addCook({ recipeId, servings, cookedAt })` | `setDoc` of a cook with a fresh `generateUuid()` id and every key; refuses (with a sentence) until the cooks listener has landed, when it failed, for a recipe not in the store, for servings outside 0 to 1,000, for a `cookedAt` more than a minute ahead and at 500 cooks for the recipe (§5d); returns the id |
| `markPlannedCooked({ plannedMealId, recipeId, servings, cookedAt })` | one `runTransaction` that reads `{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}` and writes the cook only when it doesn't exist; resolves `{ id, isNew }` (needs a connection, like every transaction); the planned meal's row calls it |
| `markPlannedPlateCooked({ plannedMealId, entries: [{ recipeId, servings }], cookedAt })` | one `runTransaction` over `{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}` for each entry, writing only the missing ones; resolves `[{ recipeId, id, isNew }]` (cooking mode's Finish from a plan) |
| `updateCook({ id, servings, cookedAt })` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of `servings`, `cookedAt`, `updatedAt` and a missing `plannedMealId` as `null` |
| `removeCook(id)` | `deleteDoc` (the toast's Undo, a planned meal row's Undo, and the history's Delete after a destructive confirm) |
| `removeCooks({ ids })` | one `writeBatch` of deletes (cooking mode's Undo, all or none) |
| `removeRecipe(id)` (hard delete) | after the recipe's delete, `deleteCooks` and `deleteNotes` each query their collection (`where('recipeId', '==', id)`, one equality, no index) and remove what they find in `writeBatch`es of 400, best effort; an archive leaves them |
| `addNote({ recipeId, text, cookId })` | `setDoc` of a NOTE with a fresh `generateUuid()` id and every key; refuses (with a sentence) until the notes listener has landed, when it failed, for a recipe not in the store, for blank or over-long text, for a cook that isn't the recipe's and at 300 notes for the recipe (§5e); returns the id |
| `updateNote({ id, text })` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of `text` and `updatedAt` plus any key an older document lacks (a TWEAK's blank text is `null`, a NOTE's is refused) |
| `removeNote(id)` | `deleteDoc` (the note sheet's trash after a destructive confirm) |
| `saveRecipe(draft)` (an edit of a cooked recipe) | the recipe's own `writeBatch` also holds the TWEAK note (`draft.tweakReason` becomes its `text`) when something the form edits changed; never for a new recipe or one with no cook (§5e). The form asks the reason first when an ingredient or step changed, and cooking mode embeds the same form |

| `useContainerActions().putAway(inputs)` then `writeContainers({ containers })` | `putAway` refuses (with a sentence) until the containers listener has landed, when it failed, for a missing name, a recipe and a meal together, servings outside 1 to 100, an eat-by that isn't a whole UTC day, and when what is in stock plus what is added would pass `SOFT_MAX_CONTAINERS` (500); then it builds the containers (a fresh `generateUuid()` id each, the full shape, one shared `createdAt`, history MADE with the cook's servings only from a cook, then FRIDGE or FREEZE with the container's own servings) and `writeContainers` commits them in ONE `writeBatch` (1 to 20 ordinary creates, no rules change); the batch is not awaited by the UI, and a refusal arriving later raises a toast |
| `undoContainerBatch({ ids })` | one `runTransaction`: reads every container of a put-away and deletes them only while none has changed since it was written (`updatedAt` still equals `createdAt`), else it leaves them and says so |
| `eatContainer({ id, count })` / `tossContainer({ id })` / `moveContainer({ id, to, eatBy })` / `changeContainerEatBy({ id, eatBy })` / `correctContainerCount({ id, count })` / `undoContainerEnd({ id, at })` | each one `runTransaction` that re-reads the container, applies the pure plan (`planEat`, `planToss`, `planMove`, `planEatBy`, `planCount`, `planUndoEnd`) and updates only the fields it owns; a plan that can't hold stops with one sentence; eating, tossing, undoing and deleting also invalidate the Finished queries |
| `writePackages({ packages })` / `undoPackageAdd({ id })` | one `writeBatch` of `setDoc`s (the add; it queues offline) and a transaction that deletes a just-added package only while `updatedAt` still equals `createdAt` |
| `consumePackage({ id, amount })` / `tossPackage({ id })` / `movePackage({ id, to, expiresAt? })` / `editPackage({ id, edit })` / `undoPackageEnd({ id, at })` | each one `runTransaction` that re-reads the package, applies the pure plan (`planUse`, `planPackageToss`, `planPackageMove`, `planPackageEdit`, `planUndoEnd`) and `transaction.update`s only the fields the plan owns (§5i); a package that changed answers a sentence and nothing is written |
| `changePackagesInPlace({ ids, action })` / `takeFromPackages({ takes })` / `undoPackageChanges({ changed })` | `runInChunks` over `planPackageBulk`, over the take-out planner's `PackageTake`s and over `planUndoEnd`/`planUndoPackageMove`, 200 per `runTransaction`, each re-reading every package it touches; each answers what it really changed, what it skipped and by which reason, and a sentence when it stopped partway |
| `removePackage({ id })` | `deleteDoc` (the form's trash after a destructive confirm that says the history goes too) |
| `removeContainer({ id })` | `deleteDoc` (the put-away toast's Undo, and the sheet's trash after a destructive confirm that says the history goes too) |
| `saveMeal(draft)` / `removeMeal(id)` | `setDoc`/`updateDoc`; meals are referenced only by `sourceMealId` (never read for display), so a meal is always deletable; `removeMeal` then takes down the meal's link (`stopSharingDeleted`) |
| `saveShare({ kind, sourceId })` / `renewShareLink(id)` / `stopSharing(id)` | each calls `changePinchShare` (`create` or `update`, `renew`, `delete`) and then waits up to 4 s for the shares listener to show the result; `saveShare` builds the copy from the store at the tap (`getState()`), so no cached object is sent whole and no kitchen document is written (§5k) |
| `stopSharingDeleted(state, kind, sourceId)` | called by `removeRecipe` (a hard delete, not an archive) and `removeMeal` after their delete has committed: an unawaited `changePinchShare` delete of that source's link, whose failure is logged and leaves the link listed as "Original deleted" |
| `addSharedRecipe` / `addSharedMeal` / `undoSharedImport` (`importShareActions.ts`, called by the public page) | one `writeBatch` of the person's new recipes, meal and custom ingredients (at most 311), rebuilt from the share by an allowlist; then, per recipe with a cover, `copyPinchShareCover` and a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `coverPath`; Undo reads the server and either deletes exactly what was written in one batch or refuses (§5k) |
| `planMeal({ date, mealType, items, name, sourceMealId })` | `setDoc` of a new planned meal with `refIds` derived; a past date is frozen in the same write |
| `updatePlannedMeal(id, fields)` | a `runTransaction` that re-reads the meal, refuses a frozen one, then updates what the builder owns (`date`, `mealType`, `items`, `refIds`, `updatedAt`) |
| `removePlannedMeal(id)` | `deleteDoc` after `useActionModal().confirm({ destructive: true })` |
| `savePlannedAsMeal(plannedId, name)` | `setDoc` of a new meal from the planned meal's items; reads the planned meal from the store at tap time |
| `freezePastMeals()` | field-scoped `snapshot` writes for the loaded, unfrozen past meals: one `writeBatch` per 100, falling back to one `updateDoc` per meal when a batch is refused |
| `repriceDay({ uid, dayKey })` | one `runTransaction` that re-reads the day's frozen meals, then replaces each one's `snapshot` (from today's prices, `repricedAt` set) and `updatedAt`; nothing else on the meal changes |

No cached document is ever written back whole. There is no cross-person concurrency; the create-if-absent cases above and the shopping list's add and unit change (two devices of one person can add at once) are the only transactions.

**Selectors** (`store/selectors.ts`): `createSelector` for everything that builds a new array or object, so no `useAppSelector` returns a fresh reference without `shallowEqual`.

- `selectIngredientsById` — catalog merged with records (a `Map`), memoized on the records
- `selectIngredientFamilies` — grouped list for Kitchen (Yours / All), archived hidden
- `selectDefaultProducts` — `Map<ingredientId, Product>`
- `selectRecipeCosts` — `Map<recipeId, RecipeCost>` computed once per change of recipes, products or ingredients
- `selectPlannedByDay` — `Map<dayKey, PlannedMeal[]>` read by the Calendar's `renderCell` and the day drawer, so a cell never filters the list
- `selectPeriodStats(state, startKey, endKey)` — one pass over the planned meals of the shown month or week (`getPeriodStats(range)`): cost, average per fully priced meal, what it leaves out, and the totals by day that the summary sheet lists (`byType` is still computed; nothing reads it now). The sheet adds `getPricedCoverage` (distinct ingredients used and how many are priced) only while open; `getPeriodCostText`, `getDayCostText` and `formatPeriodCost` word the line, the tile and the rows (`getDayCostText` formats through `formatPeriodCost` too, so a day of $1,000 or more reads "$1,235", not "$1,234.56+" in its row and "$1,235" in its drawer tile; checked at 99,999, 100,000 and 123,456 cents); `getPeriodCostTile` words the Total tile from the same figure ("$4.10" over "Total so far", "$4.10+" over "Total", `null` with nothing priced, so the line keeps saying "No price yet"); `getPeriodTotal` is what the summary sheet's tile calls (the front tile's words, or the line's "No price yet" / "Not priced" under "Total"), so the tile and the sheet it opens say one figure in one anatomy; and `getCostAverages` words "Avg per fully priced meal" and "Per g protein, fully priced" (from three such meals, protein only while it is on) once for both the Calendar's stats tiles and the sheet's reference lines (all checked by `check:pinch-math`, including a period of fully priced, partly priced, frozen and priceless meals). The period's total carries one qualifier: "so far" while a left-out ingredient sits on a meal that isn't fixed yet (`openLeftOut`), "+" once every gap is on a fixed day ("$1,805+"), so a frozen number never says "so far"; the averages carry none. The Calendar's header action (Plan and the View options eye) is drawn only once there is a recipe or a saved meal (`selectRecipes`, `selectMeals`), and the stats choice (`usePinchSettings(uid)`) and the eye's price switch (`useListViewOptions(uid, 'calendar')`) reach one place each: the Calendar's own "Show stats" (`PinchSettings.calendarStats`, off to start, saved with the account) draws `StatsTiles` under `SummaryLine` and tells the line (`isTotalBelow`, true while `getPeriodCostTile` has a tile) to leave the total to the Total tile, and the sheet (`isStatsOnFront`) to leave its reference lines out (they come from the same functions as the tiles, so each figure shows once, on the front or in the sheet); "Show prices" reaches the Week's meal rows as `showPrice`. Switching a nutrition number on in Settings changes nothing on the Calendar: the number shows in the tiles while `calendarStats` is on, and elsewhere either way. `StatsTiles` is `StatTile`s (Total, Avg per fully priced meal, Per g protein, fully priced; `isLabelWrapped`, so a long label wraps under its number, and one `getValueSizeClass` step for every number in the row, set by the longest figure (two characters of slack in a row of one or two tiles, and a phrase like "No price yet" counts for none) and never under 12px, with a three-tile row at 360px or under giving a total of eight characters or more ("$12,345+", which doesn't fit a 91px tile at 12px beside its warning icon; seven in a day's drawer, where the tile is 85px) the whole row and the other two the next) over `MacroStats` tiles for the daily nutrition (the stacked emoji, number and name tile below `sm`, the one-line tile from `sm`); the whole block is one pointer target for the summary sheet (an `aria-hidden`, `tabIndex={-1}` button behind the tiles, which are `pointer-events-none` except for their help buttons), so a keyboard reaches the line above and the warning icon, never a second tab stop for the same sheet. `PeriodLeftOutHelp` is the warning icon's text for the Total tile, the sheet's Total tile and the day drawer's. **A day's stats** are the same functions over one day: `DayDrawer` reads `selectPeriodStats(state, dayKey, dayKey)`, its Total tile is `getPeriodTotal(stats, dayKey < todayKey)` (so it is the figure the summary sheet's row for that day shows, and it says "No price yet" or "Not priced" itself since no summary line does), and `StatsTiles` takes `day` (always a Total tile, the nutrition captioned "That day"), an optional `onOpen` (no target behind the tiles inside the drawer, which has no sheet to open) and `isNutritionHidden` (the cost tiles alone, for the stats view, whose nutrition is already on the front). The nutrition block is `NutritionTiles` (`values`, `caption`: the `MacroStats` tiles for the switches that are on and known, nothing otherwise), which `StatsTiles` renders under "Daily average" or "That day" and the drawer's front renders on its own. `isStatsShown` (the Calendar's `calendarStats`) comes down as a prop from `CalendarScreen`, with `todayKey`, so the drawer follows the Calendar's clock and option: on, `StatsTiles` replaces the lone Total tile and the fixed-day note drops under it; off, the Total tile stays and `NutritionTiles` draws the day's nutrition under it ("That day": a nutrition switch that is on shows its number on the day's front without a tap, as the numbers' place in Settings says), and `getDayStatsOffer(stats, show)` returns `{ subtitle: 'Cost averages' }` only when `getCostAverages` has an average, since the Total and the nutrition are already there; its "Stats for this day" row (`ActionGroup` and `GroupRow`) swaps the cost tiles in place (`StatsTiles` with `isNutritionHidden`; `isStatsOpen`, derived to false when the offer goes away; a `BackLink` with `useBackStep`, so back and Escape step out one layer and focus returns to the row's button). A day with nothing planned renders none of this (one muted line says what can go on the day). The summary sheet's reference lines sit in its fixed head under the Total, never inside the list that scrolls (they were the last child of that list, under the fold of a 190px scroller), so a short phone's first screenful holds them; its column cap is the screen's 92% less 15rem, not the shared 22rem, because its title and footer measure about 13rem and a head with a total, references, a nudge and a shopping row left a tall phone 140px of height unused
- `selectSources`, `selectSourcesById`, `selectSourceCounts` — the sources A to Z, a `Map` of them, and the listed recipes counted per source (and without one) in one pass; the Recipes filter, the Sources list and a source's delete confirm read the counts
- `selectCollections`, `selectCollectionsById`, `selectCollectionCounts`, `selectIsCollectionsReady` — the collections A to Z, a `Map` of them, the listed recipes counted per collection (and in none) in one pass, and whether the listener has landed without error; the form's row, the Recipes filter, the drawer's chips and the Collections sheet read them. `selectMeals` puts starred saved meals first, then newest first
- `selectShoppingItems`, `selectShoppingById`, `selectShoppingOpenCount`, `selectShoppingRows`, `selectShoppingCost` — the items, a `Map` of them, how many are left (the Shopping tab's badge), the groups and "Got it" rows in one pass, and the approximate cost of what is left with what it leaves out
- `selectIdeas`, `selectIdeasById`, `selectIdeaGroups` — the ideas newest first, a `Map` of them, and `{ toTry, made }` (made = its `recipeId` names a listed recipe; built with two filters over the ideas)
- `selectCooksByRecipe`, `selectCookSummaries`, `selectCooksById`, `selectCooksLoadError` — a recipe's cooks newest first, `{ count, lastCookedAt }` per recipe in one pass, a `Map` of the cooks, and the listener's error; the recipe drawer, the delete confirm, the plan builder and cooking mode read the summaries
- `selectNotesByRecipe`, `selectNoteSummaries`, `selectNotesById`, `selectNotesLoadError`, `selectTweakTracking(state, recipeId)` — a recipe's notes newest first, `{ count, lastTweakAt }` per recipe in one pass, a `Map` of the notes, the listener's error, and `'on' | 'full' | 'off'` for whether saving an edit records a tweak (a string, so the recipe form re-renders only when the answer changes)






- `selectPackages` (in stock, soonest date first), `selectPackagesById`, `selectPackagesByIngredient` (`Map<ingredientId, Package[]>`, soonest first), `selectHasPackages`, `selectPackagesLoadError`, `selectIsPackagesReady`, `selectLeadDays` (the person's "days ahead", 1 while unset: the window of "due soon" for packages and the morning note);
- `selectShares` (the latest copy first), `selectSharesBySource`, `selectShareBySource(kind, sourceId)`, `selectShareStatuses` (stale and gone, by share id), `selectSharesLoadError`, `selectIsSharesReady`: the Share view, Settings' Shared links and the delete confirms read them
- `selectContainers` (in stock, soonest eat-by first), `selectContainersById`, `selectStockByRecipe` and `selectStockByMeal` (`Map<id, { fridge, freezer, containers, nearestEatBy, fridgeEatBy, freezerEatBy }>`, servings per place and the nearest eat-by in each, from one pass), `selectStockByMealWithRecipes` (a saved meal's stock in whole plates, a `PlaceStock`), `selectContainersByCookId`, `selectContainersLoadError`, `selectIsContainersReady`; a row reads its own entry with `shallowEqual`
- `selectUsedIngredientIds` — the ingredients a recipe, a saved meal or an unfrozen planned day uses; Kitchen's "Yours" and "Needs a price" read it (rows no longer carry a "No price yet" line)
- `selectGaps(scope)` — scope-parameterized via a small factory; takes `showNutrition` and `dismissedNudges`
- Time-dependent selectors take `todayKey` as an argument (`useNow()` supplies it in the component)

**Tabs.** `PINCH_TABS` is `calendar`, `recipes`, `kitchen`, `shopping`, `settings`, chosen by `?tab=` (Calendar, the default, has no parameter, so a reload or a deep link keeps the tab) and switched through a transition in `Pinch.tsx`, which mounts only the active tab's screen. `PinchNav` is a floating pill at the bottom of a phone, as wide as its five tabs (never stretched; below `sm` each tab is `min-w-[3.2rem]` with 4px of side padding, 298px of pill at 320px wide, the longest label "Shopping" 58px), and a rail on the left from `lg` up. The Shopping tab carries the count left to buy as a small `bg-primary` badge on its icon, capped at "99+", read by `ShoppingTabBadge` through its own selector so the nav, Kitchen and Calendar never re-render for a tick.

**Overlay coordination.** The orchestrator owns one discriminated-union state: `subview: null | PinchSubview` (`{ kind: 'products' }` (Stores & prices) `| { kind: 'sources' } | { kind: 'shares' } | { kind: 'containers'; recipeId?; mealId? } | { kind: 'pantry'; ingredientId? } | { kind: 'recipe'; id; method?; fromDrawer?; fromIdeaId?; fromNoteId? } | { kind: 'cook'; recipeId; servings?; plannedMealId?; plate? } | { kind: 'plan'; ... } | { kind: 'meal'; id }`, where `method` is the way a new recipe already chose (on a phone, or the link way for an idea that holds a link), `fromDrawer` says it was picked in the phone's start drawer, `fromIdeaId` is the idea a new recipe is being made from and `fromNoteId` the note an existing recipe is being updated from), exposed through a small context (`usePinchOverlay`) so a full-page screen can never open on top of another. The context also carries `showSettingsTab` (a View options sheet's door to Settings; it does nothing only while a subview is open) and `showShoppingTab`, which the add toast's "View" calls (it switches to the Shopping tab only while no subview is open and no dialog is on top, so it never replaces a form holding typed work or closes a sheet under typed prices), and `isStartOpen` / `setIsStartOpen` for the phone's recipe start drawer, which `Pinch.tsx` renders once so that Recipes, Calendar and a way's back button can all raise it. It also carries `showCalendarDay(dayKey)`, which the plan toast's "View" calls: it does nothing while a subview or a drawer or sheet (`hasDialogOnTop`) is open, and otherwise remounts the Calendar on that day (a `key` and `focusDayKey`, cleared on the next tab change), sets `?view=week`, and moves `shownMonth` only when that day's week falls outside the loaded window; `openSubviewFromToast(subview)`, the same guard for a toast that opens a full-page screen (the new recipe's Plan it); and `openIdeaSheet`, which mounts the New idea sheet over any screen. `Pinch.tsx` also holds the tabs' session (`PinchSessionContext`, a `Map` provided around the tab body and cleared when the signed-in person changes): `usePinchSessionState(key, initial, override?)` is `useState` whose value is kept under `recipes:view|query|source|stock`, `kitchen:query|view|filters` and `calendar:anchor`, so a tab that unmounts on a switch opens as it was (a toast's day `focusDayKey` overrides the kept anchor); nothing goes in the URL. A per-tab scroll map is written by one passive scroll listener for the shown tab only (not while a non-overlay subview hides the tabs) and restored in a layout effect when the tab or the tabs' visibility changes. Re-tapping the active tab scrolls to the top, and at the top clears that tab's session keys and remounts it (`key`). `setActiveTab` pushes only when leaving the Calendar (remembering the router's `history.state.idx`), replaces between the other tabs, and steps back with `history.back()` when going to the Calendar from the entry right above it; a toast's "View" (`showShoppingTab`, `showCalendarDay`) pushes only when it leaves the Calendar and replaces when already on a tab, like `setActiveTab`. The other drawers and sheets (day, planned meal, recipe, meal, ingredient, the calendar's summary, fill-in) are opened from the screen that owns them. The recipe form's own editors (the title, prep and cook and description drawers of `SummaryEditor`, and on a phone the ingredient, step and nutrition drawers, one level deep) are the drawers allowed over a subview, and so is a `HelpTip`'s small modal, which is how the meal builder's cost warning opens on a phone or any touch screen. A drawer's action that opens a form closes the drawer first; the in-place "swap" screens (planned meal, save-as-meal) are components inside the drawer, not new overlays.



---

## Component Encapsulation

- **Screens read the store; presentation components take props.** `CalendarScreen`, `RecipesScreen` and `KitchenScreen` use `useAppSelector`; rows, cells and badges are pure.
- **Calendar stat tiles are targets one tile at a time.** `StatTile` and `MacroStats` (`variant='tiles'`) take an optional `onOpen` that makes only that tile a button (a stretched, labelled `Button` behind its content, the help icon keeping its own tap), so the gaps between tiles and a neighbour's tap never open anything; `StatsTiles` passes `{ cost, nutrition }`: a cost tile opens `WeekSummarySheet` (the price summary), a calories or macro tile `NutritionSummarySheet` (the daily average and each day's figures, each opening its day drawer, with the nutrition gaps' nudge). The tiles inside a day's drawer pass nothing and are plain text. Before this, one `absolute inset-0` button behind the whole block opened the price summary from any tap on it, macro tiles and gaps included. A macro tile's tint is on its `li` and the `Button` inside is transparent with a hover and pressed wash, because the theme makes any `button.bg-secondary` see-through; the Nutrition summary uses compact tiles on a screen 700px tall or less, so its list keeps three rows above the footer at 844×390 and 320×568.
- **App-aware pieces stay in the app** (`DayCell`, `MonthView`, `WeekView`, `SummaryLine`, `WeekSummarySheet`, `MealChip`, `StoreLogo`, `GapNudge`, `LeftOutTip`, `RecipeStart` (and its phone drawer), `RecipeReader`, `RecipeBasics`, `RecipeCareField`, `RecipeCareSection`, `RecipeIngredients`, `RecipeSteps`, `NutritionReview`, `MealBuilder`, `ListViewControls` with `useListViewOptions`, `CookingSubview` and its step, ingredients and timer pieces with `useCookTimers`, the idea pieces `IdeasView`, `IdeaRow`, `IdeaThumbnail`, `IdeaDrawer`, `IdeaFormSheet`, `IdeaAddSheet`, `IdeaWays` and `IdeaNoteField`, and the note pieces `RecipeNotes`, `NoteEntry`, `ChangeList`, `NoteForm` and `NoteSheet`, `NoteKindPills`, `NoteStepPicker` and `NoteReasonField` with `useCookCapture`); anything whose props mention no kitchen entity comes from `src/components`, `src/hooks` or Dreamer UI: `SearchInput`, `PillGroup`, `PickOrCreate`, `SectionDivider`, `MoneyInput`, `FormSheet`, `Subview`, `CopyButton`, and the ones added with the redesign: `MacroStats` (with `src/utils/macroUtils.ts`), `HelpTip` (`variant='warning'`), `MethodPicker`, `FormSection`, `forms/Field`, `Stepper`, `FilterBar`, `PeriodSwitcher`, `ViewOptions`, `useLocalStorageState`, `SummaryEditor`, `UndoSnackbar`, `useUndoRemoval`, `useWakeLock` and `durationUtils` (`findDurations`, `formatDuration`, `formatClock`), `TickMark` (round 7), and for voice commands `useSpeechCommands`, `speechUtils` (`speakText`, `pickVoice`, `primeSpeech`) and `Subview`'s `headerAction`.
- **Shared pieces and their rules.** `MethodPicker` is rows only: every `Method` carries its own `onPick`, and what happens next (a screen, a drawer closing) is the caller's (a list of one quiet row is how a reading screen offers "Write it yourself"). `IntroCard` and `FilePickCard` carry no kitchen words: the first is a tinted block with a chip, a lead sentence and a quieter one, the second a place to choose or drop one file (its title is its action, one icon) that shows the file it holds and a line under it; the app supplies the sentences and the picked file's emoji. `Field` wraps its control in a native `<label>`, so a `Select` is not put inside one; `isCompact` gives it small captions for a grid of short fields, and `COMPACT_INPUT_CLASS` makes the input 40 px with a smaller placeholder while typed text stays 16 px so iOS doesn't zoom on focus. `ViewOptions` is the eye button (a drawer with Done in its footer, and Reset beside it once an option differs from its default, on a phone and on a screen 599px tall or less below `lg` (`isBelow('sm') || (isShort && isBelow('lg'))`, as `SummaryEditor` decides), since a popover on a landscape phone ran past the bottom edge and cut its last row, its body in two columns there so the door row never sits under the pinned footer; a popover otherwise), tinted while customized, over `ViewOptionSwitch` rows (Pinch's `ListViewControls` holds the list's own rows and ends with a door to Settings, where the nutrition switches live; `isQuiet` draws the trigger as a `HeaderIconButton` (28px, 40×40 tap area; `isHighlighted` tints it while customized and `isExpanded` sets `aria-expanded`), the Calendar's eye and Recipes' while no pill row exists (`ListViewControls` passes it through), whose `CalendarViewOptions` holds "On the calendar" (Show prices) and a door row to Settings, where the nutrition switches and About now live); `useLocalStorageState(key, default, parse)` is a `useSyncExternalStore` read of one `localStorage` key shared by every component using it, with a memory copy that is read first (so a write shows at once even when storage refuses it) and dropped when another tab changes the key (the `storage` event), and `parse` rejecting a stored value of the wrong shape. `SummaryRow` is that row without the editor: caption, value (an optional muted detail line), chevron, in the framed `SummaryEditor`'s hairline frame, for a sheet that swaps its own content to change the value instead of opening a second drawer (Add to shopping's Days, the put-away sheet's Putting away; each registers its swapped view with `useBackStep`); with no `onClick` it is a plain unframed line. `ActionChips` is also the one quiet chip for a state action (Reset, Tick all, Clear what you got; `isDestructive` is the red hairline one), never a link. `SummaryEditor` is one form field as a summary row (caption, one or two lines of value, a chevron, a red problem line) that opens its editor: a `Drawer` over the form below `sm` or on a screen under 600px tall (so a phone held sideways never gets a popover that runs off the bottom), a `Popover` under the row from `sm` up (the row is stretched over the popover's inline-block wrapper, the popover is open-state-only content), each with a full-width Done; the editor's first field is focused after a short timer (350 ms for a drawer, which takes focus while it slides in, 30 ms for the popover, which focuses its own container first), and Enter in a one-line field closes the editor and is swallowed so it never submits the surrounding form. An open `Popover` is a `[role=dialog]`, so `Subview`'s Escape handler and `useSubviewHistory`'s back-gesture handler both leave the form alone while one is open, and the gesture is re-sent to the popover as an Escape. `useUndoRemoval` + `UndoSnackbar` are the removal's undo: `showUndo(message, restore)` keeps one pending removal (a newer call replaces it), counts down from 6 by a deadline (so a throttled tab doesn't stretch it), and `restore` reads the latest rows through a ref because the form's value can change before Undo is pressed; `UndoSnackbar` is always mounted (a persistent `role=status` region says the message once, without the countdown) and draws a fixed bar that measures the nearest visible `.form-footer` and sits 12px above it, or 1rem from the bottom when there is none (it re-measures when the footer resizes). `PillGroup` never reorders what is visible: a chosen option that was hidden behind "Show all" takes the last visible slot, and the fully open list is in its original order; a pick-one group shows no "1 selected" count, a pick-several group still does. `FilterBar`'s apply button on a phone reads `doneLabel` ("Show 12 ingredients") and, at 360px and under, the optional `compactDoneLabel` ("Show 12"). `AddFieldChips` are at least 36px tall (`min-h-9`) and a long label wraps instead of clipping. `DetailSheet` takes two optional, additive props: `actions` (icon buttons drawn on the title's line; the sheet then draws its own heading, which names the dialog itself because Dreamer UI's `aria-labelledby` points at a heading it only draws for a string title, and from `sm` its own 40×40 close beside the actions in place of the modal's corner ✕) and `viewKey` (the content's current view: a new one starts at the top, a view comes back where it was scrolled, and a `ResizeObserver` holds the content at the tallest height it has had, less the footer, the padding and the node's own vertical margins (the 12px a compact drawer pulls its content up), so every view of a sheet is the same height to the pixel, 559px at 320×568 and 551 at 390×560, and a short view doesn't drop the drawer under the person's finger; the blank space a short view leaves is accepted, the collections list's 399px of 775 at 390×844 included). A sheet that passes neither renders as before.
- **The back gesture, one layer at a time (`src/hooks/useBackHistory.ts`).** One module-level stack of layers shares one `popstate` listener, attached when the module loads (so landing on an entry kept from before a reload steps on instead of costing a press, and a reload with layers open no longer leaves extra presses): a full-page subview (`usePageHistory`, behind `useSubviewHistory`), a drawer, modal or panel (`useSheetHistory`, used by `DetailSheet`, `FormSheet`, and the phone drawers of `ViewOptions`, `FilterBar` and `SortControl`), and a swapped-in step inside one, which is `BackLink` (it calls `useBackStep(true, onBack)` itself, so a view that draws one adds no step of its own: Pinch's planned meal, "Name this meal", Eat from stock, the box sheet's inner views, the put-away sheet's search, a cook's edit view, a cook's note view, New idea's ways, the recipe and saved meal drawers' views, Stores & prices' "Which ingredient is it for?") or, where the way back is a footer button or a mode, `useBackStep(isActive, onBack)` directly (Add to shopping's Days calendar, Recipes' Select mode). Escape steps back the same way: one capture-phase `keydown` listener, attached with the `popstate` one, takes Escape when the top layer is a step with a drawer or modal under it, or with no sheet or page layer under it at all (a plain page), with no dialog above that sheet (`getDialogsAbove`), no open select or menu where focus is and no tooltip showing, and calls that step's `onBack` before the sheet's own Escape listener can close it (a step inside a subview's page is left to the subview). Each open layer owns one history entry (`backLayerKey`, plus a clock-based `backLayerSeq` that orders entries across reloads) copied from the router's state, so the router sees the same location. On back, every layer whose entry was popped gets its `onBack` (a sheet's `onClose`) inside `flushSync`; a layer still open afterwards (a subview that stepped back inside itself or asked "Keep editing / Discard", "Stop timers and leave?") gets its entry back. A `HelpTip`'s modal (a phone or any touch screen) is a layer through `useSheetHistory`, so back on a tab closes the help note instead of leaving the tab. When a dialog that isn't a layer (a destructive confirm, a raw Dreamer UI drawer) sits above the top layer, the entry is restored and that dialog gets an Escape instead. Closing a layer any other way (✕, swipe, a button, Escape) steps back off its now-dead entry after a tick; a layer that opens while the current entry is dead (a drawer closing as a subview opens, Strict Mode's remount) reuses it with `replaceState`; a layer that opens while that step back is in flight waits for it; and landing on a dead entry steps back once more, so a stale entry never costs a press. A dialog layer that closes takes its steps with it. An entry left by the page before a reload is made plain on load. `useSheetHistory` also gives the dialog an `onClose` that ignores an Escape meant for a dialog opened above it (`getDialogsAbove` in `src/utils/dialogUtils.ts`, which leaves out a subview's own layer and a drawer sliding shut), so Escape and back both close the confirm first. `Subview`'s optional `onBack` is still what its header back, an overlay subview's Escape and the gesture call (`onBack ?? onClose`). Escape is captured and ignored while a drawer or modal other than the subview's own layer is open (`hasDialogOnTop`).
- **`parseMoneyToCents`, `formatCents` and `centsToInputValue` move to `src/utils/moneyUtils.ts`** in the first Pinch PR that needs them (A-List keeps them in `apps/a-list/utils/money.ts` today); A-List's imports are updated in the same PR, per the "a second mini-app needs it" rule.
- **A quantity/unit select is built once** as `UnitSelect` from `UNIT_LABELS`; the ingredient row, the product form and the fill-in sheet all use it (`triggerClassName` sizes its button where it sits beside a compact input).
- **The shopping sheets stay with their screen.** `AddToShoppingSheet` (a `FormSheet`) takes a `ShoppingRequest` (`{ kind: 'recipe', recipeId }`, `{ kind: 'items', items, label }` or `{ kind: 'range', startKey, endKey }`, a day being a range of one) and is mounted by `RecipesScreen` and `CalendarScreen`, which close the drawer before opening it; the recipe and saved meal drawers open it from an "Add to shopping list" row in ⋯ More and the day drawer and the calendar's summary sheet from `AddToShoppingRow`, a tinted row after the meals (a planned meal shops through its day). `ShoppingAddSheet` renders the same body (`ShoppingPick`) for "From your plan", with no View action on its toast. Its toast's "View" calls `showShoppingTab` from the overlay context, and `AppToast` renders an action button for any toast that gives one (and dismisses the toast when it is tapped). `showShoppingTab` switches to `?tab=shopping` through the same search-param transition the nav uses, but only when no subview is open at tap time (read through a ref the orchestrator keeps current, with the search taken from `window.location`, since the toast holds a function from an older render): a recipe form holding typed work is never replaced, and the tap then only dismisses the toast. A form sheet open over a tab is not checked. The list's row sheet and add sheet are `FormSheet`s opened from `ShoppingScreen` over the tab, and `ItemSearch` takes `isCapped` so the add sheet caps its results and only the list scrolls, and `isDisabled` so a caller can lock the results while a write is in flight.
- **Cooks' pieces.** The recipe and saved meal drawers (`RecipeDrawer`, `MealDrawer`) put nothing between their facts and their subject. Their other content is a view swapped in place (`DrawerView`: `recipe | photo | history | more` in `RecipeDrawer`, `isMoreOpen` in `MealDrawer`), each starting with the shared `BackLink`, which registers `useBackStep`, so the back gesture and Escape step back first; the main surface stays mounted and hidden (`hidden`), so its own state (an opened description) survives, and `DetailSheet`'s `viewKey` brings each view back where it was scrolled and never lets the sheet shrink between views. The doors are 40×40 icon buttons with `aria-label`, `title` and `aria-expanded`, tinted with `bg-accent` while open and toggling back on a second tap: `DetailSheet`'s `actions` puts the star on the title's line (a recipe's title line has the star and, once the recipe has cooks, the clock door, "Cooking history", which opens the history sheet; its tips and notes are the two snap rows of `RecipeEntryRows` at the foot of the body) (on a phone the heading drops 8px so it sits 12px clear of the drawer's own ✕ above it; from `sm` the sheet's Close follows the actions after a 12px gap, so the last door is never next to the button that closes the sheet), and ⋯ More sits in the footer between Modify and the primary (`max-sm:px-3` on both, so "Start cooking" stays one line at 320). Back moves focus to the door that opened the view. The history view is the Cooked `GroupRow` (opens the cooking history) and `RecipeNotes`; More owns the drawer, so it is built from the roomy variant of the shared rows (`isRoomy` on `ActionGroup`, `ActionRow` and `GroupRow`: a 40px round `bg-accent` icon chip, a 16px medium title, a 14px muted line, `min-h-16`, a chevron for a row that opens something via `hasChevron`, in an untinted hairline frame; the compact rows stay where rows sit beside other content). A captioned `ActionGroup` ("What would you like to do?") of `ActionRow`s with `isGrouped` (Start cooking or Plan it, Mark as cooked without a chevron since it records at once, `AddToShoppingRow` with `isRoomy`, Put some away), with the reasons a row is off as muted 14px lines under it, comes first; `StockRows` follows it and draws its own captioned `ActionGroup` ("In your fridge and freezer", a roomy `GroupRow` per place with the due badge under the words; a row passes its place, so Fridge & freezer opens on it). A captioned group hides, caption included, while it has no rows (`has-[>[data-action-rows]:empty]:hidden`). In both sizes a row's text sits beside its icon slot on one edge, and keyboard focus draws its ring 2px inside the row (`focus-visible:-outline-offset-2`) because the frame's `overflow-hidden` clipped an outside ring to its bottom edge. `ActionChips` (`src/components`, no app words: a wrapped row of 40px hairline buttons, each an icon, a short label and a `description` that is its `aria-label` and hover title) now carries only a planned recipe item's "Mark as cooked". The planned meal view (`PlannedMealView`) has the same anatomy inside the day drawer: ‹ Back, the meal's name as an `h3` (the same heading on its More view, over the same captioned roomy group and a lone roomy red Remove row), "On the plate", then the Cost tile and macro tiles; its footer (portaled into the day drawer's) is Modify, ⋯ More and the primary, and its views (`PlannedMealSubview`: `meal | more | name | recipe | eat`, the eat view remembering whether it came from the meal's primary or from More) are state the day drawer holds, so `DetailSheet`'s `viewKey` (`day`, or `{mealId}:{view}`) keeps each view's scroll and the sheet's height; the meal surface stays mounted and hidden under More and the views reached from it, each view's `BackLink` registers `useBackStep`, and Back focuses what opened the view (the ⋯, the More row, the primary, or the day's row for that meal). `ActionRow` (a tinted row with an icon, a label and a line under it, red with `isDestructive` for the one row that removes something; `ActionGroup` wraps related rows, `ActionRow`s or `GroupRow`s, in one `bg-muted/50` block with `divide-y`) is for a sheet or view whose content is its actions (the container sheet, the note sheet, a drawer's More) and for a lone row at the end of a drawer (the day drawer's "Add to shopping list", "Price this day with today's prices"); `AddToShoppingRow` is the thin wrapper for the day drawer's and the drawers' More rows (`isGrouped` passes through).
