A planned meal's "Mark as cooked" is `PlannedCookRow`, an `ActionChips` entry on its own recipe's item of the plate (and "(✓) Cooked · Not cooked" once marked). `useCookActions(uid)` holds what marking does and says (the add, the toast with Undo, the "Already marked" notice); `markRecipeCooked` takes `{ isToasted: false }` and `markPlannedRecipeCooked` defaults to no toast, so cooking mode, whose panel confirms itself, raises none. `RepriceDayRow` (calendar) is the tinted "Price this day with today's prices" row in the day drawer (no help icon: its confirm previews the result; it renders nothing when pricing the day again would change nothing). `PlannedCookRow` (calendar) is one recipe's row; the note pieces live in `components/notes/` (`ClampedText` and `NoteTextField` are in `src/components`, since neither names a recipe: the first clamps text at four lines with More / Less, the second is a labelled text box with a counter near its `maxLength` that is focused once a sheet has settled; `ActionRow` is the "Add to your notes" row; `FormSheet`, `PillGroup`, `DeleteIconButton` and `ModalFooterActions` are the sheet), `NoteForm` serves the sheet and the cooking history's in-place view, and `useCookCapture` holds the entry being written on cooking mode (its kind, words, why and step, saving and discarding), whose `hasDraft` is what `leave()` and Cancel ask about before discarding it; `CookingHistorySheet` and `CookEditView` (recipes) are the history, and `RecipesScreen` mounts the sheet after closing the drawer, like the other sheets.



- **Ideas' shared pieces.** `PhotoPicker` gained `uploadLabel`, `changeLabel` and an optional `onRemove` (no `onRemove`, no Remove button) so a photo that is required can say "Replace photo"; `src/lib/pageContent`'s `fetchPageContent` and `pageContentQueryOptions` take an optional `{ timeoutMs }` that sets the callable's timeout and isn't part of the query key; and the link helpers `toWebLink`, `getWebLinkHost` and `getComparableUrl` are in `src/utils/urlUtils.ts`, none of them knowing about ideas (`utils/ideas.ts` in the app holds the idea-shaped ones: `findIdeaByUrl`, `toIdeaUrl`, `toIdeaImageUrl`, `getIdeaDetail`). All are covered by `npm run check:pinch-math`.
- **Closed means unmounted:** drawers' heavy bodies (the recipe drawer's step list, the summary sheet's days, the store list) render only while open.

## UI Component Conventions Applied

- Forms: `FormSheet` for the ingredient, price and store forms and for the recipe ideas' add sheet (the list of ways, then a way's own view in its place) and edit sheet; `Subview` for the recipe form (behind the start list and the reading screens) and meal builder (`MealBuilder` renders its own `Subview`; `MealSubview` and `PlanMealSubview` are thin wrappers) and the fridge and freezer list, Stores & prices and the sources list (the shopping list is a tab, not a subview); the source form and the shopping list's row and add sheets are `FormSheet`s (a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`); `DetailSheet` for day, planned meal, recipe, meal and ingredient details; `ModalFooterActions` with Cancel left, a `DeleteIconButton` beside it on edit, the primary alone on the right.
- Titles are plain nouns ("Recipe", "Price", "Store", "Meal"); the verb is on the primary button and the destructive confirm.
- `PickOrCreate` for family, store and a recipe's source (its optional `maxLength` caps the typed label at 80, and `isFocusedOnOpen={false}` keeps the field from taking focus when the form opens with it filled in); `PillGroup` for meal type, the plan builder's day strip and "Your recent recipes and saved meals", the Month / Week switch and the Recipes / Saved meals / Ideas and Yours / All switches (`isThin`); `FilterBar` for the Kitchen ingredient and Stores & prices filters (its `trailing` slot carries the View options button; Kitchen always has it, and Recipes uses `FilterBar` once a source exists or something is put away, with the same button in `trailing`, and otherwise puts the button beside its Recipes / Saved meals pills while the shown list has something in it); `FormSection` and `Field` lay out the recipe form, whose sections are all visible (no "+ Add X" chips) and whose row editors are drawers on a phone and in place from `sm`; `AppToggle` for the four nutrition switches (the top of every View options sheet) and the View options switches. Cooking mode's timers are cards of its own (`CookStepTimer`), so Pinch no longer uses `SuggestionChips`.
- Help and warning icons are `HelpTip`, and the `noModal` prop no longer exists: on a phone (below `sm`) or on any touch screen, whatever its width (`(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` doesn't match), the icon always opens a small modal ("Got it", with a hairline border); with a mouse from `sm` up it is a tooltip rendered in a portal on `document.body`, which `HelpTip` places itself (its `placement` side, flipped when that side has no room, clamped to the viewport, the arrow aligned with the icon) and measures again for about 24 animation frames after it opens, so a drawer still sliding in doesn't leave it misplaced. The tooltip opens on mouse hover (pointer events, mouse only) and on keyboard focus (`:focus-visible` with Tab as the key before it, so not for a dialog's initial focus after Enter) and closes on leave and blur; a click opens it and keeps it open instead of toggling. Escape is captured by whatever is open, a tooltip or the modal, so only it closes and the next Escape is the drawer's or the subview's (`Subview` leaves Escape alone while a tooltip is on screen or focus is in an open dropdown). A `linkLabel` opens the modal at every size. The modal's dismiss button says "Got it" unless the caller passes `dismissLabel`; the Shopping tab's cost help says "OK", since "Got it" is the other half of its list.


- Recipes' Source filter is one pick-one `PillGroup` in a `FilterBar` (a drawer on a phone, a popover from `sm`) with no link to the Sources screen (the 📖 icon in the header is its one door); its active chips are `LabelChip`s (`@/components/LabelChip`, also the recipe drawer's source pill, which is a plain label when the source holds no other recipe, and Fridge & freezer's "Only …" chip): the pills are the sources some listed recipe has, with their counts, plus "Any" and, when needed, "No source · n"; the chip reads "From {label}" (or "No source"); an empty result offers "Clear filter". The question shows once any source exists; there is no Sources chip (the header icon opens the list, and a row there shows that source's recipes). Kitchen's two doors are `EntryTile`s (`@/components/EntryTile`: `icon`, `label`, an optional `status` in words and `ariaLabel`, no count) in a two-column grid inside a `nav` named "Also in Kitchen" (no visible caption) under the filter bar, not rendered while a filter or search narrows the list; a tile is a container-query component with three layouts: stacked (icon chip over the label, chevron top right, status 11px on one line) below 10rem, shallow from 10 to 13rem (a 24px chip beside the label, the status on one 11px line under both, the chevron on the status line) and lying flat from 13rem (about the width at which the label and a chevron fit beside the chip; status 12px on up to two lines; on a screen under 600px tall, a tile 19rem wide or more puts the status beside the name on one line and is 42px tall: Kitchen's two doors in a landscape phone and Shopping's single tile there, while a narrower tile keeps two lines), and one alone spans the grid below `lg`. Kitchen drops its subtitle while either door exists and tightens its vertical spacing to 12px under 600px tall.

- Kitchen's price filters read `defaultProducts` and `usedIds` (`applyIngredientFilters`): "Needs a price" is used by something and has no default product, "Has a price" has one, so both only ever match Yours even with All picked. The filter panel says so, the count beside Yours / All is "N of M" with M counting Yours while a price filter is on, typing with Yours picked reads "N found in all ingredients", "Add prices" hands `shown` to `FillInSheet` as `NO_PRODUCT` gaps (it ranks them itself), and the empty state offers "Look through everything" only when All would find something.
- **Shared pieces added in round 8 (fix pass).** `EmptyCard` (`src/components`): the tinted first-run block (emoji chip, one sentence, an optional action) Recipes, Shopping, Calendar and Kitchen draw instead of a bare muted line. `HeaderIconButton`: a 28px round header icon with a 40×40 tap area (`after:-inset-1.5`) for clusters spaced `gap-3`, so two areas meet and never overlap; Fridge & freezer, Pantry and Recipes use it for Select and Clear out, and Back is the same size. `SectionHeader` takes an optional `lead` (controls where the subtitle sits; Select's pills). `PillOptions` renders a thin group whose options carry counts as one scrolling line (`PillRow`'s `isScrolling`, the chosen pill brought into view by `scrollLeft` on its own change, never the page) instead of stacking the count under the label below 360px (a caller that passes `wrapsNarrow` lets it wrap below 390px instead, round 8, fifth design round). `FilterBar`'s Show button wraps its two labels in a span, not a Fragment (a Fragment child of Dreamer UI's `Button` got a `className` and logged a React warning). Kitchen's header controls drop to icons under 360px (`max-[359px]:`), and a subtitle or card that repeats the title hides under 700px tall (`[@media(max-height:699px)]:hidden`).
- **Shared pieces and rules added in round 8 (second review, design).** `DetailSheet` takes the drawer path below `sm` and on any screen 599px tall or less (`useIsShortScreen(599)`, `SummaryEditor`'s threshold), so a landscape phone keeps a pinned footer and one scroller where the modal's `calc(100dvh - 16rem)` body left 134px at 844×390 (a window 1024px wide or more keeps the modal, and at 599px tall or less its body takes `calc(100dvh - 11.5rem)`, `[@media(max-height:599px)]`: at 1280×400 the body is 144 to 216px and shows two ingredient rows above the footer, with no page scroll; 1280×900 is unchanged); `useIsShortScreen(maxHeight = 700)` takes the height as a parameter. A sheet that caps its own list for the modal path (Shopping's "Meals on your list") scopes that cap to `sm` and 600px tall or more. `CookingResumeRow` is one 48px row: the title is the Keep cooking button (the screen-reader words "Keep cooking." lead its name) with a chevron, and ✕ is the 40×40 "Not now". `HeaderIconButton` is the one icon of a header cluster (Sources, Stores & prices, Settings, Select, What you finished, Clear out), all at `gap-3`. `Subview`'s `headerAction` also takes a screen's own action cluster where the row has room: Fridge & freezer puts its three icons and Add there from `sm` up (a 320–639px phone keeps them on their own row, where the title would otherwise get 48px of 288), and under 500px tall Select mode moves Cancel there, joins the select-all chips to the place pills in one row (the phone words "All past their date" and "All in the fridge", since the long ones do not fit one line) and trims the subview's top padding and title bar. Fridge's place switch sits in a `min-w-0` wrapper: inside a wrapping flex row its scrolling pill row took its content's width and clipped at 320px. Pantry's Select shows an ingredient with several packages as one row (a checkbox for all of them, "n packages · k ticked" while only some are ticked, a 40×40 chevron that opens the package rows in place), so the list is one row per ingredient until a person asks for the packages. `getSelectionPrimary(ticked, past)` (`utils/containers.ts`, `check:pinch-math`) makes Toss the filled primary of a pick-several bar when every ticked row is past its date, and the use-up act (Ate it all, Used it up) otherwise, the other being the quiet one (Toss keeps its red outline then). `EntryTile` cuts its status to one line when stacked and two when flat (the full text stays in the button's name; Shopping's single tile is always flat, so its "For Mon dinner, Tue dinner and Adobo chicken" keeps two). `AppToast`'s action ("Undo", "View") is a 40px hairline chip with no underline, and the recipe form's blocker note ("Add an ingredient to save.") is a 28px hairline chip with an up arrow and a 40px hit area. In the cooking dock "Got it" is a labelled chip in the header and a short screen's Update is an icon beside Edit, because no labelled Apply fits the row at 320px.
- **Hit areas of 40px, measured with `elementFromPoint` (third design round).** A pseudo-element can only grow a target into room that is not clipped or taken by a later positioned neighbour, so each fix says where the room is: `AppToast`'s Dismiss (24px) takes `after:-inset-2` and the toast reserves `pr-10` so the 40px square never covers the action chip (hit 24×24 before, 40×40 after, the Undo chip still 71×40); a thin `Pill` reaches `before:-inset-x-1` as well as 8px up and down ("All" 35×40 before, 43×40 after; the 8px between pills means neighbours meet and never overlap); `LabelChip` reaches 8px above and 16px below, because a chip that is the first thing in a modal's scroller has its upper edge clipped (the recipe drawer's "In 2 collections" was 123×34 at 1280, now 123×42); `SuggestionChips` are 32px tall (`min-h-8`, they were 26) and reach 10px up and down, because in the collections popover they are the last thing in a scroller and the bottom is clipped (99×33 before, 99×41 after); the Month grid under 360px drops its 8px padding and 4px gaps for a transparent 2px cell border (`max-[359px]:`), so the seven columns are 41px wide instead of 35 and the tiles look the same (36 hit before, 42 after at 320). `HelpTip`'s warning icon needed nothing: its box is 22×20 and its hit area is 42×41 (43×40 at 1280).
- **Shared pieces and rules added in round 8 (fourth design round).** `FilterBar` takes a `className` for its root and its `leading` view switch (Yours / All) sits in the chips row under the field on a phone and beside the field from `sm` (`isPhone` already chooses the drawer or the popover, so it chooses this too); `SearchInput` takes a `className`; Recipes hides the search row with `[@media(max-height:500px)]:hidden` while selecting and nothing is typed or filtered (a narrowed list keeps its field so the person sees why it is narrowed), and draws `Select all shown` twice, once in the row and once in the title row, one hidden by `[@media(max-height:500px)_and_(min-width:420px)]` (CSS, not a hook, so no frame draws both; the title row holds the title, the pill and Cancel only from 420px). `EntryTile` has three layouts by container width: stacked below 10rem, shallow from 10 to 13rem and flat from 13rem. The stacked one compacts under 600px tall with `@max-[10rem]:[@media(max-height:599px)]` (a media variant alone beats a container variant in the generated CSS, so every compact class is scoped to its own range); the shallow one is a grid (`[auto minmax(0,1fr) auto]`: a `size-6` chip, the label across columns 2 and 3, the status across columns 1 and 2 under them, one line, and the chevron in column 3 on that second row, so a tile with no status keeps its label and chevron too, 62px), 63px at 390×844 and 375×667 and 57px at 390×560, where `py-1.5 gap-y-0.5` apply, its label fitting from a 160px tile (112px available, 107.1 needed) and lies flat on one line from 19rem there (the label's 107px, a 28px chip, a 28px chevron reserve and about 100px of status); at 140px the label needs the whole width, so no layout puts the chip and the label on one line at 320px. `ActionChip` has `isQuiet` (no fill, muted text, a softer hairline) for a dismissal beside the chip it dismisses; `GapNudge` is its sentence over two `ActionChips` ("Add prices" and "Not now"), 40px tall and 105 to 121px wide. A thin switch row with counts (`PillOptions` with `isThin` and `detail`) takes `gap-x-1.5` and `px-2` below 360px (Pantry's four pills end at 299 of a 304px gutter at 320px, 321 before) and scrolls with the chosen pill kept in view from 390px; a caller that passes `wrapsNarrow` (Pantry's switch and Fridge & freezer's) wraps onto a second line below 390px when its pills do not fit (`max-[389px]:flex-wrap`, rows 16px apart), and an option's `detailUnit` and `ariaLabel` let a count lose its word: below 360px the unit and the pill's emoji (`data-pill-emoji`) are hidden ("Fridge · 631", named "Fridge, 631 servings"). Measured with the scale kitchen's counts (631 and 780 servings, 120 packages): Fridge & freezer is one line at 320 (181px of content), two rows at 360 (the unit-kept row is 337px against 328) and one line from 375; Pantry's four pills are two rows at 320 and 360 and one line from 375; Alex's counts fit one line at 320. Pantry puts Select and Add on the title row from `sm` (`isActionOnTitleRow`, as Fridge & freezer does) and drops its subtitle there; Pantry and Fridge & freezer put the search in the switch's row from `sm` while nothing is selected and no scope chip is on (`isInlineSearch`, a flex-wrap row so the field falls under the switch when it would be under 12rem). The View options eye is the 28px `HeaderIconButton` in a header cluster whenever no filter button sits beside a search field (Recipes: `hasViewOptions`, which is false only when a `FilterBar` carries it), so the Recipes, Saved meals and Ideas pill row is a plain 24px `PillGroup`.
- **Shared pieces and rules added in round 8 (fifth design round).** `BackLink` (`src/components/BackLink.tsx`) is the one Back of a swapped-in view: a `secondary`, `rounded='full'` `Button`, 28px (`h-7!`) with a `before:-inset-1.5` hit area (40px), `text-foreground!`, a leading `ChevronLeft` and a label that truncates ("Back" by default; "All the boxes", "All the packages"), `w-fit shrink-0 align-top`; it calls `useBackStep(true, onBack)` itself, so a view that draws one adds no step of its own. The six local copies (`ContainerSheet`, `MealDrawer`, `RecipeDrawer`, `PackageSheet`, `DayDrawer`, `PlannedMealView`) are gone, and `CollectionsSheet`, `CookingHistorySheet`, `CookEditView`, `IdeaAddSheet`, `EatFromStock`, `ContainerFormSheet`, `PlateRecipe` and Stores & prices' add step draw it, their parents' own `useBackStep` for the same view deleted so no step registers twice. Stores & prices' "Which ingredient is it for?" had no back step, so the gesture closed the whole "What are you adding?" sheet; it now steps back to the chooser first. The footer Backs of `AddToShoppingSheet` and `RecipeReader` (Cancel's place) and cooking mode's bordered "Back to the last step" are left as they were, and A-List's and Nine Lives' `variant='link'` Backs (`WatchlistItemDrawer`, `ViewingDrawer`, `AddFlow`, `VisitFormModal`), which have no back step, can adopt it. `NoteEntry` draws one `ActionChips` row, Update the recipe (a change only) then Edit, in place of a header with an Edit button (entry padding `py-3`, the date line `pb-2`). `RemovableField`'s ✕ stays 28px with a `before:-inset-x-2 before:-inset-y-1.5` pseudo-element, a 44×40 hit area that stops at the 6px gap above its input (Waypoint's nine forms and Pinch's ingredient and product sheets). `SectionHeader`'s subtitle is `text-balance`, so a two-line subtitle never leaves one word alone. `Pill` takes `ariaLabel` and tags its emoji `data-pill-emoji`. `RecipeForm` leaves the `nutrition` field out of `fields` while no metric is on (`selectShowNutrition`; `isNutritionShown` is in the memo's deps), the Form keeps `data.nutrition` from `initialData` so a saved override survives a save made while the section is hidden, and `NutritionReview` returns `null` for no cells. The picker rows are one shell, `PickRowShell` (a `min-h-10` text block, a trailing kind word in a 40px band, no chip; `py-1.5!`, because Dreamer UI's `Button` keeps its own `py-2` otherwise, which made the rows 57px and Pantry's add 419px), shared by every search result (an ingredient, a recipe, a saved meal, "New ingredient") and the suggestions, so a mixed list has one left edge (no row has a chip: an ingredient has no emoji of its own and a recipe's or saved meal's kind is said by its trailing word; "New ingredient" leads with a small plus inside its text). An ingredient's muted line is the family's name (`selectIngredientFamiliesById`), since families have no emoji; rows are 52px (53 with the divider), and under 500px of height (`useIsShortScreen(499)`) the suggestions and the typed results are 40px compact rows with the name only (41px with the divider; a typed recipe row that carries a fridge or freezer line keeps its two lines). `focus-visible:outline-2 -outline-offset-2` keeps a keyboard ring inside the frame's clipped corners. Dreamer UI's `Button` clones each element child to give it a `className`, so a Fragment child makes React warn on every update (and not on the first mount, which is why a census at load misses it): `StoreHeading` and `GroupRow` hand their buttons separate elements (`GroupRow` had wrapped its icon, content and chevron in a Fragment, which the saved-meal More and Share views logged), and Dreamer UI's `CopyButton`, whose child is a Fragment, logs the same on Waypoint's trip cards and Worth the Wait's layout. A modal's overflow is `scrollHeight - clientHeight` of its `fixed inset-0 overflow-y-auto` dialog, since its card is `overflow: visible`.
- **Shared pieces and rules added in round 8 (sixth design pass).** `GroupRow`'s `onClick` is optional, as `SummaryRow`'s already was: without one the row is a `div` with no chevron (the layout classes are shared by both renderings). `getDueText` (Fridge & freezer's tile) and `getPantryDueText` (Pantry's) put the clause past its date first, each clause's words unchanged, so a status cut to one line keeps the urgent count ("31 past their date, 37 to eat by tomorrow"; the accessible name carries the same text); `pinchMath.ts` and `pinchPantry.ts` check the exact strings and, at 37 and 31, that the text starts with the past-date clause. `RecipeFront` builds the figures once (the Cost tile when priced, the macro tiles, the "See what it costs" nudge) and the links once, and orders them by `isPriced`: Cost tile, nudge, macros, then links; with no Cost tile, links, then macros, then the nudge, so the nudge ends the body. `RecipeSteps`' row remove is an `X`, like the ingredient row's (both `size-10`, the same undo snackbar), while the red "Remove step" row in a step's drawer keeps its trash. `ui/ThemeToggle` (every app's header) draws its 36×20 switch with `after:-inset-x-2.5 after:-inset-y-2.5`, a 56×40 hit box, so 8px above, below and left of it still hit it. `PantrySubview` draws its subtitle only while the list is not shown (`!isListShown`: loading or a load error) with no package, not while selecting, and not when the actions sit on the title row from `sm`; an emptied Pantry is `EmptyCard icon='🥫'`, the first-run card Recipes, Kitchen and Shopping use, in place of a muted paragraph.
- **Shared pieces and rules added in round 8 (seventh design pass).** `CollectionRows` passes `onClick` only for a collection with other recipes in it. `CollectionsSheet`'s subtitle clamps to two lines (it carried `block`, which replaces the clamp's `display`), its list has the app's three-row floor (`[@media(max-height:800px)]:min-h-36`), its intro card is hidden below 700px of height (reference text, and at 320×568 it left the second row cut), and its chevron sits 12px in from the frame like the drawer's rows (it was 23). `ItemSearch`'s `isLandscape` (`useIsShortScreen(499)`) now compacts the typed rows too (saved meal, ingredient, "New ingredient", and a recipe unless `getStockParts` finds a fridge or freezer line), and Pantry's add, Stores & prices' add and Shopping's add pass `isCapped`, which gives the typed list `max-h-[max(12rem,calc(92dvh-22rem))]` with `overflow-y-auto` and, below 500px of height, `max-h-32` (three rows, 128px; it was 160px, and only Shopping had the first cap, so at 375×667, 390×560 and 320×568 Pantry's and Stores & prices' whole drawer scrolled after typing, 618/539 and 670/539 at 375×667). Measured after: at 375×667, 390×560, 320×568, 844×390 and 390×844 only the list scrolls (262, 192, 192, 128 and 424px), the dialog's scrollHeight equals its clientHeight and the field stays put, for Pantry, Stores & prices, Shopping and the Fridge add sheet; Shopping's two-line lead paragraph is hidden below 500px of height. The Fridge add sheet's typed list (`ContainerFormSheet`) has the same cap and, below 500px of height, 40px rows with a 32px chip (it was 533 against a 390px window at 844×390). Typing "c" at 844×390 took the overlay's `scrollHeight` from 694 to 390 for Pantry's add, 730 to 414 for Stores & prices' and 466 to 434 for Shopping's (Fridge add, with its own list, is 533); nothing at 390×560, 390×844 or 1280×900 moved. `RecipeIngredients`' `openPicker` sets `isAdding` and, at 599px tall or less (`useIsShortScreen(599)`), scrolls `pickerRef` into view (`block: 'start'`, `scroll-mt-16` for the 56px header) in an animation frame, only on the tap of "Add ingredient" from `sm` (the inline path): the field top is 175 then 64px at 844×390 and one suggestion row, then two, shows above the footer (a landscape phone offers two by design), with 1280×900 unchanged. `AuthAvatar` (the signed-out header) gives Home `after:-inset-x-1 after:-inset-y-2` (32×24 draws, 40×40 hits) and Sign in `after:inset-x-0 after:-inset-y-2` (48×32 draws, 48×48 hits; 107×40 and 107×56 at 1280), with the header unchanged to the pixel and the 8px between them split so neither steals a tap; the same in A-List and Waypoint. `DetailSheet`'s title-row actions wrapper is `relative z-10` (the star hits 40x40; they hit 40x36, and 40x31 on the saved meal at 320x568, under the body scroller). `SideSheet` (the header's menu) takes a history layer (`useSheetHistory`), so the back gesture closes it and the tab stays (it changed tab, or left the app signed out), and `AuthAvatar`'s signed-in Home icon has the same 40px pseudo-element hit area as the signed-out one. `GroupRow` passes `Button` its icon, content and chevron as separate children, since `Button` clones each child with a `className`, which a Fragment rejects.
- **Shared pieces and rules added in round 8 (third design pass).** `DetailSheet` on a screen 599px tall or less (`isCompact`, drawer path only) draws its title at `text-xl` with no top padding even when it has no `actions` (a custom heading then labels the dialog), wraps a footer in `px-2! py-2! [&>div]:pb-0!` (the library clones `px-6 py-4` onto it, and the call sites' own `pb-4` made 72px of it) and lifts its content 12px (`-mt-3`) into the library's 24px of top padding; `FormSheet`'s phone drawer lifts its body the same 12px at that height. `index.css` caps every drawer at `calc(100dvh - 0.5rem)` at that height (92dvh above it) and gives the library's three close buttons (`data-drawer-close-button`, `data-modal-close-button`, `data-panel-close-button`) a 40×40 hit area by `::before` at `inset: -8.5px`, so the X (22×22) keeps its look and no layout moves; the rule is global, so it was re-driven on an A-List drawer and modal and a Waypoint sheet. `RecipeFront` (`components/recipes`) is the recipe's front (source and collections chips, description and facts, ingredients, steps, keeping it, cost, macros, links) taken out of `RecipeDrawer`: the drawer passes its own photo and collections views, the list-narrowing doors and the fill-in as optional props, and a front without them draws the cover, the "In 3 collections" pill, the source and the "Add prices" nudge as plain content, so one recipe has one front wherever it is read. On a short wide screen (`isLandscape`: 599px tall or less and from `sm`) it puts the description (one line, still with "Show more") and the facts on one line and keeps the Ingredients caption for screen readers only (`FormSection`'s `isTitleHidden`). `PlateRows` and `PlateRecipeView` (`components/recipes/PlateRecipe`) are a plate's rows and the in-place recipe: the saved meal's drawer (`MealDrawer`, `openRecipeId`, `viewKey` `recipe:{id}`) and the planned meal's view (`PlannedMealSubview` `recipe`) swap to `PlateRecipeView` (a `BackLink` that registers `useBackStep`, the recipe's name as the heading, `RecipeFront` with no door props) and drop their footer while it shows (`DayDrawer` passes none for that view); a row is a `GroupRow` only when `recipesById` still has the recipe, so an ingredient or a removed recipe is a plain row, and a planned item's `PlannedCookRow` sits under its row inside the same frame. `DayDrawer` draws its cost tile after the meals on a short screen, `IngredientStock` puts its caption and summary on one line, and `PlannedMealView` puts ‹ Back and the meal's name on one line, all by `[@media(max-height:599px)]` classes or `useIsShortScreen(599)`. `ShoppingPick` (the add-to-shopping sheet) lays its top out for `useIsShortScreen()` (700px): the name over "Adding 3 of 5" (no hint line) with "Tick all" at its end, the servings stepper under it (beside the chip from `sm` up, where the modal has the room), and the "You already have 5 servings…" sentence as one `StockNote` row, truncated to a line with a chevron that opens it in place (hidden under 500px tall, as the sentence was); a range keeps its summary row and count line. `ContainerSheet`'s filled button follows `getSelectionPrimary` (see above) and `PackageSheet`'s date is a plain pill: the "Change the date" view and its pencil are gone (Modify's form has the field, and Move it keeps its reveal chip). Toss is a `Ban` glyph in both sheets and in both Select bars, so the footer's trash is the only trash. `CookingSubview`'s footer is a two-column grid mirroring the step layout from `lg` while steps show (Back and Next sit under the step, not under both columns), and capped to `max-w-md` under the centred finish panel.
- Rows are top-aligned (`items-start`): `RecipeRow`, `MealRow` and the calendar's `MealRow` put the image or tile's top edge level with the title's and keep the chevron on the first line; `IngredientRow` and the Stores & prices rows do the same with the chevron and, on Stores & prices, the price and its unit price.
- A `StatTile` with a help icon keeps `pr-6` clear of it on a phone (the icon sits in the tile's corner); the summary's figures from $1,000 up drop their cents through `formatPeriodCost`, so a long total stays clear of the icon. A row of three (the Calendar's stats) passes `isLabelWrapped`, which lets a label wrap under its number and keeps the `pr-6` on the number's line only, so a long label doesn't shrink to make room for an icon beside the number above it; every other tile keeps its one-line label.
- The recipe step drawer focuses its text box about 350 ms after it opens, once the drawer has finished sliding in, rather than with `autoFocus`.
- Lists search from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` rows; Kitchen groups by family with `SectionDivider` and mounts a family group only near the viewport (`LazyMount`); the shopping tab groups by shop section (dividers from 8 rows) and mounts each section's rows in chunks of 12 near the viewport.
- Images from URLs (a recipe cover, a store logo) are `FallbackImage` with a themed fallback (a tinted emoji tile, a letter tile).
- Delete confirmations go through `useActionModal().confirm` with `destructive: true` and say what archiving means when something is in use.

## Theming

- `src/apps/pinch/pinch.css` overrides Dreamer UI's semantic tokens on `html.pinch-theme`: paper-cream (warm black in dark) surfaces with hairline borders, an espresso-ink `--color-primary` for the one filled action, see-through secondary buttons, peach tints (`--color-accent`, `--pinch-drizzle-soft`) for chips, groups and the selected pill, and warm orange (`--pinch-drizzle`) as the accent (today dot and the Week's "Today" pill, selected outline, active tab, the default price's star); `--radius-md` and up raised for a rounded counter feel; field text inset; hairline option pills that take a peach tint and a drizzle outline when selected; tertiary buttons use the normal text colour. `usePinchTheme()` adds the class to `<html>` only while the app is mounted.
- **Round ticks.** Every Pinch checkbox is Dreamer UI's `Checkbox` (a `button[role=checkbox]`), made round by one rule on `html.pinch-theme`: radius 9999px, a 1.5px `--color-muted-foreground` ring, and when ticked or mixed a `--color-primary` fill (with `!important`, because the component sets its background inline) and a `--color-primary-foreground` tick; keyboard focus keeps a primary outline (offset 2px, round); an `::after` (`inset: -11.5px`) makes each 20×20 box a 40×40 target. No other app is touched (the class is only on `<html>` while Pinch is mounted, and stays there under every Pinch overlay: a confirm and a `HelpTip` modal included). A mark that says something is done (a cooked meal or recipe) is the shared `TickMark` (`src/components`: a `bg-primary` circle with a `text-primary-foreground` tick, sized in `em` to its text, `aria-hidden` beside words that say it), never the ✅ emoji, which draws a green square tick box. Round 7's census drove every screen, sheet and state that can hold a tick at 390 and 1280, light and dark: the four `Checkbox` call sites (`ShoppingRow`, `AddToShoppingSheet` with its "From your plan" twin, `ProductFormSheet`'s "Make this the default", `CookIngredientsView`) were already round (radius 9999px, 20×20, a 43×43 target, ticked, unticked and disabled), no `FormFactories.checkbox`, `checkboxGroup`, `input[type=checkbox]` or `menuitemcheckbox` exists in Pinch or the shared pieces it renders (a `Select`'s chosen option is a bare ✓), and the square ones were the ✅ marks in the Week row, the day drawer, the planned meal and the recipe drawer.
- Meal-type colours are tokens, not raw palette classes (`--pinch-meal-breakfast … --pinch-meal-drink`, each with a `-soft` tint for the calendar's chips and pills), checked on a chip and a pill in light and dark.
- The Pinch rules file is written in the same PR as the shell: `.github/instructions/pinch.instructions.md` with an identical `.claude/rules/pinch.md` (metaphor: a warm kitchen; palette roles; shape language; what the emoji mean; calendar chip and pill anatomy; nudge wording; missing details appear only as in-context nudge lines).

---

## Security & Privacy Requirements

- **Private by construction, shared only by a link the person makes.** A person's kitchen is readable and writable only by them. There is no admin read; Security Rules Design Criteria #1–2 are the whole access model for it. The only thing that is public is a recipe or saved meal the person chooses to share: a copy a Function made through an allowlist (§5k, criteria 20 and 21), readable by anyone who holds its unguessable link, never a read of the kitchen. Nothing is shared unless the person taps Create link.
- **What leaves the app, and where it goes.** (1) Pasted text or a chosen file (a photo, screenshot or PDF) goes to Firebase AI (Gemini) to be read into a recipe draft; the provider sees the content, not the person's identity beyond Firebase's own request metadata, and the screen says an AI reads it and that it isn't saved. A reading can hand back how long a dish keeps and how to reheat it, but only what the source says, and a recipe's own storage notes are never sent anywhere. (2) A recipe link goes to our `fetchPageContent` function, which fetches the page from Google's servers (signed-in only, http/https only, private networks refused) and returns only what Pinch asks for (the page's text, its title and meta description, and any Recipe data) without storing anything; the client then sends that content, not the link, to the AI reader. The exception is a YouTube link, which is not fetched at all: its address goes to the AI reader, and Google fetches the video itself. (3) Store logos are requested by the person's browser from the logo service by domain only (well-known stores only); a person's own stores never leave Storage. (4) A recipe idea's link goes to the same `fetchPageContent`, asking for the page's `meta` only (its title, site name and picture address); nothing is read by an AI model, and what is stored from the answer is those three. When the idea is shown, the person's browser requests its picture address from that website (through `FallbackImage`, with no referrer). (5) While cooking mode's voice control is on, the person's browser sends what the microphone hears, other people's voices included, to its own speech service to turn it into text (Chrome's goes to Google, Safari's to Apple, Edge's to Microsoft); Pinch only receives the text, matches it against its commands and keeps none of it, and the first tap says so before the browser asks for the microphone. Answers and, when asked, steps are read aloud by the browser's voice, which prefers one on the device; where the browser only has a network voice, the text read goes to that voice's vendor, and the first tap says that too. (6) A reminder is a push to the person's own registered devices through Firebase Cloud Messaging (Google's push service for Chrome and Android, Apple's for Safari): the morning note carries at most two dish or ingredient names and a count, a timer push the step's name and length, and the person's settings page says the dish names show on a lock screen; the function that sends the note reads only that person's settings, boxes and (with the pantry switch on) packages, and nothing else about them is sent. (7) A new recipe's Photo drawer sends the recipe's title and the word "recipe" to `searchRecipeImages`, which passes that text, and nothing else (no uid, no email), to DuckDuckGo or Google Programmable Search and returns up to six pictures; the line under the block's heading says so, and Settings (or the block's Turn off chip) stops it. The thumbnails it lists are loaded by the person's browser from third-party image hosts (a search engine's thumbnail proxy in practice), which tells those hosts the person's address; the full picture loads only after a tap, from the site that hosts it, and a chosen one is shown from that site on every visit (through `FallbackImage`, no referrer). Only its address is stored, and the picture belongs to its site. (8) A recipe or saved meal the person chooses to share goes to `changePinchShare`, which copies it through an allowlist into `shareSnapshots`, and from there to anyone who opens the link (through `getPinchShare`, with the cover from our own Storage); the Share view says what is in the copy and what is left out before "Create link", and the link can be updated, replaced or stopped at any time. A visitor's page loads our callable, our Storage and our own logo, and nothing from a third party. Nothing else leaves: there is no copy-out of plans, ingredient lists, ideas or the shopping list, and no copy-out of a recipe except a share.
- **What a share stores and shows, field by field.** *Stored in `shares`, readable by its owner only:* `id`, `ownerUid`, `kind`, `sourceId`, `title`, `sourceUpdatedAt`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`. *Stored in `shareSnapshots` and shown to anyone with the link, a recipe, included:* title, description, servings, prep and cook minutes, each ingredient line (name, a `cat-` catalog id when it has one, amount, unit, note, group), steps, care (fridge and freezer days, reheat, tip), links (https only: kind, label, address) and the cover the person uploaded (everything needed to read it, cook it and make a copy). *Excluded, with the reason:* the owner's uid, name, email and photo (whose it is); prices, products, stores and costs (money); notes, tweaks, cooking history and the favorite star (opinions and private records); collections, the source label and ideas (the owner's own organisation); nutrition (a number the visitor can't derive, and their own copy works out its own); a web-search cover (a third-party site the visitor's browser would load); and every id of the owner's records, ingredient, recipe or box (they say nothing a visitor needs and a lot about how the kitchen is built). *A saved meal, included:* its name, each recipe as above (once), each item's servings and its plain ingredient items by name; *excluded:* the star, ids, plan dates, shopping state and cost. The allowlist is in the client's builders and again in the Function, and runs on write and on serve (§5k). Typed titles, notes and steps are shown as written, as text, and the Share view says what is in the copy and what is left out before it is made.
- **What a link can't protect against.** Holding the address is the only credential: no PIN, no expiry, no attempt limit and no way to see who opened it, so a link that was forwarded works until its owner stops or replaces it ("Get a new link" is the answer to a link that went somewhere it shouldn't). A visitor who adds the recipe keeps their own copy after the link is stopped. The preview a chat app draws is generic ("A recipe shared on Pinch"), but the address itself travels through whatever the person sends it by. A stopped link, a mistyped one and one that never existed answer alike, so the page can't be used to find out which ids exist.
- **The logo service's key is a publishable browser token**, domain-restricted at the provider, kept in an env var (`VITE_STORE_LOGO_TOKEN`) and sent only in the logo request's URL. It is not a secret and is not a Functions secret; if the chosen provider needs a secret, the logo route moves behind an `onCall` instead.
- **Stored data minimization.** No location, no payment details, no card data, no receipt images are stored. A product stores store, price, size and an optional link; a recipe stores what the person typed or reviewed (its storage notes are days and two lines of their own words, at most 300 characters each), and a source is only a label they typed and a cook only which recipe, when and how many servings, and a note only the person's own words (up to 2,000 characters), the cook it follows and, for a tweak, up to eight short lines naming what they changed in the recipe (ingredient names, amounts, and the first words of a step).
- **Persistence on the device.** Nothing from the person's own documents is persisted beyond Firestore's own cache. No TanStack query in the MVP carries `meta: { persist: true }` (the shared page's queries, which hold someone's recipe and a link's secret in their key, least of all); the catalog is code, not a query. The one thing Pinch writes to the browser itself is each list's View options, as small JSON in `localStorage` under `pinch:{uid}:view:{recipes|ingredients|calendar}` (Recipes and Saved meals share the first; the Calendar's holds `showPrices`, the price on each Week meal row, through `useListViewOptions(uid, 'calendar')`; a stored `showStats` (the tile row is `PinchSettings.calendarStats` now, saved with the account) and round 7's `showAverages` are ignored, and the old keys are dropped by the next write), and the calendar's view under `pinch:{uid}:calendar-view` (`"month"` or `"week"`): display and device choices and no kitchen data; the tabs' session (scroll, pill, search, period) lives in memory only (`PinchSessionContext`); the key carries the uid, so someone else signing in on the same browser doesn't read it, and nothing clears these on sign-out (only keys ending in `-session` are, see the saved cooking session). Cooking mode writes three booleans for voice commands, `pinch:{uid}:voice:agreed` (the explainer was answered with Turn on), `pinch:{uid}:voice:read-aloud` and `pinch:{uid}:voice:wake` (listen for "Hey Pepper" rather than one window per tap; the fifteen-minute lock is never stored), through `useLocalStoragePreference`; it never stores what was heard.
- **Links are https-only** and shown with `ExternalLinkText`. An idea's link may be http (a page that has no https version still opens) but only http(s), never `javascript:` or `data:` (the field, `createIdea` and the rules all check), and it opens in a new tab with `rel='noopener noreferrer'`; its picture address must be https, in the client and in the rules, and only `FallbackImage` ever loads it.
- **What a recipe idea stores, field by field.** `title`, `note` (the person's own words, shown only to them), `kind`, `url` (the link they pasted, without sign-in details), `siteName`, `imageUrl` and the page's title when the person kept it, `photoPath` (their own picture in their own Storage folder), `recipeId` and the two times. No location, no author, no page text or description. Nothing about an idea is shared, public or copied out.
- **What a note stores, field by field.** `recipeId`, `kind`, `text` (the person's own words, shown only to them), `changes` (lines the app composed from the recipe the person already owns: an ingredient's name, an amount, a time, a step's first 80 characters), `cookId` and the two times. No location, no author, no photo. A note is never shared, sent to an AI or put in a link, and it isn't in any push or email; deleting a recipe deletes its notes.
- **What a container stores, field by field.** `title` (a recipe's or meal's name, or what the person typed), `recipeId`, `mealId` and `cookId` (ids of their own documents), `servings`, `place`, `status`, `eatBy` (a day), `history` (what happened and when: kinds, instants, portions, a place, and an EDIT's note of two dates) and the timestamps. Nothing about money, location, other people or food safety, and nothing leaves the app but the morning note's dish names: there is no copy or export of a container, and a share carries no box.
- **Rules are verified against the emulator, not by the UI hiding a control:** the owner allowed and a second signed-in user denied on every path (kitchen document and all thirteen collections, plus Storage), signed-out denied; and one denied write per integrity rule (snapshot overwrite without the re-price marker, a re-price that also changes the meal, `priceCents` negative, `sizeAmount` 0, an unknown `unit`, a `date` that isn't UTC midnight, a 61st recipe ingredient, an `http://` link).
- **The share's denials are verified the same way, with the base rules beside the branch's:** against the emulator, as the owner, as another signed-in user and signed out, over Firestore (the owner reads their own share; another user and a signed-out client are denied; no one writes a share, owner included, and no one reads or writes a snapshot) and Storage (a signed-out `get` of a cover allowed; a `list` of `apps/pinch/shares/` or of a folder, a write and a delete denied), and through the callables (the response body of `getPinchShare` read as the owner, as another user and signed out for the owner's uid, the source id outside the owner's answer and any forbidden key; a malformed, unknown and deleted id byte-identical; `update`, `renew` and `delete` on someone else's share answering as for a missing one). Then the link itself is followed: opened signed in, with every store the shell writes to (presence, storage, logs) searched for the id. This is the matrix the PR's `## Regression check` records. It has been run against the emulators for everything above except the Storage half under the base rules; the outcomes (only the owner's read of their own share differs from the base rules, and the link's id is in the document URL, the callable body and the Storage path only) are under Verified in §5k.

- **Terms and licences are a gate, not a footnote:** the USDA FoodData Central licence (catalog nutrition) and the logo service's terms and attribution are each confirmed in the PR that ships the thing that uses them, with the logo credit in "About the numbers", the last line of every View options sheet. Pinch vendors no other third-party asset since the ingredient icons were removed.

---

## Performance

- **Scale fixture.** `npm run seed:pinch-scale` (`scripts/seeds/pinchScale.ts`, opt-in) fills Taylor's kitchen: 300 custom ingredients across 40 families, 150 products across 12 stores, 200 recipes of 3–8 ingredients (about seven in eight with one of 12 sources, one of them with a 73-character label, the first holding the most), 60 saved meals, about 1,100 planned meals from 45 days back to 45 days ahead (12 a day), a shopping list of 80 items across 25 families (every fifth one checked off; the 48 weighed ones say what they are for, from the planned meals of the next 14 days that use them, a few saved meals and two recipes, one added at twice its servings and one in the older shape with no servings, with amounts that add up to those shares: up to 20 sources a row, 155 on the list, a recipe planned on two days) and 80 recipe ideas (two links in three, a third of the links with a picture address that can't load offline, every seventh title over 100 characters, every ninth with a long note, every tenth already made into a recipe). Every list or per-row feature is opened on it at phone width (390×844) with the dev build throttled 4× (CDP `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`), switching Calendar ↔ Recipes ↔ Kitchen. The numbers from the last pass are in the PR that added it.
- **Collections at scale.** A kitchen of 456 recipes with a collection of 450 of them (Jamie's, on the emulator; Taylor's seed gets twelve collections over its 200 recipes when it is reseeded), 4× CPU throttle: picking the 441-recipe collection in the filter took about 570 ms to show its chip and rows, a 2-recipe one about 240 ms, back to Any about 520 ms, and a tab switch there and back stayed under a second beyond the helper's own wait; 20 rows are mounted when the list opens (the chunks mount near the viewport). The filter's options, the counts and the delete's chunk list are one pass over the recipes each, nothing runs per row, and a recipe row has no collection chip (they are in the drawer). Deleting that collection ran three transactions (200, 200 and 50 recipes) in 15 s on the loaded emulator and reported 450.
- **Never freeze the same meal twice, and freeze in batches.** The load-time freeze of ~540 past meals took the page down twice over: a write per meal raised one snapshot event each, and the list of unfrozen meals shrinking after each event started another freeze of the rest (quadratic writes, most of them refused by the write-once rule). It is now one batch per 100 meals (one event each), each meal is submitted once per visit (`useFreezePastDays`), and a refused batch falls back to a write per meal.
- **One pass, then lookups.** `selectRecipeCosts`, `selectPeriodStats` and `selectSourceCounts` compute in one walk and are memoized; no cell, row or drawer recomputes a recipe's cost. The Calendar's `renderCell` reads `selectPlannedByDay` only.
- **Build once.** The currency `Intl.NumberFormat` and the catalog lookup maps are module-level; the search index is built on first use.
- **Cap what's drawn.** A row is as tall as its lines (no `min-h` under a lone title), so the height estimates follow the lines: `FamilyGroup` adds up 20px for the divider plus 45px per ingredient (44 plus its rule) and 16px for each of the price and nutrition lines that will show, and the recipe list adds a price and a macro line to its cover-sized rows; both are within about 1% of what is measured at 390px. Kitchen mounts a family group near the viewport and the recipe list mounts twenty rows at a time (`LazyMount`); recipe and ingredient pickers show the top matches; the summary sheet lists one period's days. A calendar cell builds a hover popover only on a computer, and the card's content only while it is open; a phone's cell is just its chips. A week's agenda shows six rows a day then "Show N more", and Stores & prices caps its rows at 60 with "Show 60 more". The `LazyMount` height estimates follow the View options (a row with its price, macro or stock line hidden is shorter, and a hidden stock line adds nothing).
- **What is not ours.** A cold first load of the fixture (about 1,900 documents) spends most of its time inside the Firestore SDK ingesting them into its local cache, and so does paging far enough to move the planned-meals window (the listener restarts). Both are measured in the PR and left alone; later visits read the persistent cache.
- **Keep the tap instant.** Each tab renders in a transition and is wrapped in `memo`; the day and recipe drawers read from the store at open, not from their parents.
- **The shopping list at scale.** Groups are built in one pass by a selector, a row is a `memo` component with plain-value props and a group is a `memo` component with a comparison over its rows' ids, names, amounts, units and checked flags, so a tick re-renders the group the row left and its own row and not the other groups, a section's rows mount in chunks of 12 near the viewport (the first chunk of the first three sections at once), and a section draws 100 rows. The count left to buy is read by the tab's badge and by the Shopping screen only, through their own selectors, so Calendar, Recipes and Kitchen never subscribe to a tick (a tab mounts only while it is the active one). The sheets that add a recipe's or a day's lines are measured with the fixture's busiest day (77 lines): one scrolling list, the header and footer fixed. Checked on the fixture at 390×844 with the dev build at 4× CPU: opening the list, a tick, opening the add sheet from a recipe and from a busy day, and the three tab switches; the numbers are in the PR. With the list's sources (round 7 fixes), the names work over three Calendar → Shopping switches at 4× took 42 ms of self time before each row's line was worked out once per change of names and 13 ms after; the tab switch itself is around 300 ms either way at this size, since the names were a small part of it.
- **A tick was slow, and why.** Measured on a kitchen with 300 ingredients, 150 products and an 80-item list (16 ticked) at 390×844 with the dev build at 4× CPU, click event to the next painted frame: a tick took a median of about 595 ms over 24 ticks (435 to 785 ms) while the list was a subview. A count of component renders per tick (a stub React DevTools hook that tallies the fibers that rendered) found 294 to 319: Kitchen re-rendered with its 40 family groups because it read the open count and stays mounted under a subview, and every shopping group re-rendered because the selector builds new group objects on each snapshot, so `memo` on the group never held. The fixes: the count lives in the tab's badge and the list screen only, the list is a tab (so nothing else is mounted while it is open), and a group compares its rows. Now a tick renders 24 to 51 components and takes a median of about 150 ms (87 to 240 ms, 8 ticks). Switching into the Shopping tab takes 360 to 700 ms at 4× and back to Kitchen 660 to 900 ms, which is mounting the 300-ingredient Kitchen and was not changed here.
- **Recipe ideas at scale.** The list draws rows in chunks of twenty that mount near the viewport (`LazyMount` with `defer-offscreen`; the first chunk is eager), a row is as tall as its lines, and each row's picture is a lazy `FallbackImage` (a photo idea adds one cached download-URL query), so 80 ideas mount 20 rows at first and the rest as they are scrolled to (checked on the fixture: 20 at first, all 80 reachable, 8 under "Made into recipes"). Measured on the fixture at 390×844 with the dev build at 4× CPU, loaded unthrottled and throttled only after the page had rendered, median of 5 rounds: the Ideas pill 317 ms until its first rows are drawn, "New idea" 267 ms until the list of ways shows, tapping an idea 204 ms until its drawer shows, back to the Recipes pill 482 ms. The three tab switches, median of 7 rounds each against the same data: Recipes to Kitchen 866 ms (base 922), Kitchen to Calendar 568 ms (base 588), Calendar to Recipes 607 ms (base 661), so they are unchanged within the noise of the throttled run (single rounds reach 1.0 to 1.5 s on the base too). No "Maximum update depth" or render storm in the console.
- **Cooking mode adds nothing to the screens that stay mounted.** It mounts only while open, reads three existing selectors (recipes, ingredients and the per-recipe cook counts) and, through `CookStepDock` and `RecipeNotes`, the notes selectors and starts no listener (one `store.subscribe` callback runs the memoized recipe selector on a store change, to remember the last recipe); the overlay context value gained one function, `replaceSubviewIf`, which is a stable callback and adds no state, so no tab re-renders for it. At 4× CPU on Alex's kitchen with a 40-ingredient recipe, click to the next painted frame took a median of 268 ms to open it, 72 ms for Next, 382 ms for the ingredients view with its 40 rows and 86 ms to come back to the steps (six rounds each), and the three tab switches stayed at 45 to 63 ms. The scale fixture wasn't loaded when this was measured, so the tabs were not checked against it. A plate session reads the cooks and cook-count maps once for all its recipes and memoizes the per-recipe records; on Taylor's scale kitchen at 4× CPU (390×844) Start cooking on a two-recipe plate painted the session in 609 ms and switching recipes took 127 to 185 ms (six switches).
- **Notes at scale.** Taylor's fixture holds about 310 notes across 160 of the 200 recipes (one has twelve, for "Show N more"; a fifth are tweaks, one with the eight-line maximum, and some follow one of the recipe's cooks), generated by their own random sequence so the rest of the fixture is unchanged. The summary and grouping are built once per snapshot, the drawer draws five entries and mounts the rest on "Show N more", `RecipeRow` is now `memo`, and a note's own text measures its clamp once per entry. Measured at 4× CPU on that fixture at 390px, click to the next change on screen (medians): the recipe drawer with twelve notes opened in about 260 ms and the note sheet from it in about 180 ms (five rounds); a save of a cooked recipe that records a tweak took about 1.3 s, and about 1.8 s against 1.5 s for the same edit to a recipe with no cook on another pair of recipes (both include `freezeBeforeEdit` and the write's round trip to the emulator; five rounds); the four tab switches took 0.47 to 0.82 s over eight rounds, the largest single one 1.24 s (0.56 to 0.9 s was normal before). Un-throttled, with the whole list unfiltered, opening and closing a drawer or adding a note re-rendered no `RecipeRow` (40 before the `memo`), and a recipe save re-rendered the 20 mounted rows once or twice, as it does without notes, because each recipe snapshot gives every recipe a new object. No render storm or "Maximum update depth" appeared in any run.
- **Care at scale.** Half of Taylor's 200 fixture recipes carry care (a fifth with all four parts, a fifth fridge-only, the rest in the older shape with no key), generated without a random draw so the rest of the fixture is unchanged. Care adds no listener, selector or per-row work: the drawer reads `getRecipeCare` for its one recipe when it renders, a list row never reads it, and cooking mode derives one string from the recipe it already holds. Measured at 390×844 with the dev build at 4× CPU (throttled only after the page had rendered), click to the next change on screen, medians of 7 rounds, two passes alternating the notes branch (before) and this branch (after) on the same data: opening a recipe drawer took 162 and 226 ms before and 160 and 204 ms after for a recipe with all four parts, 208 and 240 before and 206 and 235 after for a fridge-only one, and 182 and 214 before and 227 and 223 after for one in the older shape; the four tab switches took 415 to 969 ms before and 331 to 883 ms after, so everything is level within the noise of a shared machine (single rounds reach 1.0 to 1.6 s on both builds). No "Maximum update depth" or render storm appeared in any run. The fixture's other agents were using the same emulator while this ran, so absolute numbers are loose and only the before and after of one pass are comparable.
- **Fridge & freezer boxes at scale.** Taylor's fixture holds about 300 containers in stock (a recipe with twelve, to see its row, drawer rows and put-away history with many containers at once; three in ten recipes with none, ten saved meals with one; some fridge ones past their date) and 260 finished ones, from their own random sequence so the rest of the fixture is unchanged. `getStockMaps` is one pass into two `Map`s per snapshot, each row subscribes to its own entry with `shallowEqual` (a container changing re-renders only the rows whose numbers moved), `RecipesScreen`'s chunk height estimate adds a line only for rows that have stock, and the stock subview draws 20 rows per place with "Show N more". Finished containers are never held live: the page is read when the view opens; grouping 300 boxes into batches is one pass (`pinchMath` times it). Measured numbers are in the PR.
- **The pantry at scale.** Taylor's fixture holds 120 packages over his first 60 ingredients (8 on one ingredient, two on most, one on six; some past their date or due within three days; the three kinds of measure mixed on a few so a row has to say "12.4 kg and 7.5 l in all"), written by their own sequence so the rest of the fixture is unchanged. One pass per snapshot builds `selectPackagesByIngredient` and one pass per scope builds the rows; a row is a `memo` component with plain-value props, the list draws 20 rows then "Show N more" (and mounts a package's rows only while its sheet is open), the pantry reads no other store than packages, ingredients and the lead days, and a recipe's markers read the one map. Measured at 390×844 with the dev build at 4× CPU on a copy of Taylor's real kitchen in a private emulator (the shared one was down for a while and I had first used a synthetic copy), with the 120 packages added, click to the next change on screen, a machine running nine other dev servers, so absolute numbers are high and the comparison is the point. Tab switches, medians of two rounds, the pantry branch against the commit it was cut from, run alternately: Kitchen to Recipes 1,313 against 1,300 ms, Recipes to Shopping 649 against 1,169, Shopping to Kitchen 1,216 against 1,758, Kitchen to Calendar 1,041 against 1,329, Calendar to Kitchen 1,312 against 1,295: no regression, and both sit at or just over the one-second bar on this load, which is not the pantry's to fix. The pantry itself, one run each: the Pantry tile to its rows 813 ms (Settings opens in 924 ms on the same page, so a subview's cost is the shell's), the 8-package ingredient's sheet 304 ms, the Select icon to select mode 722 ms (a checkbox per package of the 20 rows; dev JSX and native layout; no render storm, no `Maximum update depth`). A `useTransition` around `selection.start` is the first thing to try if select mode is ever too slow in a production build.
- **Cooks at scale.** Taylor's fixture holds about 330 cooks across 200 recipes (one recipe has twelve, for "Show N more"), generated by their own random sequence so the rest of the fixture is unchanged. The summary map is built once per snapshot, a Recipes row reads no cook count, a planned meal's rows re-render with any cook change (a meal has a handful of them), and the history sheet mounts only while open and draws eight rows first. The numbers are in the PR.



---
